# PROJECT_CONTEXT.md

Living status file for the Uber-like ride-hailing learning project. Claude Code reads this at the start of every session and updates it after every milestone. Milestone definitions live in `CLAUDE.md`.

## Project summary

Uber-like ride-hailing web app for learning. FastAPI + PostgreSQL/PostGIS + Redis backend with layered structure (routers, services, repositories), plain HTML/CSS/JS frontend (rider, driver, admin), simulated drivers, no ML layer.

## Current status

- Current phase: **Phase 7 (Observability, Load Testing, Polish) has started.** Phases 0 to 6 are complete (the MVP, real-time, concurrency hardening, pricing and payments, ratings, the admin dashboard, history). **M7.1 adds JSON logs with a request id and ride ids and no personal data, a token-protected `/metrics`, ride transitions counted after the commit by a session hook, a gauge task for database state, and an optional Prometheus + Grafana stack under the `observability` compose profile with a provisioned dashboard. The chaos scenario of `stress.py` compares the metrics with the database (`--metrics-token`).**
- Last completed milestone: M7.3 Documentation (2026-10-10; the demo recording not done). Before it: M7.1 Logging and metrics (2026-10-10). **M7.2 Load test is PARTLY DONE (2026-10-10): the load stack, the harness, the validation and a 3-run baseline exist; it was stopped on request before any bottleneck experiment, fix, after-fix run or revert run.**
- **M7.3 Documentation is DONE except the demo recording (2026-10-10):** `README.md` was restructured (what it is, two Mermaid architecture and ride diagrams, quick start, tour of the three apps, "How it works", the load test numbers and the open question, a demo script), with all older sections kept below a "Reference" heading. **The recording itself is a manual step and is NOT done.**
- Next milestone: nothing is required. Optional: record the demo (README "Demo script"), or finish M7.2 (the three one-variable experiments, a written prediction, ONE fix, 3 after-fix runs and a revert run; see `loadtest/RESULTS.md`)
- Last updated: 2026-10-10

## What exists now

- **Services (Docker Compose):** `db` (postgis/postgis:16-3.4, volume `postgres_data`, port 5432), `redis` (redis:7-alpine, port 6379), `osrm` (`ghcr.io/project-osrm/osrm-backend:v26.10.0-debian`, `osrm-routed --algorithm mld /data/city.osrm`, data mounted read-only from `./osrm/data`, published on `127.0.0.1:5000` for debugging only, no healthcheck), `backend` (FastAPI, port 8000, `uvicorn --reload --ws-max-size 65536`; the Dockerfile CMD has the same flag; since M4.2 it also publishes `127.0.0.1:8001` for a SECOND uvicorn process started by hand, see "How to run and test"). Backend waits for db and redis to be healthy. It does not wait for osrm: with OSRM down the API starts and routing answers 502. The `osrm` container exits with an error until `osrm/prepare.sh` has been run
- **Tables** (migrations `0001_initial_schema`, `0002_unique_vehicle_per_driver`, the data-only `0003_seed_economy_pricing_rule`, `0004_ride_offers`, and `0005_safety_net_unique_indexes` (M4.3, indexes only, no data change), and `0006_final_fare_and_cancellation_fee` (M5.1: five columns plus one data statement that sets `fare_breakdown = {"kind": "legacy"}` on every COMPLETED and CANCELLED ride that existed then: 448 + 9916 in dev); ids are integer PKs, all `created_at` are timestamptz default `now()`, money is integer paise):
  - `users`: role (rider/driver/admin), name, email (unique), phone (unique, nullable), password_hash
  - `drivers`: user_id (FK users, unique), license_number, verification_status (pending/approved/rejected, default pending)
  - `vehicles`: driver_id (FK drivers, unique `uq_vehicles_driver_id`: one vehicle per driver), plate_number (unique, stored normalized: uppercase, no spaces or hyphens), model, color, vehicle_type (default economy)
  - `rides`: rider_id (FK users), driver_id (FK drivers, nullable), pickup/dropoff lat, lng, address, status (default REQUESTED), distance_m, duration_s, fare_estimate, final_fare, otp, started_at, completed_at, and since M5.1 (migration `0006`) `actual_distance_m` (int, the BILLED distance), `actual_duration_s` (int), `fare_breakdown` (JSONB). Indexed: status, rider_id, driver_id. `otp` (String(4), nullable) is the trip code: `1234` from DRIVER_ASSIGNED until the trip starts, NULL again once the ride is IN_PROGRESS or CANCELLED (M3.5, no migration: the column already existed) **M4.3 indexes:** `uq_rides_one_active_per_driver` (unique on driver_id WHERE status IN DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, IN_PROGRESS) and `uq_rides_one_active_per_rider` (unique on rider_id WHERE status IN REQUESTED, DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, IN_PROGRESS); the conditions are plain strings because the enum values are stored as upper-case names (checked in psql).
  - **M5.2 (migration `0007_surge_zones`, no data statement):** `rides.pickup_zone` (String(12), nullable: the geohash of the pickup at precision 5, NULL for rides from before M5.2) and `rides.surge_percent` (int, not null, server default 100: the multiplier locked at request time, 100 is no surge), check constraint `ck_rides_surge_percent_range` (`surge_percent BETWEEN 100 AND 200`) and index `ix_rides_created_at` (the demand query). The check constraint is written by hand in the migration (Alembic autogenerate does not detect check constraints). `fare_estimate` is now the SURGED estimate.
  - `ride_events`: ride_id (FK, indexed), from_status (nullable), to_status, actor_user_id (FK users, nullable)
  - `ride_offers` (M3.3, migration `0004`): ride_id (FK rides), driver_id (FK drivers, indexed `ix_ride_offers_driver_id`), status (PENDING/ACCEPTED/REJECTED/EXPIRED/CANCELLED, default PENDING, stored as VARCHAR(9) with the enum NAME, which equals the value), pickup_distance_m (int, straight line from the driver to the pickup when the offer was made), created_at, expires_at (timestamptz, the deadline, compared with the backend clock), responded_at (timestamptz, set only for ACCEPTED and REJECTED). Constraints: unique `uq_ride_offers_ride_id_driver_id` (a driver is never offered a ride twice), partial unique index `uq_ride_offers_one_pending_per_ride` on (ride_id) `WHERE status = 'PENDING'` (one open offer per ride), index `ix_ride_offers_status_expires_at` on (status, expires_at) (the sweeper's query). No ORM relationships. Constants (top of `services/matching.py`): `OFFER_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 15`, `MAX_OFFERS_PER_RIDE = 5` (next to `SEARCH_RADIUS_M = 3000` and `MAX_CANDIDATES = 50`) **M4.3 index:** `uq_ride_offers_one_pending_per_driver` (unique on driver_id WHERE status = 'PENDING'; it also counts a PENDING offer past its deadline that is not yet swept, because `now()` cannot be in an index condition). All three are declared in `models.py` with `postgresql_where`, so the test database gets them from `create_all` too.
  - `payments`: ride_id (FK, **unique `uq_payments_ride_id` since M5.3**), amount (check `amount > 0`), method (cash/wallet/card, stored lower-case), status (pending/succeeded/failed/refunded, lower-case), idempotency_key (unique, `ride:<id>:charge`), gateway_ref (unique, nullable). One row per settled ride with a fare above 0, written by `payments.charge_ride` inside the settlement transaction
  - **M5.3 (migration `0008_wallet_and_payments`, one data statement):** `rides.payment_method` (cash/wallet/card, lower-case VARCHAR(6), not null, server default `cash`: chosen at request time, never changed). `wallets`: `user_id` (PK, FK), `balance` int default 0, `updated_at`; one row per user, created by the first ledger write; the balance always equals the sum of the user's entries. `wallet_entries` (the append-only ledger): `id`, `user_id`, `amount` (signed paise), `kind` (TOPUP, RIDE_CHARGE, ADJUSTMENT, stored upper-case), `balance_after`, `ride_id`, `topup_id`, `idempotency_key` String(64), `note` String(200), `actor_user_id`, `created_at`; unique `uq_wallet_entries_user_id_idempotency_key` (user_id, idempotency_key); partial unique indexes `uq_wallet_entries_one_charge_per_ride` (ride_id WHERE kind = 'RIDE_CHARGE') and `uq_wallet_entries_one_credit_per_topup` (topup_id WHERE kind = 'TOPUP'); checks `ck_wallet_entries_amount_nonzero`, `ck_wallet_entries_ride_id_matches_kind` and `ck_wallet_entries_topup_id_matches_kind` (the reference is set exactly for its kind), `ck_wallet_entries_amount_sign_matches_kind` (RIDE_CHARGE negative, TOPUP positive); index (user_id, id). `wallet_topups`: `id`, `user_id`, `amount` (check > 0), `status` (PENDING, SUCCEEDED, EXPIRED), `idempotency_key` String(64), `stripe_session_id` and `stripe_payment_intent_id` (unique, nullable), `checkout_url` Text, `created_at`, `completed_at`; unique (user_id, idempotency_key); index (user_id, id). `stripe_events`: `id` (the Stripe event id, PK), `event_type`, `created_at`. The data statement gives every ride settled before this milestone with `final_fare > 0` and not marked legacy one cash, succeeded payment (`ride:<id>:charge`): 361 rows summing to 4103175 paise in dev (equal to the count and sum measured before migrating); the downgrade deletes the payments whose key matches `ride:%:charge` first, and upgrade after downgrade re-creates exactly the same rows. The `payments` check constraint is written by hand (autogenerate does not see check constraints on an existing table) and the data statement escapes `\:charge`, or `text()` reads it as a bind parameter.
  - **M5.4 (migration `0009_driver_earnings`, one backfill and one guard):** `pricing_rules.commission_percent` (above). `ride_earnings` (one row per payment, written by `payments.charge_ride` in the payment's own transaction, never updated or deleted): `id`, `ride_id` (FK, unique `uq_ride_earnings_ride_id`), `payment_id` (FK, unique `uq_ride_earnings_payment_id`), `driver_id` (FK drivers), `kind` (String(20), `trip` or `cancellation`, check `ck_ride_earnings_kind_known`), `gross_amount` (paise, equal to the payment, check `> 0`), `commission_percent` (snapshot, check 0 to 100), `platform_fee` (check `>= 0`), `driver_earning` (check `>= 0`), `created_at` (timestamptz, default `now()`); `ck_ride_earnings_split_adds_up` (`platform_fee + driver_earning = gross_amount`); indexes `ix_ride_earnings_driver_id_id` (driver_id, id) and `ix_ride_earnings_created_at`. The backfill gives every payment that existed (535 in dev, 6819151 paise) its row at 20 percent, dated like the payment, and a guard statement (`DO $$ ... RAISE EXCEPTION 'payments without earnings after backfill'`) fails the migration loudly when any payment is left without one. The check constraint on `pricing_rules` is written by hand (autogenerate does not see it).
  - `ratings`: ride_id, from_user_id, to_user_id (FKs), score (check 1 to 5), comment (VARCHAR(500) since M0.2; a check keeps it to 300 since M6.1). Unique (ride_id, from_user_id). Since M6.1 (migration `0010_ratings`, one data statement): checks `ck_ratings_no_self_rating` and `ck_ratings_comment_length`, index `ix_ratings_to_user_id_id (to_user_id, id)`, and the new table `rating_summaries` (see "Ratings (M6.1)" below)
  - `pricing_rules`: vehicle_type (unique), base_fare, per_km, per_min, min_fare, surge_cap (float, default 2.0), and since M5.1 `cancellation_fee` (int paise, not null, server default 3000) and `free_cancel_seconds` (int, not null, server default 120), and since M5.4 `commission_percent` (int, not null, server default 20, check `ck_pricing_rules_commission_percent_range` 0 to 100). One row seeded by migration 0003 (all money in paise): `economy`, base_fare 5000 (Rs 50), per_km 1200 (Rs 12), per_min 200 (Rs 2), min_fare 8000 (Rs 80), surge_cap 2.0. The test database gets the same row after every truncate
  - Relationships: `Ride.events` (ordered by id), `Driver.user` (many to one) and `Driver.vehicle` (one to one), all `lazy="raise"`. Driver queries eager load with `selectinload`; ride events are never loaded through the ride, only via `rides_repo.list_events`. Enums are Python enums in `models.py` (`UserRole`, `VerificationStatus`, `RideStatus`, `OfferStatus`, `PaymentMethod`, `PaymentStatus`, and since M5.3 `WalletEntryKind`, `TopupStatus`), plus the constant `ACTIVE_RIDE_STATUSES` (REQUESTED, DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, IN_PROGRESS)
- **Endpoints:**
  - `GET /health`: 200 `{"status","postgres","redis"}` all "ok"; 503 with the failing one "error"
  - `POST /auth/register`: JSON `{name, email, phone?, password (min 8), role: rider|driver}`; 201 `UserResponse`; 409 if email or phone exists; 422 for role `admin`, short password, bad email
  - `POST /auth/login`: JSON `{email, password}`; 200 `{access_token, token_type: "bearer", user}`; 401 `Invalid email or password` for both wrong email and wrong password
  - `GET /auth/me`: needs `Authorization: Bearer <token>`; 200 `UserResponse`; 401 otherwise
  - `GET /drivers/me`: role driver; 200 `DriverResponse`; 404 if no profile yet
  - `POST /drivers/me/profile`: role driver; JSON `{license_number}` (stripped, 5 to 30 chars); 201 `DriverResponse` with status `pending` and `vehicle: null`; 409 if the user already has a profile; 422 for a short license
  - `POST /drivers/me/vehicle`: role driver; JSON `{plate_number, model, color}` (type is always `economy`); 201 `DriverResponse`; 409 if no profile yet, a vehicle already exists, or the plate is taken (any spelling of the same plate)
  - `GET /admin/drivers?status=&q=&limit=&after_id=` (extended in place in M6.2): see "Admin dashboard (M6.2)" below. It no longer returns `DriverResponse` rows but `AdminDriverResponse` rows (the same fields plus more), ascending by id
  - `POST /admin/drivers/{id}/approve` and `/reject`: role admin; 200 `DriverResponse`; 404 unknown id; 409 if already in that status; 409 when approving a driver with no vehicle. Rejected to approved is allowed
  - `GET /admin/surge?refresh=false` (M5.2): role admin (403 rider and driver, 401 no token); 200 `SurgeSnapshotResponse` `{computed_at (epoch s), age_seconds, zones: [{zone, demand, supply, pressure_percent, surge_percent}]}`, zones sorted by `surge_percent` (high first), then `demand` (high first), then name; `surge_percent` is the UNCAPPED table value. `refresh=true` recomputes and overwrites the cache. 503 `Cache is unavailable` when Redis is down.
  - `POST /rides/estimate`: role rider (401 without a token, 403 for driver and admin); JSON `{pickup_lat, pickup_lng, dropoff_lat, dropoff_lng}`; 200 `EstimateResponse` (`distance_m, duration_s, fare_estimate, base_fare, distance_fare, time_fare, minimum_fare_applied, path`; money in paise; `path` is a list of `[lat, lng]` pairs). 422 for out-of-range or identical points, a pickup or drop-off outside the city box (`Pickup is outside the service area`, `Drop-off is outside the service area`), a route under 200 m (`Pickup and drop-off are too close for a ride`), or no route (`No route found between these points. Choose points closer to a road.`, for OSRM `NoRoute` and `NoSegment`); 502 `Routing is unavailable` when OSRM is down, times out, or answers something unexpected; 503 `Pricing is not configured` when there is no rule for `economy`. Declared before the `/rides/{ride_id}` routes. Nothing is stored **M5.2:** the response also has `normal_fare` (after the minimum fare, before surge), `surge_percent` (100 to 200) and `surge_amount` (`fare_estimate - normal_fare`); `fare_estimate` is the surged total and `base_fare`, `distance_fare`, `time_fare` stay the normal parts. The pickup's zone is looked up in the snapshot (capped by the pricing rule); with Redis down the estimate still answers 200 with `surge_percent` 100 and one warning.
  - `POST /rides`: role rider; JSON `{pickup_lat, pickup_lng, pickup_address, dropoff_lat, dropoff_lng, dropoff_address}` (any other field the client sends is ignored). In ONE transaction (committed once) it creates the ride as REQUESTED with its first event, makes the first offer to the nearest free driver (`matching.offer_to_next_driver`), and, if nobody qualifies, moves the ride to NO_DRIVER_FOUND. Two outcomes, both HTTP 201 with `RideResponse`: status `REQUESTED` with `driver_id` null (an offer is pending and offers are being tried; no driver is assigned until one accepts), or status `NO_DRIVER_FOUND` with `driver_id` null (nobody in range or free; a normal finished ride, not an error; the rider can request again at once). After the commit it publishes `offer_created` to the offered driver. Errors: 409 `You already have an active ride` (checked first, so no routing or Redis call is made, and checked AGAIN after the rider's row is locked, see "Concurrency control"); 503 `Busy, please retry` when the rider's row stays locked by another request for more than 3 s (`LOCK_WAIT_MS`); the same 422, 502, and 503 errors as the estimate; 503 `Cache is unavailable` if Redis fails during matching; 422 for out-of-range coordinates, blank address, or identical pickup and drop-off. A failure anywhere before the commit leaves no ride, no event, and no offer behind. The server works out the estimate itself and stores `distance_m`, `duration_s`, and `fare_estimate` on the ride (the path is not stored) **M5.2:** the body also takes an optional `accepted_surge_percent` (int 100 to 200, else 422). After the early active-ride 409 and after the estimate, and before any lock: if it is present and the CURRENT multiplier is higher, 409 `Prices have increased in your area (now 1.5x). Please review the new fare and request again.` (one decimal) and nothing is created; if the current multiplier is equal or lower the ride is created at the CURRENT multiplier; absent means accept the current one. The ride stores `pickup_zone`, `surge_percent` and the surged `fare_estimate`.
  - **M5.3, wallet and payments:**
    - `GET /wallet` (rider only: 403 driver and admin, 401 no token): `{balance, reserved, available}` in paise. `reserved` is derived, never stored: the fare cap of the rider's active ride when it is a wallet ride, else 0; `available = balance - reserved`. One extra query; creates no row.
    - `GET /wallet/entries?limit=&before_id=` (rider): the caller's ledger entries newest first (`id, amount, kind, balance_after, ride_id, topup_id, note, created_at`; no actor id); `limit` 1 to 100, default 20, else 422.
    - `POST /wallet/topups` (rider; header `Idempotency-Key` required, 8 to 64 characters of `A-Za-z0-9_-`, else 422; body `{amount}`, a strict integer in paise: strings, floats and booleans are 422): 422 `Amount must be between ₹100 and ₹10,000`; 503 `Card payments are not configured` before any row is written when the key is empty or not `sk_test_` / `rk_test_`; stores a `wallet_topups` row and COMMITS it, then creates a Checkout Session at Stripe (header `Idempotency-Key: topup-<id>`) and stores the session id and `checkout_url` in a second short transaction. 201 when this call inserted the row, 200 for a replay of the same key (no second Stripe call once the url is stored), 409 `This idempotency key was already used with a different request` for another amount, 409 `Another request with this idempotency key is in progress. Retry in a moment.` when Stripe answers 409, 502 `Card payments are unavailable` for any other Stripe failure (the PENDING row stays without a session, and the same key finishes the job). `TopupResponse`: `{id, amount, status, checkout_url (only while PENDING, else null), created_at, completed_at}`.
    - `GET /wallet/topups?limit=` (rider): own top-ups, newest first, limit 1 to 50 (default 10). `POST /wallet/topups/{id}/sync` (rider; someone else's or an unknown id is 404): for a PENDING top-up that has a session it asks Stripe and applies the answer (`credit_topup` when paid, EXPIRED when the session expired); 502 when Stripe fails; a SUCCEEDED or EXPIRED top-up, or one without a session, is returned without a Stripe call.
    - `POST /webhooks/stripe`: no login, see "Webhook (M5.3)" below.
    - `POST /admin/wallets/{user_id}/adjust` (admin; header `Idempotency-Key`; body `{amount, note}`, amount a non-zero strict integer of at most Rs 10,000 either way (else 422), note stripped, 1 to 200 characters): the target must be a rider (404 otherwise). It takes the wallet lock FIRST, then looks for the key: the same amount and note is a replay (200, the same entry), anything else with that key is 409; a debit larger than the balance is 409 `Adjustment would make the balance negative`. Otherwise 201 with a new ADJUSTMENT entry (the admin is stored as `actor_user_id` and never shown).
  - **`POST /rides` (M5.3):** the body takes `payment_method`, `cash` (the default, so scripts keep working) or `wallet` (anything else is 422). Order of checks: the active-ride 409, the estimate (route), the stale-price 409, then for a wallet ride `402 Your wallet balance (₹X) is below the ₹Y this trip can cost. Add money or pay with cash.` when `balance < fare_cap(fare_estimate)` (a plain read before any lock), then the locks as before. `RideResponse` has `payment_method`; `EstimateResponse` has `max_fare` (the fare cap).
  - **M5.4, money views** (amounts in integer paise; `since` is inclusive and `until` exclusive, both `AwareDatetime` query parameters: a time without a timezone is a 422 from FastAPI, `until` not after `since` is a 422 `until must be after since`; a `+` in a URL must be written `%2B`):
    - `GET /drivers/me/earnings?since=&until=` (driver role: 403 rider and admin, 401 no token; 404 `You have no driver profile yet`; a pending or rejected driver may read their own): `{since, until, trips, cancellation_fees, total, cash, wallet, settlement}` where a bucket is `{rides, gross, platform_fee, driver_earning}` and `settlement` is `{owed_to_driver, owed_by_driver, net}`. `trips` and `cancellation_fees` count rows by kind. `total` counts every payment method (a `card` payment counts here only), `cash` and `wallet` only their own.
    - `GET /drivers/me/earnings/entries?since=&until=&limit=&before_id=` (driver): the caller's earning rows, newest first by id (`limit` 1 to 100, default 20, else 422; `before_id` pages): `id, ride_id, kind, payment_method, gross_amount, commission_percent, platform_fee, driver_earning, pickup_address, dropoff_address, distance_m, duration_s, created_at`. Nothing about the rider.
    - `GET /admin/revenue?since=&until=` (admin): the same answer over all drivers; `total.platform_fee` is the platform's revenue. `GET /admin/drivers/{driver_id}/earnings?since=&until=` (admin; 404 `Driver not found`): equal to that driver's own answer.
    - `GET /rides/{ride_id}/receipt` (rider only: 403 driver and admin, 401 no token; another rider's ride and an unknown id are 404 `Ride not found`; 409 `There is no receipt for this ride` unless the ride is COMPLETED or CANCELLED and has a payment). `ReceiptResponse`: `receipt_number` (`RCPT-` and the ride id padded to 8 digits), `issued_at` (the payment's time), `ride_id`, `kind`, `status`, `pickup_address`, `dropoff_address`, `started_at`, `ended_at` (completed_at, or the time of the last CANCELLED event), `driver_name`, `vehicle` (`plate_number, model, color`), `estimated_fare`, exactly one of `trip` (`distance_m, duration_s, distance_source, base_fare, distance_fare, time_fare, minimum_fare_applied, normal_fare, surge_percent, surge_amount, computed_fare, capped, total`) and `cancellation` (`fee, reason, cancelled_by`), and `payment` (`method, amount, status, wallet_balance_after`, null for cash). Derived from the stored breakdown, payment row and ledger entry, never recomputed; breakdowns written before M5.2 are read as `normal_fare` = `computed_fare`, surge 100 and 0. No otp, no commission, no earning, no driver id or contact data.
  - **Settlement now charges (M5.3):** `driver_set_status` (COMPLETED) and `cancel` (CANCELLED) call `payments.charge_ride` after the settlement and right before the commit (in `cancel` after the pending-offer handling, so that `post_entry` is the last write): `final_fare > 0` creates one payment of the ride's method and, for a wallet ride, one `RIDE_CHARGE` entry of `-final_fare` linked to the ride. A fee of 0, a driver cancel and NO_DRIVER_FOUND create nothing. Any failure rolls the whole settlement back (the ride keeps its status and the driver can retry). It uses no Redis. A grep of every `change_ride_status` caller shows that `driver_set_status` and `cancel` are the only code that moves a ride to COMPLETED or CANCELLED (`create_ride` and `offers.py` only reach REQUESTED, DRIVER_ASSIGNED and NO_DRIVER_FOUND).
  - `GET /drivers/me/offer` (M3.3): role driver (403 otherwise, 401 without a token); 200 `OfferResponse` for the driver's oldest PENDING offer whose `expires_at` is still in the future (so an overdue offer is gone even before the sweeper runs), else 404 `No pending offer` (also 404 `You have no driver profile yet`). `OfferResponse`: `id, ride_id, pickup_address, pickup_lat, pickup_lng, dropoff_address, dropoff_lat, dropoff_lng, trip_distance_m, trip_duration_s, fare_estimate (paise), pickup_distance_m, expires_in` (float seconds left from the backend clock, never negative, one decimal). The pickup and drop-off names equal `RideResponse` on purpose. No rider name, email, phone, or id
  - `POST /offers/{offer_id}/accept` (M3.3): role driver; 200 `RideResponse` (status DRIVER_ASSIGNED, `driver_id` = this driver's `drivers.id`, event actor = the driver's user id). 404 `Offer not found` for an unknown id or someone else's offer; then the ride row is locked, then the offer row; 409 `This offer is no longer available` when the offer is not PENDING (answered, expired and swept, or cancelled); 409 `This offer has expired` when `expires_at` is at or before now (the deadline is hard even before the sweeper runs); 403 `Your driver account is not approved (status: X)`; 409 `You already have an active ride`; 409 `You are offline. Go online first.` (no presence key); 503 `Busy, please retry` when the driver's row stays locked for more than 3 s (M4.2). Nothing is changed on a refusal. After the commit it publishes `ride_updated` to the rider and `offer_closed` (reason `accepted`) to the driver
  - `POST /offers/{offer_id}/reject` (M3.3): role driver; 204 with no body. Same 404 and the same two 409s as accept. The offer becomes REJECTED (`responded_at` set) and `finish_offer` offers the ride to the next driver, or ends it as NO_DRIVER_FOUND (null actor) when there is none
  - `GET /rides/{ride_id}/cancellation-fee` (M5.1): role rider only (403 driver and admin, 401 no token); the ride's own rider gets 200 `{fee, reason}` (paise; what a cancel would cost right now, from the same function as the real cancel, `pricing.cancellation_fee`); another rider and unknown ids 404 `Ride not found`; 409 `This ride can no longer be cancelled` unless the ride is REQUESTED, DRIVER_ASSIGNED, or DRIVER_ARRIVED; 503 when a needed pricing rule is missing. No lock, nothing written
  - `GET /rides/active`: role rider or driver; their active ride, or 404 `No active ride`; 403 for admin. Declared before `/rides/{ride_id}`
  - `GET /rides/{ride_id}` and `GET /rides/{ride_id}/events`: any logged-in user who is the ride's rider, its assigned driver, or an admin. Everyone else, and unknown ids, get the same 404 `Ride not found`
  - `GET /rides/{ride_id}/driver` (M3.2): any logged-in user who is the ride's rider, its assigned driver, or an admin (everyone else and unknown ids get `404 Ride not found`, no token gets 401); 404 `No driver assigned to this ride` when `driver_id` is null. 200 `RideDriverResponse`: `driver_id, name, vehicle (id, plate_number, model, color, vehicle_type, or null), location (lat, lng, updated_at, or null)`, and nothing else: no email, phone, license number, or verification status. `location` comes from Redis (`get_presence`) ONLY while the ride is in `ACTIVE_RIDE_STATUSES`; for COMPLETED and CANCELLED it is always null even if the driver is online for another ride, and a missing or expired presence is null as well. 503 `Cache is unavailable` when Redis is down, although name and vehicle come from Postgres
  - `POST /rides/{ride_id}/arrive`, `/start`, `/complete`: role driver (403 otherwise), must be the assigned driver (404 otherwise); 200 `RideResponse`; 409 `Cannot change ride from X to Y` if the transition is not allowed. `/start` sets `started_at` (and now needs the trip code, next item); `/complete` sets `completed_at` and (M5.1) settles the ride in the same transaction: `final_fare`, `actual_distance_m`, `actual_duration_s`, `fare_breakdown` (503 `Pricing is not configured` with no pricing rule: the ride stays IN_PROGRESS and the driver can retry). Since M3.5 each of them also sends `ride_updated` to both people (see the WebSocket events)
  - `POST /rides/{ride_id}/start` (M3.5): JSON body `{"otp": "<4 digits>"}` (`StartTripRequest`, pattern `^[0-9]{4}$`: no body, a missing field, a number, `"12"`, `"abcd"`, `"12345"`, and full-width digits all give 422). The STATE is checked first: a ride that is not DRIVER_ARRIVED gives 409 whatever the code. On a DRIVER_ARRIVED ride a wrong code, or a ride with no stored code, gives 400 `Incorrect trip code` with no status change, no event row, and nothing published (the comparison is `secrets.compare_digest`). The right code moves the ride to IN_PROGRESS
  - `GET /rides/{ride_id}/otp` (M3.5): role rider only (403 for driver and admin, 401 without a token). The ride's own rider gets 200 `{"otp": "1234"}` while the ride is DRIVER_ASSIGNED or DRIVER_ARRIVED; another rider and an unknown id get 404 `Ride not found`; every other status (REQUESTED, IN_PROGRESS, COMPLETED, CANCELLED, NO_DRIVER_FOUND) gives 409 `Trip code is not available`. The only place the code leaves the backend
  - `POST /rides/{ride_id}/cancel`: role rider or driver; the rider from REQUESTED / DRIVER_ASSIGNED / DRIVER_ARRIVED, the assigned driver from DRIVER_ASSIGNED / DRIVER_ARRIVED (a driver gets 404 on a REQUESTED ride: no driver is assigned yet); 409 after IN_PROGRESS or when already ended. Cancelling ends the ride. Since M5.1 it also sets the cancellation fee (`final_fare`, possibly 0) and a `cancellation` breakdown in the same transaction (503 `Pricing is not configured` if the rule is needed and missing). Since M3.3, if the ride has a PENDING offer it is set to CANCELLED in the same transaction (ride locked first) and the offered driver gets `offer_closed` (reason `ride_cancelled`) after the commit. Since M3.5 the rider and the assigned driver also get `ride_updated` (a ride cancelled from REQUESTED has no driver, so only the rider gets it; the offered driver gets only `offer_closed`)
  - `POST /drivers/me/online`: role driver; JSON `{lat, lng}` (lat -90 to 90, lng -180 to 180); 200 `PresenceResponse` (`online, lat, lng, updated_at` in epoch seconds; lat, lng, and updated_at are null when offline). 404 no profile; 403 `Your driver account is not approved (status: X)`; 422 `Location is outside the service area` (checked with `is_inside_bounds` and the `CITY_*` settings), or a bad body. Calling it while online just moves the driver (200)
  - `POST /drivers/me/location`: role driver; same body and checks; the heartbeat. 409 `You are offline. Go online first.` when there is no presence key (it never brings an offline driver back); otherwise 200 with the request's position and the new `updated_at`. Since M5.1, a ping of a driver whose ride is IN_PROGRESS is also added to the trip meter (below). Since M3.2, after a successful ping it looks up the driver's active ride (one extra Postgres read) and, if there is one, publishes a `driver_location` event to that ride's rider (see the WebSocket section); the response is unchanged. `POST /drivers/me/online` publishes nothing
  - `POST /drivers/me/offline`: role driver; 200 `{online: false}`, also when already offline; 409 `Finish or cancel your current ride before going offline` while the driver has an active ride
  - `GET /drivers/me/presence`: role driver; 200 `PresenceResponse` with the stored position (read back from `GEOPOS`), or `online: false` with nulls
  - Rejecting a driver (`POST /admin/drivers/{id}/reject`) also removes them from Redis at once. Any Redis failure on any endpoint answers 503 `Cache is unavailable` (one `RedisError` handler in `main.py`)
  - `GET /places/map-config`: any logged-in user; 200 `MapConfigResponse` (`city_name, center_lat, center_lng, zoom, south, west, north, east`) built from the `CITY_*` and `MAP_ZOOM` settings
  - `GET /places/search?q=`: any logged-in user; `q` 3 to 200 chars (422 otherwise, also when it is shorter than 3 after trimming and collapsing spaces); 200 list of `PlaceResponse` (`display_name, lat, lng`, floats), up to 5, limited to the city box. 429 `Place search is busy, try again in a second` with `Retry-After: 1` when another uncached Nominatim call ran in the last 1.1 s; 502 `Place search is unavailable` when Nominatim fails or answers non-200
  - `GET /places/reverse?lat=&lng=`: any logged-in user; 200 `PlaceResponse` with the requested coordinates and Nominatim's address; 422 outside the city box (checked with `utils/geo.py`) (or lat/lng out of range); 404 `No address found for this location`; 429 and 502 as for search
  - `RideResponse` (M5.3 adds `payment_method`): `id, rider_id, driver_id, pickup_*/dropoff_* (lat, lng, address), status, distance_m, duration_s, fare_estimate, final_fare, actual_distance_m, actual_duration_s, fare_breakdown, created_at, started_at, completed_at`. No `otp`. `RideEventResponse`: `id, from_status, to_status, actor_user_id, created_at`. The code is deliberately not in `RideResponse` (the ORM model has the attribute and `from_attributes` would expose it): it has its own schema `OtpResponse` and endpoint. `StartTripRequest` is the start body (M3.5) **M5.2:** also `surge_percent` (int); `pickup_zone` is NOT exposed.
  - `DriverLocation` (M3.2): `lat, lng, updated_at` (int epoch seconds). `RideDriverResponse`: `driver_id, name, vehicle (VehicleResponse or null), location (DriverLocation or null)`
  - `DriverResponse`: `id, license_number, verification_status, created_at, user (UserResponse), vehicle (id, plate_number, model, color, vehicle_type, or null)`
- **Admin dashboard (M6.2).** Migration `0011_pricing_rule_editor` (no data statement; the seven check constraints are written by hand because autogenerate does not see them on an existing table).
  - **Schema.** `pricing_rules` gains `version` (int, not null, default 1: the optimistic version), `updated_at` (timestamptz, default `now()`), `updated_by` (FK users, nullable) and seven checks: `ck_pricing_rules_base_fare_nonneg`, `_per_km_nonneg`, `_per_min_nonneg`, `_cancellation_fee_nonneg`, `_free_cancel_seconds_nonneg` (each `>= 0`), `_min_fare_floor` (`min_fare >= 100`, so every trip is chargeable) and `_surge_cap_range` (`surge_cap BETWEEN 1.0 AND 2.0`, matching `rides.surge_percent`); `commission_percent_range` stays. The upper bounds are API-only. New table `pricing_rule_changes` (append-only audit): `id`, `rule_id` (FK), `actor_user_id` (FK, not null), `version_before`, `version_after`, `changes` (JSONB list of `{field, old, new}`), `created_at`; `uq_pricing_rule_changes_rule_id_version_after`, `ck_pricing_rule_changes_version_step` (`version_after = version_before + 1`), index `ix_pricing_rule_changes_rule_id_id`. New index `ix_ride_offers_created_at` (the stats windows). Existing rows got version 1, `updated_at` now, `updated_by` NULL. The conftest TRUNCATE builds its table list from the metadata, so it already covered the new table.
  - **Endpoints** (all `require_role("admin")`: 401 without a token, 403 for a rider or a driver; none returns `otp` or a password hash):
    - `GET /admin/live`: `{generated_at, drivers[], rides[], drivers_total, rides_total, truncated, counts: {drivers: {online, free, offered, on_ride}, rides: {REQUESTED, DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, IN_PROGRESS}}}`. Online drivers are the GEO members that have a presence key (a stale member is skipped), sorted by id and cut at `LIVE_MAX_DRIVERS` (1000, lowest ids kept); a driver is `{id, name, plate_number, lat, lng, state, active_ride_id}` with state `on_ride` (a ride in DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED or IN_PROGRESS), else `offered` (a PENDING offer), else `free`. Rides are the four active statuses, newest first, cut at `LIVE_MAX_RIDES` (1000), with coordinates, addresses, rider name and fare estimate. Driver counts are computed from the returned drivers, ride counts and totals from one grouped query. It is plain reads (Redis, then several unlocked statements), NOT one atomic snapshot, and the code says so. Redis down: 503 `Cache is unavailable`. No driver email, phone or license.
    - `GET /admin/drivers?status=&q=&limit=&after_id=`: `AdminDriverResponse` rows (every field of `DriverResponse` plus `user_id`, `online`, `state` (offline, free, offered, on_ride; on a ride wins even when the presence key expired), `active_ride_id`, `rating_count`, `rating_average` (the real average through `utils/ratings.average`, even for one rating), `completed_trips`), ascending by id, `limit` 1 to 200 (default 50), `after_id` pages, `q` (stripped, 1 to 100 characters, else 422) matches name, email, license and plate with `ILIKE` (`%`, `_` and the backslash escaped, so they match themselves) and the driver id when `q` is 1 to 9 ASCII digits. The `online` flag comes from one `MGET` of presence keys (so this endpoint is 503 when Redis is down). Approve and reject are unchanged.
    - `GET /admin/rides?status=&rider_id=&driver_id=&since=&until=&limit=&before_id=`: `AdminRideRow` rows (`id, created_at, status, rider_id, driver_id, pickup_address, dropoff_address, fare_estimate, final_fare, surge_percent, payment_method`), newest first by id, `limit` 1 to 100 (default 25), `since` inclusive and `until` exclusive (naive time or `until` not after `since` is 422). `GET /admin/rides/{id}` (404 `Ride not found`): the row plus coordinates, distances and times, `fare_breakdown`, `rider` (`id, name, email`), `driver` (`id, user_id, name, email, plate_number, model, color`, or null), `events` (each with `actor`: `system` for no actor, `rider`, `driver`, else `other`), `offers`, `payment`, `earning`, and `ratings` with comments and `from_role`. The query selects every ride column except `otp` (`RIDE_COLUMNS`).
    - `GET /admin/stats?since=&until=&bucket=&utc_offset_minutes=`: see "Stats definitions" below.
    - `GET /admin/pricing-rules`: `{rules: [...], active_rides, limits}` (each rule has every editable field, `version`, `updated_at`, `updated_by`, `updated_by_name`; `limits` is `RULE_LIMITS`; `active_rides` counts REQUESTED, DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED and IN_PROGRESS rides right now). `PATCH /admin/pricing-rules/{vehicle_type}`: `{version, <any of the eight fields>}` (see "Pricing editor rules"), answers `{rule, active_rides, changes}`. `GET /admin/pricing-rules/{vehicle_type}/changes?limit=` (1 to 100, default 10; newest first; `id, actor_name, version_before, version_after, changes, created_at`). An unknown vehicle type is 404.
  - **Stats definitions.** `since` and `until` are required `AwareDatetime`s (`until` after `since`), `bucket` is `hour` (window at most `HOUR_MAX_DAYS` = 14 days) or `day` (at most `DAY_MAX_DAYS` = 366), `utc_offset_minutes` is within plus or minus `OFFSET_MAX_MINUTES` (840); everything else is 422 with a clear message. **Two cohorts:** ride counts, rates and trip averages are by REQUEST time (`rides.created_at`); `money` is exactly `earnings.get_revenue(db, since, until)` (by SETTLEMENT time, `ride_earnings.created_at`). Windows are `[since, until)`. `rides`: `requested` (all created in the window), `completed`, `cancelled`, `no_driver_found`, `active`, and `completion_rate`, `cancellation_rate`, `no_driver_rate` = percent with one decimal rounded half up in integers, `(n * 2000 + d) // (2 * d) / 10`, with `d = completed + cancelled + no_driver_found` (finished rides only: an active ride has no outcome yet), null when `d` is 0. `trips`: `avg_fare`, `avg_distance_m`, `avg_duration_s` (integers, half up, over COMPLETED rides created in the window that have a `final_fare`; each average has its own count so a ride settled before M5.1 cannot drag it), `assigned_rides`, `mean_time_to_assign_s` and `median_time_to_assign_s` (the DRIVER_ASSIGNED event minus the ride's creation, exact numerics, one decimal half up, null with none; the median is `percentile_cont(0.5)`). `offers`: counts per status of offers created in the window and `acceptance_rate = accepted / (accepted + rejected + expired)`. `surge`: `rides_surged` (`surge_percent > 100`) and `max_surge_percent` (100 when none). `users`: `new_riders`, `new_drivers` (admins are left out). `now`: `online_drivers` (the number of presence keys, or null with ONE warning log when Redis raises `RedisError`: the endpoint stays available), `active_rides` per status, `pending_offers` (an expired offer the sweeper has not handled yet is still PENDING). **Buckets:** the index is `floor((epoch + offset_seconds) / bucket_seconds)` (in SQL for the counts, in integers for the series), a bucket starts at `index * size - offset`; the series is zero-filled in the service from the bucket of `since` to the bucket of `until` minus one second, each entry `{start, rides, completed, gross, platform_fee}`. The offset is fixed (no timezone database), so a window that crosses a daylight saving change is off by an hour on one side.
  - **Pricing editor rules.** The editable fields and their API bounds (`RULE_LIMITS` in `schemas.py`, returned by the GET): `base_fare` 0 to 100000, `per_km` 0 to 50000, `per_min` 0 to 20000, `min_fare` 100 to 500000, `cancellation_fee` 0 to 100000 (all paise, `StrictInt`), `free_cancel_seconds` 0 to 3600, `commission_percent` 0 to 100, `surge_cap` 1.0 to 2.0 (an int or a float with at most two decimals, never a string or a boolean). `extra="forbid"`, `version` is a required `StrictInt`, at least one field besides `version`, an explicit null is 422. **The update:** lock the rule row (column-only `SELECT id ... FOR UPDATE`), read the rule and check the version in a SEPARATE statement after the lock (stale: 409 `This rule was changed by someone else. Reload and try again.`, nothing changes), compute the changes (`surge_cap` compared after rounding to hundredths), no change means 200 with `changes: []` and no write, no version bump and no audit row; otherwise update the values, `version + 1`, `updated_at`, `updated_by`, insert the audit row, commit, then read `active_rides`. A replay of an applied request is therefore a 409, so no `Idempotency-Key`. The rule row is a leaf lock and `get_rule` takes no lock. **Verified effects of an edit** (read from the code): `calculate_fare` and the estimate read the whole rule per call, so new estimates use the new `base_fare`, `per_km`, `per_min` and `min_fare` at once; a ride already in progress is settled at completion with the NEW base fare, per-km, per-minute and minimum fare (not only per-km and per-minute), but never above 150 percent of its own `fare_estimate`; the surge cap is applied at lookup time to the uncapped cached snapshot, so it changes new quotes at once, while a ride keeps the multiplier locked at request time; the commission is read by `charge_ride` at settlement, so only settlements after the edit use it (earning rows keep their snapshot); the cancellation fee and the free window are read at cancel time, so they apply to the next cancellation, including of a ride already assigned.
  - **The admin page** (`frontend/admin/`, one long `admin.js` by approved exception; nothing under `shared/` changed). Nav of five buttons; the tab comes from the URL hash on load only (`^#(overview|live|drivers|rides|pricing)(?:/(\d{1,9}))?$`, anything else is overview; the page writes the hash back with `history.replaceState`). One `setInterval` of 3 s with a tick counter, skipped while a refresh is running, never paused in a background tab: Live and Drivers every tick, Overview every third tick, Rides only an open ride that is not finished, Pricing never; a failed poll shows its message and keeps the last data. **Overview:** period buttons (Today hourly, Last 7 days, Last 30 days, from the browser's local midnight with `-new Date().getTimezoneOffset()`), cards (null shows "no data"), a note about the two cohorts, a money group with the settlement line, a "Right now" group, and two inline SVG charts built by `drawBars` (bars with a `<title>`, `role="img"`, `aria-label`, `viewBox`, a max label and first, middle and last labels). **Live map:** Leaflet with the tags and hashes copied from the rider page, `preferCanvas`, `maxBounds` from `GET /places/map-config`, driver dots (green free, amber offered, blue on a ride) and ride pickup rings (orange, purple, red), tooltips "Driver N" and "Ride N" from a verified integer id, a side panel (textContent) for the clicked item with Open ride and Open driver buttons, the drop-off marker and a dashed line for the selected ride, "No longer active" when it leaves the list, driver and ride toggles, a Fit map button (the only mover of the map besides the first fit), a counts line, a truncation warning, "Showing data from HH:MM:SS", and the surge zones table (top 10, every third tick). **Drivers:** status filter and a search form (Enter or the button, never as you type), a table with rating and trips, Approve and Reject (Reject asks `confirm()` and warns about an online driver), Load more (to 200 rows), and a detail panel (all-time earnings, last 5 rides, last 5 ratings) loaded once per open (`driverDetailKey` is set before the calls) and again after approve or reject. **Rides:** filters (status, rider id, driver id, period), a table with Load more, and the detail of one ride (summary, timeline, offers, payment and earning, ratings with comments, breakdown rows; "Ride not found" for a 404). **Pricing:** the form for the first rule (economy) with the allowed ranges from the API, the active ride count, a `confirm()` that lists every change ("base fare ₹50.00 → ₹60.00") and the active rides, only changed fields sent with the version the form was loaded with, "Saved. Version N.", the server's 422 and 409 messages in the message area, a Reload values button after a 409, the last 10 changes. The inputs are written only by handlers (open, save, reload), never by `render()` or a poll.
- **Ride state machine** (`ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` and `change_ride_status()` in `services/rides.py`; the only code that sets `ride.status` after creation, apart from `create_ride`, which inserts REQUESTED and writes the first event with `from_status` null):

  | From | Allowed to |
  |---|---|
  | REQUESTED | DRIVER_ASSIGNED, CANCELLED, NO_DRIVER_FOUND |
  | DRIVER_ASSIGNED | DRIVER_ARRIVED, CANCELLED |
  | DRIVER_ARRIVED | IN_PROGRESS, CANCELLED |
  | IN_PROGRESS | COMPLETED |
  | COMPLETED, CANCELLED, NO_DRIVER_FOUND | nothing |

  Every transition loads the ride with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, writes a `ride_events` row, and commits once. The table did not change in M3.3, but the meaning did: REQUESTED now means "offers are being tried", and DRIVER_ASSIGNED happens only when a driver accepts an offer (actor = that driver's user id). The system's own change is REQUESTED to NO_DRIVER_FOUND (actor null), made by `create_ride` (nobody qualifies for the first offer) and by `finish_offer` (no next driver after a reject or expiry); there is no endpoint for it. `change_ride_status` takes `actor_user_id: int | None` (type hint only; the logic is unchanged). **Trip code (M3.5):** `change_ride_status` sets `ride.otp = FAKE_OTP` (`"1234"`, a constant at the top of `services/rides.py`; a real system would use `secrets` for random digits) when the ride becomes DRIVER_ASSIGNED and sets it to NULL when the ride becomes IN_PROGRESS or CANCELLED; nothing else changes the field and `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` is unchanged. Only the ride's rider can read it (`GET /rides/{id}/otp`) and it never appears in `RideResponse`, ride events, WebSocket events, or logs. `driver_set_status(db, user, ride_id, new_status, otp=None)` checks the code (state first, then code), changes the status, commits, and then calls `notify_ride_updated(db, ride)`, the private helper that `cancel` also calls: it publishes `ride_updated` `{ride_id, status}` to the rider's user id and, when `ride.driver_id` is set, to the driver's user id (looked up with one extra scalar query, `drivers_repo.get_user_id`).
- **Matching and offers (M2.4, reworked in M3.3):** `services/matching.py` has one function, `offer_to_next_driver(db, ride)` (since M4.2 it also locks the chosen driver, see "Concurrency control"; it replaced `find_driver`, which was inlined into it because it now has one caller). It reads the drivers already offered this ride (`offers_repo.list_driver_ids_for_ride`, any status); at `MAX_OFFERS_PER_RIDE` (5) or more it returns None. Otherwise: `GEOSEARCH` around the PICKUP (`SEARCH_RADIUS_M = 3000`, `MAX_CANDIDATES = 50`, nearest first, with distances), presence keys with one `MGET` and `ZREM` of stale GEO members (as in M2.4), `get_available_ids` (approved, no ride in `ACTIVE_RIDE_STATUSES`, and no PENDING offer: a driver deciding on an offer is busy), minus the drivers already offered this ride; since M4.2 the candidates are sorted by `(distance_m, driver_id)` and tried in that order: `drivers_repo.try_lock` (skip when the row is locked), then `get_available_ids(db, [driver_id])` again as its own statement (skip when unavailable), then it creates the PENDING offer for the first driver that passed both (`pickup_distance_m` rounded from the GEOSEARCH distance, `expires_at` = backend now + 15 s) and returns it. It never changes the ride, never publishes, never commits. The candidate list is recomputed for every offer from live presence and availability, not fixed when the ride is requested. `repositories/drivers.py` has `search_nearby`, `get_online_ids`, `remove_from_geo`, `get_available_ids`, and (M3.3) `get_user_id(db, driver_id)` (events are addressed to user ids). `repositories/drivers.py` also has `try_lock` and `lock` (M4.2)
- **Offers (M3.3):** `repositories/offers.py` (Postgres only, flush but never commit, no rules): `create`, `get_ride_and_driver_ids` (selects ONLY the two columns: the locked read of the entity that follows must be its first load in the session, or the identity map would return an older unlocked copy and the status check under the lock would be stale; I chose columns-only over `populate_existing`), `get_by_id(db, id, for_update)`, `list_driver_ids_for_ride`, `get_pending_for_ride`, `get_oldest_pending_for_driver(db, driver_id, now)`, `list_due(db, now, limit)` (columns `id, ride_id` of PENDING offers with `expires_at <= now`, ordered by `expires_at`), `set_status(db, offer, status, responded_at=None)`. `services/offers.py`: `get_pending`, `accept`, `reject`, `finish_offer`, `expire_due_offers`, `sweep_forever`, with `OFFER_SWEEP_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 1` and `DUE_BATCH_SIZE = 50`. LOCK ORDER everywhere (accept, reject, expiry, cancel): the RIDE row first (`FOR UPDATE`), then the OFFER row (and, since M4.2, accept takes the DRIVER row third). `finish_offer(db, ride, offer, new_status, reason)` is the one private helper (used by reject and expiry): sets REJECTED/EXPIRED (`responded_at` only for REJECTED), calls `offer_to_next_driver`, moves the ride to NO_DRIVER_FOUND (null actor) if there is no next offer, commits, and then publishes `offer_closed` to this offer's driver and either `offer_created` to the next driver or `ride_updated` (NO_DRIVER_FOUND) to the rider. Events are published AFTER the commit. One INFO log line per offer event (created, accepted, rejected, expired, cancelled) with offer, ride, and driver ids, and one when a ride ends as NO_DRIVER_FOUND after offers. `sweep_forever()` is one asyncio task per backend process (started and cancelled in the `lifespan` next to the WebSocket listener): every `OFFER_SWEEP_INTERVAL_SECONDS` it calls `expire_due_offers()`, which opens its own sessions through `database.async_session` (looked up at call time, so tests replace it): one session lists up to 50 due offers, then a NEW session per offer locks the ride, loads the offer `FOR UPDATE`, skips it unless it is still PENDING and still overdue, and calls `finish_offer(..., EXPIRED, "expired")`. An exception aborts the pass (the first failure stops it); `sweep_forever` logs ONE warning when a failure streak starts and ONE info line when it recovers, and keeps looping. State is in Postgres, so a restart loses nothing, and several processes can sweep at once (the ride lock makes the second one see a non-PENDING offer and skip). `events.publish` is now best-effort: it catches `RedisError`, logs one warning with the event type and user id, and returns 0, so a failed publish never fails or undoes a request that already committed (nothing in the code or tests relied on it raising)
- **Fare and routing (M2.2):** `services/routing.py` `get_route()` calls `{OSRM_URL}/route/v1/driving/{lng},{lat};{lng},{lat}` with `overview=full&geometries=geojson&steps=false&radiuses=300;300` (timeout 5 s, a new `httpx.AsyncClient` per call), reads the body whatever the HTTP status, and returns `{distance_m, duration_s, path}` with the path converted to `[lat, lng]`. Observed against the real OSRM: `NoSegment` and `NoRoute` both come back as HTTP 400 with the code in the body; `Ok` is 200. `services/pricing.py` `calculate_fare()` loads the rule and uses integer paise only: `distance_fare = (per_km * distance_m + 500) // 1000`, `time_fare = (per_min * duration_s + 30) // 60`, `fare = max(base + distance_fare + time_fare, min_fare)`; no surge, no rounding to whole rupees. `services/rides.py` has `MIN_TRIP_DISTANCE_M = 200`, `estimate_ride()` (bounds check with `utils/geo.is_inside_bounds`, route, minimum distance, fare) and `create_ride()` (409 check, then `estimate_ride()`, then insert). The seed rule gives Rs 169.16 for the MG Road to Koramangala test route (7794 m, 769 s). The service layer calls `routing.get_route(...)` through the module, so tests replace it
- **OSRM data:** `osrm/prepare.sh` (run once, safe to run twice) reads `CITY_*` and `OSM_EXTRACT_URL` from `.env` with grep and cut, downloads the regional extract to `osrm/data/region.osm.pbf` if missing (HEAD check first; stops on a non-200), clips it with osmium in a throwaway `debian:12-slim` container to the city box plus 0.05 degrees (`--strategy=complete_ways`) into `city.osm.pbf`, then runs `osrm-extract -p /opt/car.lua`, `osrm-partition`, `osrm-customize` (MLD) with the pinned image as the host user. It stops the `osrm` container before rewriting the files. For Bengaluru and the southern-zone extract: download 558 MB, clipped file 40 MB, 856 MB in `osrm/data/`, about a minute after the download. `osrm/data/` is in `.gitignore`
- **Tests:** `docker compose exec backend pytest` (497 tests, about 260 s; 444 before M5.4, 385 before M5.3, 328 before M5.2, 302 before M5.1, 263 before M4.3, 255 before M4.2, 204 before M3.5, 178 before M3.3, 161 before M3.2, 131 before M3.1). They run on a separate database `<POSTGRES_DB>_test` (created if missing; tables dropped and created from the models at session start, every table truncated with `RESTART IDENTITY CASCADE` before each test). `conftest.py` refuses to import unless the database name ends with `_test`, uses `NullPool` so simultaneous requests get separate connections, and overrides `get_db`. After each TRUNCATE it inserts the economy pricing rule (same values as the seed), and an autouse fixture replaces `routing.get_route` with a fake (5000 m, 900 s, a two-point path), so no test needs OSRM. Redis: `conftest.py` sets `REDIS_DB=1` before any app module is imported, and an autouse fixture checks `CLIENT INFO` reports database 1 (and the setting is 1) before it runs `FLUSHDB`, so dev data in database 0 is never touched. `test_presence.py` (23 tests) covers: auth on all four routes, no profile 404, pending and rejected 403, Redis contents after going online (GEO member, key, TTL 1 to 30), the longitude-first check with a direct `GEOPOS`, 422 cases with nothing written, ping moving the position and resetting the TTL (EXPIRE 5, back above 25), ping while offline 409, simulated expiry (stale GEO member still there), offline twice, active ride blocking offline, rejecting an online driver, two independent drivers. Mutation-checked: removing `XX` fails 2 tests, removing the approval check in `go_online` fails 1. Fixtures: `client`, `db`, `rider`, `driver` (approved, with vehicle), `admin`, `make_user(role, approved=True)` for more users (a driver comes back as `{user, driver, headers}`, `driver.id` is the `drivers.id`), `put_online(who, lat, lng)` (the real go-online API), and `insert_ride(rider, status, driver=None)` (a row straight into the database, no matching). Since M3.3 rides that must be DRIVER_ASSIGNED (or later) come from the `assign_ride(rider, driver)` fixture in `conftest.py` (the driver goes online at the pickup, the rider requests, the driver reads `GET /drivers/me/offer` and accepts; use it with one driver), built on a second small fixture `accept_offer(driver)`; rides that must be REQUESTED are inserted. `test_matching.py` (15 tests after M3.3; the first test now reads "the pending offer goes to the nearest driver, the ride stays REQUESTED, and accepting assigns it"; it has an `offers_of(db, ride_id)` helper that selects columns only; the descriptions below are the M2.4 originals, converted to offers), offsets due north of the city center, all at least 200 m from the radius edge) covers: nearest of three wins (drivers.id, events, the driver's `/rides/active`), outside the radius (3.3 km no, 2.8 km yes), nobody online, a stale GEO member skipped and removed, a busy driver skipped, one driver and two riders in a row, not approved (pending and rejected, Redis left alone), the tie-break by lower id (ids 9 and 10, chosen because the strings sort the other way), available again after complete and after cancel, a Redis failure leaves zero rows in rides and ride_events (503), 409 for an active rider without matching being called, 403/401 for non-riders and the assign route gone (405). Mutation-checked in M2.4: farthest-first fails 3 tests, no presence filter 1, no active-ride filter 2, no approved filter 1. The concurrent double assignment is deliberately not tested (M4.1). Users get a token directly from `create_access_token` (no login). **M3.1 additions:** `conftest.py` has the async fixture `live_server` (the real app under `uvicorn.Server` on port 0 inside the test's own event loop, because httpx cannot speak WebSocket and Starlette's TestClient runs the app in another thread and loop; a `QuietServer` subclass overrides `capture_signals` so Ctrl+C still reaches pytest; `database.async_session` is replaced with the `_test` session maker; it waits until `PUBSUB NUMSUB ws:events:1` shows the listener, asserts `connections` is empty first, and waits for the listener to unsubscribe on teardown), the fixtures `expired_token(user)` and `tampered_token(user)` (functions that return a token), and `wait_for_listener(count)`. `test_websocket.py` (30 tests): auth for the three roles; 4401 for garbage, expired, tampered, deleted-user tokens and for seven bad first messages (no token, no data, non-string token, ping first, not JSON, JSON array, binary), none of which register a socket; 4408 with `AUTH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` patched to 0.3; ping/pong, the `error` reply for an unknown type, and 4400 for five kinds of bad frame; publish reaches only the addressed user; two tabs; publish to nobody; normal and abrupt close (`transport.abort()`) clean the registry; the 6th socket closes the oldest with 4409; 100 ordered events while the client pings; bad channel messages dropped (raw publish to the hand-written channel `ws:events:1`); `CLIENT KILL TYPE pubsub` recovery (note: this also drops the dev backend's listener, which reconnects by itself within a second); a message on the channel of another Redis database index is not delivered; a stalled client is dropped and delivery continues (a hand-made TCP client with a 4 KB receive buffer, because a websockets library client keeps reading in the background and the server never blocks); `/auth/me` 401 and 200 regression. Mutation-checked in M3.1: removing the auth timeout fails the 4408 test, sending to every user fails the addressed-user test, dropping the `REDIS_DB` suffix makes `live_server` fail (every websocket test errors), removing the registry cleanup errors the first test. `test_rides.py` covers: all 49 status pairs against a hand-written list of legal pairs, happy path with events (the second event has a null actor), cancel rules, one active ride per rider, 404/403 access rules, two simultaneous cancels, `/rides/active`, ride body validation. `test_pricing.py` covers: the fare formula against hand-worked values (including the exact-minimum boundary), half-up rounding with a custom rule, the estimate endpoint and its breakdown, outside-the-city, identical, too-short, and no-route errors, a missing rule (503), a ride storing the server's numbers and ignoring the client's, 409 before any routing, and 401/403 for non-riders. Mutation-checked: `max` to `min` and the rounding offsets 500 and 30 to 0 each fail the pricing tests. Ride coordinates in the tests are derived from the `CITY_*` settings. `pytest.ini`: `asyncio_mode = auto`, session-scoped event loop, `pythonpath = .`. **M3.3 additions:** an autouse fixture in `conftest.py` replaces `database.async_session` with the `_test` session maker for EVERY test (it used to be only `live_server`), because the sweeper and `expire_due_offers()` open their own sessions; `live_server` runs the real lifespan, so the sweeper and the listener run in those tests and are cancelled at teardown. `test_offers.py` (26 tests; expiry is made deterministic by moving `expires_at` into the past in the database and calling `expire_due_offers()` once): one pending offer with distance and a 10 to 16 s deadline and only the REQUESTED event; the `GET /drivers/me/offer` payload (every field, no rider data, 404 for another driver, 403 rider/admin, 401); only the offered driver gets exactly one `offer_created` with exactly `{offer_id, ride_id}`; accept (response, ACCEPTED with `responded_at`, events with the driver as actor, `ride_updated` to the rider, `offer_closed` "accepted" to the driver); reject with a second driver (REJECTED, new PENDING offer to the next, never back, nothing for the rider, then the second accepts); reject with nobody left (NO_DRIVER_FOUND with a null actor, `ride_updated`, the rider can request again); expiry through `expire_due_offers()` with a next driver and with none, an overdue offer already hidden from `GET /drivers/me/offer`, and a second pass changing nothing; accept and reject of an expired but unswept offer give 409 `This offer has expired` and the sweep still handles it; the real sweeper ending a ride on its own (`OFFER_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` patched to 1); who may answer (other driver 404, rider/admin 403, no token 401, unknown id 404); answering twice and in the wrong order; the rider cancelling closes the pending offer (CANCELLED, `offer_closed` "ride_cancelled", the driver's accept 409, the driver offered the next ride) and cancelling after the accept closes nothing; `MAX_OFFERS_PER_RIDE` patched to 2; a driver with a pending offer is not offered a second ride; accept refused for a deleted presence key (409 offline) and for pending/rejected drivers (403), ride stays REQUESTED; a Redis failure during matching leaves zero rows in rides, ride_events, and ride_offers (503); a failed `redis_client.publish` does not fail create or accept; events are published only after the commit (a spy on `events.publish` reads the database through a NEW connection at publish time and sees the committed data); the two constraints raise `IntegrityError`; two simultaneous accepts (one 200, one 409) and two simultaneous rejects (one 204, one 409, exactly one new offer). The double-booking races and accept-versus-sweeper races are left for M4. Mutation-checked in M3.3: see the verification paragraph. **M3.2 additions:** `test_tracking.py` (17 tests). A `spy` fixture subscribes to the raw channel `ws:events:1` so "nothing was published to anyone" does not depend on any socket; an `assigned` fixture makes a DRIVER_ASSIGNED ride through matching; it imports the `open_socket` fixture and helpers from `test_websocket.py`. Covers: exactly one event per ping during DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, and IN_PROGRESS with exactly the keys `ride_id, lat, lng, updated_at`, the request's values, and `updated_at` equal to the ping response's; nothing published after COMPLETED and after CANCELLED, for a driver with no ride, for a ping while offline (409) or from an unapproved driver (403, with an active ride inserted so the check means something), and for go online during a ride (the next ping does publish); addressing (both tabs of the rider receive; another rider and the driver's own socket do not); 50 sequential pings in order; the ping response unchanged; the details endpoint's access matrix (8 cases), its exact keys and that no email, phone (set on purpose), or license number appears anywhere, a location within 0.0001 degrees of the ping, a null location after the presence key is deleted, and a null location for COMPLETED and CANCELLED rides while the driver is online. Mutation-checked in M3.2: publishing to `user.id` instead of `ride.rider_id` fails 5 tests, swapping lat and lng 4, and returning a location for finished rides 2. **M3.5 additions:** `test_trip.py` (51 tests, about 32 s): the code is stored on accept and no response or WebSocket message contains an `otp` key (a `call()` helper checks every response and a `get()` helper every message in the file), the `/otp` matrix (200 in DRIVER_ASSIGNED and DRIVER_ARRIVED, 409 for REQUESTED, IN_PROGRESS, COMPLETED, CANCELLED, 404 other rider and unknown id, 403 driver and admin, 401), start with the right code (IN_PROGRESS, `started_at`, `otp` NULL, `/otp` 409, a second start 409), a wrong code (400, nothing changed or published to the raw channel `spy` or two sockets, the right code works afterwards) and a ride with no stored code (400, not 500), nine badly formed bodies (422), start from four other states with a right and a wrong code (409), who may start, `ride_updated` to both people and nobody else for arrive, start, and complete, cancel by rider and by driver from DRIVER_ASSIGNED and DRIVER_ARRIVED (CANCELLED, `otp` NULL, event actor, both sockets), cancel from IN_PROGRESS and COMPLETED (409, nothing published), cancel from REQUESTED with a pending offer (`offer_closed` and no `ride_updated` for the offered driver, `ride_updated` for the rider), a whole trip through the API and re-matching, events go out after the commit (a spy on `events.publish` reads through a NEW connection), a failed `redis_client.publish` does not fail arrive or cancel, two simultaneous starts (200 and 409, one IN_PROGRESS event), and a start racing a cancel five times (alternating the canceller; one winner, status and last event agree). `conftest.py`: `insert_ride` gives DRIVER_ASSIGNED and DRIVER_ARRIVED rides `otp="1234"` unless `with_otp=False`. Every `/start` call in `test_rides.py`, `test_matching.py`, and `test_tracking.py` now sends `{"otp": "1234"}` (`test_offers.py` and `test_presence.py` never call it). Three old tests changed meaning: `test_cancelling_after_the_accept_has_no_offer_to_close` now expects the driver's `ride_updated` CANCELLED (and still no `offer_closed`), and the two `test_a_ping_after_the_ride_ended_publishes_nothing` cases drain the spy first because the status changes now publish. Mutation-checked in M3.5: removing the code check fails 2 tests, sending `ride_updated` only to the rider 8, adding `otp` to `RideResponse` 46, not clearing the code on start 6 **M4.2 additions:** `test_concurrency.py` (8 tests, about 5 s) and in `conftest.py` the plain function `widen(monkeypatch, module, name, seconds, first_call_only=False)`, which wraps an async repository function so it sleeps AFTER computing its result (services call repositories through the module, so patching the module attribute works). The tests: (1) one driver, two riders, `get_available_ids` widened by 50 ms: one REQUESTED, one NO_DRIVER_FOUND, one live pending offer; (2) `test_a_stale_prefilter_cannot_create_a_second_offer`, deterministic with two `asyncio.Event`s and no sleeps (rider 2's FIRST `get_available_ids` call returns the real free answer and then waits; rider 1 completes; rider 2 then holds a stale prefilter and must end as NO_DRIVER_FOUND, which only the check after the lock can do); (3) 8 riders and 3 drivers, three rounds in one test: exactly 3 offers to 3 distinct drivers and 5 NO_DRIVER_FOUND; (4) one rider sending 4 requests at once (`get_active_for_rider` widened): one 201 and three 409, one active ride; (5) two offers of one driver accepted at once (`get_active_for_driver` widened): one 200, one 409, one active ride, the loser's ride still REQUESTED; (6) a driver whose row another session holds `FOR UPDATE` is skipped (the ride goes to the second driver, and to the nearest one after the holder lets go); (7) no row lock is left after a success, a NO_DRIVER_FOUND, and a request that fails halfway (the check is a `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` from a separate session); (8) with `database.LOCK_WAIT_MS` patched to 300, accept and `POST /rides` answer 503 `Busy, please retry` after about 300 ms and change nothing. The `hold_row` fixture closes every holder session at teardown, because a failing test that left a row locked made the next test's TRUNCATE wait forever (see the bugs log). Mutation-checked (each makes at least one test fail, and all pass again after the revert): removing `try_lock` fails 3 tests, removing the check after the lock fails the stale-prefilter test and the fan-out test, removing `drivers.lock` in accept fails the accept race and test 8, removing `users.lock` fails the rider race and test 8, removing only the second check in `create_ride` fails the rider race, removing the `lock_timeout` from `users.lock` fails test 8. During the comparison the same tests ran in three modes (see "Fix and compare results"): in mode none every race test failed as expected (strict xfail), in redis and postgres they passed **M4.3 additions:** `test_edge_cases.py` (39 tests, about 30 s, see the "Edge cases" section), fixture `locks_disabled` in `conftest.py` (replaces `users_repo.lock`, `drivers_repo.lock`, and `drivers_repo.try_lock` with versions that never block, skip, or fail, and returns a Counter of how often each was called) and a `logged` fixture in `test_edge_cases.py` (attaches caplog to uvicorn's logger). ONE existing test needed a new setup: `test_two_offers_of_one_driver_accepted_at_once_assign_one_ride` in `test_concurrency.py` inserted two PENDING offers for one driver, which `uq_ride_offers_one_pending_per_driver` now forbids; it became `test_one_offer_accepted_twice_at_once_assigns_one_ride` (the same driver accepts the same offer twice with `get_active_for_driver` widened: it reaches the same driver lock and "no active ride" check in accept; one 200, one 409, one active ride). Every other test passed untouched. Mutation-checked in M4.3 (each fails at least one test, reverted, suite green again): no presence check in the sweeper 6, each of the three indexes made non-unique 3 / 2 / 2, no savepoint around the offer insert 2, no IntegrityError handling in accept 1, in create_ride 1, no per-offer error isolation 1, no deadline check in accept 3, no offer cancellation in cancel 7.
- **Webhook (M5.3):** `POST /webhooks/stripe` reads the RAW body. In this order: no webhook secret set is 503; a body over 262144 bytes is 413; the signature (`Stripe-Signature: t=<unix>,v1=<hex HMAC-SHA256 of "t.body">`, several `v1` allowed, other schemes ignored, `hmac.compare_digest`, timestamp within 300 s either way) else 400 `Invalid signature` with a WARNING that names only the category (missing, malformed, no match, too old); invalid JSON or no `id`, `type`, `data.object` is 400. Then ONE transaction: the event id is inserted into `stripe_events` (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`; nothing inserted answers `{"status": "duplicate"}` and does no work); `checkout.session.completed` and `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded` call `credit_topup` for the top-up named by `client_reference_id` (an ASCII digit string of at most 9 digits, else the event is ignored with a warning); `checkout.session.expired` moves PENDING to EXPIRED (`UPDATE ... WHERE status = PENDING`, so a SUCCEEDED top-up is never touched); any other type is ignored; commit; `{"status": "processed" | "ignored"}`. An unexpected exception propagates as a 500 with nothing committed (the event id rolls back too), so Stripe retries. **`credit_topup`** (shared by the webhook and sync) locks the top-up row, re-reads its status as its own statement (SUCCEEDED is `ignored`), requires `payment_status == "paid"`, and trusts only OUR row: the session id must equal `stripe_session_id`, `amount_total` the top-up amount, the currency `inr` (a mismatch logs an ERROR with the top-up id and the field names, credits nothing, answers `ignored`), then `post_entry` TOPUP and marks the top-up SUCCEEDED with the payment intent id. An EXPIRED top-up that turns out to be paid is credited. Every event we deliberately ignore answers 200.
- **Config and constants (M5.3):** `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `STRIPE_API_URL` (default `https://api.stripe.com`), `APP_BASE_URL` (default `http://localhost:8000`, the Checkout success and cancel addresses); read at call time through `settings`; `.env.example` explains both setups. One startup line says `payments: stripe not configured`, `payments: stripe configured (test key), webhook secret set` (or `NOT set`), or `payments: stripe REJECTED: key is not a test key`, never a value. `services/payments.py`: `TOPUP_MIN_PAISE` 10000, `TOPUP_MAX_PAISE` 1000000, `CURRENCY` inr, `STRIPE_TIMEOUT_S` 10, `CHECKOUT_EXPIRES_S` 3600, `WEBHOOK_TOLERANCE_S` 300, `MAX_WEBHOOK_BYTES` 262144, `TEST_KEY_PREFIXES`. `services/wallet.py`: `ADJUST_MAX_PAISE` 1000000, `ENTRIES_DEFAULT_LIMIT` 20, `ENTRIES_MAX_LIMIT` 100.
- **Lock order (M5.3):** ride row, then wallet row (settlement); top-up row, then wallet row (credit); an adjustment takes only the wallet row. **The wallet row is a LEAF:** nothing else is locked while it is held and `post_entry` is the last write before the commit, so it adds no cycle to the Phase 4 order (ride, offer, driver). No lock is held across a Stripe call (the top-up row is committed before it).
- **`call_stripe` (M5.3):** a new `httpx.AsyncClient` per call (timeout 10 s, basic auth with the key and an empty password, form-encoded body with Stripe's bracket keys, the `Idempotency-Key` header only when given, no `Stripe-Version`); any network error, timeout, invalid JSON or non-2xx is 502, except a 409 from Stripe (409 in progress); only the status and Stripe's error code are logged. The Checkout `expires_at` is the top-up row's `created_at` plus one hour, NOT now: Stripe refuses a retry with the same `Idempotency-Key` whose parameters differ, so a retry after a crash must send exactly the same form.
- **Fake Stripe (M5.3, `simulator/fake_stripe.py`, a development tool, standard library only):** Checkout Session create (basic auth with an `sk_test_` key; honors `Idempotency-Key`: the same key and parameters return the stored answer, the same key with other parameters is 400, the same key while the first call is in flight is 409) and retrieve, a pay page (`GET /pay/{id}`; `POST /pay/{id}/complete` sends a signed `checkout.session.completed` in the background, 3 attempts, and redirects to `success_url`), `POST /_expire/{id}`, `POST /_replay/{event}?times=N` (re-sends a stored event with a fresh signature, N at once, returns the statuses), `GET /_events` (events with their delivery results, and the number of sessions). Flags `--host` (it needs `0.0.0.0` on this machine, because the container reaches the host through the Docker bridge, not 127.0.0.1; it warns), `--port`, `--public-url`, `--webhook-url`, `--webhook-secret`, `--delay-ms`. It does not model refunds, disputes, declines, API versions, expiry by the clock, rate limits, or other currencies. `docker-compose.yml` gives the backend `extra_hosts: host.docker.internal:host-gateway`.
- **Pages (M5.3):** `/rider/` has a Wallet section for every logged-in rider (balance, the reserved amount, "Add money" in whole rupees, the last 5 top-ups with "Check status" on pending ones, the last 10 ledger entries as a label per kind, the signed amount and the balance after; refreshed by the existing 3 second refresh), payment method radios in the request form (cash is the default; wallet shows "Your wallet: X available. This trip can cost up to Y." and adds "Add money or pay with cash." when short; nothing is disabled, the backend's 402 decides), "Payment: wallet|cash" on the ride and "Paid X from your wallet." or "Pay X in cash to the driver." in the finished view (also for a cancellation fee; rides from before M5.3 show the cash text). The Add money handler calls `fetch` itself, the one exception to "always `api.js`", because `shared/api.js` cannot send the header (same error rules); it keeps one `crypto.randomUUID()` key until the request succeeds or the amount changes, and goes to `checkout_url` only when its protocol is http or https. On return (`?topup=success&id=N`, `?topup=cancelled`) it calls sync once, shows "Payment received" or "Payment is still being confirmed" or "Top-up cancelled", and removes the parameters. `/driver/`: the finished-ride lines end with "Collect X in cash from the rider." or "Paid from the rider's wallet." (also the cancellation-fee line).
- **Commission, earnings and receipts (M5.4):** the split is in `payments.charge_ride` and nowhere else. After the payment row it reads the economy pricing rule (a missing rule is the existing 503 and the whole settlement rolls back), computes `platform_fee = (gross * commission_percent + 50) // 100` (half up) and `driver_earning = gross - platform_fee`, and calls `repositories/earnings.create`; then `post_entry` (wallet rides) is still the last write before the commit. Worked example: a fare of 13999 paise at 20 percent gives `(13999 * 20 + 50) // 100 = 2800` for the platform and 11199 for the driver; a real wallet trip of the dev run: 9912 gives 1982 and 7930. Commission covers the whole payment, surge included, and a cancellation fee is split with the same percent. **Settlement definitions:** a cash ride means the driver collected the fare and owes the platform `platform_fee`; a wallet ride means the platform collected it and owes the driver `driver_earning`; `owed_to_driver` is the sum of `driver_earning` over wallet payments, `owed_by_driver` the sum of `platform_fee` over cash payments, `net = owed_to_driver - owed_by_driver` (positive: the platform owes the driver). Derived from the rows, no balance table, no payouts. `services/earnings.py` has `fold_rows` (the one private helper: the rows of ONE grouped query by kind and method become the answer, used by three callers), `get_driver_summary`, `list_entries`, `get_admin_driver_summary`, `get_revenue`; `services/receipts.py` has `get_receipt`; `repositories/earnings.py` has `create`, `summarize` (one grouped query joining payments) and `list_for_driver`. `create_payment` now returns the payment id, `payments.get_payment_by_ride` and `wallet.get_charge_entry` feed the receipt. Constants: `ENTRIES_DEFAULT_LIMIT` 20 and `ENTRIES_MAX_LIMIT` 100 (earnings), `RECEIPT_PREFIX` `RCPT-` (receipts).
