The earning insert takes no explicit lock; the foreign key takes a KEY SHARE lock on the driver's row until the commit (see the open issues). No new events, WebSocket messages or Redis keys.
- **Pages (M5.4):** `/driver/` has an Earnings section (hidden until `GET /drivers/me/earnings` answers, so hidden without a profile): buttons Today, Last 7 days, All time (`state.earningsPeriod`, default Today; the window starts at the browser's local midnight, `toISOString()`, nothing for All time) and Refresh; lines "Trips", "Cancellation fees", "Fares", "Platform fee", "Your earnings", the cash line ("you collected X in cash and owe the platform Y commission"), the wallet line ("Z will be paid to you"), "Balance with the platform: the platform owes you / you owe the platform / nothing owed", the note that payouts are not built; the latest 10 entries of the period with "Show more" (pages with `before_id`, same window). `loadEarnings()` (the one new function) runs at the first refresh after login or page load, on a period button, on Refresh, and once when a ride finishes (`state.earningsKey`, set before the call); never on the 3 second poll. The finished-ride view adds "You earn E (G fare minus F platform fee)." when the entry of the shown ride is in the list. `/rider/` has a Receipt section (a top-level section, hidden until `state.receipt`) fetched once per finished ride with `final_fare > 0` and a trip or cancellation breakdown (`state.receiptKey`, set before the call): receipt number, issue time, route, driver and vehicle, distance and time ("estimated, tracking was not available" for an estimated distance), the lines (base, distance, time, minimum fare, high-demand pricing, cap, total) or the cancellation fee and its reason, then "Paid from your wallet (balance after: B)" or "Paid in cash to the driver"; cleared on "Request a new ride" and on logout; a "Print receipt" button (`window.print()`, not through `act()`: it changes no state) and an `@media print` block that hides every other top-level section, the header and the map section (Leaflet's own styles are untouched). Only `textContent` is used.
- **Final fare and cancellation fees (M5.1):** all money logic is in `services/pricing.py`. `rides.final_fare` is what the rider owes, set once in the same transaction and under the same ride lock as the status change: COMPLETED the trip fare, CANCELLED the cancellation fee (may be 0), NO_DRIVER_FOUND and active rides NULL. Charged inside the same transaction since M5.3 (see the M5.3 bullets).
  - **Constants** (top of `services/pricing.py`): `FARE_CAP_PERCENT = 150`, `MAX_PLAUSIBLE_SPEED_MS = 45`, `MIN_PING_GAP_S = 1`, `UNRELIABLE_BELOW_PERCENT = 50`, `TRIP_TTL_SECONDS = 86400`, `EARTH_RADIUS_M = 6371000`. Pricing rule (economy): cancellation fee 3000 paise (Rs 30), free window 120 s.
  - **Trip meter:** `update_location` calls `pricing.record_trip_point(ride_id, lat, lng)` for a ride that is IN_PROGRESS. First ping creates the hash (distance 0, pings 1); a ping less than 1 s after the stored one is ignored; otherwise the haversine segment (written inline) is added, or counted as a jump (`jumps += 1`, no distance) when segment / seconds is above 45 m/s; the stored point, `ts`, and `pings` always move. A `RedisError` is logged (one warning) and swallowed. `repositories/rides.py` has `get_trip`, `save_trip` (hash + TTL in one pipeline) and `get_last_event_time`.
  - **Completion** (`settle_completed_ride`): duration = `completed_at - started_at` rounded half up, at least 1 s. Distance: tracked (rounded) unless `no_tracking` (no hash, a RedisError at read, or fewer than 2 pings) or `unreliable_tracking` (jumps seen AND tracked distance below 50 percent of the estimate); then the estimated `ride.distance_m` is billed. `computed = calculate_fare(distance, duration)`, `final_fare = min(computed, fare_estimate * 150 // 100)`. No lower bound.
  - **Cancellation** (`cancellation_fee`, used by the cancel and by the quote): driver any 0 `driver_cancelled`; rider from REQUESTED 0 `no_driver_yet`; from DRIVER_ASSIGNED at most `free_cancel_seconds` after the latest DRIVER_ASSIGNED event 0 `within_free_window`, later `cancellation_fee` `late_cancellation`; from DRIVER_ARRIVED `cancellation_fee` `driver_arrived`. The rule is looked up only when the table needs it.
  - **`fare_breakdown` formats** (JSONB; amounts, never rates). Trip: `{"kind": "trip", "distance_m": 2977, "duration_s": 402, "distance_source": "tracked", "fallback_reason": null, "tracked_pings": 134, "jumps_ignored": 0, "base_fare": 5000, "distance_fare": 3572, "time_fare": 1340, "minimum_fare_applied": false, "computed_fare": 9912, "fare_cap": 14544, "capped": false}` (`distance_source` is `tracked` or `estimate`; `fallback_reason` null, `no_tracking`, or `unreliable_tracking`). Cancellation: `{"kind": "cancellation", "fee": 3000, "reason": "driver_arrived", "cancelled_by": "rider"}`. Legacy: `{"kind": "legacy"}` (rides settled before M5.1; `final_fare` NULL).
  - **Pages:** `/rider/` shows a Fare section for a settled ride (trip: total, estimate, distance with "(estimated, tracking was not available)" when the estimate was billed, time as "14 min 5 s", base/distance/time lines, "Minimum fare applied", "Your fare was capped at 150% of the estimate."; cancellation: "Cancellation fee: Rs X" with the reason text, or "No cancellation fee."; none for legacy or NO_DRIVER_FOUND rides). The Cancel button asks the quote endpoint first and shows `confirm()` with the fee (a failed quote shows the error and cancels nothing). `/driver/` shows "Trip fare: Rs X (5.2 km, 14 min)" for a completed ride and the cancelled-ride note says "You cancelled this ride. The rider was not charged.", "The rider cancelled and was charged a cancellation fee of Rs X." or "The rider cancelled. No fee was charged."
  - **Stress tool:** `simulator/invariants.sql` has I8 `completed_without_fare`, I9 `cancelled_without_settlement`, I10 `fare_on_unsettled_ride`, I11 `fare_over_cap` (legacy rides left out of I8 and I9); `stress.py` checks I1 to I11 and the chaos summary prints the completed rides with their fare sum and the cancelled rides with a fee and their sum.
- **Surge pricing (M5.2):** all surge logic is in `services/pricing.py`; no ML, counts only.
  - **Zones:** the geohash of the PICKUP at `ZONE_PRECISION = 5`, from `geohash_encode(lat, lng, precision)` in `utils/geo.py` (in-house, standard algorithm, no decoder; (42.6, -5.6) gives `ezs42`; checked against `pygeohash` for 2000 random points inside the city bounds: 0 mismatches). For the configured city a cell is 0.0439 degrees on each side: 4.89 km tall, 4.77 km wide at latitude 12.98, and 72 zones cover the city box (34.5 x 32.5 km).
  - **Demand** = DISTINCT riders with a ride whose `pickup_zone` is the zone, created in the last `DEMAND_WINDOW_S = 180` s, status REQUESTED or NO_DRIVER_FOUND (`rides_repo.count_unmet_demand_by_zone`). **Supply** = AVAILABLE drivers (`drivers_repo.get_available_ids`, matching's own definition: presence key, approved, no active ride, no pending offer) whose position (`drivers_repo.get_online_positions()`: one ZRANGE, one MGET, one GEOPOS) falls in the zone. **Pressure** = `demand * 100 // max(supply, 1)`.
  - **Multiplier:** `SURGE_STEPS = ((100, 100), (150, 120), (200, 150), (300, 180))` (pressure up to the first value gives the second, in percent), `SURGE_MAX_PERCENT = 200` above the last step, `MIN_DEMAND_FOR_SURGE = 3` (below it never surge). **Cap:** `get_surge_percent` returns `min(snapshot percent, max(100, int(rule.surge_cap * 100 + 0.5)))`; the snapshot stores the uncapped value, so a changed rule applies at once.
  - **Snapshot** (`get_surge_snapshot(db, force=False)`): one city-wide snapshot of every zone with demand or supply, cached in Redis under `surge:snapshot` for `SURGE_CACHE_TTL_S = 15` s (0 or less: no read, no write). A recompute = 1 Postgres query (demand), 1 Redis read (positions), 1 Postgres query (availability, skipped without positions), a loop, 1 Redis write, one INFO log line `Surge snapshot computed: N zones, M above 1.0x, T ms`. No lock: simultaneous requests at expiry may recompute twice (accepted). `get_surge_percent(db, lat, lng)` returns `(zone, percent)`; a `RedisError` gives one warning and 100; a missing pricing rule 503. `get_surge_zones(db, force)` feeds `GET /admin/surge`. Surge code uses `datetime.now(timezone.utc)`, never the `time` module (the M5.1 `fake_clock` replaces `time` inside `services/pricing.py`).
  - **Fare:** `calculate_fare(db, distance_m, duration_s, surge_percent=100, vehicle_type="economy")` returns `normal_fare` (after the minimum fare), `fare_estimate = (normal_fare * surge_percent + 50) // 100` (half up, so the minimum fare is multiplied too), `surge_percent`, `surge_amount`; `base_fare`, `distance_fare`, `time_fare` stay the normal parts. `settle_completed_ride` passes `ride.surge_percent` (the locked one, never the current snapshot); the breakdown keeps every M5.1 key (`computed_fare` is now the surged amount) and gains `normal_fare`, `surge_percent`, `surge_amount`. The 150 percent cap works on the surged estimate. Cancellation fees are never surged.
  - **Constants** (top of `services/pricing.py`): `ZONE_PRECISION = 5`, `DEMAND_WINDOW_S = 180`, `MIN_DEMAND_FOR_SURGE = 3`, `SURGE_STEPS`, `SURGE_MAX_PERCENT = 200`, `SURGE_CACHE_TTL_S = 15`. All are read inside function bodies so tests can monkeypatch them; `tests/conftest.py` has an autouse fixture `no_surge` that sets `MIN_DEMAND_FOR_SURGE` to 10**9 for the whole suite, and `test_surge.py` puts 3 back and sets the cache TTL to 0 unless a test is about caching.
  - **Rider page (`/rider/`):** the estimate panel shows `High demand in your area: fares are 1.5x higher right now.` plus `Normal fare` and `High-demand pricing: +X` lines when `surge_percent > 100`, and nothing extra otherwise. Request ride sends `accepted_surge_percent` from the estimate on screen. After ANY failed `POST /rides` the page asks for the estimate again (`fetchEstimate()`, shared with the point selection, keeps the request counter) and keeps the backend's message in the message area; when the route is unchanged the old path array is reused, so the map does not move. The ride view and the finished fare section add `(includes 1.5x high-demand pricing)` after the estimated fare, and a COMPLETED trip whose breakdown has `surge_percent` above 100 shows `High-demand pricing: 1.5x (+X on a normal fare of Y)`. Not tested in a browser (see Surge results).
  - **Stress tool (M5.3):** `simulator/invariants.sql` has six more invariants I13 `wallet_balance_mismatch`, I14 `ledger_running_balance_mismatch`, I15 `negative_wallet`, I16 `ride_payment_mismatch`, I17 `wallet_charge_mismatch`, I18 `topup_credit_mismatch`; every scenario checks I1 to I18 (I1 to I20 since M5.4). `chaos` funds every stress rider's wallet to Rs 5,000 through the admin API (key `stress-fund-<rider>-<unix time>-<balance>`, one adjustment is at most Rs 10,000), pays half of the rides from the wallet, adds the `chaos_admin` agent (every 2 s a credit of Rs 10 to Rs 100 to a random stress rider, 30 percent of them sent twice at once, all answers for one key must carry one entry id) and ends with `final balance == starting balance + credits - wallet charges` for every stress rider. The new `payments` scenario (`--webhook-secret`, 20 riders, 5 rounds, against the fake Stripe) repeats the top-up request 5 times at once, delivers each signed paid event 10 times plus a second event id 3 times, then three bad events (wrong secret, tampered body, 10 minutes old); it ends `PAYMENTS CLEAN` and exits 2 with `INVARIANTS NOT CHECKED: Stripe is not configured on the backend ...` when the backend has no test key. `api()` and `burst()` carry headers and raw bodies for it.
  - **Stress tool (M5.4):** `invariants.sql` has two more invariants, I19 `earning_payment_mismatch` (every payment has exactly one earning row that agrees with it: same ride, gross equal to the payment amount, the ride's own driver, the breakdown's kind) and I20 `earning_math_mismatch` (the split adds up and the fee is the half-up percent; nothing negative; gross positive; percent 0 to 100); every scenario checks I1 to I20. Neither can be violated even for an instant, because the earning row is written in the payment's transaction. After the settle period, and before the cleanup, `scenario_chaos` prints the earning rows of the run (gross must equal fees plus earnings) and compares the money views with its own grouped SQL: the all-time `GET /admin/revenue` (counts, buckets, settlement), the all-time `GET /drivers/me/earnings` of every stress driver, and up to 20 receipts of the run (amount, method, `RCPT-` number); a difference is asked again once after 2 s and what remains is `money_view_mismatch` (the first five are printed); any non-zero value is a violation (exit 1).
  - **Stress tool:** `simulator/invariants.sql` has I12 `surge_settlement_mismatch` (a settled trip whose breakdown HAS a `surge_percent` key: the breakdown multiplier equals the ride's, `normal_fare + surge_amount = computed_fare`, and `(normal_fare * surge_percent + 50) / 100 = computed_fare`); every scenario checks I1 to I12. In `chaos`, 20 percent of the requests first call `POST /rides/estimate` and send `accepted_surge_percent`; a 409 `Prices have increased...` is counted as `surge_409` (normal). The summary prints rides above 1.0x, the highest multiplier, and the surge part of the completed trips (read-only SQL).
- **JWT:** HS256, signed with `JWT_SECRET`. Claims: `sub` (user id as string), `role`, `exp` (`ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRE_MINUTES`, default 60). `get_current_user` (through `user_from_token`) returns 401 + `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` for a missing, malformed, tampered, or expired token, or a user that no longer exists. `require_role("rider", ...)` returns 403 for other roles.
- **Admin creation (script only):** `docker compose exec backend python create_admin.py --email ... --name ... --password ...` (prints the id; does nothing if the email exists)
- **Static files:** `frontend/` is mounted at `/` with `html=True` after the API routes, so `/rider/` serves `rider/index.html` (`/rider` redirects to `/rider/`)
- **Backend files:** `app/config.py` (settings from env), `app/database.py` (`Base` with constraint naming convention, async engine, `async_session`, `get_db` dependency, Redis client), `app/models.py` (all models), `app/main.py`; `app/schemas.py`, `app/security.py` (argon2 hashing, JWT, `get_current_user`, `require_role`), `app/routers/auth.py`, `app/routers/drivers.py`, `app/routers/admin.py`, `app/services/auth.py`, `app/services/drivers.py`, `app/routers/rides.py`, `app/services/rides.py`, `app/repositories/users.py`, `app/repositories/drivers.py`, `app/repositories/rides.py`, `app/routers/places.py`, `app/services/places.py`, `app/repositories/places.py`, `app/services/routing.py`, `app/services/pricing.py`, `app/services/matching.py`, `app/repositories/pricing.py`, `app/utils/geo.py` (`is_inside_bounds`, used by `services/places.py` and `services/rides.py`), `app/routers/websocket.py` and `app/repositories/events.py` (M3.1), `app/routers/offers.py`, `app/services/offers.py`, `app/repositories/offers.py` (M3.3), `alembic/versions/0004_ride_offers.py`, `backend/create_admin.py`, `backend/pytest.ini`, `backend/tests/conftest.py`, `backend/tests/test_rides.py`, `backend/tests/test_pricing.py`, `backend/tests/test_presence.py`, `backend/tests/test_matching.py`, `backend/tests/test_websocket.py`, `backend/tests/test_tracking.py` (M3.2), `backend/tests/test_offers.py` (M3.3), `backend/tests/test_trip.py` (M3.5), `backend/tests/test_concurrency.py` (M4.2); `alembic.ini` and `alembic/` (async template; `env.py` takes the URL from settings); `osrm/prepare.sh`. `schemas.py` has `EstimateRequest` (range checks and the "must differ" rule), `EstimateResponse`, and `RideCreate`, which inherits from `EstimateRequest` and adds the two addresses
- **Settings (`.env`, all required, read in `config.py`; `REDIS_DB` is optional, default 0):** `NOMINATIM_URL`, `NOMINATIM_USER_AGENT`, `OSRM_URL` (`http://osrm:5000`), `CITY_NAME`, `CITY_CENTER_LAT`, `CITY_CENTER_LNG`, `MAP_ZOOM`, `CITY_SOUTH`, `CITY_WEST`, `CITY_NORTH`, `CITY_EAST` (floats except `MAP_ZOOM`). `OSM_EXTRACT_URL` is also in `.env` but is read only by `osrm/prepare.sh` (the backend ignores unknown variables). Compose reads `.env` only when a container is created, so run `docker compose up -d` after editing it
- **Redis keys:** `places:search:<normalized query>` (JSON list of places, TTL 24 h, empty list cached too), `places:reverse:<lat 4 decimals>:<lng 4 decimals>` (JSON `{display_name}`, TTL 24 h), `places:slot` (`SET NX PX 1100`, the 1-request-per-second limiter), and (M5.1) `ride:{ride_id}:trip` (hash: `distance_m` float, `lat`, `lng`, `ts` epoch seconds float, `pings` int, `jumps` int; TTL 24 h refreshed on every save; never deleted). Failures (429, 502, 404 no address) are never cached **M5.2:** `surge:snapshot`, a JSON string `{"computed_at": <epoch s>, "zones": {"<geohash5>": {"demand": int, "supply": int, "pressure_percent": int, "surge_percent": int}}}` with a 15 s TTL (`SET ... EX`), the UNCAPPED step values; absent when `SURGE_CACHE_TTL_S` is 0 or less.
- **Driver presence (M2.3), Redis only, nothing in Postgres:** `drivers:geo` is a GEO set whose member is `drivers.id` as a string (not the user id). `driver:{driver_id}:presence` is a string holding the epoch seconds of the last update, with a 30 second TTL (`PRESENCE_TTL_SECONDS`) that every update refreshes. The presence key is the source of truth for "online". The GEO set is only a spatial index and can keep stale members (a driver whose key expired, since there is no cleanup), so M2.4 must check the presence key for every member it reads. `repositories/drivers.py` has `set_online`, `refresh_location` (`SET ... XX EX 30`, then `GEOADD` only if the key existed), `set_offline` (`DEL` then `ZREM`), and `get_presence`; longitude goes first into `GEOADD` and comes first out of `GEOPOS`, handled only there. Services: `go_online`, `go_offline`, `update_location`, `get_presence` in `services/drivers.py`; `set_verification` forces a driver who is not approved offline. The `REDIS_DB` setting (default 0) picks the Redis database index; the tests set it to 1
- **Driver simulator (M2.5):** `simulator/simulator.py` (one file; plain functions `haversine_m`, `api`, `get_route`, `run_driver`, `main`) and `simulator/requirements.txt` (`httpx==0.28.1`, the same pin as the backend). It runs on the HOST in a venv (`python -m venv .venv-sim`, `pip install -r simulator/requirements.txt`), calls only the API (`127.0.0.1:8000`) and the local OSRM (`127.0.0.1:5000`), and has no database, Redis, backend import, or Nominatim/OSM access. No backend, frontend, or test files changed.
  - **Offers (M3.3):** REST only, still no WebSocket. When `GET /rides/active` answers 404 the driver calls `GET /drivers/me/offer` (never while a ride is active). The first time it sees an offer id it decides ONCE: a random choice by `--accept-rate` (default 0.7) and `--reject-rate` (0.15), the rest ignore the offer; and a due time of now plus a random delay between `--response-delay-min` (1) and `--response-delay-max` (6) seconds, capped so it is at least 2 seconds (`OFFER_SAFETY_MARGIN_S`) before the offer's `expires_in` and never negative. When the due time has passed it posts accept or reject; a 404 or 409 means the offer is gone and the decision is forgotten (an offer id already decided is not decided again). Ignored offers need no call. Response times are rounded up to the 3-second tick. The decision is logged once (`offer 12 (ride 7): will accept after 3.4 s`) and the 15-second summary and the final line carry `offers seen N (accepted A, rejected R, ignored I)`. After an accept the existing logic takes over from `GET /rides/active` on the next tick; drivers keep wandering while an offer is pending. Startup exits with a message if a rate is negative, the two rates add up to more than 1, or min is above max.
  - **Command:** `python simulator/simulator.py --drivers N --admin-email ... --admin-password ...` (or env `SIM_ADMIN_EMAIL` / `SIM_ADMIN_PASSWORD`; the admin must already exist). Flags: `--drivers` (default 20, 1 to 200), `--api-url`, `--osrm-url`, `--center-lat` / `--center-lng` (both or neither, default the city center from `/places/map-config`), `--radius-km` (5), `--speed-kmh` (30), `--seed` (a `random.Random` per driver, seeded from seed and driver number, so each driver's random choices repeat), `--otp` (M3.5, default `1234`: the trip code sent with `POST /rides/{id}/start`; a wrong one makes every driver wait at the pickup and log "wrong trip code for ride N" once per ride). Startup exits with a message for: `--drivers` out of range, no admin credentials, one of lat/lng only, a center outside the city bounds, a center more than 300 m from a road, API or OSRM unreachable, a failed admin login, or a non-admin account. Without httpx it prints the pip command.
  - **Accounts (idempotent):** driver n is `sim-driver-{n:03d}@sim.example.com` (the domain passes email validation), name `Sim Driver {n:03d}`, password `sim-driver-pass`, license `SIM-LIC-{n:03d}`, plate `SIM{n:03d}`, "Sim Car", "Grey". Setup per driver skips what is done: login (401: register, login) then `GET /drivers/me` (404: profile; no vehicle: add it), approve through the admin API unless approved, `GET /drivers/me/presence` (online: resume from that position; otherwise a random start), `POST /drivers/me/online`. Setup runs 5 drivers at a time, 30 s HTTP timeout. The log line says what was done ("registered, profile created, ...", "account reused", or "resumed from its last position").
  - **Start point and wander:** a random point in the circle (`r = radius * sqrt(random())`, random bearing, inside the city box), snapped with OSRM `nearest` (at most 300 m away), and it must have a route to the fleet center (a road cut off from the network would park the driver for good); up to 10 tries. A wander leg is an OSRM route from the current position to a random point in the circle (up to 10 tries on "no route"; if OSRM itself fails the driver stays put and tries again next tick).
  - **Ticks:** one task per driver, started after a random 0 to 3 s delay, then every 3 s on a fixed schedule (no drift). Each tick: (1) `GET /rides/active` (and, with no ride, the offer step above); (2) advance by speed x real seconds since the last tick (per-driver factor 0.8 to 1.2, linear interpolation between polyline points, cumulative haversine lengths); (3) finished leg; (4) `POST /drivers/me/location` clamped to the city box. A plan is rebuilt whenever (kind, ride id) changes, so a restart resumes mid-trip:

    | Active ride | Plan | Leg finished |
    |---|---|---|
    | none (404) | wander to a random point | next wander leg on the next tick |
    | DRIVER_ASSIGNED | route to the pickup | `POST /rides/{id}/arrive` |
    | DRIVER_ARRIVED | stay put | after 2 ticks, `POST /rides/{id}/start` with `{"otp": <--otp>}` |
    | IN_PROGRESS | route to the drop-off | `POST /rides/{id}/complete` |

    A 409 from arrive, start, or complete is ignored (the next tick resyncs). A 400 from start (wrong code, M3.5) is logged ONCE per ride as `action: wrong trip code for ride N` (the same text every tick, so the existing change-only logging prints it once, then `action recovered` when the ride is gone) and retried on the next tick (at most one try per tick); a 422 is logged once like any other unexpected answer. A ride that disappears (rider cancelled) is dropped and the driver wanders on. Ping answers: 200 ok; 409 means presence expired, so `POST /drivers/me/online` with the current position; 401 means log in again and retry once; 403 means rejected, log it and stop that driver only; 422, 502, 503, network errors, and timeouts are a failed ping and the driver keeps going. At most one retry per tick.
  - **Output:** `logging` with timestamps (httpx's own request log is silenced). Only changes are logged: online (and how), ride assigned with the straight-line distance to the pickup, arrived, trip started, trip completed, ride gone, a problem when it starts (once per kind and text, such as `ping: ping answered 503`) and `... recovered`. Every 15 s one summary line: `running R/N, on a ride X, pings ok A failed B, avg ping M ms, rides completed C` (interval values, except rides completed). Ctrl+C (a signal handler on POSIX; on Windows asyncio.run cancels the main task instead, which the code handles but was not run on Windows) stops the drivers, sends `POST /drivers/me/offline` for each (a 409 for a driver on a ride is ignored; its presence just expires), prints a `final:` line, and exits within about half a second for 50 drivers.
  - **Measured (this machine, one uvicorn worker with `--reload`):** see the open issues for the 50 and 200 driver numbers. Movement matched speed x factor within 2 percent over 15 s; 20 samples of one driver were at most 0.2 m from the nearest road; all start points were 0.8 to 4.7 km from the center (limit 5); a 30 driver fleet at 90 km/h completes a 2.7 km trip in about 2.5 minutes including the approach; the matched driver was always the nearest from `GEOSEARCH`.
- **Concurrency control (M4.2):** Postgres row locks, `FOR UPDATE`, one mechanism, no switch. Three critical sections, each = lock, then the deciding check as its OWN statement, then the write, then the commit (which releases the lock): **C1** `create_ride` locks the rider's `users` row (`users_repo.lock`) after `estimate_ride` (so never across OSRM) and re-runs `get_active_for_rider`; the first check stays as the cheap early exit that costs no routing call. **C2** `matching.offer_to_next_driver` keeps the prefilter (`get_available_ids` over all candidates; stale is fine, it only avoids useless locks), then walks the candidates in `(distance, driver id)` order: `drivers_repo.try_lock` (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`; a locked driver is SKIPPED, never waited for), then `get_available_ids(db, [driver_id])` again; the first driver that passes both gets the offer. It is also used by `finish_offer`, so reject and expiry are covered. **C3** `offers.accept` takes `drivers_repo.lock` (blocking `FOR UPDATE` on the driver row) after the existing ride and offer locks and BEFORE the status, expiry, approved, active-ride, and presence checks. All lock queries are column-only selects (`select(Driver.id)`, `select(User.id)`), so the identity map cannot return a stale entity. `users.lock` and `drivers.lock` first run `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = <database.LOCK_WAIT_MS>` (3000, an `int()` formatted into the statement, read at call time so tests can patch it). When that expires SQLAlchemy raises `sqlalchemy.exc.DBAPIError` with `error.orig.sqlstate == "55P03"` (the cause is `asyncpg.exceptions.LockNotAvailableError`); the service wraps ONLY the lock call in `try/except DBAPIError`, re-raises anything that is not 55P03, and answers **503 `Busy, please retry`**. There is no 409 for lock contention any more. The timeout stays set for the rest of the transaction. **Lock order:** blocking locks only as ride row, offer row, driver row (accept), or the rider's user row alone (create_ride); every lock taken while another is held in matching is non-blocking (SKIP LOCKED), so no cycle can form. Verified: 0 `deadlock detected` lines in the Postgres log over the whole session (every stress run, including two processes, and every test run). Tests widen the race window with `widen()` (see Tests); production code has no delay. The race itself and the evidence are in "Known races" and "Fix and compare results".
- **Safety net (M4.3):** the three partial unique indexes above are the last line of defense behind the locks. Each place that can hit one catches `sqlalchemy.exc.IntegrityError` narrowly: `create_ride` wraps the ride creation through the commit (rollback, 409 "You already have an active ride"); `matching.offer_to_next_driver` wraps the offer insert in a savepoint (`db.begin_nested()`), catches the error outside it, logs WARNING `offer skipped for driver <id>: <error text>`, and tries the next candidate (the transaction and the request survive); `accept` wraps everything from the first change through the commit (rollback, 409 "You already have an active ride"). With the locks working none of these ever fires (see "Edge cases" for the numbers), so an `offer skipped` warning means a lock failed. `get_available_ids` excludes a driver with ANY pending offer (live or overdue), which is what the pending-offer index counts.
- **Offers sweeper (M4.3):** `expire_due_offers` reads ALL PENDING offers (`offers_repo.list_pending(db, 200)`, oldest `expires_at` first, replaces `list_due`), makes ONE `drivers_repo.get_online_ids` call for their drivers (never with an empty list), and treats an offer as due when its deadline has passed (reason `expired`) or else when its driver has no presence key (reason `driver_offline`: the driver went offline, an admin rejected them, or their page died and the key expired). Each due offer gets a NEW session: lock the ride, load the offer under its lock, skip it unless it is still PENDING and still due (for `driver_offline` presence is re-checked), then `finish_offer(..., EXPIRED, reason)` (`responded_at` stays null). Outage errors (`OUTAGE_ERRORS` = `RedisError`, `OSError`, `sqlalchemy.exc.InterfaceError`, `sqlalchemy.exc.OperationalError`) abort the whole pass, and `sweep_forever` logs the streak once; any other exception logs ONE warning per offer id (`Offer sweeper could not handle offer N`) and the pass goes on (module-level set `failed_offer_ids`, pruned to the still-pending ids at the start of each pass). Provoked classes when Postgres stopped: `InterfaceError` ("connection is closed", a dead pooled connection) and `socket.gaierror` (an `OSError`, the host `db` unresolvable); during the few seconds of shutdown a plain `DBAPIError` ("the database system is shutting down") also appeared, which is handled as a per-offer failure (one warning per offer, the next pass recovers). Offer closed reasons on `offer_closed`: accepted, rejected, expired, ride_cancelled, driver_offline.
- **Concurrency stress tool (M4.1):** `simulator/stress.py` (one file, host only, httpx only, no backend import, no Redis, no Nominatim/OSM: only our API and OSRM) and `simulator/invariants.sql` (plain read-only SQL, runnable by hand: `docker compose exec -T db psql -U uber -d uber -At -F '|' < simulator/invariants.sql`). Built in M4.1 to reproduce and measure the race (nothing under `backend/` changed then); since M4.2 the same commands are the proof of the fix and **exit code 0 is the expected result**, exit code 1 means an invariant broke. M4.2 added: `--api-url` takes a comma-separated list (every URL gets a `/health` check at startup; the requests of a burst are spread over the URLs by request index, so the repeats of one rider alternate between processes), `--label` (free text in the settings line and the summary header), and in the `drivers` scenario `offers N/E` per round (E = min(riders, drivers)) plus the summary line `lost matches: <rounds with N below E>` and the total offers against the total expected (fewer offers than expected is reported but is not an invariant, so the exit codes stay 0, 1, 2).
  - **Invariants** (`\echo '== name'` then one SELECT, one row per offender, empty = holds; statuses are stored as the upper-case enum names in VARCHAR, no CHECK constraint): **I1 driver_pending_offers** (no driver with more than one PENDING offer whose `expires_at` is in the future), **I2 driver_active_rides** (no driver with more than one ride in DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, IN_PROGRESS: the real double assignment), **I3 rider_active_rides** (no rider with more than one ride in REQUESTED, DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, IN_PROGRESS), **I4 stuck_requested** (no REQUESTED ride without a PENDING offer), and since M4.3 **I5 overdue_pending_offers** (no PENDING offer more than 10 s past its deadline: a dead or slow sweeper), **I6 orphan_pending_offers** (no PENDING offer on a ride that is not REQUESTED), **I7 assigned_without_accepted_offer** (every DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, or IN_PROGRESS ride has an ACCEPTED offer for its own driver). I4 to I7 cannot be legitimately violated even for an instant (each pair of changes is one transaction), so any sighting at any snapshot counts. `check_invariants` runs all seven and a per-round line shows only the non-zero ones (`I1-I7 ok` otherwise).
  - **Command:** `python simulator/stress.py --scenario drivers|riders|fleet|chaos --admin-email ... --admin-password ...` (or `SIM_ADMIN_EMAIL` / `SIM_ADMIN_PASSWORD`; the admin must already exist; not needed for `--cleanup-only`). Flags: `--api-url`, `--osrm-url`, `--center-lat/--center-lng` (both or neither, default the city center, must be inside the city), `--rounds` (5, 1 to 50), `--riders` (20, 2 to 100), `--drivers` (default 3 for drivers, min(2 x riders, 100) for riders, unused for fleet; 1 to 100), `--repeat` (2, 2 to 5, riders scenario), `--spread-m` (150), `--watch-seconds` (30, fleet), `--seed`, `--sequential` (control run: one request after another), `--keep-last-round`, `--cleanup-only`, `--psql-user/--psql-db` (default `POSTGRES_USER`/`POSTGRES_DB` read from `.env` by string splitting). M4.3 flags: `--chaos-seconds` (60, 10 to 600), `--settle-seconds` (45, 30 to 300), `--tolerate-5xx`, `--otp` (1234); `--riders` now defaults to 20 (30 for chaos) and `--drivers` to 10 for chaos. Bad arguments exit 2 with a usage message.
  - **Chaos scenario (M4.3):** `chaos_rider` and `chaos_driver` (the only new functions besides `scenario_chaos`; they call `api` and nothing else), one asyncio task per rider and per driver, each with its own `random.Random(f"{seed}-rider-{n}")` (seed random and printed when `--seed` is not given). A pool of 60 snapped pickup/drop-off pairs is made before the agents start. **Rider:** `POST /rides` (10 percent: the same request twice at once), then 25 percent cancel after 0 to 10 s, otherwise poll `GET /rides/active` every 2 s until it is gone (the final status is read through `GET /rides/{id}`), cancelling after 90 s; pause 0 to 3 s; again. **Driver (a tick every second):** location ping every 3 s while online; with a ride, the next step after 0 to 3 s (assigned: arrive, 10 percent cancel; arrived: start with `--otp`, 10 percent cancel; in progress: complete after 1 to 4 s); without one, `GET /drivers/me/offer` and accept 50, reject 15, ignore 15, offline for 2 to 5 s 10, accept twice at once 10 (percent). At the deadline a driver finishes a trip in progress. The main loop snapshots I1 to I7 and one counts query about every 2 s and prints a progress line every 10 s (elapsed, requests, rides and offers by status, 409s, 5xx, violations). After the agents stop the settle period polls until no REQUESTED ride and no PENDING offer is left (or `--settle-seconds` pass); what remains is STUCK and printed with its offers; assigned, arrived, and in-progress rides left are counted and are not stuck. Summary: action counts, final ride and offer status counts, 5xx and connection errors, offenders per invariant, stuck rides, warnings for any action type that never happened, and the verdict `CHAOS CLEAN` or `CHAOS FOUND PROBLEMS: ...`. Exit 0 clean, 1 any violation or stuck ride or (without `--tolerate-5xx`) any 5xx or connection error, 2 could not run. The first five 5xx bodies are printed. A duplicate ride request often gets two 201s (not a violation): when the first ride ends at once as NO_DRIVER_FOUND it is not active, so the second is a new ride.
  - **Scenarios.** `drivers` (run WITHOUT the simulator): `--drivers` stress drivers go online inside a 2 km disc, every stress rider sends `POST /rides` at once, snapshot (I1), then EVERY live PENDING offer of the stress drivers is accepted in one burst with its own driver's token, snapshot (I2). Warns "other drivers are online, stop the simulator, this round is diluted" when an offer went to a non-stress driver. `riders` (WITHOUT the simulator): every rider sends the same request `--repeat` times at once, snapshot (I3), per-rider 201/409 counts in the summary. `fleet` (simulator already running, for example `--drivers 30 --speed-kmh 30 --seed 1`): riders request at once, then the invariants are snapshotted about every second for `--watch-seconds`, then the ride statuses are reported; exit 2 if no `sim-driver-%` user exists or if EVERY ride of the run ended NO_DRIVER_FOUND (proves nothing). Every round starts and ends with the cleanup.
  - **Firing together:** one shared `httpx.AsyncClient` (200 connections, keepalive expiry 2 s), warmed up with as many `GET /health` calls as there are requests, one task per request parked on one `asyncio.Event`, set after 0.5 s; each answer records status, body, and `response.elapsed`. More than 200 requests (riders x repeat) queue on the pool.
  - **Accounts (idempotent, never deleted):** `stress-driver-NN@sim.example.com` ("Stress Driver NN", license `STR-LIC-NN`, plate `STRNN`, "Stress Car", Grey, approved through the admin API), `stress-rider-NNN@sim.example.com`; password is a constant at the top of the file (`stress-pass-1234`). Setup runs 5 accounts at a time (argon2 blocks the backend's loop) with a 30 s HTTP timeout.
  - **psql:** `docker compose exec -T -e PGOPTIONS='-c default_transaction_read_only=on' db psql ... -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -At -F '|'`, SQL on stdin, run from the repository root, 30 s timeout. Postgres itself refuses any write on those sessions (checked: `CREATE TABLE` fails with "read-only transaction"). Geometry: random points from `random.Random(--seed)` (repeatable only for the points, not for the timing), snapped with OSRM `nearest` (at most 300 m, 10 tries), drop-offs 1500 to 3000 m from the center, fixed address strings.
  - **Cleanup** (start and end of every round, in a `finally`, on Ctrl+C, and `--cleanup-only`): lists the active rides of all stress riders FROM THE DATABASE (so it also clears a rider with two active rides and leftovers of a crashed run), cancels each with that rider's token (404 and 409 ignored), repeats until a query finds none (max 5 passes; after Ctrl+C or a failure it first waits 3 s, because requests still in flight are finished by the server and create rides after the first listing), then takes the stress drivers offline (all of them for `--cleanup-only`, otherwise this run's). `--keep-last-round` skips it after a run that finished normally.
  - **Output and exit codes:** timestamped `logging`; the settings at startup; one line per round (requests together, burst time, median and max latency, outcome counts such as `201 REQUESTED x13, 201 NO_DRIVER_FOUND x7`, accept results, and `I1 3 drivers (max 8 offers), I2 ..., I3 ok, I4 ok`); each offender's full row once per round; a final summary (rounds run, per invariant the rounds violated and the worst case, HTTP status counts of the run, latency per endpoint) and one verdict. **0** no violation observed (`No invariant violations observed in N rounds`), **1** at least one (`RACE REPRODUCED: I1 in 4 of 5 rounds, I2 in 2 of 5 rounds`), **2** the tool could not do its job (setup or login failed, API unreachable, `INVARIANTS NOT CHECKED: <reason>` when psql cannot run, no snapshot taken, fleet absent or all NDF). Ctrl+C runs the cleanup, prints the summary so far, and uses the same codes. Functions (no classes): `main`, `scenario_drivers`, `scenario_riders`, `scenario_fleet`, `setup_accounts` (one account; callers gather), `burst`, `check_invariants`, `cleanup`, and the low-level `api`, `sql`, `snap`.
- **WebSocket (M3.1):** `ws://<host>:8000/ws`. Built in M3.1; since M3.2 the rider page uses it and since M3.3 the driver page does too (both through `frontend/shared/ws.js`); the admin page and the simulator do not open a socket. Every message in both directions is a JSON text frame `{"type": "<string>", "data": {...}}`.
  - **Client to server:** `auth` with `{token}` as the FIRST message within `AUTH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (never in the URL); afterwards `ping` with `{}`.
  - **Server to client:** `auth_ok` `{user_id, role}`; `pong` `{}`; `error` `{detail}` for an unknown type (socket stays open); and any event sent through `repositories/events.publish`, forwarded as `{"type": <type>, "data": <data>}`. `auth_ok`, `pong`, and `error` are reserved for the protocol (publish does not enforce this).
  - **Close codes** (constants at the top of `routers/websocket.py`): 4400 bad message after auth (not a text frame, not a JSON object, or no string `type`), 4401 unauthorized (the first message is not a text frame holding a JSON object with `type` "auth" and a string token, or the token is invalid, expired, tampered, or its user is gone), 4408 no first message in time, 4409 replaced by a newer connection. uvicorn adds 1009 (frame over the 64 KB limit, seen as "frame exceeds limit of 65536 bytes") and 1012 (server shutting down or reloading).
  - **Auth:** any logged-in user (rider, driver, admin). `security.user_from_token(db, token)` returns the user or None for any problem and is used by `get_current_user` (HTTP behavior unchanged) and by the socket. The socket opens a short `database.async_session()` for that lookup only. The token is checked only at connect.
  - **Registry:** `connections: dict[int, list[WebSocket]]` in `routers/websocket.py` (user id to that process's sockets, oldest first). At `MAX_CONNECTIONS_PER_USER` (5) the oldest is removed from the list and closed with 4409 (no `await` between the count check and the append). The endpoint's `finally` removes its socket, deletes the user's key when the list is empty, and tolerates the socket already being gone. One log line on connect and one on disconnect: `WebSocket connected: user 5 (rider), 2 connection(s)`.
  - **Redis pub/sub:** one channel `ws:events:{REDIS_DB}` (dev `ws:events:0`, tests `ws:events:1`) carrying JSON `{"user_id": int, "type": str, "data": object}`. `repositories/events.py` has `CHANNEL`, `publish(user_id, type, data)` (returns the subscriber count Redis reports: the number of backend processes listening) and `subscribe()` (a `PubSub` that ignores subscribe confirmations). Later milestones call `events.publish` straight from their services. Each backend process runs ONE listener task (`listen_for_events`, started by the `lifespan` in `main.py`, cancelled and awaited on shutdown) that sends each message to that user's local sockets only; a process with no socket for the user does nothing. A message that is not JSON, not an object, has a non-int `user_id` (a bool is rejected), a non-string `type`, or a non-object `data` is dropped with one warning line. A send that fails or takes longer than `SEND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` removes the socket from the registry, logs a warning, and closes it. On `RedisError` the listener logs ONCE that it is down, retries every `RECONNECT_DELAY_SECONDS`, and logs ONCE when it recovers; the pubsub object is always closed first. redis-py also reconnects a killed pub/sub connection by itself (seen: back in under a second, no log line). The backend starts and serves HTTP with Redis down.
  - **Constants** (top of `routers/websocket.py`): `AUTH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5`, `MAX_CONNECTIONS_PER_USER = 5`, `SEND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5`, `RECONNECT_DELAY_SECONDS = 1`.
  - **Events (M3.2):** `driver_location` with `{ride_id: int, lat: float, lng: float, updated_at: int}`. Sent to `ride.rider_id` (a users.id) and to nobody else, from `services/drivers.update_location` through `events.publish`, once per successful ping while the driver has a ride in `ACTIVE_RIDE_STATUSES`. `lat` and `lng` are the ping request's values and `updated_at` is the epoch seconds `refresh_location` returned (the same number as in the ping response). No driver id, user id, name, or other personal data. Nothing is published by go online, by a ping that answers 403, 409, or 422, by a driver with no ride, or after the ride is COMPLETED or CANCELLED
  - **Events (M3.3):** all tiny, no personal data, always published AFTER the commit, all through `events.publish` (best-effort now, see Offers). `offer_created` `{offer_id, ride_id}` to the offered driver's USER id (from `create_ride` for the first offer and from `finish_offer` for the next). `offer_closed` `{offer_id, ride_id, reason}` to the offered driver's user id, `reason` one of `accepted`, `rejected`, `expired`, `ride_cancelled`. `ride_updated` `{ride_id, status}` to the ride's rider user id when the offer flow changes the ride: accepted (`DRIVER_ASSIGNED`) or offers exhausted (`NO_DRIVER_FOUND`). Extended in M3.5 (next item). Pages react to an event by calling their existing REST refresh **M4.3:** `offer_closed` has a fifth reason, `driver_offline` (the sweeper withdrew the offer because the driver's presence key was gone); the driver page shows "The offer was withdrawn because you went offline." for it.
  - **Events (M3.5):** `ride_updated` `{ride_id, status}` (status is the new status as a string) is now sent after EVERY status change made by arrive, start, complete, and cancel, to BOTH the ride's rider and its assigned driver (user ids), after the commit and best-effort. The accept flow is unchanged: the rider gets `ride_updated` DRIVER_ASSIGNED and the driver gets `offer_closed` "accepted" (not `ride_updated`). A ride cancelled from REQUESTED has no driver, so only the rider gets `ride_updated` and the offered driver gets `offer_closed` "ride_cancelled". A refused change (409, wrong code 400) publishes nothing. The actor's other tabs also receive it. The trip code is never in an event.
  - **Delivery guarantees:** none. Pub/sub is fire and forget: an event is lost if the user is not connected or the listener was down at that moment. The REST API stays the source of truth; since M3.4 the pages re-fetch their state over REST after every reconnect (verified live: an event published while the backend was down, Redis reported 0 subscribers, was never delivered).
  - **uvicorn 0.54.0 facts:** it uses the `websockets-sansio` implementation (websockets 17.2, pinned in `requirements.txt` because the tests import it). Its default ping interval and timeout are 20 s and 20 s (it pings idle connections, which removes half-dead sockets); default `ws_max_size` is 16 MiB, lowered to 65536 with `--ws-max-size` in compose and the Dockerfile. redis-py is 8.1.0.
- **Frontend pages** (plain HTML/CSS/ES modules, no libraries except Leaflet on the rider page; each app is one page with its login form inside; all poll every 3 s):
  - `/rider/` (`rider.js`): log in or register (role rider, phone sent only if filled). Logged in as another role: "This account is a X. Open /X/ instead." Rider: after login it fetches `/places/map-config` once and shows a Leaflet 1.9.4 map (OSM tiles, attribution, zoom 10 to 19, locked to the city box). With no ride, the rider picks Pickup and Drop-off by typing a place and pressing Enter or Search (results are buttons; one request per search, none while typing, under 3 characters shows a message with no request), or by clicking the map (the "Next map click sets" radio picks which point; it switches to the empty one automatically). A click outside the city shows a message; a click calls `/places/reverse` and falls back to `lat, lng` (5 decimals) if there is no address. Markers are green (Pickup) and red (Drop-off) circle markers with fixed-text tooltips. As soon as both points are set, and again whenever either changes (never from polling or `render()`), it calls `POST /rides/estimate`: the old estimate and route line are cleared at once, "Estimating route..." shows, a request counter makes sure only the latest answer is applied, and the map fits the route once per answer (`fitBounds`, padding 40, not animated). An estimate panel shows distance and time ("7.8 km, 13 min"), the estimated fare, the base, distance, and time parts, and "Minimum fare applies" when it does; money uses `Intl.NumberFormat("en-IN", INR)` on paise / 100. A 422 or 502 shows the backend message and keeps Request ride disabled. The route is a `L.polyline` (weight 5, behind the markers) created, updated, and removed in `render()`. Request ride is enabled only when both points and a current estimate exist, and calls `POST /rides` with those points. With a ride, the form is hidden, the map is view-only with the ride's two markers and fits to them once per ride id (polling never moves the map). Request ride sends `POST /rides` and remembers the ride from the answer (a `NO_DRIVER_FOUND` ride is not returned by `/rides/active`, so the id could not come from there). Status texts (the M3.5 wording is in the trip-flow item below): REQUESTED "Looking for a driver", DRIVER_ASSIGNED "Your driver is on the way", NO_DRIVER_FOUND "No drivers are available nearby right now. Please try again in a moment." Active ride shows id, status text, addresses, distance and time, estimated fare (a dash when the ride has no stored values), the driver id once assigned, the "Your driver" section (below), and the events timeline; the ride's route is asked for once per ride (`POST /rides/estimate` with the ride's coordinates, `routeRideId` set before the call so a failure shows once and is not retried every poll) and drawn, also after a reload and on the finished ride; Cancel (with `confirm()`) in REQUESTED / DRIVER_ASSIGNED / DRIVER_ARRIVED. A finished ride (COMPLETED, CANCELLED, NO_DRIVER_FOUND; no Cancel button) stays on screen (fetched once with `GET /rides/{id}` after `/rides/active` turns 404) with its markers, its route, the timeline, and a "Request a new ride" button, which clears the selection, inputs, results lists, markers, estimate, route line, and the driver details and position
  - **Live tracking on `/rider/` (M3.2):** after login, register, or a page load with a rider session the page opens ONE WebSocket through `shared/ws.js` (`connect(socketHandlers)`; since M3.3 the socket code lives there, the page has no `openSocket` function any more), which sends `auth` as the first message and reports `onStatus("open")` after `auth_ok`; the page handles `driver_location` (ignored unless `data.ride_id` is the ride on screen and lat, lng, `updated_at` are finite numbers) and, since M3.3, `ride_updated` for the ride on screen (it calls its existing `refresh()`, so a driver's accept or the end of the offers shows within a moment instead of up to 3 s). It is never opened for a wrong-role account, logout closes it, and every handler in `ws.js` first checks that its socket is still the current one. Since M3.4 the socket reconnects by itself (see `shared/ws.js` below). `refresh()` calls `GET /rides/{id}/driver` once per ride and driver (`state.driverKey` is set BEFORE the call, so a failure shows once and is not retried on every poll). That fills the "Your driver" section (name, "color model, plate PLATE", the tracking line (see M3.4 below), and "Last location update: <time>"; a finished ride shows only name and vehicle) and gives the first position. A blue `L.circleMarker` (radius 10, `interactive: false`, fixed tooltip "Driver") is created by `render()` while the ride is DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, or IN_PROGRESS and a position exists (the map is fitted once to pickup, drop-off, and driver at that moment, and never moved by the page afterwards), and removed when the ride ends, on "Request a new ride", and on logout. Only `applyDriverLocation` and `animateDriver` move it: an update with an older `updated_at` than the stored one is dropped; otherwise the marker glides from where it is drawn now to the new position with `requestAnimationFrame` over `ANIMATION_MS = 3000` (time-based, linear), or jumps at once when the move is over `SNAP_DISTANCE_M = 500` or `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` is on (read at each update)
  - `/driver/` (`driver.js`): same login/register with role driver. Shows the driver id, then walks profile form, vehicle form, and the verification text (pending / rejected / approved). When approved: a "Your position" section above the ride section with a Leaflet map (same tiles, center, zoom, and bounds as the rider page, created once from `/places/map-config`, no Nominatim calls), a blue circle marker with the tooltip "You", the status line ("Offline" or "Online, last update <time>"), and Go online / Go offline buttons. Clicking the map inside the city sets the position (a click outside shows "That location is outside <city>" and changes nothing); while online a click also sends `POST /drivers/me/location`. Go online is enabled when offline with a position set; Go offline shows only when online and is disabled during an active ride, with the note "Finish or cancel your ride to go offline". The heartbeat is inside the 3 s `refresh()`: it reads the presence, takes the position from it after a reload, and pings `POST /drivers/me/location` every poll while online. Then "No ride assigned yet." or the ride with buttons by status (arrived, start, complete, cancel) and the same finished-ride behavior. **M3.2:** while the driver has an active ride, a green "Pickup" and a red "Drop-off" `L.circleMarker` (radius 9, `interactive: false`, so a click on them still moves the driver; drawn under the driver's own marker; fixed tooltips) are created, moved, and removed in `render()` from `state.ride`, and the map is fitted once per ride id to them and the driver's own position; a finished ride removes them. (M3.3 added a WebSocket to this page: see the next item.)
  - **Offers on `/driver/` (M3.3):** the page opens one WebSocket through `shared/ws.js` when a driver session starts (login, register, or a page load with a driver session; never for the wrong-role message) and closes it on logout. A line "Live updates: ..." (texts in the M3.4 item below) shows for every driver. `offer_created` and `offer_closed` events only call the page's `refresh()` (for `offer_closed` the notice is set first: "The offer expired." for `expired`, "The rider cancelled the request." for `ride_cancelled`, nothing for `accepted` and `rejected`; an event about an older offer than the one on screen sets no notice). The 3-second `refresh()` ALSO calls `GET /drivers/me/offer` (404 means none), but only for an approved driver with no active ride: that polling is the safety net and stays even when the socket works. `state.offer` holds the offer; the first time an offer id is seen `state.deadline = performance.now() + expires_in * 1000` and `state.offerTotal = Math.ceil(expires_in)` are set and `state.notice` is cleared, and a later poll of the SAME id never resets the deadline (a reload therefore shows the remaining time). A section "New ride request" (hidden unless there is an offer and no active ride; textContent only) shows the pickup and drop-off addresses, "Pickup is X.X km from you", "Trip: X.X km, N min", "Fare: Rs ..." (same `Intl.NumberFormat("en-IN", INR)` as the rider page), "Respond within N seconds", a `<progress>` (max and value set as numbers), and Accept / Reject buttons that go through `act()` and call `POST /offers/{id}/accept` or `/reject` (both buttons are disabled while a request runs; a refusal such as "You are offline. Go online first." shows in the message area). One `setInterval` (250 ms) starts when an offer appears and is cleared when it goes: `tickCountdown()` (the one allowed function besides `act()` on this page) sets `state.secondsLeft = Math.max(0, Math.ceil((deadline - performance.now()) / 1000))` and renders; at 0 it sets `state.offer = null` and `state.notice = "The offer expired."` and refreshes once. The countdown uses the clock, so a background tab shows the right value on return. While an offer (and no active ride) is shown the pickup (green) and drop-off (red) markers are drawn by the same code as for a ride, from `hasActiveRide ? state.ride : state.offer` (the field names match), and the map is fitted once per `state.fittedKey` ("offer:12", "ride:7"), never by polling; clicks that move the driver still work. `state.notice` has its own element (`#notice`) and is cleared by the next action (`act()`) or the next offer
  - **Trip flow on `/rider/` and `/driver/` (M3.5):** both pages react to `ride_updated` by calling their existing REST refresh (the driver page for any `ride_updated`, since a driver only receives them for their own rides; the rider page when `data.ride_id` is the ride on screen), and the 3 s poll stays as the safety net. Status texts: rider DRIVER_ASSIGNED "Your driver is on the way", DRIVER_ARRIVED "Your driver has arrived. Tell them your trip code to start the trip", IN_PROGRESS "Trip in progress"; driver DRIVER_ASSIGNED "Drive to the pickup point", DRIVER_ARRIVED "Ask the rider for their trip code", IN_PROGRESS "Take the rider to the drop-off". **Rider:** `state.otp` and `state.otpKey`: while the ride is DRIVER_ASSIGNED or DRIVER_ARRIVED and `otpKey` differs from the ride id, `refresh()` sets `otpKey` BEFORE calling `GET /rides/{id}/otp` (so a failure shows once and is not retried every poll) and stores the code; both are cleared when the ride is IN_PROGRESS or finished, on "Request a new ride", and on logout. A "Trip code" section (hidden until there is a code; text through `textContent`; 2.5rem monospace) shows "Tell your driver this code when they arrive:" and the code. **Driver:** buttons by status: DRIVER_ASSIGNED "I have arrived" and Cancel; DRIVER_ARRIVED the code form (`<form id="start-form">` always in the HTML, hidden except in DRIVER_ARRIVED: label "Trip code from the rider", text input with `inputmode="numeric"`, `maxlength="4"`, `pattern="\d{4}"`, `autocomplete="off"`, `required`, and a "Start trip" submit button; Enter submits) and Cancel; IN_PROGRESS only "Complete trip". The submit handler runs through `act()` and posts `{otp: <input value>}`. The input is written ONLY by event handlers (cleared after a successful start, and by the cancel, "Wait for a new ride", and accept clicks so nothing typed for an earlier ride is left over), left alone after an error, and `render()` only shows or hides the form: polling never wipes what is typed. **Both:** a note element `#ride-note` for a CANCELLED ride, taken from the last ride event with `to_status` CANCELLED: its actor equal to `getSession().user.id` gives "You cancelled this ride.", a null actor "Ride cancelled.", anyone else the other side's wording (rider page "The driver cancelled this ride. You can request a new one.", driver page "The rider cancelled this ride."). A finished ride still stays on the driver page until "Wait for a new ride" (existing behavior); the driver can be offered rides meanwhile. No Cancel button shows in IN_PROGRESS on either page.
  - `/admin/` (`admin.js`): login only (admins come from `create_admin.py`), a wrong-role message, logout, the session in sessionStorage. Since M6.2 it is the dashboard with five tabs, see "Admin dashboard (M6.2)" below
  - **`shared/ws.js` (M3.4):** owns the connection: connect, reconnect with backoff, heartbeat, and watchdog. Pages only pass callbacks and never create a `WebSocket`. It imports `./api.js` (relative, so it is the same module instance as the pages' `/shared/api.js`). Public API: `connect({ onEvent, onStatus })` (stops any previous connection, stores the callbacks, starts connecting), `disconnect()` (clears every timer, detaches and closes the socket, forgets the callbacks: nothing is called back afterwards), `reconnectNow()` (cancels a pending retry, resets the backoff, connects at once; does nothing while connecting or open or after `disconnect()`). `onEvent(type, data)` gets every message except `auth_ok`, `pong`, and `error`. `onStatus(status, info)` is called once per change: `"connecting"` `{attempt}` (0 for the first try), `"open"` `{reconnected}` (sent on `auth_ok`; true when it follows a lost connection), `"reconnecting"` `{code, attempt, retryInSeconds}` (attempt starts at 1), `"closed"` `{code}` (4409 and 4400 only). Allowed functions inside: the three public ones plus `openSocket`, `sendPing`, `connectionLost` (the `connect` function also calls `disconnect`, as in M3.3). Two listeners are registered once at load (`visibilitychange` on `document`, `online` on `window`, one shared arrow function).
    - **Reconnect policy by close code:**

      | Situation | Action |
      |---|---|
      | 4401 unauthorized | `clearSession()` and `location.reload()` like a REST 401. No retry, nothing reported |
      | 4409 replaced by a newer connection | Report `"closed"`. No automatic retry. Resumes on `reconnectNow()` (the Reconnect button) or when the page becomes visible or the browser goes online |
      | 4400 bad message | Report `"closed"`. Only the Reconnect button resumes (our client never sends a bad message, so this is a bug) |
      | any other code (1000, 1001, 1006, 1011, 1012, 4408, ...), a connection that fails to open, or a watchdog timeout (reported as code 4000, our own) | Retry with backoff |
      | `disconnect()` was called | Nothing, ever |

    - **Constants (top of the file):** `BASE_DELAY_MS` 1000, `MAX_DELAY_MS` 30000, `STABLE_AFTER_MS` 10000, `AUTH_TIMEOUT_MS` 10000, `PING_INTERVAL_MS` 20000, `PONG_TIMEOUT_MS` 8000, close codes 4400, 4401, 4409, and the internal 4000.
    - **Backoff:** equal jitter. Before the n-th retry (n from 0) `cap = min(30000, 1000 * 2^n)` and `delay = cap / 2 + random * cap / 2`: 0.5 to 1 s, 1 to 2, 2 to 4, 4 to 8, 8 to 16, then 15 to 30 s. The counter returns to 0 only when a connection had been authenticated for at least 10 s before it was lost, or on `reconnectNow()`; a server that accepts and then drops us keeps escalating the backoff.
    - **Watchdog:** one timer per connection. Armed with 10 s when the socket is created (a connect that hangs, or a server that never answers `auth`). After `auth_ok` a heartbeat sends `{"type":"ping","data":{}}` every 20 s and arms the watchdog with 8 s unless one is already running, so a dead connection is found within 28 s. Any frame received clears it (before `auth_ok` only `auth_ok` does; the server sends nothing else then). When it fires the old socket is detached (all four handlers set to null) and `close()` is called inside a try/catch, and the loss is handled at once as code 4000, without waiting for the browser's close event (a half-dead connection can take minutes to report one). Every handler of a socket first checks it is still the current one. The token is read from `getSession()` at every attempt; with no session nothing is opened and nothing is reported (a logout is in progress).
    - **Wake-up listeners:** when the page becomes visible (hidden is ignored) or the browser goes online: while waiting for a retry or closed with 4409 call `reconnectNow()`; while open send a ping at once (verifies a connection that slept); otherwise nothing (connecting, closed with 4400, no connection).
    - **What the pages do (M3.4):** both store `state.socketStatus` and `state.socketInfo` and call `render()` on every status. On a reconnected `"open"` the rider sets `state.driverKey = null` and runs `refresh()` now (skipped when a refresh or an action is in flight; the 3 s poll catches up), the driver runs `refresh()` now. A Reconnect button (`#reconnect-button`, goes through `act()` and calls `reconnectNow()`) shows only when the status is `"closed"`. Rider status line: "Live tracking: connecting...", "Live tracking: connected", "Live tracking: connection lost. Reconnecting (attempt N), next try in about S s. Updating every few seconds meanwhile.", "Live tracking paused: this account is open in too many tabs. Updating every few seconds meanwhile." (4409), "Live tracking stopped (code N). Updating every few seconds meanwhile.". Driver status line: "Live updates: connecting...", "Live updates: connected", "Live updates: connection lost, reconnecting (attempt N). New offers still arrive within a few seconds.", "Live updates paused: this account is open in too many tabs. New offers still arrive within a few seconds.", "Live updates stopped (code N). New offers still arrive within a few seconds.". REST fallback on the rider page: in `refresh()`, while the ride is DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, or IN_PROGRESS, `state.driver` exists and the status is not `"open"`, it also calls `GET /rides/{id}/driver` and passes `location` to `applyDriverLocation` (stale answers are dropped as before), so the marker moves every 3 s while the socket is down (not smoothly) and the fallback stops by itself when the socket is open again. The driver page polling is unchanged.
  - `shared/api.js`: `api(method, path, body)`, `saveSession`, `getSession`, `clearSession`. Session lives in `sessionStorage` under the key `session` (`{token, user}`). Thrown errors carry `.status` and a readable message (`detail`, or `field: message` for a 422). A 401 with a token clears the session and reloads; a 401 without a token (wrong password) just throws. `shared/base.css` is the common stylesheet; each app has one small CSS file
  - Each page has one `state` object, one `render()` (shows/hides sections, sets `textContent`, never touches inputs), one `refresh()`, and one `act(fn)` used by every button
- **Ratings (M6.1):** two-way, after a COMPLETED trip.
  - **Tables (migration `0010_ratings`, written by hand where Alembic cannot see: the two CHECKs on the existing `ratings` table; one data statement, the backfill of summaries from existing ratings):** `rating_summaries` (`user_id` PK and FK users, `rating_count` int not null default 0 with `ck_rating_summaries_count_not_negative`, `rating_total` int not null default 0 with `ck_rating_summaries_total_within_range` = `rating_total BETWEEN rating_count AND rating_count * 5`, `updated_at`). One row per RATED user, created by the first rating; reading never creates one. On `ratings`: `ck_ratings_no_self_rating` (`from_user_id <> to_user_id`), `ck_ratings_comment_length` (NULL or at most 300 characters), `ix_ratings_to_user_id_id (to_user_id, id)`; the old `ck_ratings_score_range` and `uq_ratings_ride_id_from_user_id` stay.
  - **Endpoints (new `routers/ratings.py`, full paths, no prefix, `require_role("rider", "driver")`):** `POST /rides/{ride_id}/rating` `{score, comment?}` answers 201 with `{id, ride_id, score, comment, created_at}` (no user ids); `GET /rides/{ride_id}/rating` answers `{can_rate, reason, expires_at, mine}`; `GET /ratings/me` answers `{count, average}`. Admin: `GET /admin/ratings?user_id=&max_score=&limit=&before_id=` (newest first by id; `user_id` filters `to_user_id`, `max_score` 1 to 5 and `limit` 1 to 100, default 20, else 422) with `{id, ride_id, from_user_id, to_user_id, score, comment, created_at}`. `GET /rides/{id}/driver` gained `rating: {count, average}`.
  - **Rules (`services/ratings.create_rating`):** `rides.load_ride_for_user` (404 for a ride that is not yours; an admin is 403 from the guard), status must be COMPLETED (409 `You can only rate completed trips`, also for a CANCELLED ride with a fee and for NO_DRIVER_FOUND), then the window (`RATING_WINDOW_DAYS` = 7, read at call time, `now > completed_at + window` is 409 `The rating period for this trip has ended`; exactly at the boundary is allowed), then the counterpart (a rider rates `drivers.user_id`, a driver rates `ride.rider_id`), then `ratings_repo.insert_if_new` (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (ride_id, from_user_id) DO NOTHING RETURNING`; no row back is 409 `You have already rated this trip`), then `ratings_repo.add_to_summary` as the LAST write (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (user_id) DO UPDATE SET rating_count = rating_count + 1, rating_total = rating_total + <score>, updated_at = now()`), then the commit and one INFO log line (ids and score, never the comment). No ride lock (a COMPLETED ride never changes); the summary row is a leaf lock. A rejected driver can still rate. `RatingCreate`: `score` is `StrictInt` 1 to 5 (`"5"`, `4.5`, `true`, null, missing are 422), `comment` stripped, empty becomes NULL, at most 300 characters.
  - **Status (`get_rating_status`):** `not_completed` (any other status, `expires_at` null), `already_rated` (`mine` set), `window_closed`, else `can_rate`. `expires_at` is `completed_at` + 7 days. Only the caller's own rating is read.
  - **The average (`utils/ratings.average(count, total)`):** integer hundredths half up, `(total * 100 + count // 2) // count`, divided by 100; None for count 0. Worked: scores 5, 4, 4 (count 3, total 13) give `(1300 + 1) // 3 = 433`, so 4.33; count 8 and total 37 give 4.63 (`round(4.625, 2)` is 4.62). `PUBLIC_MIN_RATINGS` = 3 is applied inline by the caller.
  - **Who sees what:** your own `count` and `average` are always real (`/ratings/me`); another person's (the driver details) show `count` always and `average` only from 3 ratings; individual scores and comments are never shown to the rated person or to anyone but the author (their own, in `mine`) and admins (`GET /admin/ratings`). No new events and no WebSocket traffic.
  - **Pages:** `/rider/` and `/driver/` each got one helper `loadRatings()` (your summary, and the status of a COMPLETED finished ride), called by `refresh()` when `state.ratingKey` differs from the wanted key (the finished ride's id, or "none"; set BEFORE the calls, so a failure is not retried on every poll) and by the submit handler. A "Your rating: ★ 4.8 (23 ratings)" / "no ratings yet" line; the rider's "Your driver" section shows "Rating: ★ 4.7 (31 ratings)" or "New driver" (average null); a "Rate your driver" / "Rate the rider" section in the finished COMPLETED ride (five radios named `score`, a `maxlength=300` textarea, "You can rate until <date>", "You rated this trip ★★★★☆ (4 of 5)" plus the comment, "The rating period for this trip has ended."). No star chosen sets `state.error` "Choose a star rating first" without a request. Input values are written only by the submit handler (`form.reset()` after success, kept after an error), "Request a new ride" and "Wait for a new ride" reset the form and the key, everything is `textContent`. A rating can only be given from that view or the API until M6.3.
  - **Stress tool:** `simulator/invariants.sql` has I21 `rating_summary_mismatch` and I22 `rating_participants_mismatch` (22 invariants in all, `check_invariants` and every scenario use them). The chaos agents rate (see "Ratings results"), `scenario_chaos` compares `GET /ratings/me` and the driver details with its own SQL (`rating_view_mismatch`), and the new `ratings` scenario (`--riders` default 20, `--rounds` default 5, one driver, three copies of every rating request at once) ends `RATINGS CLEAN`. `burst()` now also returns `seconds`.

- **Trip history and saved places (M6.3).** Migration `0012_saved_places` (one table, no data statement; `alembic revision --autogenerate` is empty afterwards, so Alembic compares the expression index correctly).
  - **Table `saved_places`:** `id`, `user_id` (FK users, not null), `label` (VARCHAR(30)), `address` (VARCHAR(200)), `lat`, `lng` (double precision), `created_at` (timestamptz, default `now()`); checks `ck_saved_places_label_length` (1 to 30), `ck_saved_places_address_length` (1 to 200), `ck_saved_places_lat_range`, `ck_saved_places_lng_range`; the unique expression index `uq_saved_places_user_id_lower_label` on `(user_id, lower(label))` and `ix_saved_places_user_id_id` on `(user_id, id)`. No relationships and no foreign key from `rides`. The conftest TRUNCATE builds its list from the metadata, so it already covers the table. Note: the column types fire first, so a 31-character label or a 201-character address is refused by `VARCHAR(30)`/`VARCHAR(200)` ("value too long"), never by the upper half of the length checks; the lower half (an empty text) is the checks' job.
  - **Endpoints.** `GET /rides/history?status=&since=&until=&limit=&before_id=` (rider only; declared BEFORE `/rides/{ride_id}`, a test proves it is not captured) and `GET /drivers/me/history?...` (driver only; 404 `You have no driver profile yet`; a pending or rejected driver may read their own). Finished rides only: a rider's list holds COMPLETED, CANCELLED and NO_DRIVER_FOUND, a driver's COMPLETED and CANCELLED; an active, unknown, or (for a driver) NO_DRIVER_FOUND status is 422 (`Only finished trips are listed` for a known status that is not listed). Newest first by ride id; `limit` 1 to 50 (default 20, else 422); `before_id` pages (exclusive); `since` (inclusive) and `until` (exclusive) are `AwareDatetime` filters on `rides.created_at` (naive is 422, `until` not after `since` is 422 `until must be after since`). `GET /saved-places`, `POST /saved-places` (201), `PATCH /saved-places/{id}` (200, `{label}` only) and `DELETE /saved-places/{id}` (204), all rider only (403 driver and admin, 401 no token); bodies use `extra="forbid"`, label and address are stripped, 1 to 30 and 1 to 200 characters with no control characters (`^[^\x00-\x1f\x7f]+$`), `lat` and `lng` strict numbers in range. Errors: 422 `Location is outside the service area` (before any lock), 409 `You can save up to 10 places. Delete one first.`, 409 `You already have a place with that name.`, 404 `Place not found` (unknown id and another rider's id alike). Renaming to the identical label is a 200 that writes nothing; a change of letter case only is allowed.
  - **Row semantics (one query per page).** `RiderTripRow`: `id`, `status`, `created_at` (request time), `ended_at` (the `created_at` of the ride's LAST `ride_events` row by id, a scalar subquery; null with no events), `pickup_address`, `dropoff_address`, `distance_m` and `duration_s` (`COALESCE(actual_*, estimate)`), `final_fare` (the stored value: null for NO_DRIVER_FOUND and legacy rides, 0 for a free cancellation), `payment_method`, `driver_name` (outer join drivers and users; null when no driver was ever assigned), `cancelled_by` (`fare_breakdown->>'cancelled_by'`, `rider` or `driver`; null otherwise), `has_receipt` (a payment row exists), `my_rating` (the score THIS caller gave, outer join on `(ride_id, from_user_id)`), `can_rate` (COMPLETED, `my_rating` null, `completed_at` set and `now <= completed_at + RATING_WINDOW_DAYS`, the same rule as `create_rating`). `DriverTripRow`: the same first nine fields, `fare` (the stored `final_fare`), `payment_method`, `platform_fee` and `driver_earning` (outer join `ride_earnings`; null without an earning row), `cancelled_by`, `my_rating`, `can_rate`. `completed_at` is read for `can_rate` and not in the response. Privacy: the schemas are the allow-list; no row has the trip code, a driver id or contact data (rider rows), anything about the rider (driver rows), commission or earning (rider rows), the other side's rating, or a comment.
  - **Saved places rules.** Create and rename: bounds check, then `lock_owner` (column-only `SELECT id FROM users WHERE id = :id FOR UPDATE`, plain blocking like `lock_wallet`, a leaf lock), then `count` (409 at 10), then `label_exists` (case-insensitive, excluding the row being renamed), then insert or update, then commit. The cap is enforced by the lock because the database cannot count; the unique index is the safety net for the label and an `IntegrityError` there is not caught. Delete takes no lock (`DELETE ... WHERE id AND user_id RETURNING id`). A rename whose place was deleted meanwhile (the delete does not lock) answers 404. One INFO line per create, rename and delete with the place id and user id only.
  - **Pages.** Rider: `state.view` is `ride`, `trips` or `places`, a nav of three buttons (`aria-current="page"` on the current one); the header, message, rating line and wallet are in every view; the map section is touched only in the Ride view and re-measured once with `invalidateSize({animate:false, pan:false})` when the view returns; in the other two views an active ride shows a banner "You have a ride in progress: <status text>." with "Go to ride". My trips has a status select, a period select (all, 7, 30 days from local midnight), Refresh and Load more, and loads when the view opens, on a filter change, on Refresh and on Load more, never on the 3-second poll. "Open" refuses while a ride is active ("Finish or cancel your current ride first."), otherwise fetches the ride, sets `state.ride` and `state.rideId`, clears the per-ride data (`ridePath` and `routeRideId`, `driver*`, `otp*`, `receipt` and `receiptKey`, `ratingStatus` and `ratingKey`), sets `state.fromTrips` and switches to Ride; the existing `refresh()` then loads the events, route, driver, receipt and rating exactly as after a trip. "Back to my trips" and "Request a new ride" share one handler that forgets the ride (Back then opens the list). Saved places: a "Save this place" form under the pickup and under the drop-off (the point's address text cut to 200 characters), a quick-pick list in the request form whose Pickup and Drop-off buttons call `choosePoint` (extracted from the search-result handler; it sets the point and centers the map at zoom 16 without animation), and a Saved places view with "N of 10", an inline Rename form (one static form moved into the row; its input is written only by the handlers), Delete with `confirm()`; labels and addresses are `textContent` only. `loadPlaces()` runs with the page data once, when the view opens and after every change. Driver: nav Drive and My trips (approved drivers), same filters (All, Completed, Cancelled) and loading rules, rows show fare, platform fee, "You earned" (or "No earnings"), cash or wallet, who cancelled and the rating cell, nothing about the rider; "Open" is refused with "Finish your current ride or answer your offer first." while there is an active ride or a pending offer, stores the row in `state.openedTrip` so the earnings line works for a trip older than the loaded earnings; the offer panel is in both views; a banner with "Go to ride" shows in My trips while a ride is active.
  - **Stress tool.** `invariants.sql` I24 `saved_places_invalid` (no user over 10 places, only riders have any); `cleanup` deletes the stress riders' saved places through the API (found by SQL); chaos riders manage saved places in 15 percent of their loops (`saved_actions`, `saved_ok`, `saved_409`, `saved_limit_409`, `saved_404`) and after the settle period `saved_view_mismatch` and `history_view_mismatch` compare the API with SQL (retried once after 2 s); the new `places` scenario (see "History and saved places results").
  - **Tests.** `test_saved_places.py` (87) and `test_history.py` (58): 145 new tests, no existing test changed.
- **Observability (M7.1).** No migration, no schema change. New dependency: `prometheus-client==0.26.0`. Files: `app/observability/{context,logs,metrics,middleware,hooks}.py`, `app/routers/metrics.py`, `observability/` (Prometheus config, Grafana provisioning, the dashboard), three test files. Edited: `main.py` (setup, middleware, three background tasks, router), `config.py`, `security.py` (one line), `routers/websocket.py`, `services/offers.py` (heartbeat and the audit fixes), `services/matching.py` (audit fix), and one `timed_external` wrap each in `services/routing.py`, `places.py`, `payments.py`.
  - **Settings:** `LOG_LEVEL` (default `INFO`) and `METRICS_TOKEN` (default empty = `/metrics` answers 404) in `config.py`, `.env.example` and the dev `.env`; `GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` is read by `docker-compose.yml` only. Compose: backend command and Dockerfile CMD have `--no-access-log`.
  - **Log schema.** One JSON object per line on stdout: `ts` (UTC, milliseconds, `Z`), `level`, `logger`, `msg`, then context fields.
