The formatter (`logs.JsonFormatter`) outputs ONLY these `extra` keys and silently drops every other: `request_id, user_id, role, component, ride_id, driver_id, offer_id, method, route, status, duration_ms, db_queries, db_ms, from_status, to_status, ws_close_code, sqlstate, constraint, exc_type, stack, service, outcome, count`. `request_id`, `user_id`, `role` and `component` are read from the request context at format time, so every line of a request carries them. New lines use a fixed event name as `msg` (`http_request`, `ride_transition`, `ws_connected`, `ws_closed`, `sweeper_error`, `gauges_error`, `observability_hook_error`, `observability_middleware_error`); the older free-text lines stay (their message holds ids and fixed words only). An error in the formatter returns a fixed `log_format_error` line.
  - **Levels.** `http_request` is INFO for every route except `/health` and `/metrics` (DEBUG) and any status 500 or more (ERROR). `ride_transition`, `ws_connected`, `ws_closed` are INFO. `sweeper_error`, `gauges_error`, `observability_*_error` are ERROR (type and stack only; the sweeper logs one per failure streak). httpx, httpcore and websockets are pinned to WARNING (httpx logs full URLs with coordinates and search text at INFO, websockets logs every frame, with the token, at DEBUG). uvicorn's access log is off; uvicorn's `"WebSocket /ws?..." [accepted]` line (client address and the path with its query string) is dropped by a filter on the handler.
  - **Request id.** The pure ASGI middleware (a closure returned by `observability_middleware`, not `BaseHTTPMiddleware`) creates one mutable dict per request in a `ContextVar` (`request_id`, `user_id`, `role`, `db_queries`, `db_ms`; a background task's dict holds only `component`). `X-Request-ID` is kept only if it matches `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{8,64}$` (`fullmatch`, first header only), otherwise `uuid4().hex`; always returned in the `X-Request-ID` response header (also on 4xx and 500). WebSockets get a context too (no header). The auth dependency adds `user_id` and `role` with `request_context.get({}).update(...)`. Background tasks start through `hooks.start_background_task(component, coroutine)` (`ws_listener`, `sweeper`, `gauges`) and log with `component` and no `request_id`.
  - **Access line.** One `http_request` per HTTP request, written after the response (so a failure in it cannot change the response): `method` (anything outside GET/POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE/HEAD/OPTIONS is `OTHER`), `route` (the TEMPLATE `scope["route"].path`; `unmatched` when no route matched or the frontend mount answered 404; `static` for a served file), `status`, `duration_ms`, `db_queries`, `db_ms`, `user_id` and `role` when authenticated, `ride_id` only when the route has a `ride_id` path parameter that is all digits. The path and the query string are never logged. SQL timing: `before_cursor_execute` and `after_cursor_execute` listeners on the `Engine` CLASS (so the tests' engine is timed too) add to the dict (only when it has `db_queries`, so background tasks never pile up) and observe `db_query_duration_seconds`.
  - **Exception policy.** Never the message (SQLAlchemy messages carry SQL parameters, httpx messages carry URLs). The formatter writes `exc_type` and `stack` (`traceback.format_tb`: frames and source lines of the code, no values), and for a `DBAPIError` `sqlstate` and `constraint` (`error.orig.__cause__.constraint_name`). An unhandled exception is caught by the middleware, which sends the generic 500 (`Internal Server Error`, with the request id header) itself and puts `exc_type` and `stack` on the 500 `http_request` line: ONE ERROR line per such request, and nothing for uvicorn to log again (Starlette's outer error middleware would send the 500 without our header and re-raise, and uvicorn would log a second line). If the response had already started the exception is re-raised and uvicorn logs it (the access line then has no exception).
  - **Metrics** (prometheus_client, default registry, so the process and Python collectors are there too; label values only from fixed sets or route templates):

    | Metric | Type | Labels | Meaning |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `http_requests_total` | Counter | method, route, status | finished HTTP requests (500 for an unhandled exception) |
    | `http_request_duration_seconds` | Histogram | method, route | buckets 0.005 ... 10 s |
    | `http_requests_in_flight` | Gauge | none | decremented in a `finally` |
    | `db_query_duration_seconds` | Histogram | none | per SQL statement, buckets 0.001 ... 2.5 s |
    | `db_pool_size`, `db_pool_checked_out`, `db_pool_overflow` | Gauge | none | the engine pool, refreshed by the gauge task (`overflow` is the pool's raw counter: connections created minus the pool size) |
    | `external_call_duration_seconds` | Histogram | service (`osrm`, `nominatim`, `stripe`), outcome (`ok`, `error`, `timeout`) | `timed_external(service)` around the network call; a response that the existing code turns into an error (Nominatim non-200, Stripe 409 or non-2xx) is inside the wrap and counts as `error`; OSRM is wrapped around the call and the body parse |
    | `ws_connections` | Gauge | none | accepted sockets not yet closed (decremented in the handler's `finally`) |
    | `ws_closes_total` | Counter | code | 1000, 1001, 1006, 4400, 4401, 4408, 4409, else `other`; uvicorn reports an abrupt drop as 1005 and it is counted as 1006 |
    | `ride_transitions_total` | Counter | from_status, to_status | committed ride events (`none` for the creation) |
    | `rides_active` | Gauge | status | the four active statuses, refreshed by the gauge task |
    | `offers_pending` | Gauge | none | pending offers, expired-but-unswept included |
    | `drivers_online` | Gauge | none | drivers with a presence key |
    | `sweeper_tick_duration_seconds` | Histogram | none | one sweeper iteration |
    | `sweeper_last_success_timestamp_seconds` | Gauge | none | epoch seconds of the last tick that did not raise |
    | `sweeper_errors_total` | Counter | none | failed ticks |
    | `event_loop_lag_seconds` | Histogram | none | how much later than requested a 0.1 s sleep of `hooks.loop_lag_monitor` woke up (the overshoot, never below 0); buckets 0.001 ... 2.5 s; M7.2 |
    | `observability_errors_total` | Counter | source (`hook`, `gauges_db`, `gauges_redis`, `middleware`) | failures of the observability code itself |

  - **`GET /metrics`** (`routers/metrics.py`, not in the OpenAPI schema): `METRICS_TOKEN` empty = 404 `Not Found`; otherwise `Authorization: Bearer <token>` compared with `hmac.compare_digest` on bytes; missing, `Basic`, wrong, a prefix, or a user JWT = 401 `Unauthorized` (same fixed body). The token is never logged. The answer is `generate_latest()` with `CONTENT_TYPE_LATEST` (about 23 KB, 8 ms). It appears in its own `http_requests_total` with route `/metrics`.
  - **Transition hook** (`hooks.py`). `after_insert` mapper event on `RideEvent` calls `note_transition` (the ride events are ORM objects created by `repositories/rides.add_event`, so that file is untouched): it appends `(transaction, ride_id, from_status, to_status)` to `session.info["pending_transitions"]`, where `transaction` is `session.get_nested_transaction() or session.get_transaction()`. `after_soft_rollback` drops the items of a ROLLED BACK savepoint only (`previous_transaction.nested`); `after_commit` emits (counter first, then the INFO `ride_transition` line) only for the outermost commit (it also fires for the release of a savepoint, checked by `session.in_nested_transaction()`); `after_transaction_end` of the outermost transaction clears the list (commit, rollback or close), so a reused session starts clean. A failure per item is caught: ERROR `observability_hook_error` (type and stack), `observability_errors_total{source="hook"}`, and the request is unaffected. Savepoints are one level deep (`matching.py`); a deeper nesting would need the ancestry of the transaction.
  - **Gauge task** (`hooks.refresh_gauges_forever`, `METRICS_REFRESH_SECONDS = 5`): two separate try blocks. Postgres: `admin_repo.count_active_rides_by_status`, `stats_repo.count_pending_offers`, the pool; Redis: `len(drivers_repo.get_online_positions())`. A failure counts `observability_errors_total` (`gauges_db` / `gauges_redis`), logs ERROR `gauges_error` with `service`, keeps the old values and the other block still runs. Reuses the repository functions; no new query.
  - **Sweeper heartbeat** (`services/offers.sweep_forever`): observes `sweeper_tick_duration_seconds` every iteration, sets `sweeper_last_success_timestamp_seconds` only after a tick that did not raise, and on an exception counts `sweeper_errors_total` and logs `sweeper_error` once per failure streak. Behavior is otherwise as before.
  - **WebSocket handler:** gauge up after `accept`, down in the outer `finally` (also for an abnormal close); the close code is tracked at every exit and counted once; `ws_connected` (with `count`) and `ws_closed` (with `ws_close_code`) carry the request id; message contents are never logged. The protocol is unchanged.
  - **Compose profile `observability`:** `prometheus` (`prom/prometheus:v3.14.0`, `127.0.0.1:9090`, volume `prometheus_data`, 3 days retention, scrape 5 s of `backend:8000/metrics`; the container's start command writes `$METRICS_TOKEN` to `/tmp/metrics_token`, `authorization.credentials_file` reads it, then `exec`s prometheus) and `grafana` (`grafana/grafana:13.2.3`, `127.0.0.1:3000`, volume `grafana_data`, anonymous access off, the Prometheus datasource and the dashboard from provisioning files, `GF_SECURITY_ADMIN_PASSWORD` from `GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD`; its start command refuses to run with an empty password). All three of backend, prometheus and grafana use the `json-file` driver with `max-size: 10m`, `max-file: "3"`. `docker compose up` (no profile) starts the same four services as before. Run: `docker compose --profile observability up -d`; stop only these: `docker compose --profile observability rm -sf prometheus grafana`.
  - **Dashboard `Ridehail`** (`observability/grafana/dashboards/ridehail.json`, 17 panels, 22 queries, each with a title, a unit and a description with the multi-process note): request rate by route; latency p50/p95/p99 by route; 5xx and 4xx rate; requests in flight; ride transitions per minute by new status; active rides by status; pending offers; drivers online; WebSocket connections and closes by code; database pool in use against its size; database query p95; external call p95 by service and outcome; sweeper tick p95; seconds since the sweeper last succeeded; observability errors; process CPU; process memory. Counters are summed across processes, database-state gauges use `max`.
  - **Tests:** `test_logging.py` (32), `test_metrics.py` (32), `test_observability_hooks.py` (12): 76 new tests. Nine existing tests changed (see "Observability results").
  - **Stress tool:** `--metrics-token` (chaos only; see the results section for the five checks and what skipping means).

- **Load test (M7.2, partly done).** `docker-compose.loadtest.yml` (used only with `-f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.loadtest.yml`; a plain `docker compose up` still starts db, redis, backend, osrm): `postgres-load` (same image, `pg_stat_statements`, volume `uber_postgres_load_data`, no host port), `redis-load` (no host port) and `backend-load` (`extends: backend`, same image tag, `127.0.0.1:8100`, own database and Redis, Stripe key blank and Nominatim address dead so a stray call fails). The dev `osrm` is shared (stateless); the dev backend, db and redis are not used and the dev backend is stopped during runs. **State:** `ridehail_load_template` is the seeded database (migrations, 3 admins `loadadmin0..2@loadtest.example.com`, 1000 riders `rider<n>@loadtest.example.com` with the odd ones funded 500000 paise, 80 `sim-driver-*` accounts from the unchanged simulator); every run starts with `reset` (stop backend-load, drop and recreate `ridehail_load` from the template, flush redis-load, start backend-load, wait for `/health`: about 3.3 s). Password: `common.DEFAULT_PASSWORD` or `LOADTEST_PASSWORD` (`.local` e-mail domains are refused by the API, hence `example.com`).
  - **Files (`loadtest/`, run in `.venv-load`, Locust 2.46.7):** `run.py` (`seed`, `reset`, `probe`, `run`, `report`; constants at the top), `locustfile.py` (RiderSession weight 9, HistoryBrowser weight 1, AdminLiveViewer and AdminOverviewViewer one each: the request lists of `rider.js refresh()` and `admin.js loadTab()` on a 3.0 s cadence; OUTCOME events `completed`, `cancelled`, `no_driver_found`, `price_changed`, `ride_assigned_wait`), `shape.py` (step shape, `steps.json`, collapse stop), `probes.py`, `collect.py`, `stats.py` (pure), `report.py`, `seed.py`, `common.py`, `test_stats.py` (36 tests), `RESULTS.md`. Results in `loadtest/results/<run id>/` (gitignored).
  - **SLOs on the hold period:** core `GET`s p95 < 300 ms and p99 < 1000 ms, `POST /rides/estimate` and `POST /rides` p95 < 800 ms, error rate < 0.5 percent; capacity = highest step where all hold and the step is not INVALID (Locust process above 70 percent of one core); knee = first violating step. Client latency (Locust) and server latency (histogram) are both reported.
  - **Collected per run:** per-request JSONL, `/metrics` every 5 s plus at step boundaries, `docker stats`, `pg_stat_activity` every second, backend log, `pg_stat_statements` top 20, Locust and simulator CPU, machine facts; after the run the correctness checks (I1 to I24, transitions equal `ride_events`, `POST /rides` 201 counts, `observability_errors_total` 0, nothing active).
  - **New metric:** `event_loop_lag_seconds` (Histogram, task `hooks.loop_lag_monitor`, started in the lifespan, component `loop_lag`, one log line per failure streak, failures counted in `observability_errors_total{source="hook"}`), one Grafana panel (18 panels now), 4 new tests (990 tests pass).

## Baseline for M4.2

Measured in M4.1 on this machine: one uvicorn worker with `--reload`, dev Postgres/Redis/OSRM, simulator stopped (except the fleet run), seeds as shown. M4.2 must rerun the SAME commands and expect exit code 0 and zero violations. None of these numbers is artificial: the natural reproduction rate was 100 percent, so the "add `asyncio.sleep(0.05)`" experiment was not needed and was not run.

| Command (`python simulator/stress.py ...`) | I1 rounds | I2 rounds | I3 rounds | Worst case | `POST /rides` latency (median / max) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--scenario drivers --sequential --rounds 3 --riders 10 --drivers 3 --seed 1` (control) | 0 of 3 | 0 of 3 | 0 of 3 | none, exit 0; each round 3 offers, 7 NO_DRIVER_FOUND, 3 accepts all 200 | 25 / 54 ms |
| `--scenario riders --sequential --rounds 2 --riders 5 --seed 1` (control) | 0 of 2 | 0 of 2 | 0 of 2 | none, exit 0; every rider 2 x 201 over 2 rounds and 2 x 409 (the second request of each pair) | 15 / 29 ms |
| `--scenario drivers --rounds 1 --riders 2 --drivers 1`, 10 runs (smallest form) | 10 of 10 runs | 10 of 10 runs | - | 2 offers and 2 DRIVER_ASSIGNED rides on the one driver in every run, exit 1 | about 70 / 78 ms |
| `--scenario drivers --rounds 20 --seed 5` (20 riders, 3 drivers) | 20 of 20 | 20 of 20 | 0 | 10 offers on one driver; 9 active rides on one driver | 404 / 525 ms |
| `--scenario drivers --riders 50 --drivers 5 --rounds 10 --seed 6` | 10 of 10 | 10 of 10 | 0 | 10 offers; 6 rides on one driver | 690 / 1055 ms |
| `--scenario drivers --riders 100 --drivers 10 --rounds 5 --seed 6` | 5 of 5 | 5 of 5 | 0 | 11 offers; 7 rides on one driver | 1236 / 2020 ms |
| `--scenario riders --rounds 10 --seed 9` (20 riders, 40 drivers, repeat 2) | 10 of 10 | 0 of 10 | 10 of 10 | 12 to 13 of 20 riders got TWO 201 answers per round; 2 active rides per rider; 10 offers on one driver | 588 / 779 ms |
| `--scenario fleet --rounds 5 --riders 20 --seed 10` with `simulator.py --drivers 30 --speed-kmh 30 --seed 1` | 5 of 5 | 0 of 5 | 0 of 5 | 10 offers on one driver (4 to 7 drivers with several offers per round) | 421 / 564 ms |

- **Default run, what the losing requests returned (20 riders, 3 drivers).** A correct system gives at most 3 offers and 17 or more `201 NO_DRIVER_FOUND`. Observed: `201 REQUESTED` x5 to x19 per round and `201 NO_DRIVER_FOUND` x1 to x15; no request was refused or failed (no 409, 5xx, or timeout in the burst). The accept burst then answered `200 DRIVER_ASSIGNED` for most offers (271 accepts over 20 rounds: 222 x 200, 49 x 409; the 409s are the accept path serializing by luck: "You already have an active ride").
- **Scaling.** The violation rate does not change (100 percent at 2, 20, 50, and 100 riders); the severity does. Requests that got an offer: 5 to 19 of 20 (3 drivers), 15 to 20 of 50 (5 drivers), 19 to 35 of 100 (10 drivers); correct would be at most 3, 5, 10. Latency of `POST /rides` grows with concurrency (the backend has one event loop, one Postgres pool of 15, and routes through OSRM per request).
- **Timing of the offers (round kept with `--keep-last-round`, seed 7).** Driver 256 held offers 939 and 940 created 4.8 ms apart (`created_at` is the transaction start); driver 257 held nine offers created within 8.0 ms. Both rides of driver 256 reached DRIVER_ASSIGNED 4.5 ms apart (events 4148 and 4149), both with actor = the same driver's user id.
- **Damage on that kept state (observed, not fixed).** The driver: `GET /rides/active` answers 200 with ONE ride (ride 1686; the other is invisible because the repository uses `.scalars().first()`, so there is no traceback), `GET /drivers/me/offer` 404, `GET /drivers/me/presence` 200 online, `POST /drivers/me/offline` 409 "Finish or cancel your current ride before going offline", `POST /rides/1686/arrive` and `/1687/arrive` BOTH 200 (the same car "arrived" at two pickups). Each rider: `GET /rides/{id}/driver` 200 with the same driver, vehicle STR02, and `GET /rides/active` 200 with their own ride. `docker compose logs --tail 200 backend` had no traceback; the relevant lines were `Offer 939 created: ride 1686 to driver 256 (569 m away)`, `Offer 940 created: ride 1687 to driver 256 (623 m away)`, `Offer 940 accepted: ride 1687 assigned to driver 256`, `Offer 939 accepted: ride 1686 assigned to driver 256`. Also: `update_location` publishes `driver_location` only for the ride `get_active_for_driver` returns, so only one of the two riders would get live movement. The simulator's driver loop in that state (read from the code, not run): `GET /rides/active` returns one ride per tick, so it drives that ride; the second ride waits, DRIVER_ASSIGNED with no car coming, until the first ends, then `/rides/active` returns the second and the driver drives to its pickup (the driver cannot go offline meanwhile).
- **Fleet scenario (I2 did not show).** The simulated drivers look for an offer only while `GET /rides/active` is 404, decide on ONE offer at a time (`GET /drivers/me/offer` returns the oldest), and answer one at a time; after the first accept they have a ride, so a later accept would be refused with 409 and the hidden extra offers expire unseen. I2 needs two accepts at the same moment, which only the controlled `drivers` scenario sends. Fleet rounds ended with DRIVER_ASSIGNED x11 to 14, NO_DRIVER_FOUND x6 to 8, and REQUESTED x1 in two rounds after 30 s; 37 of 100 rides were NO_DRIVER_FOUND, and no round was all NO_DRIVER_FOUND.
- **Ctrl+C.** Three interrupts in the middle of a 100-rider round (SIGINT to the process): the summary printed, exit code 1, the cleanup left 0 active stress rides and no stress driver in Redis (presence key and GEO member absent). Before the cleanup loop was added, one interrupt left 6 rides active (see the bugs log).

## Fix and compare results

M4.2 built two mechanisms behind a temporary switch and ran the SAME stress commands against each on one development machine (one uvicorn worker with `--reload`, dev Postgres/Redis/OSRM, simulator stopped except for the fleet run): **none** (today's code, the control), **redis** (`SET key token NX PX 10000` with a Lua compare-and-delete release, keys `lock:driver:<id>` and `lock:rider:<id>`, 409 when held, no waiting, no renewal), and **postgres** (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` for matching, `FOR UPDATE` with a 3 s `lock_timeout` for rider and accept, 503 when it expires). An artificial delay (`EXPERIMENT_DELAY_MS`) was put inside the three critical sections to widen the window. The loser, the switch, and the delay were deleted before the milestone ended; this section is the record.

**Runs (all rows: `python simulator/stress.py ...`).** a = `--scenario drivers --rounds 20`; b = `--scenario riders --rounds 10`; c = `--scenario drivers --riders 100 --drivers 10 --rounds 3`; d = `--scenario fleet --rounds 5 --riders 20` with `simulator.py --drivers 30 --speed-kmh 30 --seed 1`; e and f = a and b at delay 50; g and h = a and b at delay 50 against TWO backend processes (`--api-url http://127.0.0.1:8000,http://127.0.0.1:8001`). I columns show rounds violated out of rounds run and the worst case. "Offers / expected" is the total live offers against min(riders, drivers) summed over rounds, "lost" the rounds below expected (only the drivers scenario measures it; more offers than expected is the bug itself, or a diluted round). 409 and 503 count every HTTP answer of the run (setup, cleanup, and the 200 expected 409s of the riders scenario included: a correct riders run has exactly 200 = 20 riders x 10 rounds). 5xx excludes 503 and counts tool-side errors; there was none. Latency is of `POST /rides`.

- **Result.** none violates I1 and I2 (drivers) or I1 and I3 (riders) in every run, at delay 0 and 50, with one and two processes (the control proves the tool still sees the bug and the delay spots are in the right place). redis and postgres: exit 0, zero violations, zero lost matches, zero 5xx, zero 503, in all 16 runs (8 per mechanism). In the riders scenario none also breaks I1 (several riders get the same nearest driver among the 40), as in M4.1. In the fleet runs (d) a correct system still ends some rides as NO_DRIVER_FOUND (about 40 of 100 in all three modes: the 30 simulated drivers are spread over 5 km and only those within 3 km of the pickups count), which is supply, not a lost match.
- **Notes on the runs.** (1) The first delay-50 `drivers` run of each mode was DILUTED (rounds 1 to 7 had offers going to about 16 simulator drivers whose presence keys had not yet expired after the fleet run, flagged by the tool's warning); the invariants held in them too, but they were rerun after the keys expired and the table has the clean reruns (the first runs are not in the table). (2) One `POST /rides` answered 422 in the none, two-process, drivers run (likely a random point pair that OSRM had no route for or that was too close; not reproduced, not related to locking). (3) `pkill` does not exist in the backend or db containers, so the second process was stopped by recreating the container (and later by a small /proc scan).
- **Failure experiment F1: the holder crashes (simulated).** One free stress driver, a ride requested about every 1 to 2 s. **Redis:** `SET lock:driver:255 dead NX PX 10000` made the driver unmatchable for the whole TTL: the first request that matched came about 10.3 s after the SET (8.3 s after the loop started, which began 2 s later), NO_DRIVER_FOUND before that. **Postgres, the holder from the plan** (`BEGIN; SELECT ... FOR UPDATE; SELECT pg_sleep(600);` in `psql -c`): the driver was skipped (NO_DRIVER_FOUND) the whole time, and killing the psql CLIENT with `kill -9` did NOT release the lock: the server backend stayed `active / PgSleep` and the driver was still unmatchable 31 s later (and for the full 600 s if left), because Postgres does not notice a dead client while a query is running (`client_connection_check_interval` is 0, `pg_sleep` never reads the socket). This was different from the expectation in the plan ("almost at once"). **Postgres, a holder idle in its transaction** (what a crashed app process looks like when it dies while awaiting OSRM, Redis, or a timer: the server is waiting for the next statement): killing the client released the lock at once, the very next request matched the driver (0.0 s later). Settings in this database: `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` = 0 (disabled), `lock_timeout` = 0 (the app sets 3 s per transaction), `client_connection_check_interval` = 0. If the holder HANGS instead of crashing: in Redis the lock expires by itself after 10 s while the holder is still alive, so the protection silently ends and the holder's late write can duplicate (that is F2); in Postgres the lock lasts as long as the holder's transaction, so matching skips that driver (and accept or `POST /rides` for that driver or rider answer 503 after 3 s) until the transaction ends, the server kills the session, or an admin does; nothing becomes inconsistent, the cost is availability of that one driver or rider. `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` is the setting that would bound it (not set; out of scope).
- **Failure experiment F2: the lock expires while held (redis only, ARTIFICIAL).** Both Redis TTLs set to 100 ms, delay 300 ms, `--scenario drivers --rounds 10`: **I1 violated in 7 of 10 rounds** (2 offers on one driver, 39 offers against 30 expected), I2 0 of 10, and the log had **269 `lock expired before release` warnings**. The same delay 300 ms in mode postgres: **0 violations in 10 rounds**, 30 of 30 offers, no 503, median `POST /rides` 703 ms (the redis F2 run: 706 ms). The TTL constants and the delay were then reverted (the saved copy of `locks.py` was identical, and the file was deleted with the loser).
- **Logs.** `docker compose logs db | grep -ci "deadlock detected"` = 0 over the whole session (every run of every mode and every test run). A per-mode pass (delay 50, drivers x20 and riders x10 per mode, backend logs saved before the next recreate) found, for none, redis, and postgres: 0 deadlocks, 0 `lock expired before release`, 0 tracebacks, 0 HTTP 500, 0 HTTP 503. The 15 `canceling statement due to lock timeout` lines in the Postgres log are all expected: 2 per test run (test 8) and 1 from the probe that found the exception class.
- **Rubric, in order.** (1) Invariants: both mechanisms 0 violations in every run (8 of 8 each, F2 excluded). (2) Lost matches: both 0 (offers exactly 60 of 60, 30 of 30). (3) 5xx and deadlocks: both 0. (4) F1 and F2: redis can leave a free driver unmatchable for up to 10 s after a holder crash and loses correctness when a lock expires inside its critical section (no fencing); postgres releases at once when the holder dies idle, keeps the lock when the holder dies mid-query, and never loses correctness when the holder is slow. (5) Code and places that must remember to release: redis about 50 added production lines (a 21-line `repositories/locks.py`, about 23 lines in the three services, a `held_locks` parameter on `offer_to_next_driver`) and 4 places that must release (three `finally` blocks in `create_ride`, `accept`, and `finish_offer`, plus the early release when the re-check says unavailable); postgres about 30 added production lines (the `git diff --stat` of the final code is 75 insertions and 11 deletions in six backend files, comments included) and 0 places to release (commit and rollback do it). (6) Latency, medians of `POST /rides` redis vs postgres: 424 vs 438 ms (a), 594 vs 625 (b), 1345 vs 1374 (c), 478 vs 510 (d), 429 vs 447 (e), 624 vs 673 (f), 273 vs 280 (g), 453 vs 514 (h): postgres is 2 to 14 percent slower at the median here and the maxima are within the noise of one development machine.
- **Decision: Postgres row locks win.** Both are correct on the first three criteria (and the lost-match count shows the "skip a busy driver" rule works in both), so the tie-break from the plan applies: fewer added lines and fewer failure modes. Postgres adds about 30 lines against about 50, nothing to release by hand (a commit, a rollback, or a dead connection does it, so a forgotten `finally` cannot leak a lock), no TTL to tune against the length of the critical section (the Redis lock is only as safe as 10 s is longer than the slowest request, and F2 shows what happens otherwise), and a lock that lives in the same transaction as the data it protects, so it holds across any number of backend processes without more machinery (verified with two). What it costs: a request can hold a database connection for up to 3 s while it waits, the answer to contention is 503 instead of a fast 409, and a holder stuck mid-query keeps its lock (F1). Redis' one real advantage, failing fast without taking a connection, is not needed here: matching never waits, and the waiting calls are rare (one rider, one driver). The Redis path, the `none` path, the switch (`CONCURRENCY_MODE`, `EXPERIMENT_DELAY_MS`), the three delay spots, and `repositories/locks.py` were deleted.

## Known races

Every place in the backend that reads availability or an active-ride count and then writes. "Reproduced" means M4.1 saw the invariant break; the evidence is in "Baseline for M4.2". All rows below are **fixed in M4.2 (lock) and backed by a constraint in M4.3** (a partial unique index that refuses the bad state even if a lock is ever missed, with the IntegrityError turned into a 409 or a skipped candidate) unless the status says otherwise; the proof is the regression tests in `test_concurrency.py` (which fail without the lock) and the stress results in "Fix and compare results".

| Function (file) | What it checks | What it writes | What went wrong | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `create_ride` (`services/rides.py`) through `rides_repo.get_active_for_rider` | The rider has no ride in REQUESTED, DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, IN_PROGRESS | Inserts the ride, its first event, and the first offer, one commit at the end | Two simultaneous requests from one rider both passed the check and both committed: two active rides (I3) | **Fixed in M4.2.** The rider's `users` row is locked after the routing call and the check is repeated (`users_repo.lock`). Tests: `test_one_rider_sending_four_requests_gets_one_ride` (one 201 and three 409), mutation-checked. Stress: I3 0 of 10 rounds in the riders scenario, at delay 0 and 50, one and two processes (was 10 of 10) **Backed in M4.3** by `uq_rides_one_active_per_rider`; 409 on IntegrityError. Test without the lock: `test_without_locks_four_requests_of_one_rider_still_give_one_ride` (one 201, three 409, one row). |
| `offer_to_next_driver` (`services/matching.py`) through `drivers_repo.get_available_ids` and `offers_repo.create` | The candidate is approved, has no ride in an active status, and has no PENDING offer, plus online in Redis | Inserts a PENDING offer for the nearest candidate | Uncommitted offers are invisible to other transactions, so many rides picked the same nearest driver: a driver with several live offers (I1) | **Fixed in M4.2.** The candidate's `drivers` row is locked with `SKIP LOCKED` and availability is checked again as its own statement. Tests: `test_one_driver_two_riders_gives_one_offer`, `test_a_stale_prefilter_cannot_create_a_second_offer` (event-gated, proves the check after the lock matters), `test_eight_riders_three_drivers_gives_exactly_three_offers`, `test_a_driver_held_by_someone_else_is_skipped_not_failed`. Stress: I1 0 of 20 rounds (was 20 of 20, worst 10 offers on one driver), offers exactly 60 of 60 expected **Backed in M4.3** by `uq_ride_offers_one_pending_per_driver`; the insert is in a savepoint and a violation skips the candidate (WARNING `offer skipped`). Tests without the lock: two riders and one driver give one offer and one NO_DRIVER_FOUND, eight riders and three drivers give exactly three offers to three drivers. |
| `finish_offer` (`services/offers.py`, used by `reject` and `expire_due_offers`) calling `offer_to_next_driver` | The same availability check, for the NEXT driver | Marks the offer REJECTED or EXPIRED and inserts the next PENDING offer | The same race, between a re-offer and a new request | **Fixed in M4.2** by the same code (it calls `offer_to_next_driver`). Not attributed separately in the stress runs; the fleet runs (expiries and rejects at work) gave I1 0 of 5 rounds in both mechanisms (was 5 of 5). The ride and offer rows stay locked first, then the driver row is taken without waiting **Backed in M4.3** by the same index (it goes through `offer_to_next_driver`). |
| `accept` (`services/offers.py`) through `rides_repo.get_active_for_driver` | The offer is PENDING and not expired, the driver is approved, has no active ride, and is online | Offer to ACCEPTED, `change_ride_status` to DRIVER_ASSIGNED, `ride.driver_id`, one commit | Two offers of one driver for two rides, accepted at once, locked different rows and both saw no active ride: two DRIVER_ASSIGNED rides (I2) | **Fixed in M4.2.** The driver's row is locked (blocking, 3 s) after the ride and offer locks and before the checks. Test: `test_two_offers_of_one_driver_accepted_at_once_assign_one_ride` (one 200, one 409, loser's ride still REQUESTED), mutation-checked. Stress: I2 0 of 20 rounds (was 20 of 20, worst 9 active rides on one driver) **Backed in M4.3** by `uq_rides_one_active_per_driver`; everything from the first change to the commit is in a try/except IntegrityError (rollback, 409). Test with a stale check: `test_accept_turns_an_index_violation_into_a_409_and_undoes_everything`. |
| `accept` through `drivers_repo.get_presence` (Redis read) | The driver's presence key exists | Same write as above | The key can expire or be deleted between the read and the commit, so a driver who just went offline is assigned a ride | **Not fixed** (Redis is not part of the row lock; see the open issues). M4.3 narrowed the damage: a driver who is gone loses their PENDING offer to the sweeper (`driver_offline`) within about a second |
| `go_offline` (`services/drivers.py`) through `get_active_for_driver`, then `drivers_repo.set_offline` | The driver has no active ride | Deletes the presence key and the GEO member (Redis) | An accept that commits between the check and the delete leaves a driver with an active ride who is offline | **Not fixed, out of scope in M4.3** (no constraint can express it; the driver keeps the ride, which only goes on once someone cancels it, see the open issues) |

Not racy by design: `cancel`, `driver_set_status`, `reject`, and the sweeper lock the ride row first (lock order ride, then offer), so two changes on ONE ride serialize; the M3.3 and M3.5 tests cover two simultaneous accepts, rejects, starts, and a start against a cancel on one ride. M4.2 added the locks on the DRIVER and on the RIDER that were missing.

## Edge cases

Built in M4.3. Every case below is a test in `backend/tests/test_edge_cases.py` unless another file is named; the stress evidence comes from the runs in the next paragraph. Races are tested with `asyncio.gather`, ten rounds each, alternating which request goes first, asserting consistency and not who wins.

| Case | Expected behavior | Test | Stress evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Driver vanishes with an offer (offline, admin reject, page died, presence key gone) | The sweeper withdraws the PENDING offer within about a second: EXPIRED, `responded_at` null, `offer_closed` reason `driver_offline` to the driver, the next free driver is offered, or the ride ends NO_DRIVER_FOUND (null actor) and the rider gets `ride_updated`; the ride stays REQUESTED with no new event when another driver exists | `test_a_driver_who_vanishes_loses_the_offer_and_the_next_driver_gets_it`, `..._with_nobody_else_ends_the_ride`, `test_the_real_causes_of_a_vanished_driver` (offline API and admin reject), `test_the_real_sweeper_withdraws_the_offer_of_a_vanished_driver_on_its_own` | Chaos: 9 to 34 `driver_offline` withdrawals per run. By hand: withdrawn at the second poll (0.8 to 1.5 s) after `POST /drivers/me/offline` and after an admin reject, the next driver offered with `expires_in` 14.1 s |
| Online driver, live offer | A sweep leaves it alone | `test_a_sweep_leaves_the_offer_of_an_online_driver_alone` | Every chaos run: no live offer is withdrawn (I5 and I6 never fired) |
| Overdue and driver gone | Time wins: reason `expired` | `test_time_wins_when_the_offer_is_overdue_and_the_driver_is_gone` | - |
| Driver page died, offer shorter than the presence TTL | The offer times out normally (reason `expired`); the presence key dies later | by hand: offer 15 s, presence key gone at 30 s | by hand (curl): `Offer 11035 expired`, key gone at +34 s |
| Accept by a driver who is gone | 409 offline before the sweep, 409 "no longer available" after | `test_a_driver_who_is_gone_cannot_accept_before_or_after_the_sweep` | by hand: 409 "This offer is no longer available" |
| Accept racing a rider cancel | The ride always ends CANCELLED, no PENDING offer left, the driver matchable again; either (offer CANCELLED, accept 409, no driver, events REQUESTED, CANCELLED) or (offer ACCEPTED, driver set, events REQUESTED, DRIVER_ASSIGNED, CANCELLED) | `test_accept_racing_a_cancel_always_ends_cancelled` (10 rounds) | Chaos: 29 to 109 rider cancel attempts per run (9 to 39 accepted as cancels) while drivers accept; I6 and I7 never fired |
| Sweeper racing an accept | Overdue offer: accept 409, the sweep handles it exactly once (one NO_DRIVER_FOUND event). Live offer: the sweep does nothing and the accept wins | `test_a_sweep_racing_an_accept_of_an_overdue_offer`, `..._of_a_live_offer_does_nothing_and_the_accept_wins` (10 rounds each) | - |
| Two sweeps at once | One next offer, one `offer_closed`, at most one NO_DRIVER_FOUND event | `test_two_sweeps_at_once_handle_an_overdue_offer_once` (with and without a next driver) | Two backend processes both run a sweeper in every two-process run: 0 stuck, 0 violations |
| Reject racing a sweep | Exactly one of them processes the offer, exactly one next offer; for an overdue offer the reject is always 409 | `test_a_reject_racing_a_sweep_of_an_overdue_offer`, `test_a_reject_racing_the_withdrawal_of_a_gone_driver_processes_the_offer_once` | Chaos: 8 to 31 rejects per run |
| Cancel racing a sweep | The ride ends CANCELLED and no PENDING offer is left, whichever goes first | `test_a_cancel_racing_a_sweep_leaves_a_cancelled_ride_and_no_open_offer` | - |
| Duplicate accepts | Five at once: one 200 and four 409, one DRIVER_ASSIGNED event, one `ride_updated` to the rider; a later accept 409 (stays a 409, not idempotent) | `test_five_accepts_of_one_offer_at_once_give_one_winner`; `test_one_offer_accepted_twice_at_once_assigns_one_ride` (`test_concurrency.py`) | Chaos double accepts: every one returned exactly one 200 (7 to 22 per run); by hand: five curls, 200 + 4 x 409, one DRIVER_ASSIGNED event |
| Accept or reject after the deadline | 1 ms past: 409 "This offer has expired", nothing changed, the sweeper still handles the ride | `test_answering_one_millisecond_after_the_deadline_is_409_and_changes_nothing` (accept and reject) | by hand: 409 "has expired" at deadline + 0.2 s; 409 "no longer available" at deadline + 1 s (the sweeper was first) |
| Two drivers accepting two different offers | Both 200 | `test_two_drivers_accepting_two_different_offers_at_once_both_win` | - |
| Indexes alone (no locks) | A second pending offer per driver, a second active ride per driver and per rider raise IntegrityError naming the index; finished offers and rides never block a new one | `test_the_indexes_refuse_what_the_locks_prevent` and three allowed-case tests (all with `locks_disabled`) | Never fired in any stress or chaos run (Postgres log: 0 unique violations) |
| Race with the locks disabled | Two riders and one driver: one offer, one NO_DRIVER_FOUND, no 5xx, `offer skipped` logged once; eight riders and three drivers: three offers to three drivers; four requests of one rider: one 201 and three 409, one row | `test_without_locks_*` (3 tests) | - |
| Accept hits the index | A stale "no active ride" check lets accept reach the database: 409, ride still REQUESTED, no new event, offer still PENDING (full rollback) | `test_accept_turns_an_index_violation_into_a_409_and_undoes_everything` | - |
| Offer insert fails for the first candidate | The savepoint undoes only the insert: the second candidate gets the offer, the ride is REQUESTED with exactly one PENDING offer | `test_a_failed_offer_insert_skips_that_driver_and_the_request_goes_on` | - |
| One offer cannot be handled | The other offers in the pass are processed; the failing one logs exactly one warning across passes; a Redis error aborts the pass | `test_one_failing_offer_does_not_block_the_others_and_is_logged_once`, `test_a_redis_error_aborts_the_pass`, `test_a_redis_error_while_handling_one_offer_aborts_the_pass_too` | Chaos: 0 `could not handle` warnings |
| Presence lookup | Never called with an empty list; one call for all pending offers | `test_the_presence_lookup_is_never_called_with_an_empty_list`, `test_one_presence_lookup_serves_all_pending_offers` | Cost under the heavy chaos load (60 riders, 20 drivers): 6 pending offers on average (max 10); `list_pending` median 2.7 ms (max 22.7), the MGET median 0.6 ms (max 2.0) |
| Backend restart in the middle of a run | Nothing lost: state is in Postgres, the sweeper starts again | chaos run (`--tolerate-5xx`), `docker compose restart backend` at about 38 s | 52 connection errors during the restart, then 0 stuck rides, 0 violations, exit 0 |
| Redis outage in the middle of a run | Requests 503 while it is down, the sweeper logs one failure and one recovery, nothing stuck afterwards | chaos run, `docker compose stop redis` for 10 s | 27 x 503 (mostly `Cache is unavailable`, at least one `Busy, please retry` from the 3 s lock wait), sweeper: one `Offer sweeper failed, retrying every 1 s: network:ConnectionError` and one `Offer sweeper recovered`, 0 stuck rides, 0 violations, exit 0 |
| Sweeper dead or paused (`docker compose pause backend` for 40 s) | I5 lists the overdue offers; recovery is automatic | by hand | 4 offers listed by I5 after 40 s (presence keys had expired too); after `unpause` I5 was empty at the first poll (under 1 s) and all 4 rides were resolved (NO_DRIVER_FOUND, reason `expired`, since no driver was online any more) |

**Chaos and stress results (M4.3, one development machine, one `--reload` uvicorn worker unless stated; every run exit 0, 0 violations of I1 to I7, 0 stuck rides, 0 `offer skipped`, 0 tracebacks, 0 deadlocks).** Regression: `--sequential` controls (drivers 3 rounds 9 of 9 offers; riders 2 rounds), `drivers --rounds 20` 60 of 60 offers (`POST /rides` median 442 ms, M4.2: 438), `riders --rounds 10` (625 ms, M4.2: 625; 200 x 201 and 200 x 409), `drivers --riders 100 --drivers 10 --rounds 3` 30 of 30 (1345 ms, M4.2: 1374), fleet (`simulator.py --drivers 30 --speed-kmh 30 --seed 1`, 5 rounds, 100 rides, 41 NO_DRIVER_FOUND from supply as before), two processes drivers 20 rounds 60 of 60 (249 ms) and riders 10 rounds. Chaos (30 riders, 10 drivers, 60 s, settle 45 s unless stated):

| Run | Rides requested | Rider cancels (ok) / driver cancels | Duplicate requests (409s) | Accepts won / refused | Double accepts (one 200) | Rejects / ignored / offline | Trips completed | Final rides: completed / cancelled / no driver found / left active | Final offers: accepted / expired / rejected / cancelled | `driver_offline` withdrawals | 5xx | Stuck | Exit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| seed 1 | 384 | 41 (17) / 6 | 42 (9) | 60 / 8 | 8 (8) | 12 / 6 / 8 | 33 | 37 / 23 / 353 / 4 | 60 / 15 / 15 / 4 | 10 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| seed 2 | 398 | 35 (9) / 7 | 46 (10) | 48 / 7 | 7 (7) | 11 / 11 / 11 | 28 | 29 / 16 / 385 / 4 | 48 / 27 / 11 / 1 | 14 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| seed 3 | 386 | 39 (9) / 10 | 36 (9) | 55 / 10 | 10 (10) | 15 / 7 / 10 | 29 | 33 / 19 / 357 / 4 | 55 / 23 / 17 / 1 | 14 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| seed 1, two processes | 386 | 29 (13) / 6 | 41 (7) | 49 / 2 | 2 (2) | 10 / 8 / 9 | 33 | 34 / 19 / 366 / 1 | 49 / 22 / 13 / 5 | 13 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| heavy: 60 riders, 20 drivers, 120 s | 1479 | 109 (39) / 19 | 149 (22) | 167 / 22 | 22 (22) | 31 / 54 / 25 | 108 | 114 / 58 / 1427 / 7 | 167 / 90 / 36 / 12 | 34 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| seed 5, `docker compose restart backend` at about 38 s | 393 | 32 (12) / 7 | 35 (6) | 49 / 7 | 7 (7) | 16 / 12 / 9 | 28 | 30 / 19 / 355 / 6 | 49 / 21 / 16 / 6 | 12 | 0 (52 connection errors, tolerated) | 0 | 0 |
| seed 6, Redis stopped for 10 s at about 30 s | 344 | 23 (8) / 6 | 37 (5) | 40 / 8 | 8 (8) | 8 / 12 / 7 | 23 | 23 / 14 / 314 / 5 | 40 / 22 / 8 / 2 | 9 | 27 x 503 (tolerated) | 0 | 0 |

Every action type happened in every run (duplicates, cancels from both sides, double accepts, rejects, ignores, offline events, completed trips, `driver_offline` withdrawals), so the runs do test those cases. What they do not do well: most rides (85 to 89 percent) end at once as NO_DRIVER_FOUND because 10 drivers serve 30 riders who request again after 0 to 3 s, so only about one request in five gets an offer (94 to 269 offers per run); and the left-active count is the trips the agents left when they stopped (cleanup cancels them). The pending-offer index and the active-ride indexes never fired in any run, so the chaos evidence is for the locks and the sweeper; the indexes are proven by the `locks_disabled` tests.


## Final fare results

Measured on 2026-10-09 against the dev stack. Route: pickup (12.9716, 77.5946) to (12.9896, 77.6046), OSRM 3005 m / 327 s, estimate Rs 96.96 (9696 paise), cap 14544. OSRM from the first tracked point to the drop-off: 3005.2 m.

**Speed variants** (simulator, 20 drivers, seed 1, same driver 004 each time, per-driver factor 0.90; the 10 km/h run used `--radius-km 0.7` around the pickup so the approach was short):

| Simulator speed | Estimate | Tracked distance | Duration | Computed fare | Final fare | Capped |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 30 km/h | 9696 | 2977 m (0.9 percent under OSRM) | 402 s | 9912 | 9912 | no |
| 90 km/h | 9696 | 2869 m | 135 s | 8893 | 8893 | no |
| 10 km/h | 9696 | 3002 m | 1206 s | 12622 | 12622 | no |

The 30 km/h trip by hand: distance fare (1200 x 2977 + 500) // 1000 = 3572, time fare (200 x 402 + 30) // 60 = 1340, 5000 + 3572 + 1340 = 9912. Expected duration 3005 / (8.33 x 0.9) = 400.7 s (band for factors 0.8 to 1.2: 300 to 451 s); psql `completed_at - started_at` = 401.9996 s. The fast trip costs less than the estimate (90 km/h loses about 136 m, the first and last 3 s interval, and pays little for time). **The slow trip did NOT hit the cap:** 10 km/h x 0.9 is 2.5 m/s, 3.7 times the estimated duration, but time is only part of the fare (base and distance are fixed), so the fare is 130 percent of the estimate. The cap needs a time fare above 14544 - 5000 - 3602 = 5942, i.e. about 1780 s (about 6 km/h actual speed, `--speed-kmh` 6.7 or less). The cap is proven by `test_the_fare_is_capped_at_150_percent_of_the_estimate` (an inflated distance and a 3 hour duration) and was not reproduced live.

**Fallbacks** (own driver, no simulator, real pings every 3 s):

| Case | Billed distance | Source and reason | Fare |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) arrive, start, complete, no pings | 3005 m (estimate) | estimate, `no_tracking` | 8613 (2 s) |
| (b) 11 pings jumping around the city for 30 s | 3005 m (estimate) | estimate, `unreliable_tracking`, 11 pings, 10 jumps | 8716 (33 s) |
| (c) 8 m/s for 60 s, one ping 5 km off, 585 m ride | 432 m | tracked, 21 pings, 2 jumps | 8000 minimum fare (63 s) |
| (d) 4 pings in about 10 s, 585 m ride | 60 m | tracked | 8000 minimum fare (12 s) |
| Redis stopped, ride 23534 | 3005 m (estimate) | estimate, `no_tracking`, one warning, no traceback | 8659 |

**Cancellation matrix** (free window temporarily 5 s in the dev database, restored to 120 afterwards; every quote equalled the charge):

| Who and when | Quote = charge | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| rider, REQUESTED | 0 | no_driver_yet |
| rider, ASSIGNED within 5 s | 0 | within_free_window |
| rider, ASSIGNED after 10 s | 3000 | late_cancellation |
| rider, ARRIVED | 3000 | driver_arrived |
| driver, ASSIGNED after 10 s | 0 | driver_cancelled |
| driver, ARRIVED | 0 | driver_cancelled |
| rider or driver, IN_PROGRESS | 409 (quote 409 too) | |

NO_DRIVER_FOUND rides keep a NULL `final_fare`. Dev totals after the runs (trips): 82 + 7 billed by the estimate with `no_tracking` (chaos drivers complete within seconds), 1 `unreliable_tracking`, 51 tracked, 47 at the minimum fare, 0 capped. Cancellations: 220 `no_driver_yet`, 141 `within_free_window`, 43 `driver_cancelled`, 22 `driver_arrived` and 1 `late_cancellation` (3000 each).

**Stress and chaos on the final code** (all exit 0, I1 to I11 clean, 0 stuck rides): `--sequential` 5 rounds; drivers 20 rounds (60 of 60 offers); riders 10 rounds; fleet 3 rounds with 30 simulated drivers (60 rides); chaos seed 1 (377 rides, 30 completed with fares summing to 276743 paise, 2 cancel fees summing to 6000); seed 2 (27 completed, 246602 paise; 4 fees, 12000); two processes on 8000 and 8001, seed 3 (32 completed, 291269 paise; 3 fees, 9000); `docker compose restart backend` at the halfway point with `--tolerate-5xx`, seed 4 (28 completed, 267490 paise; 4 fees, 12000; 0 5xx, 37 connection errors tolerated; I8 to I11 clean after settling). Regression: 30 drivers at 90 km/h, 10 riders, all 10 completed with a tracked fare of 8000 to 8116 paise (2236 to 2286 m).

## Surge results

Measured on 2026-10-09 against the dev stack (Bengaluru, zone `tdr1v` around the city center, fake rides from the `stress-rider-NNN` accounts, route pickup (12.9716, 77.5946) to (12.9896, 77.6046), OSRM 3005 m / 327 s, normal fare 9696 paise).

**By hand** (4 riders requested with no driver in range, 1 free approved driver online in the zone): demand 4, supply 1, pressure 400 (above the last step 300) gives 200; the estimate was `(9696 * 200 + 50) // 100` = 19392 (surge amount 9696). The same estimate 10 km south (zone `tdr1m`) stayed at 100 (10413 for its own 3750 m route). The first four riders saw 100 each: the snapshot of their first request was cached for 15 s.

**The multiplier ladder** (9 distinct unmet riders in one zone, drivers brought online and taken offline until 9, 8, 6, 5, 4, 3, 2, 0 were available; the whole ladder took 4 s, inside the 3 minute window):

| Available | Demand | Pressure | Multiplier | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 9 | 9 | 100 | 100 | 100 |
| 8 | 9 | 112 | 120 | 120 |
| 6 | 9 | 150 | 120 | 120 |
| 5 | 9 | 180 | 150 | 150 |
| 4 | 9 | 225 | 180 | 180 |
| 3 | 9 | 300 | 180 | 180 |
| 2 | 9 | 450 | 200 | 200 |
| 0 | 9 | 900 | 200 | 200 |

No difference from the expected steps. The quote for a rider (`/rides/estimate`) followed each step (9696, 11635, 11635, 14544, 17453, 17453, 19392, 19392).

**Aging** (zone left alone): the newest unmet ride left the 180 s window at t = 0. `/rides/estimate` WITHOUT `refresh` still said 200 at t = -12.7 s (5 riders left in the window), and said 100 at the first poll after t = 0 (+3.8 s; a cached snapshot lives at most 15 s). `refresh=true` returned no zones at all.

**Quote, lock-in, and settlement** (6 unmet riders, 3 free drivers outside the matching radius: pressure 200, 150): the estimate said 14544 = `(9696 * 150 + 50) // 100`. `POST /rides` with no `accepted_surge_percent`, with 150, and with 200 all gave 201 at 150 and 14544 (a higher accepted value is charged at the current price). With 120 it gave 409 `Prices have increased in your area (now 1.5x). Please review the new fare and request again.` and the ride row count was 25982 before and after. A real trip was then requested at 120 (demand 6, supply 4, quote 11635); after the driver accepted, 3 more riders requested and the free drivers went offline, so the zone went to 200 (demand 9, supply 0, a new rider's quote 200). The trip completed with `surge_percent` 120 (the locked one): distance 180 m, 9 s, minimum fare applied, normal fare 8000, surge amount 1600, computed fare 9600 = `8000 * 120 / 100`, cap 11635 * 150 // 100 = 17452, final 9600.

**Real traffic, simulator** (`--speed-kmh 30 --seed 1`, 20 stress riders requesting at the same instant around the center, then re-requesting every 15 to 40 s after a NO_DRIVER_FOUND for about 8 minutes):
- **5 drivers:** only 1 driver was in the zone and few in range, so the burst gave 2 REQUESTED and 18 NO_DRIVER_FOUND, all quoted 100 (the snapshot was empty). Right after, the zone showed demand 20, supply 0, pressure 2000, multiplier 200 and the next estimate was 19392. It stayed at 200 while the riders kept asking: 315 NO_DRIVER_FOUND rides at 200 in the run. Two simulated drivers became free and took surged rides that completed at 2.0x: ride 26229 (2815 m, 366 s: normal 9598, surge amount 9598, fare 19196, estimate 18790, cap 28185) and ride 26268 (2926 m, 396 s: normal 9831, fare 19662, estimate 19286). The two rides of the burst completed at 100.
- **40 drivers:** the burst gave 19 REQUESTED and 1 NO_DRIVER_FOUND, all quoted 100. For a few seconds the zone showed demand 20, supply 2, multiplier 200, and one rider's re-request was quoted and charged 200. Then the offers were accepted, demand fell to 3 (supply 2, 120) and later 3/3 (100). Final rides: 19 assigned at 100, 1 at 200, NO_DRIVER_FOUND 3 at 100, 4 at 120, 2 at 200. **Surge appeared, but only as a short spike caused by the offer wave:** a rider waiting for a driver's answer counts as unmet demand (REQUESTED) and the driver with the pending offer is not available, so a burst of requests raises the pressure for the 10 to 20 seconds an offer takes. With enough drivers it is not sustained. See the open issues.

**Cancellation fee** (zone at 200, demand 8, supply 0 while the rider cancelled, `free_cancel_seconds` temporarily 5 in the dev pricing rule and restored to 120): ride requested at 200, quote `{"fee": 3000, "reason": "late_cancellation"}`, cancel charged `final_fare` 3000, breakdown `{"kind": "cancellation", "fee": 3000, ...}`. Not surged.

**Redis down** (`docker compose stop redis`): `POST /rides/estimate` 200 with `surge_percent` 100, correct fare 9696 and one warning `Could not read the surge snapshot: quoting without surge` (no traceback); `POST /rides` 503 `Cache is unavailable` (no ride row; its own quote logs one more warning, so one warning per quote); `GET /admin/surge` and `?refresh=true` 503 `Cache is unavailable`. `/health` was ok again after `docker compose start redis`.

**Two processes** (a second uvicorn on 8001, 40 simulated drivers online, an estimate against each port every second for 60 s): 120 estimates, 0 pairs with different answers, 4 distinct snapshots (`computed_at`) in 60 s, each served by both processes (they share `surge:snapshot`). 5 recompute log lines (3 on port 8000, 2 on 8001) for 4 windows: one duplicate at expiry, as accepted. **One recompute took 6 to 19 ms** with 40 drivers online in 7 zones (15 to 19 ms for the first one in a fresh process), and 3 to 4 ms with no driver online. A second run created 5 unmet riders at second 12: both processes flipped from 100 to 200 at second 15 (the cache expiring), in the same second.

**Query plan** (`EXPLAIN ANALYZE` of the demand query, 26045 rides, 5.4 MB heap, 108 rows in the window): Bitmap Index Scan on `ix_rides_created_at`, then a Bitmap Heap Scan, sort and aggregate: 0.32 ms, 33 buffers. With index and bitmap scans disabled it is a Seq Scan of the whole table: 9.6 ms, 678 buffers. The planner takes the index because the 180 s window matches about 0.4 percent of the table; at this size even the scan would be cheap, but it grows with the table and the index does not.

**Stress and chaos on the final code** (all exit 0, I1 to I12 clean, 0 stuck rides): `--sequential` 5 rounds; drivers 20 rounds (60 of 60 offers); riders 10 rounds; fleet 3 rounds with 30 simulated drivers (60 rides, 25 NO_DRIVER_FOUND); chaos seed 1 (30 completed trips; rides above 1.0x 426, highest 200, surge part of the completed trips 267944 paise, `surge_409` 2); seed 2 (29 completed; rides above 1.0x 434, highest 200, surge part 285578 paise, `surge_409` 0); two processes on 8000 and 8001, seed 3 (30 completed; 427 above 1.0x, highest 200, surge part 296105 paise, `surge_409` 0, 0 5xx); `docker compose restart backend` at about 50 s with `--tolerate-5xx`, seed 4 (28 completed; 412 above 1.0x, highest 200, surge part 261580 paise, 0 5xx, 43 connection errors tolerated; I8 to I12 clean after settling). **Surge really occurred in the runs:** by multiplier of the rides requested, drivers scenario 20 at 180 and 380 at 200; riders scenario 159 at 100 and 40 at 200; fleet 60 at 180; chaos seed 1 120 at 150 and 306 at 200 (every ride of a chaos run was surged: the previous run's NO_DRIVER_FOUND rides keep the zone surging for the next 3 minutes, the effect the README warns about); chaos seeds 2, 3 and 4 every ride at 200. The settled-trip path with surge (I12) was exercised by 125 completed trips in the four chaos runs (35, 30, 32, 28). Final fares of the chaos runs are about double those of M5.1 (595696 paise for 35 trips against 276743 for 30).

**Regression** (`--drivers 30 --speed-kmh 90 --seed 1`, 10 riders, pickups within 150 m of the center, drop-offs 1.3 to 1.7 km away in each direction): burst of 10 at the same instant: 8 completed within the window and a 9th (rider 8) completed later, each with a tracked fare (9216 to 11691 paise, 3088 to 4623 m, all quoted 100, minimum fare not needed); rider 4 got NO_DRIVER_FOUND with no offer at all (every candidate in range was skip-locked or busy at that instant: the M4.2 behavior, matching is untouched). Second run, requests 2 s apart: 7 quoted at 100, then 3 at 150 (the earlier rides were still waiting for an answer); 9 of 10 completed with a fare (8543 to 16806), rider 13 ended NO_DRIVER_FOUND after the simulated drivers rejected three offers and ignored one (the simulator does that on purpose). Rides at 100 give the M5.1 numbers (`normal_fare` = `computed_fare` = the M5.1 formula). `simulator/simulator.py` is unchanged in `git diff --stat`; `/rider/`, `/driver/`, `/admin/` and `/health` answer 200.

**Mutation checks** (each made at least one test fail, then reverted and the suite passed again): (a) settlement uses the current snapshot instead of `ride.surge_percent` (`test_the_trip_is_settled_with_the_multiplier_of_the_request_not_the_current_one`); (b) every status counts as demand and (c) rides instead of distinct riders (`test_demand_counts_distinct_unmet_riders_in_the_window_and_the_zone`); (d) no minimum demand (`test_the_step_table[2-0-200-100]`); (e) every online driver counts as supply (`test_supply_counts_only_available_drivers_in_the_zone`); (f) the rule's cap ignored (`test_the_cap_comes_from_the_pricing_rule...[1.5-150]`); (g) no `accepted_surge_percent` check (`test_a_lower_accepted_multiplier_is_a_409_and_creates_nothing[100]`); (h) surge before the minimum fare (`test_the_minimum_fare_is_multiplied_too`); (i) the cancellation fee surged: by the ride's multiplier after the arrival, by the ride's multiplier when late, and by the zone's current multiplier when late (three variants: one survived at first, the arrived-driver branch, so `test_cancelling_after_the_driver_arrived_is_not_surged_either` was added); (j) rounding down (`test_surge_rounds_half_up[1-150-2]`).

**Not tested in a browser.** This session had no browser tool. What was checked: `node --check frontend/rider/rider.js` (in a throwaway container), every element id that `rider.js` looks up exists in `index.html`, the pages answer 200, and the backend behavior behind each screen (estimate fields, 409 text, ride and breakdown fields) was exercised by curl-like scripts and tests. Left for a person: the manual click-through (surge text and lines in the estimate panel, the failed request showing the 409 message while the panel updates by itself and the map stays still, the lower-price request, the `(includes Nx high-demand pricing)` text in the ride view, the high-demand line in the finished fare, phone width).

## Payments results

Measured on 2026-10-09 against the dev stack (the local fake Stripe, `STRIPE_*` set to the fake values; no real Stripe key was available, see "Live Stripe not run" in the open issues).

**Migration.** Before migrating: 361 settled rides with a fare and not legacy, summing to 4103175 paise. After `alembic upgrade head`: 361 payments summing to 4103175, all cash and succeeded, keys `ride:<id>:charge`. `\d` showed every constraint and partial index with its WHERE clause; the forbidden inserts inside `BEGIN ... ROLLBACK` (second payment per ride, a payment of 0, a positive RIDE_CHARGE, a second RIDE_CHARGE for one ride, an amount of 0, a reference that does not match the kind, a duplicate (user, key)) each failed with their own constraint name. `downgrade -1` removed the backfilled rows, `upgrade head` created the same 361, and `alembic revision --autogenerate` produced an empty migration (deleted).

**By hand.** Wallet: +Rs 500 with one key answered 201, then 200 with the same entry id; the same key with another amount 409; a debit of Rs 600 against Rs 500 409; psql showed one entry (`balance_after` 50000, the admin as actor) and the wallet row.
Top-up through the fake: key K, Rs 300 gave 201 and a PENDING row with a session; K again (5 at once) 200 and one row; K with Rs 400 409. A FRESH key with 5 requests at once gave one 201 and four 200, the same id, one row, one session (the fake received 5 create calls, all carrying `Idempotency-Key: topup-<id>`, and answered the stored session). `POST /pay/<session>/complete` (the Pay button): the webhook arrived, the top-up was SUCCEEDED with the payment intent, one TOPUP entry, balance 50000 + 30000. `POST /_replay/<event>?times=10`: ten 200, balance and entries unchanged, one `stripe_events` row; the backend logged `processed` once and `duplicate` for the rest, and no log line held a key or a secret.
Crash safety: with the fake stopped, a new key gave 502 and psql showed a PENDING row with no session and no url; with the fake back, the same key gave 200, one row, one session. A top-up paid on a fake whose webhook pointed at a dead port stayed PENDING (balance unchanged); `POST /wallet/topups/<id>/sync` credited it once (balance +10000, one entry) and a second sync answered 200 without calling the fake (its GET count stayed 1).
Webhook rejection (throwaway signing script): a valid unknown-type event 200 `ignored` (recorded); wrong secret 400, tampered body 400, no header 400, malformed header 400, a 10 minute old timestamp 400, an oversized body (262145 bytes) 413; `stripe_events` grew by exactly one row (the valid one).
Races (threads released together): 10 deliveries of one event to :8000 gave one `processed` and nine `duplicate`, balance +Rs 100; 10 deliveries of a NEW event alternating :8000 and a second uvicorn on :8001 gave the same; 20 adjustments of +Rs 1 with distinct keys all 201, balance +2000, 20 entries, 20 distinct `balance_after` equal to the running sums; 10 adjustments with one key gave one 201 and nine 200, one entry id, one entry.
**Application guards switched off one at a time** (temporary edits, reverted and compared with saved copies), with 10 simultaneous deliveries: (1) the status re-check in `credit_topup` removed: with five different event ids twice each, one `processed`, one `duplicate` and eight 500s (the unique index `uq_wallet_entries_one_credit_per_topup`), exactly one TOPUP entry; (2) the event-id insert made to always say "new": one `processed`, nine `ignored` (the status re-check holds the line), one entry; (3) both removed: one `processed` and nine 500s from the unique index, balance +Rs 100 and equal to the sum of the entries. The database alone prevents the double credit; the guards turn the second attempt from an error into an `ignored`.

**Rides.** Simulator (20 drivers, 30 km/h, seed 1), a Rs 500 wallet: a rider with Rs 10 asking for a wallet ride got `402 Your wallet balance (₹10.00) is below the ₹145.44 this trip can cost. Add money or pay with cash.` and no ride. The funded rider's wallet ride during the trip showed `balance 50000, reserved 14544, available 35456` (150 percent of the estimate 9696) and after COMPLETED `reserved 0`: distance 2977 m tracked, 402 s, final fare **9912** (the same as the M5.1 hand calculation, so cash fares did not change), cap 14544; one payment (wallet, succeeded, 9912, `ride:28907:charge`), one entry `-9912` linked to the ride, balance 40088 = 50000 - 9912. Cash ride: payment (cash, 8000) and no entry. Wallet ride cancelled late (free window 2 s in the dev rule during the browser run, restored to 120 afterwards, checked): fee 3000, one payment, one entry. Redis stopped just before completing a wallet ride: 200, billed at the estimate (`no_tracking`), still charged 8613 from the wallet. With `post_entry` temporarily made to raise: complete answered 500, the ride stayed IN_PROGRESS, `final_fare` NULL, no breakdown, no payment, no entry, no COMPLETED event; after the revert the retry gave one payment and one entry.

**Mutation checks** (each reverted and the suite green again): (a) skip signature verification, (b) accept any timestamp, (c) skip the event-id dedup, (d) skip the status re-check, (e) no wallet lock in `post_entry`, (f) skip the wallet balance check at request, (g) charge cash rides from the wallet, (h) skip the payment on cancellation fees, (i) credit the amount from the webhook instead of the top-up row, (j) skip the adjust replay check, (k) `balance_after` from the wrong base, (l) accept non-test keys: **all 12 made at least one test fail.**

**Tests.** 444 tests (385 before + 59 in `test_payments.py`), run twice, both green. The only existing test changed is `test_trip.py::test_the_code_is_stored_on_accept_and_is_in_no_response_or_message`: its exact key set of `RideResponse` gained `payment_method`.

**Stress and chaos on the final code** (all exit 0, I1 to I18 clean, 0 stuck rides): `--sequential` 3 rounds (10 riders, 3 drivers, 9 of 9 offers); `--scenario drivers --rounds 20` (60 of 60 offers); `--scenario riders --rounds 10`; fleet 3 rounds with 30 simulated drivers at 30 km/h (60 rides, 25 NO_DRIVER_FOUND); **chaos seed 1** (387 rides requested; 37 completed, final fares 673816 paise: 20 wallet rides charged 355852 and 17 cash rides 317964; 3 cancel fees of 9000 paise in all, 1 from the wallet (3000) and 2 cash (6000); 30 adjustments of the admin credited 175600 paise, 11 of them sent twice at once, 0 replay mismatches; 206 wallet rides requested, 0 answered 402); **seed 2** (385 rides; 32 completed: 20 wallet 354784, 12 cash 218472; 5 fees, 15000 paise, 1 wallet (3000) and 4 cash (12000); 30 adjustments, 171700 credited, 9 sent twice, 0 mismatches; 194 wallet rides, 0 answered 402); **two processes on :8000 and :8001, seed 3** (399 rides; 39 completed: 20 wallet 346536, 19 cash 352644; no fee happened in this run; 30 adjustments, 151500 credited, 6 sent twice, 0 mismatches; 176 wallet rides, 0 answered 402; 0 5xx); **`docker compose restart backend` at about the halfway point with `--tolerate-5xx`, seed 4** (368 rides; 24 completed: 12 wallet 230688, 12 cash 228624; 1 cash fee of 3000; 29 adjustments, 172800 credited, 6 sent twice, 0 mismatches; 184 wallet rides, 0 answered 402; 0 5xx, 50 connection errors tolerated). In every chaos run `final balance == starting balance + credits - wallet charges` held for 30 of 30 stress riders, I1 to I18 were clean, and 0 rides were stuck (4 to 7 rides were legitimately left assigned, arrived or in progress). 402s and double adjustments: wallet rides, cash rides, cancel fees from both methods, and adjustment replays all happened; a 402 did NOT happen in chaos (the wallets are funded) and is covered by the tests, the by-hand run and the browser run. **The `payments` scenario** (20 riders, 5 rounds, the fake Stripe): 100 top-ups created, 500 of 500 create requests succeeded (0 needed the retry after a 409), 1300 webhook deliveries all answered 200: 100 `processed` (exactly one per top-up), 100 `ignored` (the first delivery of the other event id), 1100 `duplicate`; 15 bad events (wrong secret, tampered body, 10 minutes old) all 400 with the database unchanged; I1 to I18 clean after every round; `PAYMENTS CLEAN`. The same with `--api-url http://127.0.0.1:8000,http://127.0.0.1:8001`: identical counts, and one create request got the fake's 409 in progress and succeeded on the retry; `PAYMENTS CLEAN`. **Regression:** `simulator.py --drivers 30 --speed-kmh 90 --seed 1` with 10 riders (5 wallet, 5 cash) requesting at once: all 10 completed with tracked fares of 15863 to 16227 paise (estimate 17453, a 1.8x surge left by the stress runs), each with exactly one succeeded payment of its fare and method, and one ledger entry for each of the 5 wallet rides and none for the cash rides; `simulator/simulator.py` is unchanged in `git diff --stat`; `/rider/`, `/driver/`, `/admin/`, `/docs` and `/health` answer 200; `docker-compose.yml` has exactly the three `extra_hosts` lines.

**Browser** (headless system Chrome driven by Playwright from a throwaway venv, script outside the repo; OSM tiles and `/places/reverse` mocked, everything else real, the fake Stripe included): 35 of 35 checks passed: the Add money flow through the fake checkout page, "Payment received", URL parameters removed, cancel and "Check status" and expire, five replays changing nothing, two tabs and two separate top-ups paid once each, the 402 message and hint, wallet ride (reserved shown, "Paid X from your wallet.", balance down by exactly the fare, "Ride payment", driver tab "Paid from the rider's wallet."), cash ride (both pages tell the cash text, balance unchanged), a wallet ride cancelled with a fee, the same adjustment key twice (the balance rose once), a note `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` shown as text with no element and no alert, and no horizontal scroll at 375 px.

## Money views results

Measured on 2026-10-09 and 2026-10-10 against the dev stack (simulator drivers, the local fake Stripe for the payments scenario, headless system Chrome for the browser run).

**Migration `0009`.** Before: 535 payments summing to 6819151 paise. After `alembic upgrade head`: 535 `ride_earnings` rows (449 trips, 86 cancellations; after the later runs the dev counts differ), `sum(gross_amount)` 6819151, `sum(platform_fee)` 1363826 plus `sum(driver_earning)` 5455325 is 6819151, every `commission_percent` 20 (and the sum of fees equals the sum of `(amount * 20 + 50) / 100` over the payments). `\d ride_earnings` and `\d pricing_rules` showed every constraint and index. The guard worked: inside `BEGIN ... ROLLBACK`, deleting one earning row and running the guard statement raised `payments without earnings after backfill`, and on the real data it passed. The forbidden inserts inside one rolled-back transaction each failed with their own constraint: a second earning for one payment (`uq_ride_earnings_payment_id`), for one ride (`uq_ride_earnings_ride_id`), fee plus earning not equal to gross (`ck_ride_earnings_split_adds_up`), a negative fee (`..._platform_fee_not_negative`), a negative earning (`..._driver_earning_not_negative`), gross 0 (`..._gross_positive`), percent 101 and -1 (`..._commission_percent_range`), kind `tip` (`..._kind_known`); percent 0 with fee 0, percent 100 with earning 0 and a cancellation were accepted; a rule with commission 101 or -1 was refused (`ck_pricing_rules_commission_percent_range`) and 0 and 100 accepted. `alembic downgrade -1` then `upgrade head`: the earning rows came back identical (the same md5 over their columns without ids), no other table changed (rides, payments, wallet entries, wallets and the pricing rule had the same counts, sums and md5 before and after), and `alembic revision --autogenerate` was empty (file deleted).

**The money by hand** (simulator, 30 km/h, a Rs 500 wallet per rider; each payment row, earning row and the arithmetic were printed; `platform_fee = (gross * 20 + 50) // 100`, `driver_earning = gross - platform_fee`):

| Ride | Case | Gross | Fee | Earning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 31275 | wallet trip completed (the same 9912 as the M5.1 and M5.3 hand calculations) | 9912 | (198240 + 50) // 100 = 1982 | 7930 |
| 31276 | cash trip completed | 9750 | 1950 | 7800 |
| 31277 | wallet ride cancelled late (free window temporarily 2 s, restored to 120 and shown) | 3000 | 600 | 2400 |
| 31279 | cash trip, a later ride of driver 36 | 9944 | 1989 | 7955 |
| 31280 | wallet cancellation fee, driver 36 | 3000 | 600 | 2400 |
| 31281 | wallet trip at 30 percent (commission temporarily 30 in the dev rule, restored to 20 and shown) | 9944 | (298320 + 50) // 100 = 2983 | 6961 |

The rows of the earlier rides were unchanged after the commission edit (shown in one query). **Atomic failure by hand:** with `earnings.create` temporarily raising, completing wallet ride 31283 answered 500 and psql showed the ride IN_PROGRESS, `final_fare` NULL, no breakdown, no payment, no earning, no wallet entry and no COMPLETED event; after the revert (identical to the saved copy) the retry gave 200 with one payment (8689, id 907: id 906 was used by the rolled-back attempt, sequences are not transactional), one earning (1738 plus 6951) and one ledger entry (-8689, balance 41311).

**Driver views by hand** (driver 36 = `sim-driver-001`, who also had older rows; nine in all). All-time summary `trips 7, cancellation_fees 2, total {9, 79035, 16802, 62233}, cash {6, 50030, 10007, 40023}, wallet {3, 29005, 6795, 22210}, settlement {owed_to_driver 22210, owed_by_driver 10007, net 12203}`. Checked by hand from the nine rows: cash gross 8089 + 10158 + 3000 + 10129 + 8710 + 9944 = 50030, fees 1618 + 2032 + 600 + 2026 + 1742 + 1989 = 10007; wallet 16061 + 3000 + 9944 = 29005, earnings 12849 + 2400 + 6961 = 22210; **the settlement example: the platform owes the driver 22210 for wallet rides, the driver owes the platform 10007 for cash rides, so net 12203 in the driver's favour.** `since=2026-10-09T17:52:30Z` (between the cash trip and the cancellation fee) gave rides 2, gross 12944, fee 3583, earning 9361, net 9361; `until` the same instant gave rides 7, gross 66091, net 2842; the same `since` written as `2026-10-09T23:22:30%2B05:30` gave the same numbers and echoed the +05:30; a naive `since` answered 422 (FastAPI `timezone_aware`) and `until` before `since` 422 `until must be after since`. `entries?limit=2` then `before_id` paging gave [541, 540], [539, 430], [408, 286], [244, 242], [223]: nine ids, unique, newest first, equal to the SQL ids. Driver 40 saw only its own rows.

**Admin views by hand.** `GET /admin/revenue` all time: 541 rows (535 migrated plus the six new rides), equal to the SQL `count(*)`, gross 6864701, fee 1373930, earning 5490771, cash 447 rides and wallet 94 rides, kinds 453 and 88, all equal to `SELECT count(*), sum(...) FROM ride_earnings` and the grouped query by method. A window `since=17:00Z until=18:00Z`: 5 rows, gross 35606, fee 7121, earning 28485, equal to SQL. `GET /admin/drivers/36/earnings` is byte for byte the driver's own answer; unknown id 404; rider and driver tokens 403; no token 401.

**Receipts by hand.** The receipts of the wallet trip (31275: total 9912 equal to `final_fare`, the payment and the ledger entry -9912 with `balance_after` 90088 shown as `wallet_balance_after`), the cash trip (31276: 9750, `wallet_balance_after` null) and the late cancellation (31277: fee 3000, `late_cancellation`, `cancelled_by` rider, balance after 47000) were compared with the ride JSON, the payment row and the ledger entry (asserted by the script). A free cancellation (31282) and the NO_DRIVER_FOUND ride (31278) answered 409 `There is no receipt for this ride`; another rider 404, the ride's driver 403, an admin 403, no token 401. After `UPDATE pricing_rules SET base_fare = 9999, per_km = 1, commission_percent = 55, cancellation_fee = 777` all three receipts hashed identically (SHA-256 before, after, and after restoring the rule). A ride from before M5.2 exists in the dev database (ride 23527, no surge keys in its breakdown): its receipt has `surge_percent` 100, `surge_amount` 0 and `normal_fare` equal to `computed_fare` 9912.

**Query plans** (`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` of the SQL that SQLAlchemy sends, 538 rows in `ride_earnings`, 11 of them driver 40's): the all-drivers summary is a hash join of two sequential scans (`ride_earnings` 6 buffers, `payments` 8) and a HashAggregate, 4.8 ms: at this size a sequential scan is the cheapest way to read every row, so no index is used. The one-driver summary with a 7 day window and the entries list both use **`ix_ride_earnings_driver_id_id`** (a bitmap index scan on `driver_id = 40`, 11 rows, then a filter on `created_at`), `pk_rides` for the join to the ride, and a sort for `ORDER BY id DESC` (2.4 ms and 1.9 ms; `payments` is still a sequential scan inside a hash join because it is tiny). With sequential scans switched off (session setting only) the same queries use `pk_payments` too and run in 0.2 to 0.3 ms. **`ix_ride_earnings_created_at` is not used** at this size: the windows are wider than the rows the driver index already narrows to, and a window on the all-drivers summary matches most of a small table. A driver's summary reads all that driver's rows in the window (the open issue below), and the list never sorts more than that driver's rows in the window.

**Mutation checks** (each reverted, files compared with the saved copies, suite green again): (a) fee rounded down: failed the 13999 split case; (b) no earning on cancellation fees: failed the split tests; (c) summaries recompute the split from the current rule: failed the snapshot test; (d) settlement sign swapped; (e) `since` ignored: failed the window test; (f) only cash in `total`; (g) receipts recompute `base_fare` from the current rule: failed the immutability test; (h) another rider reads a receipt (the owner check and `load_ride_for_user` bypassed): failed the privacy test; (i) an earning for the payment of another ride: failed with a unique violation in the snapshot test; (j) the `kind` column left out of the row: failed with a NOT NULL violation. **All 10 made at least one test fail.**

**Tests.** 497 tests (444 before + 53 in `test_earnings.py`), run twice, both green (257 s and 258 s). No existing test needed a change; `conftest.py` got an explicit `commission_percent=20` in the pricing rule insert and the `settled_ride` fixture (a ride, its payment and its earning row inserted directly with a chosen amount, method, kind, percent and time; `with_earning=False` and `old_breakdown=True` options). The 53 tests: the split (seven hand-worked cases through a real late cancellation, trip earnings for wallet and cash, no earning for a free cancellation, a driver cancellation, NO_DRIVER_FOUND or a fee of 0, the commission snapshot, atomic failure of `earnings.create`, `post_entry` and the cancel, two simultaneous completions, the constraints), the driver views (zeros, a mixed hand-worked summary, card in `total` only, windows, 422s on all four endpoints, isolation and roles, no profile 404 and a rejected driver, entries paging with no rider data), the admin views, and the receipts (keys, surge, wallet balance, cancellation, 409s, access, immutability, pre-M5.2 breakdown, privacy).
