**Stress and chaos on the final code** (all exit 0, I1 to I20 clean, 0 stuck rides, `money_view_mismatch` 0): `--sequential` 3 rounds (offers 12 of 9 expected); `--scenario drivers --rounds 20` (61 offers for 60 expected); `--scenario riders --rounds 10` (200 requests accepted once, 200 refused with 409); fleet 3 rounds with 30 simulated drivers at 30 km/h (60 rides, 24 NO_DRIVER_FOUND); **chaos seed 1:** 393 rides, 32 completed (587686 paise) and 4 fees (12000), earning rows of the run 36, gross 599686 = fees 119936 + earnings 479750, wallet rides 17 completed and cash rides 15 completed, 30 of 30 wallets balanced; **seed 2:** 395 rides, 28 completed (499682) and 5 fees (15000), 33 rows, gross 514682 = 102938 + 411744; **two processes (:8000 and :8001), seed 3:** 389 rides, 35 completed (621524) and 1 fee (3000), 36 rows, gross 624524 = 124903 + 499621; **`docker compose restart backend` at about the halfway point with `--tolerate-5xx`, seed 4:** 380 rides, 27 completed (500216), no fee, 27 rows, gross 500216 = 100044 + 400172, 0 5xx and 42 connection errors tolerated. In every chaos run the cross-check compared the all-time admin revenue (counts, the three buckets, the settlement), the all-time summary of the 10 stress drivers and 20 receipts of the run with the SQL: 0 mismatches. Wallet trips, cash trips, cancellation fees (seeds 1, 2 and 3; seed 4 had none, covered by seeds 1 to 3), double accepts and the receipt sample all happened. The payments scenario (20 riders, 5 rounds, fake Stripe): 100 top-ups, 500 of 500 create requests succeeded, 1300 webhook deliveries (100 processed, 100 ignored, 1100 duplicate), 15 bad events all 400, `PAYMENTS CLEAN`, and the same with two processes. **Regression:** `simulator.py --drivers 30 --speed-kmh 90 --seed 1` with 10 riders (5 wallet, 5 cash) requesting at once: all 10 COMPLETED, each with one payment of its fare and method AND one earning row (fees 1763 to 1803 for fares 8813 to 9015), and a ledger entry for each of the 5 wallet rides only; `simulator/simulator.py`, `simulator/fake_stripe.py`, `docker-compose.yml`, `frontend/shared/` and `frontend/admin/` are unchanged in `git diff --stat`; `/rider/`, `/driver/`, `/admin/`, `/docs` and `/health` answer 200; invariants I1 to I20 are clean on the dev database.

**Browser** (headless system Chrome driven by Playwright from a throwaway venv, script outside the repo; OSM tiles and `/places/reverse` mocked, everything else real, a driver tab and a rider tab, the simulator off): 33 of 33 checks passed. Driver: zeros before any ride; "You earn E (G fare minus F platform fee)." after a cash ride, a wallet ride's receipt, and a late cancellation by the rider (E 24.00 of 30.00 minus 6.00), each entry at the top of the list with its percent and method, the summary updated without waiting for the poll; Today, Last 7 days and All time equal the API answers; the cash, wallet and balance lines and the payouts note; Refresh, "Show more" (10, then 20 entries with none repeated, and paging to the end listed 24 of 24 rows once each); a pickup address of `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` (ride created through the API) shown as text in the offer, the list and the receipt, no `img` element and no dialog; no horizontal scroll at 375 px on both pages. Rider: the receipt with number, issue time, route, driver and vehicle, lines and total equal to the receipt JSON, "Paid in cash to the driver", "Paid from your wallet (balance after: B)" equal to the wallet, the late cancellation's fee and reason; no receipt section after a free cancellation, a driver cancellation or NO_DRIVER_FOUND; cleared by "Request a new ride"; no word commission, platform fee or earning on the receipt; the print media emulation showed only the receipt (no button, no map box; a PDF and a screenshot were taken). 10 seconds of polling an unfinished ride made no earnings or receipt request, and a reload mid-ride made exactly the two start-up earnings requests and nothing on later polls. Not browser-tested: "Print receipt" opening the real print dialog (the print styles were checked with media emulation), the old receipt after a reload (the page keeps no history: the receipt of a finished ride exists only in the page that saw it, checked through the API instead), and touch devices.

## Ratings results

Measured on 2026-10-10 against the dev stack (a throwaway dev admin `m61-admin@example.com`; the simulator and the local fake Stripe only where a step says so; headless system Chrome driven by Playwright from a throwaway venv, scripts outside the repo).

**Prerequisites.** Before any change: `git status` clean, the suite 497 passed in 280 s, `simulator/invariants.sql` clean (I1 to I20), `SELECT count(*) FROM ratings` was 0, and admins existed.

**Migration `0010`.** `\d ratings` and `\d rating_summaries` showed every new constraint and index. Inside `BEGIN ... ROLLBACK`, with a savepoint around each: a self rating (`ck_ratings_no_self_rating`), score 0 and 6 (`ck_ratings_score_range`), a 301-character comment (`ck_ratings_comment_length`), a duplicate (ride, rater) (`uq_ratings_ride_id_from_user_id`), a summary with total below count and above 5 times count (`ck_rating_summaries_total_within_range`) and a negative count (`ck_rating_summaries_count_not_negative`) each failed with their own name; a 300-character comment, the other direction on one ride, and summaries 0/0, 2/2 and 2/10 were accepted. `downgrade -1` then `upgrade head` with 20 ratings in the table (left by a smoke run): the backfill rebuilt the same 6 summary rows (md5 over user, count and total identical), and rides, payments, users and earnings had identical counts and sums before and after. `alembic revision --autogenerate` was empty (file deleted). The generated migration was root-owned inside the container, so the first hand edit failed and the first `upgrade` ran WITHOUT the two CHECKs and the backfill; it was downgraded, the file recreated as the normal user, edited and upgraded again (the M5.1 slip again, see the bugs log).

**By hand (curl).** One driver (user 631, driver row 320: the two ids differ on purpose) completed 4 rides for riders 632 to 635. Before any rating the details of rider 4's ride showed `rating {count 0, average null}`. Rider 1 rated 5, rider 2 rated 4: details `{count 2, average null}`. Rider 3 rated 4: details `{count 3, average 4.33}` (13 / 3 = 4.333), `GET /ratings/me` of the driver `{3, 4.33}`, summary row `(3, 13)`. The driver rated riders 1 to 4 with 5 "Polite", 3 "Late to the drop-off", 2 "Left a mess" and 4 (no comment): each rider's own `/ratings/me` showed `{1, 5.0}`, `{1, 3.0}`, `{1, 2.0}`, `{1, 4.0}` and the four summary rows were `(1, 5)`, `(1, 3)`, `(1, 2)`, `(1, 4)`. Rules: a duplicate with another score 409; IN_PROGRESS (rider and driver), CANCELLED and NO_DRIVER_FOUND rides 409; another rider's ride 404; an admin 403; score 0, `"5"`, 4.5, true and a missing score 422; a 301-character comment 422; a blank comment stored as NULL (row 76, `comment IS NULL` true; rider 4's rating made the driver `(4, 18)`). Window (a TEMPORARY DATA EDIT on one disposable completed ride, ride 33738, restored to `now()` afterwards): `completed_at` 8 days back gave 409 and no row, 6 days 23 hours back gave 201. The rejected duplicate consumed a sequence value, so rating ids skip (75 was never used): sequences are not transactional.

**Privacy by hand.** Rider 1's comment "Smooth ride" and the driver's "Polite" on ride 33732: the driver's `GET /rides/{id}`, `/driver`, `/events`, `/ratings/me`, `/drivers/me/earnings`, the entries, `/drivers/me/offer` and presence never contained "Smooth ride" nor a `"score"` key; rider 1's ride, driver details, `/ratings/me`, receipt, wallet, entries and events never contained "Polite" nor a `"score"` key. The status endpoint of each side held only their own rating. `GET /admin/ratings?user_id=` showed the comments and both user ids.

**Races (the `ratings` scenario, every run exit 0, I1 to I22 clean after every round, exactly one 201 and two 409 per rater).**

| Run | Ratings created | Duplicate answers (409) | Burst (requests, duration, median latency) | The driver's summary |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 20 riders x 5 rounds | 200 | 400 | 120 requests, 1081 to 1342 ms, median 685 to 853 ms | 10/39 to 110/351, SQL 110/351 |
| 60 riders x 3 rounds | 360 | 720 | 360 requests, 4370 to 4617 ms, median 1792 to 1873 ms | 110/351 to 290/941, SQL 290/941 |
| two processes, 20 x 5 | 200 | 400 | 120 requests, 1369 to 1504 ms, median 792 to 945 ms | 290/941 to 390/1225, SQL 390/1225 |
| two processes, 60 x 3 | 360 | 720 | 360 requests, 4348 to 4442 ms, median 1522 to 1551 ms | 390/1225 to 570/1777, SQL 570/1777 |

Every round the summary rows of the driver and of every rider equalled what they were before the run plus the scores of the winning requests (the tool checks this itself). The burst time grows with the number of requests (about 10 to 12 ms each) and two processes did not make it faster, which fits the one hot row serializing the 60 ratings of a round (and the database work) rather than Python; it was not profiled, so that is a reading, not a measurement.

**Query plans** (`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` of the SQL the repository sends, printed by compiling the statements; 1149 rows in `ratings`, 66 in `rating_summaries`). The admin list for a user with 5 ratings uses **`ix_ratings_to_user_id_id`** (bitmap index scan, then a sort of 5 rows, 0.1 ms). For the busiest user (570 of the 1149 rows) the planner walks `pk_ratings` backwards and filters, because half the table matches and the first 20 hits are found after 43 entries. The list without a filter is the same backward walk of `pk_ratings` with the limit (22 buffers); with `max_score` and `before_id` it is the walk with `id < N` as the index condition and `score` as a filter. The summary read is a **sequential scan** of 66 rows (2 buffers, 0.2 ms); with sequential scans switched off it uses `pk_rating_summaries` (0.07 ms), so at this size the table is simply too small for an index to matter. The "my rating of this ride" read uses **`uq_ratings_ride_id_from_user_id`**. The new index pays off for a user with few ratings in a big table, which is the usual admin question ("what was said about this driver").

**Mutation checks** (each reverted, files compared byte for byte, the ratings tests green again): (a) skip the summary update, (b) add 1 to the total instead of the score, (c) skip the status check, (d) skip the window check, (e) set `to_user_id` to the rater, (f) skip the participant check, (g) the status endpoint returns the other party's rating, (h) remove the public minimum, (i) round the average down, (j) remove `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, (k) `drivers.id` instead of the driver's user id: **all 11 made at least one test fail.**

**Tests.** 550 tests (497 before + 53 in `test_ratings.py`), run twice, both green (314 s and 301 s). Existing tests changed: only `test_tracking.py::test_the_details_hold_only_name_vehicle_and_location` (the exact key set of the driver details gained `rating`). `conftest.py` got the `completed_ride` factory (a COMPLETED ride with a chosen `completed_at`, inserted directly); its TRUNCATE already covers `rating_summaries` because it takes the table list from the metadata. The 53 tests: creating (both directions, validation, status and access, rejected driver, duplicates and five at once, the window and the constant read at call time), averages (eight hand-worked sequences through the API, the pure function, the summary equal to the table after each insert, 40 simultaneous requests for 20 ratings, atomicity with `add_to_summary` raising, the constraints by SQL), and views (own summary, details hidden below 3, status, privacy across ten endpoints, the admin list with paging and 422s).

**Stress and chaos on the final code** (all exit 0, I1 to I22 clean, 0 stuck rides, `money_view_mismatch` 0, `rating_view_mismatch` 0): `--sequential` 3 rounds (offers 9 of 9); `--scenario drivers --rounds 20` (60 of 60); `--scenario riders --rounds 10` (199 requests accepted once and 199 refused; 2 answers were 422, the same random too-close pickup and drop-off sent twice by one rider); fleet 3 rounds with 30 simulated drivers at 30 km/h (60 rides, 26 NO_DRIVER_FOUND). Chaos (30 riders, 10 drivers, 60 s):

| Run | Rides | Completed | Ratings created (by riders / by drivers) | 409 on duplicates | Pairs sent at once (`rating_dup_error`) | Attempts on rides that did not complete (all 409) | Average, rider to driver / driver to rider | Views checked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| seed 1 | 373 | 26 | 32 (16 / 16) | 1 | 1 (0) | 20 | 2.62 / 2.50 | 40 `/ratings/me`, 20 driver details |
| seed 2 | 372 | 25 | 32 (19 / 13) | 1 | 1 (0) | 16 | 3.00 / 3.00 | same |
| two processes, seed 3 | 369 | 32 | 42 (22 / 20) | 2 | 2 (0) | 17 | 2.77 / 2.30 | same |
| `docker compose restart backend` at about the halfway point, `--tolerate-5xx`, seed 4 | 378 | 22 | 26 (14 / 12) | 1 | 1 (0) | 16 | 2.86 / 2.58 | same; 0 5xx, 63 connection errors tolerated |

Ratings in both directions, duplicate pairs and attempts on cancelled or driverless rides all happened in every run (no "proves nothing" warning was printed), but duplicate pairs are rare in chaos (1 or 2 per run: 10 percent of about 30); the `ratings` scenario is what exercises duplicates heavily. The `payments` scenario (20 riders, 5 rounds, the fake Stripe): 100 top-ups, 500 of 500 create requests succeeded, 1300 webhook deliveries (100 processed, 100 ignored, 1100 duplicate), 15 bad events all 400, `PAYMENTS CLEAN`.

**Regression.** `simulator.py --drivers 30 --speed-kmh 90 --seed 1` with 10 riders (5 wallet, 5 cash) requesting at once: all 10 COMPLETED (fares 9664 to 15143 paise at a 1.2x surge), each with exactly one succeeded payment of its fare and method AND one earning row, and one ledger entry for each of the 5 wallet rides only; the riders then rated their simulated drivers (201, and 409 for the two who already had) and the details showed `{count 1, average null}`. (The first attempt of this run started the simulator inside a command that `pkill -f` also killed, so all 10 rides ended NO_DRIVER_FOUND; the run was repeated properly.) `git diff --stat` is empty for `simulator/simulator.py`, `simulator/fake_stripe.py`, `docker-compose.yml`, `frontend/shared/`, `frontend/admin/` and the pricing, payments, wallet, earnings, receipts, offers and matching services and the WebSocket router; `/rider/`, `/driver/`, `/admin/`, `/docs` and `/health` answer 200; the invariants are clean on the dev database after all of it.

**Browser** (system Chrome 151 through Playwright; OSM tiles mocked, everything else real; a driver tab and rider tabs, the simulator off; rides were requested through the API except the NO_DRIVER_FOUND one, which used the rider page's map clicks; ride 1 was driven through the driver page: Accept, "I have arrived", the code, Start, Complete): 30 checks ran and passed; the first of them ("driver page loads") cannot fail and proves nothing, so 29 are meaningful. Covered: the finished view with five stars, the labels, the comment box and "You can rate until"; submit with no star showed "Choose a star rating first" and sent no POST; half a comment survived 10 seconds of polling and the polls made no rating request (counted from the network events after fixing the script, see the bugs log); 4 stars and a comment gave "You rated this trip ★★★★☆ (4 of 5)" with the comment as text, the form empty and hidden, and "Your rating" still "no ratings yet"; the driver tab's "Rate the rider" with 5 stars and its own line (★ 4.0 (1 rating), the real value for the owner); a second tab on the same ride showing "You have already rated this trip" and turning into the "You rated" view; the rider's line "★ 5.0 (1 rating)" after the next page load and exactly one `/ratings/me` request on load and none on the next 9 seconds of polls; "New driver" with 1 and 2 ratings and "Rating: ★ 4.3 (3 ratings)" after the third; a CANCELLED ride (cancelled in the UI) and a NO_DRIVER_FOUND ride with no rating section; "The rating period for this trip has ended." after a temporary data edit moved `completed_at` 8 days back right before the page asked; `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` as plain text with no `img` element and no dialog; at 375 px no horizontal scroll and the stars, textarea and button inside the screen. **Not browser-tested:** reconnect behavior, the wallet, earnings and receipt sections in this run (the fare sections were checked to be visible), requesting rides through the rider page's map and search (except the NO_DRIVER_FOUND case), touch devices, the print view.

## Admin dashboard results

Measured on 2026-10-10 against the dev stack. Throwaway accounts: an admin `m62admin@example.com`, riders `m62rider1` to `m62rider6`, and a hostile admin, rider and driver named `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>`; they and their few rides stay in the dev database (like the earlier test data). The simulator and the local fake Stripe ran only where a step says so. The browser was system Chrome 151 in headless mode driven over the DevTools protocol by a small throwaway script (no Playwright), scripts outside the repo.

**Prerequisites.** `git status` was clean. The dev pricing rule (`economy`: base 5000, per km 1200, per minute 200, minimum 8000, cancellation fee 3000, window 120 s, commission 20, cap 2.0) was inside every new check. Callers of `GET /admin/drivers`: only `admin.js:34`; the simulator and `stress.py` only POST approve, the earnings tests use `/admin/drivers/{id}/earnings`, no test asserted the key set of the list. The suite was 550 tests; after the model change 14 failed (see below) and were fixed. `invariants.sql` clean.

**Migration (step 2).** `alembic upgrade head` ran `0011`; `\d pricing_rules`, `\d pricing_rule_changes` and `\d ride_offers` show every column, constraint and index. Inside `BEGIN ... ROLLBACK` on the dev database each forbidden write failed with its constraint: `min_fare = 99` (`ck_pricing_rules_min_fare_floor`), `surge_cap` 0.99 and 2.01 (`..._surge_cap_range`), a negative base fare, per km, per minute, cancellation fee and window, `commission_percent = 101`, an audit row with `version_after = 3` after version 1 (`ck_pricing_rule_changes_version_step`) and a duplicate `(rule, version_after)` (`uq_pricing_rule_changes_rule_id_version_after`); the boundary values (`min_fare` 100, cap 1.0 and 2.0, zeros, commission 0 and 100) succeeded. `downgrade -1` removed the columns, checks, table and index, `upgrade head` brought them back, `alembic revision --autogenerate` was empty (file deleted), no other rows changed.

**Pricing editor by hand (steps 3 and 4).** `PATCH` per km 1200 to 1500 with version 1: 200, version 2, `changes` `[{per_km, 1200, 1500}]`, `updated_by_name` "Ada Admin", one audit row. The same body again: 409; version 1 with another value: 409; version 2 with the same value: 200 `changes: []`, version unchanged, no new row. 422 for cap 2.01, minimum fare 99, `"1500"`, an unknown field, an empty patch and cap 1.555. Race: 20 simultaneous `PATCH`es with one version, three rounds on `:8000` alone, then three rounds alternating `:8000` and a second uvicorn on `:8001`: every round exactly one 200 and nineteen 409, the version up by one, one audit row, the stored value equal to the winner's (versions 2 to 5, then 5 to 8; the audit table had 7 rows after the six rounds plus the first edit).

**Effects on money by hand (step 5)** (simulator at 30 km/h; three cash rides in progress at the same time, edits made while they were IN_PROGRESS):

| Ride | Estimate (cap) | Edit while in progress | Tracked | Settlement by hand | Final |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 36626 | 8451 (12676) | per km 1200 to 1600 | 2305 m, 312 s | 5000 + (1600*2305+500)//1000 = 3688 + (200*312+30)//60 = 1040 = 9728 | 9728, not capped, commission 20: fee 1946, earning 7782 |
| 36627 | 12040 (18060) | per km 1600 to 5000 | 4631 m, 555 s | 5000 + 23155 + 1850 = 30005 | capped at 18060 (`capped: true`), fee 3612, earning 14448 |
| 36628 | 15238 (22857) | commission 20 to 30 (per km still 5000) | 6724 m, 927 s | 5000 + 33620 + 3090 = 41710 | capped at 22857, commission 30: fee (22857*30+50)//100 = 6857, earning 16000 |

Earning rows settled before the commission edit (rides 36621, 36622, 36625) kept 20 percent. Surge cap: with the simulator stopped, four riders in zone `tdr1v` got NO_DRIVER_FOUND (demand 4, supply 0, pressure 400, step 200); the next estimate was normal 17278, surged 34556; after `surge_cap` 2.0 to 1.0 the next estimate was 17278 (surge 100) while `GET /admin/surge` still showed 200 (the snapshot stays uncapped). The original values were restored with one `PATCH` (version 12 to 13; per km 1200, commission 20, cap 2.0); `invariants.sql` was clean afterwards.

**Drivers list by hand (step 6).** 313 drivers paged by 20 with `after_id`: 16 pages, every id once and ascending, equal to `SELECT id FROM drivers ORDER BY id`. Ten drivers (six online, four offline): `online`, `state` and `active_ride_id` equal `redis-cli EXISTS driver:<id>:presence` plus an active-ride query and a pending-offer query (for example driver 36: online, on_ride, ride 36628; driver 37: free; driver 322: offline). `completed_trips`, `rating_count`, `rating_average` for driver 255 (662, 581, 3.12 from a sum of 1812: (1812*100+290)//581 = 312), drivers 257 and 260 (91, 9, 2.11) and driver 1 (0, 0, null) equal my own SQL. `q` by name, email, plate (lower case, 6 characters), license and id found the driver; `q=%` returned 0 rows (SQL: 0 rows with a literal percent sign).

**Live snapshot by hand (step 7).** With 20 simulated drivers and 3 rides: 20 drivers returned, 20 presence keys (`ZCARD drivers:geo` was 21: one stale member without a presence key, correctly left out); the id sets were equal; counts `{online 20, free 17, offered 0, on_ride 3}` and ride counts `{DRIVER_ASSIGNED 3}` equal my own SQL; five positions equal `GEOPOS` (which returns LONGITUDE first, so `lat` is the second number); two moving drivers differed in the 4th decimal because they moved between the two reads. The text contains no `@`, `phone`, `license`, `LIC`, `password` or `otp`. Timing (20 sequential calls, simulator running, all drivers logged in first): 20 drivers mean 17 to 21 ms (median 15 to 19), 100 drivers mean 50 to 68 ms (median 34 to 64), 200 drivers mean 25 to 76 ms (median 23 to 43); the numbers are noisy because the simulator's own pings share the one server process, and a first measurement taken while 200 drivers were still logging in showed hundreds of ms. With Redis stopped: `/admin/live` 503 and `/admin/drivers` 503 `Cache is unavailable`, while `/admin/stats` (null online drivers, one warning), `/admin/rides` and `/admin/pricing-rules` answered 200; after `docker compose start redis` the live call was 200 again.

**Rides list and detail by hand (step 8).** Three pages of 25 equal `SELECT id FROM rides ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 75`; the filters status, rider, driver plus status, and since/until each equal their own SQL. The detail of a completed wallet ride (36612), a late-cancelled ride (31324), a NO_DRIVER_FOUND ride (36624) and an active ride (36628) matched psql for events (with the actors rider, driver, driver, driver, driver, and so on), offers, payment, earning, ratings and the stored `fare_breakdown`; no text contained `"otp"`, and the stored trip code `1234` of the active ride did not appear as a JSON string.

**Stats by hand (step 9)** (window 2026-10-09T18:30Z to 2026-10-10T06:30Z, local 00:00 to 12:00 at +5:30, hour buckets, offset 330; every number below equals the API): 5288 rides requested = 836 completed + 809 cancelled + 3643 no driver found, 0 active; completion (836*2000+5288)//(2*5288)/10 = 15.8 (exact 15.8094), cancellation 15.3 (15.2988), no driver 68.9 (68.8918); 836 completed with a fare, fares 10128273 so average (10128273*2+836)//1672 = 12115, distance 3007021 m so 3597, duration 6164 s so 7 (the simulator runs trips at warp speed); 1202 assigned rides, mean 1.4124 s (1.4), median 0.3029 s (0.3); offers accepted 1202, rejected 139, expired 192, cancelled 443, pending 0, acceptance (1202*2000+1533)//(2*1533)/10 = 78.4; 4131 surged rides, highest 200; 47 new riders and 3 new drivers; money 869 payments, gross 10227273, platform fee 2047750, driver earning 8179523, settlement owed by drivers (cash fees) 1560955, owed to drivers (wallet earnings) 1947172, net 386217. The 12 hour buckets equal SQL bucketed with the timezone database (`date_trunc('hour', created_at AT TIME ZONE 'Asia/Kolkata')`, which is a fixed +5:30): for example 00:00 IST 2377 rides, 134 completed, gross 2373994, fee 474799; 09:00 2359, 651, 6905257, 1381061; 10:00 548, 51, 897377, 179475; 11:00 4, 0, 50645, 12415; the day buckets (offset 330) were 31243 and 5288 rides. `now`: 0 online drivers (no presence keys), 0 active rides, 0 pending offers. Two throwaway NO_DRIVER_FOUND rides at 00:29:59Z and 00:30:00Z (deleted afterwards) landed in the local hours 05:00 and 06:00 (buckets starting 23:30Z and 00:30Z), and a window starting exactly at 00:30:00Z included the second one while one ending exactly there excluded it. 422: missing `since` (`Field required`), a 15-day hour window (`The window can be at most 14 days with hour buckets`), offset 900 (`utc_offset_minutes must be between -840 and 840`).

**Query plans (step 10)** (real SQL captured from SQLAlchemy, `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)`; tables: rides 36591, ride_events 84037, ride_offers 15389, ride_earnings 1456, drivers 313, users 647; the stats window held 5284 rides):

| Query | Plan at this size | Time |
|---|---|---|
| rides list, no filter, `before_id`, since/until | backward scan of the primary key, stops after 25 rows (it does not use `ix_rides_created_at`) | 0.06 to 0.10 ms |
| rides list, status | the same scan with a filter (195 rows removed to find 25) | 0.18 ms |
| rides list, rider id | bitmap scan of `ix_rides_rider_id`, then a sort | 0.07 ms |
| rides list, driver id 255 | backward primary key scan with a filter, 1974 rows removed (it does not use `ix_rides_driver_id`) | 0.89 ms |
| drivers list, 50 rows | primary key scan of drivers, then per row the user (index), the rating summary (index) and the correlated trips count (`ix_rides_driver_id` and `ix_rides_status` bitmaps) | 4.6 ms |
| drivers list, `q` (text or number) | sequential scans of users, drivers and vehicles (all tiny), `ILIKE` cannot use an index | 3.1 to 3.2 ms |
| live: driver states, names, active rides, rides by status | sequential scan of drivers (313) with a probe of `uq_rides_one_active_per_driver` per row; the active rides use `uq_rides_one_active_per_rider`; the status counts an index-only scan of `ix_rides_status` | 0.07 to 0.38 ms |
| stats: rides by status, trip averages, surge summary, ride series | bitmap scan of `ix_rides_created_at` (5284 rows), then an aggregate | 1.4 to 13.3 ms |
| stats: offers by status | `ix_ride_offers_created_at`, the new index | 1.1 ms |
| stats: assignment times | bitmap scan of the rides in the window, then a sequential scan of `ride_events` (83 thousand rows, `to_status` has no index) | 11.3 ms |
| stats: money series | sequential scan of `ride_earnings` (1456 rows: too small for `ix_ride_earnings_created_at`) | 0.65 ms |
| stats: ride series over 30 days | sequential scan of all 36591 rides (the window covers the table) | 28.5 ms |

Where it will hurt at much larger sizes: the stats queries cost in proportion to the rides in the window (a day-bucket year is a scan of a year of rides); `assignment_times` scans every `ride_events` row; the rides list filtered by a rare driver or status walks the primary key backwards with a filter and can read the whole table (an index on `(driver_id, id)` or `(status, id)` would fix it); the drivers search is `ILIKE` over sequential scans (a trigram index would be needed); the live snapshot reads every online position and presence key on every call, so many open admin tabs multiply that cost.

**Mutation checks (step 11)**, each reverted afterwards; each made at least one test fail: (a) version check skipped: `test_a_stale_version_is_a_409_and_changes_nothing`; (b) audit insert skipped: `test_an_edit_changes_the_values_bumps_the_version_and_writes_one_audit_row`; (c) cap limit raised to 3.0: `test_the_seeded_rule_is_listed_with_its_limits`; (d) `q` as a raw LIKE pattern: `test_percent_underscore_and_backslash_in_a_search_match_themselves`; (e) `after_id` ignored: `test_paging_by_after_id_returns_every_driver_once_in_id_order`; (f) latitude and longitude swapped: `test_only_drivers_with_a_presence_key_are_on_the_map_with_their_own_coordinates`; (g) a stale GEO member counted as online: the same test; (h) the trip code in the ride detail: `test_the_detail_of_a_completed_wallet_ride_shows_everything_and_never_the_trip_code`; (i) UTC offset ignored: `test_hour_buckets_with_an_offset_of_330_minutes_split_at_half_past`; (j) rides cohorted by `completed_at`: `test_the_window_edges_are_half_open`; (k) rates rounded down: the 2 of 3 case; (l) active rides left in the denominator: the 1 completed, 1 cancelled, 2 active case; (m) cancelled offers in the acceptance denominator: `test_the_acceptance_rate_leaves_out_cancelled_and_pending_offers`; (n) `active_rides` without REQUESTED: `test_active_rides_counts_exactly_the_four_active_statuses`; (o) a rider let into the admin routes: the pricing auth matrix.

**Tests (step 12).** Full suite twice on the final code: 765 passed (356 s and 346 s). That is 216 new tests (129 in `test_pricing_rules.py`, 47 in `test_admin.py`, 40 in `test_stats.py`) and one fewer old test than the 550 before (a removed parameter, see below). Existing tests changed because of the new database checks: `test_earnings.py::test_a_receipt_does_not_change_when_the_rule_or_surge_changes_later` (its `set_rule` used `min_fare=1`, now 100; the result does not depend on it), `test_pricing.py::test_fare_rounds_half_up` (8 cases) and `test_surge.py::test_surge_rounds_half_up` (4 cases) (they need a minimum fare of 0, which the floor of 100 forbids, so they use an in-memory `PricingRule` returned by a monkeypatched `get_rule` instead of a database row), and `test_surge.py::test_the_cap_comes_from_the_pricing_rule_and_the_snapshot_stays_uncapped` lost its `(0.5, 100)` case (a cap below 1.0 is now impossible). `conftest.py` needed no change. No test asserted the key set of `GET /admin/drivers`. The tests use the `_test` database and Redis database 1; the dev databases were not touched by the suite.

**Stress and chaos on the final code (step 13)** (the simulator stopped except for the fleet run; credentials of the throwaway admin): `--sequential` control (3 rounds, 10 riders): exit 0, offers 9 of 9. `drivers` 20 rounds: exit 0, 60 of 60 offers, no violation. `riders` 10 rounds: exit 0, 20 201s and 20 409s in every round. `fleet` (simulator at 30 drivers) 5 rounds: exit 0 (100 rides, 37 of them NO_DRIVER_FOUND, as the fleet is far from some pickups). `payments` (fake Stripe, 20 riders, 5 rounds): PAYMENTS CLEAN. `ratings` (20 riders, 5 rounds): RATINGS CLEAN. Chaos, all with I1 to I23 clean, 0 stuck rides, `money_view_mismatch` 0, `rating_view_mismatch` 0 and `admin_view_mismatch` 0:

| Run | Result | Pricing edits made (sent twice at once, each answering exactly one 200 and one 409) | Rule version start to final | Walked rides / ride details compared | Longest `/admin/live` | 5xx, connection errors |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| seed 1 | CHAOS CLEAN | 6 (2 duplicates, `pricing_dup_error` 0) | 13 to 20 | 402 / 10 | 98 ms | 0, 0 |

Every run restored the original values with one `PATCH` and confirmed they were back (`pricing rule restored ... original values back: True`), `pricing_409_unexpected` and `pricing_edit_error` were 0, and each run read the three admin views about 85 times for structure. Edits, duplicates and the cross-checks (stats, the rides list walked page by page, 40 drivers by email, 10 ride details) really ran in every run. The run with the restart warned that no rating was sent twice at once, which proves nothing about that one case in that run (it is covered by the others and by `ratings`).
