## History and saved places results

Measured on 2026-10-10 against the dev stack. Throwaway accounts stay in the dev database like the earlier test data: an admin `m63-admin@example.com`, riders `m63-rider-a/b-*`, a history rider `m63-hist-rider-*` (rides 39224 to 39229), a driver `m63-driver-*` (rides 39230 and after) and the usual stress and `sim-driver-*` accounts. The simulator and the local fake Stripe ran only where a step says so. **There was no browser tool in this session, so the pages were exercised headlessly: the real `rider.js` and `driver.js` and the real HTML ran in jsdom (Node 22, in a throwaway Docker container, scripts under the session scratchpad and not committed) against the live backend, with Leaflet and `ws.js` replaced by stubs.** That checks the render logic, the handlers, the requests and the DOM; it does not check real Leaflet, phone width, the print preview, the Network tab or whether text really looks right.

**Prerequisites.** `git status` clean; the suite was 765 passed (350 s); `invariants.sql` returned no offender; finished rides in the dev database: CANCELLED 12430, COMPLETED 2003, NO_DRIVER_FOUND 24652 (39190 rides in all).

**Migration.** `alembic upgrade head` ran `0012`; `\d saved_places` shows the seven columns, the four checks, the foreign key, `ix_saved_places_user_id_id` and `uq_saved_places_user_id_lower_label` on `(user_id, lower(label::text))`. Inside `BEGIN ... ROLLBACK` each forbidden insert failed with its constraint: empty label (`ck_saved_places_label_length`), empty address (`..._address_length`), lat 91 (`..._lat_range`), lng -181 (`..._lng_range`), `Home` and `HOME` for one user (`uq_saved_places_user_id_lower_label`), while a 31 character label and a 201 character address failed earlier with "value too long for type character varying(30)" and "(200)". `alembic downgrade -1` and `upgrade head` worked, and `alembic revision --autogenerate` afterwards was EMPTY (the file was deleted), so Alembic compares the expression index and no other table changed.

**Saved places by hand (curl).** 10 creates gave ten 201; the 11th `409 You can save up to 10 places. Delete one first.`; `Home` then `HOME` for another rider `409 You already have a place with that name.`; a point in Delhi `422 Location is outside the service area`; a bell character in the label `422` (pattern); `"lat": "12.9"` `422`; an extra `user_id` `422`. Rename `Place 1` to `Flat` 200, to `flat` (case only) 200, `Place 2` to `FLAT` 409, `Place 2` to `Place 2` 200 with no write, another rider's id 404, an `address` in the body 422 (missing label and extra field). Delete 204, again 404, another rider's id 404. Driver and admin tokens 403 on GET and POST, no token 401. psql showed the rows.

**Saved places concurrency by hand.** 25 simultaneous creates with distinct labels for one rider, three times on `:8000` alone: `{201: 10, 409: 15}`, 10 rows in psql, every time, and the same three times alternating `:8000` and a second uvicorn on `:8001`: `{201: 10, 409: 15}`, 10 rows. Six creates of `Gym`, `gym`, `GYM`, `gYm`, `Gym`, `GyM` at once: one 201 and five 409 on both setups, one row. Renaming two places to `Z` and `z` at once: `[200, 409]`, the list holds `Z` and `B`. (The second process was started with `docker compose exec -d backend ... uvicorn ... --port 8001` after the code was final, and removed by recreating the container.)

**History by hand.** One new rider with a funded wallet and the simulator running (`--drivers 20 --speed-kmh 90 --seed 1`): ride 39224 completed (wallet), 39225 completed (cash), 39226 cancelled by the rider after the free window (`free_cancel_seconds` TEMPORARILY set to 0 in SQL, restored to 120 right after: checked), 39227 cancelled by the rider inside the window, 39228 cancelled by its driver (logged in as `sim-driver-008`), 39229 NO_DRIVER_FOUND (pickup at the south west corner of the city). `GET /rides/history` returned 6 rows, and EVERY field of every row matched my own SQL (0 differences; the SQL computed the last event time, the `COALESCE`s, the `->>'cancelled_by'`, an EXISTS on payments and the rider's own rating on its own). A worked row, ride 39224: `distance_m` 4267 and `duration_s` 201 (the tracked values; the estimate was 4366 and 354), `final_fare` 10790, `payment_method` wallet, `driver_name` Sim Driver 005, `cancelled_by` null, `has_receipt` true, `my_rating` 4, `can_rate` false (already rated), `ended_at` 09:34:34.880236Z (its last event; `completed_at` is 09:34:34.887307). The same ride on the driver's list: `fare` 10790, `platform_fee` 2158 (20 percent, half up), `driver_earning` 8632. Other rows: 39226 `final_fare` 3000 with a receipt, 39227 and 39228 `final_fare` 0 with `has_receipt` false (the page says "No receipt (no charge)"), 39229 `final_fare` null, no driver. The five drivers' lists also matched SQL (fare, fee, earning, `cancelled_by`, distance, time, rating, `ended_at`). Filters: `status=COMPLETED` gave 2 rows, `CANCELLED` 3, `NO_DRIVER_FOUND` 1, `IN_PROGRESS` 422 `Only finished trips are listed`, `BOGUS` 422, `limit=0` and `51` 422, a naive time 422, `until` before `since` 422 `until must be after since`. Windows with `Z` times: `since=<ride 39227 request time>` gave 39229, 39228, 39227 (inclusive), `until=` the same instant gave the three older ones (exclusive). Paging with `limit=2` and `before_id` gave [39229, 39228], [39227, 39226], [39225, 39224], every id once. A second rider saw `[]`. The raw responses had no `otp`, no `1234`, no `@` and no comment (rider rows), and the driver responses had nothing about the rider.

**Rating consistency by hand.** Ride 39225 inside the window: `can_rate` true, the rating 201, then `can_rate` false and `my_rating` 5. Ride 39224 for its driver with `completed_at` TEMPORARILY moved (a data edit, restored to its original value afterwards: checked): 8 days ago `can_rate` false and the rating `409 The rating period for this trip has ended`; 6 days 23 hours ago `can_rate` true and the rating 201, then `can_rate` false and `my_rating` 3.

**Query plans** (`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` of the real SQL, which was compiled from the repository functions with literal values; 39190 rides, 90187 events, 1712 payments, 1640 ratings). The busiest rider (395, 1668 rides), first page: 1.5 ms. The planner walks `pk_rides` BACKWARD with a filter on `rider_id` and the status list (20 rows needed 314 read, 294 removed by the filter), so there is no sort; it did not use `ix_rides_rider_id`. The last event is a correlated subquery through `ix_ride_events_ride_id` with a top-1 sort of 2 to 5 rows; the payment test was a hashed subplan over a sequential scan of `payments`; the rating is a nested loop over a sequential scan of `ratings` (28 rows for this caller); the driver name is `pk_drivers` and `pk_users`. A page with `before_id` and a 30-day window: 1.2 ms, `pk_rides` backward with `id < before_id` as the index condition (554 removed by the filter). The busiest driver (255): 0.2 ms, `pk_rides` backward, `uq_ride_earnings_ride_id` and `uq_ratings_ride_id_from_user_id` per row. A rider with 26 rides (683): `Bitmap Index Scan on ix_rides_rider_id`, then a small quicksort, `Index Only Scan using uq_payments_ride_id` and `uq_ratings_ride_id_from_user_id` per row: 0.2 to 0.4 ms. The saved places list: a sort over a sequential scan of a one-row table (the planner will use `ix_saved_places_user_id_id` when the table is larger). What would hurt at much larger sizes: the backward walk over `pk_rides` reads every ride (of every rider) between the wanted rows, so a person whose rides are far apart in id (or a long list with a status filter) makes it read a large part of the table; the sequential scans of `payments` and `ratings` are choices of the planner at this size and become per-row index lookups when the tables grow. A `(rider_id, id)` and a `(driver_id, id)` index would make every case an ordered index scan with no sort and no filter (not added: the rides table is out of scope). **Statements per request** (the statement-count test): a rider's page is 2 statements (the caller's user row and the page) for 5 rows and for 50 rows, a driver's page is 5 (the user row, the driver with its user and vehicle, and the page).

**Mutation checks.** Each of the 17 changes made at least one test fail (run on `test_history.py` and `test_saved_places.py`, reverted afterwards, the app compared with a copy): (a) active rides listed too, (b) other riders' rides listed, (c) status filter ignored, (d) `before_id` inclusive (first run: the suite HUNG, see Bugs hit; after the fix the test fails), (e) the estimated distance always used, (f) the rating window ignored in `can_rate`, (g) an existing rating ignored, (h) the other side's rating read, (i) the cap skipped, (j) the owner lock skipped in create, (k) a case-sensitive label check, (l) the bounds check skipped, (m) the driver role allowed to create, (n) rename of another rider's place, (o) the trip code in a row (my first version of (o) was a syntax error and did not count; redone), (p) the rider's name in a driver row, (q) `/rides/history` declared after `/rides/{ride_id}`.

**Tests.** 145 new tests (87 in `test_saved_places.py`, 58 in `test_history.py`), no existing test changed. Full suite twice on the final code: 910 passed (422 s and 404 s), which is the 765 before plus the 145 new ones.

**Stress and chaos on the final code** (admin `m63-admin@example.com`; the simulator stopped except for the fleet run; every run exit 0, I1 to I24 clean in all, zero stuck rides): `--sequential` control (drivers, 5 rounds) 0; `--scenario drivers --rounds 20` 0 (offers 60 of 60, no lost matches); `--scenario riders --rounds 10` 0; fleet (30 simulator drivers at 30 km/h, 3 rounds) 0 (60 rides, 23 NO_DRIVER_FOUND, not all of them); chaos seed 1, seed 2, seed 3 with two processes, and seed 4 with `docker compose restart backend` at about 35 s and `--tolerate-5xx` (0 5xx answers, 45 connection errors during the restart): all CHAOS CLEAN with `money_view_mismatch`, `rating_view_mismatch`, `admin_view_mismatch`, `saved_view_mismatch` and `history_view_mismatch` all 0; `--scenario payments --riders 20 --rounds 5` PAYMENTS CLEAN (100 top-ups, 1300 webhook deliveries: 100 processed, 1100 duplicate, 100 ignored; 15 bad signatures refused); `--scenario ratings --riders 20 --rounds 5` RATINGS CLEAN (200 ratings, 400 duplicate answers); `places` with 8 riders (545 creates, 600 limit answers, 200 name-taken answers, 40 winning renames, 200 delete-against-create races), the same on two processes (585 creates), and with 20 riders (1348 creates, 1500 limit answers, 500 name-taken answers, 100 renames, 500 races): PLACES CLEAN. The chaos saved-place numbers: seed 1 had 134 actions (121 ok, 13 name-taken 409), 64 places at the end and 43086 history rows compared; seed 2 170 actions, 85 places, 43544 rows; seed 3 (two processes) 148 actions, 80 places, 44000 rows; seed 4 (restart) 160 actions, 75 places, 44440 rows. **The history cross-checks and the saved-place actions did run; the limit of 10 was never reached in a chaos run (0 limit answers in all four) and no 404 for a vanished place happened**, so the cap and the races are covered by the `places` scenario and the tests, not by chaos. The `places` burst numbers (one round of 8 riders): 200 creates at once took about 1.1 s with a median latency of 0.63 s (they queue on 8 owner rows, 25 per row); the same label in different cases (48 requests) about 0.37 s; the rename pair (16 requests) 0.14 s; a delete against a create (16 requests) 0.12 s. With 20 riders: 500 creates 4.1 s (median 1.4 s). After the runs the stress riders had no saved places left (checked with SQL: 0), and the dev `invariants.sql` was clean.

**Headless pages** (jsdom, see above; all the checks printed PASS). Rider: the nav has three buttons, Ride is `aria-current`; the wallet stays in every view; My trips requested the list once when it opened, on a filter change and on Refresh and not once during 7.5 s of polling; the six rows of the history rider read as expected (the NO_DRIVER_FOUND row has no money line and no driver; free cancellations say "No charge. You cancelled." and "No receipt (no charge)"; the driver cancellation says "The driver cancelled"; the fee row "Cancellation fee ₹30.00" with the driver name; the wallet row "Paid ₹107.90 from your wallet." and "You rated ★★★★☆"); Open on the wallet trip landed in the Ride view with the receipt number `RCPT-00039224`, the driver, the fare, the timeline, "You rated this trip ★★★★☆ (4 of 5)", Back to my trips and Print receipt, and the requests were exactly `/rides/39224`, `/events`, `/driver`, `/receipt`, `/rating`; Back reloaded the list; opening a fee trip then a free cancellation showed the fee note and receipt and then no receipt (no stale receipt); "Request a new ride" cleared the Back button; returning to the Ride view called `invalidateSize({animate:false, pan:false})` once and `setView` never. Saved places: the save buttons are disabled until the point is set; Home and Work saved and listed in the quick-pick and in the Saved places view ("2 of 10 places saved"); after a reload both were still there; Work -> Pickup and Home -> Drop-off set the points with the saved addresses, wrote the inputs, called `setView(..., 16, {animate:false})` twice and requested the estimate; rename `Home` to `home` accepted, to `Work` showed the name-taken message with the form kept open, polling did not touch the typed text, Cancel closed it, Delete removed one after the confirm; saving until the limit showed "You can save up to 10 places. Delete one first." and the label `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` was plain text in the quick-pick and the list (no `img` element, no alert). A rider and a driver in one flow: the rider rated a trip from My trips ("Rate this trip" -> Open -> five stars and a comment containing `<b>` shown as text -> Back -> "You rated ★★★★★"); the driver's row had fare, platform fee, "You earned ₹64.00", "(cash)" and "Rate this rider" and nothing about the rider, Open showed "You earn ₹64.00 (₹80.00 fare minus ₹16.00 platform fee)." from the row and the rating form, and the driver rated the rider; with the driver in My trips a new offer showed the offer panel with "Respond within 14 seconds", Accept worked from there, the banner read "You have a ride in progress: Drive to the pickup point.", Open was refused with "Finish your current ride or answer your offer first."; the rider in My trips saw "You have a ride in progress: Your driver is on the way.", Open was refused with "Finish or cancel your current ride first.", the banner changed to "Your driver has arrived..." by itself, "Go to ride" showed the ride, and the driver's return to Drive re-measured the map. A stress driver with hundreds of trips: Load more gave 20, 40, 60 rows with no repeated row and Load more stayed visible; opening the oldest loaded trip showed "You earn ₹180.77 (₹225.96 fare minus ₹45.19 platform fee)." from the row; returning to Drive called `invalidateSize` once with `animate:false, pan:false` and `setView` never. **Not run, left for you:** a real browser (Leaflet drawing, pan and zoom, grey areas after returning to the Ride view, the Network tab, the print preview of the receipt, phone width, the focus after Rename) and the click-through steps that depend on them.

**Regression.** `simulator.py --drivers 30 --speed-kmh 90 --seed 1` with 10 stress riders requesting at the same instant: all 10 rides COMPLETED (9 within 7 minutes, the last a minute later). The full suite is in the Tests paragraph. `git diff --stat` shows no change to `simulator/simulator.py`, `simulator/fake_stripe.py`, `frontend/admin`, `frontend/shared` or `docker-compose.yml`; `/rider/`, `/driver/`, `/admin/`, the shared files and `/docs` answer 200 and `/health` 200 (`/` answers 404 as before: there is no root page).

## Observability results

All numbers from 2026-10-10, one development machine, one uvicorn process (`--reload` for the dev backend), Postgres, Redis, OSRM and the other containers on the same host.

**One ride from log to SQL.** Ride 44125 (one of the 10 rides of the regression run, 30 simulated drivers, `--speed-kmh 90 --seed 1`, all 10 completed) with the second `jq` filter of the README, which joins on the `request_id` of the ride's transitions (the creation and the accept have no `ride_id` in their path). The five `ride_transition` lines are in the same order and count as the five `ride_events` rows; each log line is a few milliseconds after its row's `created_at`, because it is written after the commit.

```
12:25:53.683  Surge snapshot computed: 7 zones, 0 above 1.0x, ...        (request 25bd3b85)
12:25:54.158  ride_transition  none -> REQUESTED                          (request 25bd3b85, user 715 rider)
12:25:54.158  Offer 17929 created: ride 44125 to driver 56 ...             (request 25bd3b85)
12:25:54.192  http_request     POST /rides 201                            (request 25bd3b85)
12:26:02.632  ride_transition  REQUESTED -> DRIVER_ASSIGNED               (request 2223eb3b, user 121 driver)
12:26:02.632  Offer 17929 accepted: ride 44125 assigned to driver ...      (request 2223eb3b)
12:26:02.639  http_request     POST /offers/{offer_id}/accept 200         (request 2223eb3b)
12:27:38.608  ride_transition  DRIVER_ASSIGNED -> DRIVER_ARRIVED          (request e12d99b2)
12:27:38.613  http_request     POST /rides/{ride_id}/arrive 200, 8 queries, 21.0 ms, ride_id 44125
12:27:47.609  ride_transition  DRIVER_ARRIVED -> IN_PROGRESS              (request 489fcd95)
12:27:47.614  http_request     POST /rides/{ride_id}/start 200, 8 queries, 22.7 ms, ride_id 44125
12:28:11.630  ride_transition  IN_PROGRESS -> COMPLETED                   (request 518b7dae)
12:28:11.639  http_request     POST /rides/{ride_id}/complete 200, 13 queries, 46.7 ms, ride_id 44125
-- SQL: SELECT coalesce(from_status::text,'none'), to_status FROM ride_events WHERE ride_id = 44125 ORDER BY id
none -> REQUESTED             12:25:52.924
REQUESTED -> DRIVER_ASSIGNED   12:26:02.608
DRIVER_ASSIGNED -> DRIVER_ARRIVED  12:27:38.594
DRIVER_ARRIVED -> IN_PROGRESS  12:27:47.593
IN_PROGRESS -> COMPLETED       12:28:11.594
```

A cancelled ride (41655: `none -> REQUESTED`, `REQUESTED -> CANCELLED`, one `request_id` tying together the surge line, the transition, the offer line and the access line) and a ride ended by the SWEEPER (41657: a manual driver online near a far pickup never answered; 15 s later the lines `ride_transition REQUESTED -> NO_DRIVER_FOUND`, `Offer ... expired` and `Ride ... ended as NO_DRIVER_FOUND` had `component: "sweeper"` and no `request_id`) matched `ride_events` the same way. A pickup far outside the fleet ended NO_DRIVER_FOUND at creation (41656) with the same two lines.

**By hand on the real stack** (`docker compose logs backend --no-log-prefix | jq`). Every line since the restart parsed as JSON; the only plain-text lines are the first three of every start (`INFO:     Will watch for changes ...`, `Uvicorn running on ...`, `Started reloader process`) and `WARNING:  WatchFiles detected changes ...`, written by uvicorn's reloader supervisor before the app is imported. A valid `X-Request-ID` (`my-valid-id-123`) was kept and echoed; one with a space and a missing one got a 32 hex id; every `http_request` line had the matching id. A `?q=secret@example.com` query, an unknown path (`unmatched`), a 422 with `someone@@leaky-domain.org` in the body and a wrong-password login left none of those strings in the logs (grep count 0). The forced 500 (the db container stopped for one login) gave the generic `Internal Server Error` with an `X-Request-ID` header and ONE ERROR line: `status 500`, `exc_type: gaierror`, a frames-only `stack`, no message and no connection string (the only "postgresql" in it is a library file path). The `jq` check of every key against the whitelist found none outside it.

**Privacy scan on the real stack** (`--scenario chaos --seed 1`, simulator off, the backend logs from the start of the run): 7,251 lines, all valid JSON and all INFO (0 WARNING, 0 ERROR); by `msg`: 5854 `http_request`, 946 `ride_transition`, 204 `Saved place N created/renamed/deleted`, 182 `Offer N <verb>`, 31 `Rating N created`, 26 `Ride N ended as NO_DRIVER_FOUND`, 8 `Surge snapshot computed`. `grep -c` counts, all 0: the stress email domain `sim.example.com` and `example.com`, `stress-driver` and `stress-rider`, `password` (any case) and `passw`, the stress password value, `Bearer`, `eyJ`, `otp` (any), `"1234"`, `Stress pickup`, `Stress drop-off`, `Stress saved place`, `Stress rating`, `STR-LIC-`, the plate and vehicle texts (`STRnn`, `Stress Car`, `Grey`), any decimal with 4 or more places, and any `lat`/`lng` key. (The test `test_nothing_personal_reaches_the_logs` makes the same checks on a full flow, including a WebSocket auth, with unique strings.)

**Metrics against the database, by hand** (10 riders at once, short rides, 20 simulated drivers): the delta of `ride_transitions_total` per `(from, to)` equalled my own SQL over `ride_events` for every pair (10 `none -> REQUESTED`, 10 `REQUESTED -> DRIVER_ASSIGNED`, 10 `DRIVER_ASSIGNED -> DRIVER_ARRIVED`, 10 `DRIVER_ARRIVED -> IN_PROGRESS`, 11 `IN_PROGRESS -> COMPLETED` because an earlier 5 km ride finished in the window); `POST /rides` 201 rose by 10 and 10 rides were created; `rides_active` (all 0), `offers_pending` (0) and `drivers_online` (20) equalled SQL and `redis-cli --scan` (20 presence keys) after the 5 s refresh. The simulator opens no WebSocket, so `ws_connections` was tested with sockets held open by a script: 4 and 3 on the two processes, equal to the sockets in `/proc/net/tcp` inside the container (the host `ss` cannot see them; 6 established on port 8000 are the 4 WebSockets plus 2 HTTP keep-alive connections of the simulator and curl). Pool gauges while 50 concurrent requests ran: `db_pool_checked_out` rose to 9 with `overflow` 5 and fell back; it never exceeded `size + overflow`. After 200 distinct ride ids and 1000 random unknown paths the series count of `http_requests_total` went from 25 to 26 (the `unmatched` route).

**Two processes** (a second uvicorn on :8001, 4 and 3 WebSockets held, 6 rides through each port): the SUMMED transition deltas equal SQL (12 of each of the five kinds); process 8000 counted the 6 creations and all 48 later transitions (the simulator talks to it), process 8001 only its 6 creations; `drivers_online` was 20 and `offers_pending` 0 in BOTH processes (equal, not to be summed); each process's `ws_connections` covered only its own sockets.

**Prometheus and Grafana** (`docker compose --profile observability up -d`, images pulled by tag): `promtool check config` is valid; the backend target is `up` with the token read from the file; Grafana `/api/health` ok, anonymous access 401, `admin/admin` 401, the datasource health OK, the dashboard `ridehail` provisioned. Every one of the 22 panel expressions answers `success`; the empty or NaN ones are legitimate: `histogram_quantile` over a route with no traffic in the window gives NaN (latency p50/p99 lines of idle routes, external calls when no OSRM, Nominatim or Stripe call happened in the last 5 minutes), and 5xx / observability errors / WebSocket closes are 0 or flat in a healthy run. Values against direct computation: `http_requests_total{GET,/auth/me,401}` 53 in Prometheus and 53 in `/metrics` after a burst of 50; the p95 of that route 0.004936274509803921 in Prometheus and exactly the same in my own bucket interpolation; `increase(ride_transitions_total[1m])` CANCELLED 3.27 and REQUESTED 4.36 for 3 and 4 events in SQL (PromQL extrapolates a 55 s window to 60 s, factor 1.09); `rides_active` DRIVER_ASSIGNED 1 equal to SQL; `drivers_online` 1 equal to redis; the pool gauges equal to `/metrics`. Ports 9090 and 3000 listen on 127.0.0.1 only. Restarting Prometheus kept its data (79 samples of `drivers_online` in the last 30 minutes before, 80 after, the oldest from before the restart). With `GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` empty, compose prints no warning (the file uses `${GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD:-}`, not `:?`, which would stop every compose command including the plain stack) and `docker compose config --services` is unchanged, but the grafana container exits 1 with `GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD is not set in .env`. **`docker compose --profile observability down` does NOT leave the default stack untouched:** it removed ALL containers and the network of the project (db, redis, backend, osrm, too); only the named volumes survived, and the Redis container, which has no volume, lost its ephemeral state. To stop only the new services use `docker compose --profile observability rm -sf prometheus grafana` (the README says so).

**Overhead and volume.** 1000 sequential authenticated `GET /auth/me`, the previous commit (a `git worktree`) and the new code each in a container of the same image with the same environment, alternating three times: median 2.68 ms before (2.68, 2.72, 2.65) and 2.76 ms after (2.74, 2.75, 2.80), +0.08 ms or about 3 percent; p95 3.25 ms before (3.10, 3.61, 3.04) and 3.31 ms after (3.37, 3.41, 3.15). Acceptable: the cost per request is two timer reads, a dict, one JSON line and a few metric updates, and the SQL events add two `perf_counter` calls per statement. At higher load it grows with the request rate, not with the payload: at 1000 requests per second that is about 80 ms of CPU per second (8 percent of one core) before the cost of writing 300-byte lines to stdout and of Docker's `json-file` driver, which is the larger part. **Log volume (idle fleet, only pings and offer polls, one line per request at INFO):** 20 drivers 20.1 lines/s (357,854 bytes/minute, about 20 MB/hour), 100 drivers 100.4 lines/s (1,787,810 bytes/minute, about 102 MB/hour), 200 drivers 121.9 lines/s (2,177,825 bytes/minute, about 124 MB/hour; the single process was saturated, the simulator's average ping was 1.4 s). Docker keeps 30 MB per service (3 x 10 MB), which is about 84 minutes at 20 drivers and about 17 minutes at 100. One gauge refresh took a median 4.1 ms at 20 drivers, 5.0 ms at 100 and 7.0 ms at 200 (the maximum of five runs was 27 to 30 ms). `/metrics` is about 23 KB (474 lines at the start) and takes about 8 ms.

**Stress and chaos on the final code** (all exit 0, I1 to I24 clean, zero stuck rides, every earlier mismatch counter and `metrics_mismatch` 0): `--sequential` 3 rounds; `drivers` 20 rounds; `riders` 10 rounds; `fleet` 5 rounds with 30 simulated drivers (100 rides, 41 NO_DRIVER_FOUND); `chaos --seed 1 --metrics-token`: transitions metrics 942, SQL 942 (9 kinds), `POST /rides` 201 metrics +418 and the tool saw 418, 30 `http_requests_total` series, `observability_errors_total` +0, `rides_active` equal to SQL; `chaos --seed 2`: 916 and 916, +402 and 402, 30 series; two processes (`--api-url 8000,8001`, seed 1): 948 and 948 summed, +412 and 412, series [30, 18], errors [0, 0]; `chaos --seed 3 --tolerate-5xx` with `docker compose restart backend` after 60 s: `metrics transitions` and `post_rides` were SKIPPED with the reason printed ("a backend process restarted during the run, so its counters began again from 0"), 49 connection errors tolerated, the series, error and `rides_active` checks ran and were clean, CHAOS CLEAN; `payments --webhook-secret ... --riders 20 --rounds 5` PAYMENTS CLEAN; `ratings --riders 20 --rounds 5` RATINGS CLEAN; `places --riders 8 --rounds 5` PLACES CLEAN. The metrics checks only exist in the chaos scenario; the other scenarios ignore `--metrics-token`.

**Mutation checks** (17, each applied alone to the app, the three new test files run, the file restored, the suite green again afterwards): (a) metrics labelled with the raw path: 1 test failed; (b) the query string in the access line: 9; (c) the exception message added to `stack`: 4; (d) transitions counted when the event is flushed (before the commit): 3; (e) pending transitions not cleared at the end of the transaction: 1 (the reused session); (f) all extras logged without the whitelist: 1; (g) any inbound `X-Request-ID` accepted: 5; (h) the token compared with `!=`: 1 (the `compare_digest` spy); (i) `/metrics` without a token: 2; (j) no `ws_connections` decrement on an abnormal close: 1; (k) an unhandled exception counted as status 200: 3; (l) no in-flight decrement on an exception: 1; (m) the Authorization header added to the access line (and to the whitelist): 4 (the whitelist test, the privacy scan, the spy test); (n) `user_id` bound with `ContextVar.set` of a new dict instead of changing the dict: 1; (o) the request dict in a module-level variable: 1 (the 20 concurrent requests); (p) the sweeper heartbeat set before the tick: 1; (q) a savepoint rollback dropping the whole pending list: 1. The trip-code variant of (m) was not mutated separately; the privacy scan asserts the unique test code never appears.

**Tests.** 986 tests pass (910 before, 76 new: `test_logging.py` 32, `test_metrics.py` 32, `test_observability_hooks.py` 12); the full suite ran twice, 986 passed in 436 s and again in 446 s; dev Postgres and Redis were not touched by the tests (they use `uber_test` and Redis database 1). Nine existing tests changed, all for the same reason: they wrapped a client call in `pytest.raises(RuntimeError)` and relied on the ASGI transport re-raising an unhandled exception in the test, but the middleware now answers it with the generic 500 (so that the response carries the request id and one ERROR line is written); they now assert the 500 and keep their original checks (nothing left behind, the retry works): `test_concurrency.py::test_no_lock_is_left_behind`; `test_earnings.py::test_a_failing_earning_or_ledger_write_undoes_the_whole_completion` (2 parameters) and `::test_a_failing_earning_write_undoes_a_cancellation_too`; `test_payments.py::test_a_failure_while_charging_undoes_the_whole_settlement`, `::test_a_failure_while_charging_a_cancellation_fee_undoes_the_cancel` and `::test_a_failure_in_the_middle_leaves_nothing_behind_and_the_retry_credits`; `test_pricing_rules.py::test_a_failure_halfway_leaves_neither_the_rule_nor_an_audit_row` (2 parameters); `test_ratings.py::test_a_failing_summary_update_undoes_the_rating`; `test_saved_places.py::test_a_request_that_fails_halfway_leaves_no_lock_behind`. No test that used `caplog` needed a change (the `logged` fixture attaches the handler to uvicorn's logger directly; the two `test_edge_cases.py` tests that read the "offer skipped" warning kept passing after the audit fix kept the prefix and the constraint name).

**The UI was not tested in a browser** (no browser tool in this session). What remains for a person: the click-through at the end of the M7.1 request (Grafana panels loading and moving with the simulator and the rider and driver pages, the stopped backend showing gaps, `docker compose logs -f backend | jq` while clicking, and the three pages looking and behaving as before). The pages themselves were not changed (`git diff --stat` shows no change under `frontend/`) and every page answers 200.

## Load test results

Full tables and the method are in `loadtest/RESULTS.md`; this is the summary. All from 2026-10-10, one machine, one backend process (`--reload`), the generator and the 80-driver fleet on the same host. **Stopped at the baseline: no bottleneck experiment, fix, after-fix run or revert run was done, so the bottleneck is a hypothesis.**

- **Baseline, 3 runs: capacity 100 users, knee 150 users in every run** (core p95 112-130 ms at 100 users, 1847-2156 ms at 150; throughput 143 req/s at 100 users and 202-207 req/s at 150, flat at about 225 req/s up to 400). All correctness checks passed in all three runs; no step was INVALID (Locust used 2 to 26 percent of a core).
- **Four signals for "the single event loop is the limit":** backend container at 102 percent of one core from 150 users with a constant 3.8 ms of CPU per request (total server throughput flat at 264-270 req/s); Postgres idle (about 1 ms per request, mean statement 0.02 ms, 0.3 connections active against 10.7 idle in transaction); the extra time is outside the database (`GET /wallet` 57 ms to 775 ms on the server while its database time stays at 26 to 30 ms); event loop lag over 10 ms in 0.6 percent of wake-ups at 10 users, 20 percent at 100 and 58-65 percent from 150. **Not ruled out:** the pool (15 connections) is full from 100 users with 236 to 576 requests in flight; no pool-size, `LOG_LEVEL` or cProfile experiment was run.
- **Probes (20 users, fleet running):** health 449 req/s; `/rides/active` 236; `/wallet` 197; `/saved-places` 235; `/rides/history` 150; `/admin/surge` 272; `/rides/estimate` 41; `/admin/live` 64; `/admin/stats` 45; login 6 req/s (p50 1.8 s: the argon2 hashing, about 110 ms of event loop per login).
- **Harness validation:** per-route request counts equal the server's `http_requests_total` (0.00 percent on 15 routes); percentiles equal Locust's within 3 percent on the aggregate (the p99 of 264 samples differs by one rank); the Locust CPU at 100 users was 14 percent; the same seed gives the same trips in different runs; a 10 s Postgres stop shows up as 99 percent errors in 12 ms and ends the run through the collapse rule 9.9 s later; a 10 s Redis stop shows up as 2 to 6 s slow reads (see the bugs log) and a failed `observability_errors_total` check; the lag monitor adds nothing measurable (median `/health` 3.28 ms without, 3.04 ms with, noise 2.6-4.3 ms).
- **Not done:** the after-fix and revert runs, the mutation checks (a) to (g) of the brief, the regression list (stress scenarios on the dev backend), the browser check of the Grafana panel (no browser tool). The `pytest loadtest` and the backend suite (990 tests) pass.

## Decisions log

One line per decision: milestone, what was chosen, what was rejected, why.

| Milestone | Chosen | Rejected | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Setup | Layered backend (routers, services, repositories, utils), plain functions, plain HTML/CSS/JS frontend | Frontend framework, ML layer | Learning focus: backend, real-time, concurrency |
| M0.1 | Repo root is `uber/` (not `uber-clone/`); built in place | Nested `uber-clone/` folder | The project folder already exists and holds CLAUDE.md |
| M0.1 | `postgis/postgis:16-3.4`, `redis:7-alpine`, `python:3.12-slim` | `latest` tags | Reproducible builds |
| M0.1 | Compose mounts `./backend` at `/app` and `./frontend` at `/frontend`; `main.py` serves `/frontend` | Serving `../frontend` by relative path | Container only sees mounted paths; same code works in the container |
| M0.1 | `/health` checks each dependency in its own try/except and returns `status: "error"` plus 503 if either fails | Failing on the first error | Reports both dependencies even when one is down |
| M0.2 | Lat/lng are plain `Float` columns; PostGIS extension is enabled by the migration but unused | `geometry` columns, geoalchemy2 | Driver locations live in Redis GEO and distances are computed in Python/Redis; avoids a dependency |
| M0.2 | Enums are Python enums stored as `VARCHAR` (`native_enum=False`); enum names equal values | Native Postgres enum types | Adding a value later needs no enum-type migration |
| M0.2 | Money columns are `Integer` in minor units (paise) | Float, Numeric | No rounding errors; matches the project rule |
| M0.2 | `MetaData` naming convention (`ix_`, `uq_`, `ck_`, `fk_`, `pk_` prefixes) | Postgres auto-generated names | Predictable constraint names for later migrations |
| M0.2 | Alembic `include_object` ignores any database table that is not one of our models | Default autogenerate | The PostGIS image ships `spatial_ref_sys`, `tiger.*`, `topology.*`; unfiltered, autogenerate wrote `drop_table` for ~40 of them |
| M0.2 | Migration ids are manual and readable (`0001`, file `0001_initial_schema.py`) via `--rev-id`; downgrade leaves the postgis extension | Hash-only ids | Readable ordering |
| M1.1 | JSON body for `/auth/login` | OAuth2 password form | Frontends send JSON everywhere; Swagger's Authorize still works via `HTTPBearer` (paste a token) |
| M1.1 | Admins are created only by `create_admin.py` | An admin role on `/auth/register`, a bootstrap endpoint | No API path can ever create an admin; `RegisterRequest.role` is `Literal["rider","driver"]` |
| M1.1 | Only rider and driver can self-register | Any role | Same reason as above |
| M1.1 | `get_current_user` calls `repositories/users.py` directly | Routing it through a service | Dependency with no business rule; a service would be a pass-through. Exception applies only there |
| M1.1 | Wrong email and wrong password return the same 401 body; unknown email still runs an argon2 verify against a dummy hash | Different messages, or returning early for unknown email | Attacker cannot tell which part failed, by message or by response time |
| M1.1 | `HTTPBearer(auto_error=False)` and our own 401 | Default `auto_error` | One 401 response with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` for every auth failure, including a missing header; Swagger still shows Authorize |
| M1.1 | Added `get_by_phone` to the users repository | Catching the unique-constraint error | Needed for the 409 on duplicate phone; explicit check keeps the error message clear |
| M1.1 | Pinned: PyJWT 2.15.1, argon2-cffi 25.1.0, email-validator 2.3.0 | Unpinned | Current stable at 2026-10-08 |
| M1.2 | One vehicle per driver, enforced by a unique constraint on `vehicles.driver_id` (`uq_vehicles_driver_id`) | Checking only in the service | The constraint is the real protection against two simultaneous requests; the service check just gives a clear message |
| M1.2 | `vehicle_type` is always set to `economy` by the server; not in `VehicleCreate` | A client-supplied type | Single ride type for now; no way to claim a type that has no pricing rule |
| M1.2 | Plates are normalized before storing and checking: uppercase, spaces and hyphens removed. `VehicleCreate` requires at least one letter or digit | Storing plates as typed | `ka 01-ab 1234` and `KA01AB1234` are the same plate; the pattern stops `---` becoming an empty plate |
| M1.2 | Approving a driver requires a vehicle (409 otherwise). Rejecting does not | Allowing approval of a profile alone | An approved driver must be able to take a ride |
| M1.2 | A rejected driver can be approved again by an admin | One-way rejection | Admin may change their mind; no re-submission flow exists yet |
| M1.2 | `lazy="raise"` on `Driver.user` and `Driver.vehicle`; driver queries eager load both with `selectinload` and `populate_existing` | Default lazy loading | Async cannot lazy load, so a forgotten eager load fails loudly instead of `MissingGreenlet`. `populate_existing` refreshes a driver already in the session after a flush or commit (new `created_at`, new `vehicle`) |
| M1.2 | `IntegrityError` on flush or commit of a profile or vehicle is rolled back and returned as 409 | Letting it surface as 500 | Two simultaneous requests can both pass the check; the second hits the unique constraint |
| M1.2 | Admin router uses a router-level `require_role("admin")` dependency; the approve/reject handlers do not need the admin user | Passing the admin into every handler | No audit of who approved yet, so the user is unused |
| M1.3 | One `ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` dict and one `change_ride_status()`; every other path (arrive, start, complete, cancel, and in M2.4 the system's assignment in `create_ride`) calls it | Per-endpoint status checks | One place defines the rules, so the audit trail and the rules cannot drift apart. `create_ride` is the one exception (no previous status) |
| M1.3 | Every transition loads the ride with `FOR UPDATE` (row lock per ride) | Optimistic checks, or no locking | Two simultaneous requests on one ride serialize: the second sees the new status and gets 409. Verified: the simultaneous-cancel test fails 3 of 3 runs without the lock |
| M1.3 | A ride you are not part of returns 404, same as an unknown id; wrong role returns 403 | 403 for other people's rides | Does not reveal which ride ids exist |
| M1.3 | A driver cancelling ends the ride (CANCELLED); it does not return to REQUESTED | Re-opening the ride for matching | State machine has no backward edge; a re-request is a new ride (rematching can be added with M3.3 if wanted) |
| M1.3 | `POST /admin/rides/{id}/assign` as a temporary stand-in for matching | Waiting until M2.4 to test full rides | Lets a ride be clicked through every state. Removed in M2.4, together with `assign_driver` and `AssignDriverRequest`: matching replaces it and nothing can use it any more |
| M1.3 | Tests use a separate `<db>_test` database with `NullPool`, a guard on the name, and truncation before each test | Rolling back a transaction per test, or the dev database | Rollback-per-test cannot test simultaneous requests (they need separate committed connections); the name guard stops tests wiping dev data |
| M1.3 | Tests skip trivial CRUD and the M1.1 / M1.2 endpoints | Full coverage | Per the working agreement: test state machine, matching, concurrency, payments |
| M1.3 | Deliberately no protection against one driver on two rides or one rider with two rides under simultaneous requests: only the plain check in `create_ride` and, since M2.4, the plain availability query in matching | A lock or unique index now | M4.1 reproduces the driver double-assignment on purpose. **Reproduced in M4.1:** I2 (two active rides on one driver) in 20 of 20 rounds and I3 (two active rides for one rider) in 10 of 10 rounds, see "Baseline for M4.2". **Fixed in M4.2** (row locks on the driver and the rider) |
| M1.3 | Pinned test packages: pytest 9.1.1, pytest-asyncio 1.4.0, httpx 0.28.1 | Unpinned | Current stable at 2026-10-08 |
| M1.4 | Session in `sessionStorage` (per tab) | `localStorage` | Rider, driver, and admin can be logged in at once in three tabs of one browser |
| M1.4 | Poll every 3 s with `setInterval` (skipped while a previous refresh or a button action is running; not paused in background tabs) | WebSockets now | WebSockets come in M3; polling lets a ride be clicked through today. Background tabs keep polling because several tabs are used for testing |
| M1.4 | Minimal admin page (drivers table, approve/reject; the assign form was removed in M2.4) built now | Waiting for M6.2, or using curl | Without it, drivers cannot be approved and rides cannot be assigned in the browser |
| M1.4 | API data goes into the page with `textContent` or `createElement` only, never `innerHTML` | `innerHTML` templates | Names and addresses are user input (checked with a rider and driver named `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>`) |
| M1.4 | `render()` never touches inputs; forms are only reset by their submit handler after a successful login or register | Re-rendering whole sections | Polling re-renders every 3 s and would erase what the user is typing (checked: 10 s of polling keeps a half-filled form) |
| M1.4 | Ride coordinates were typed by hand (prefilled placeholders). Replaced by the map and place search in M2.1 | A map or geocoder | Map and Nominatim arrived in M2.1 |
| M1.4 | Admin table is only redrawn when its data changed | Redrawing on every poll | A redraw between mouse down and mouse up on Approve would swallow the click |
| M1.4 | Thrown API errors carry `.status` | Matching on message text | Pages need to tell a 404 (no ride, no profile) from a real failure |
| M1.4 | No backend change: `main.py` already mounted `frontend/` with `html=True` | Editing the mount | Nothing to fix |
| M2.1 | Backend proxy for Nominatim: the browser never calls it; the backend sends our User-Agent, caches, and rate-limits | Browser calling Nominatim directly | The public server requires an identifying User-Agent, caching, and 1 request per second, none of which can be enforced from many browsers |
| M2.1 | Search runs only on Enter or the Search button (one request per search) | Search-as-you-type, autocomplete | The public Nominatim usage policy forbids autocomplete |
| M2.1 | 1 request per second limiter is a Redis `SET places:slot 1 NX PX 1100`; a busy slot returns 429 with `Retry-After: 1` | Queueing or sleeping until the slot frees | Simple, no request is held open, and the rider just presses Search again. 1100 ms leaves a margin over 1 s. Only uncached calls take the slot |
| M2.1 | 24-hour cache for search (normalized: trimmed, lowercased, spaces collapsed) and reverse (key = coordinates rounded to 4 decimals, about 11 m). Empty search results are cached; failures and "no address" are not | Shorter or no TTL, caching failures | Addresses rarely change; repeats cost nothing and do not use the limiter. A failure cached for a day would hide a recovered server |
| M2.1 | Reverse returns the clicked coordinates, not Nominatim's snapped ones, and only the address text is cached | Returning (and caching) the snapped point | The marker must sit where the rider clicked; two clicks 1 m apart share one cache entry but each keep their own coordinates |
| M2.1 | Search is `bounded=1` to the city viewbox; reverse rejects points outside the box with 422 before any call | Unbounded world-wide search | One city only; saves calls and keeps results usable |
| M2.1 | City, center, zoom, and bounding box live in `.env` and are served to the frontend through `/places/map-config` | Constants duplicated in JS and Python | One source of truth; changing city is an `.env` edit |
| M2.1 | `call_nominatim(path, params)` is the one allowed private helper: search and reverse both need the slot check, the request, and the error mapping. It holds the service's only try/except (network failures are legitimate) and a new `httpx.AsyncClient` per call | Inlining it twice; a shared client | Used in two places, calls no other helper. A per-call client is fine at 1 request per second |
| M2.1 | Markers are `L.circleMarker` with fixed-string tooltips ("Pickup", "Drop-off"); no address or API data is ever passed to Leaflet | Default pin markers with popups showing the address | Leaflet renders tooltip and popup content as HTML, and addresses come from OpenStreetMap and from users. Default pins also need image files from the CDN |
| M2.1 | Leaflet 1.9.4 from unpkg with SRI hashes (`integrity` + `crossorigin`), script before the module script | Unpinned CDN, a vendored copy, npm | Pinned and verified, with no build step. The leafletjs.com quick-start page now documents 2.0 alpha and no longer lists the 1.9.4 hashes, so the hashes come from Leaflet's docs at the "Update docs release 1.9.4" commit (`7c0f675`) and equal what we computed from the files on unpkg and jsdelivr |
| M2.1 | The map is created the first time its section is visible, then `invalidateSize()` once; markers are created, moved, and removed in `render()` by comparing against the wanted points; fit-to-ride happens once per ride id | Creating the map at page load, fitting on every poll | Leaflet cannot size a hidden container. Polling must never move the map or the rider's view |
| M2.1 | Search results are rebuilt only when their state object changes (identity check); search inputs and the radio are written by event handlers, never by `render()` | Rebuilding results on every poll | Same lesson as the M1.4 admin table: a redraw between mouse down and mouse up swallows the click, and polling must not wipe typing |
| M2.1 | The ride request sends the address stored with the chosen point (cut to 255 characters), not whatever is typed in the input afterwards | Sending the input text | The input may hold a half-typed new search; Nominatim `display_name` can exceed the 255-character limit on `POST /rides` |
| M2.1 | No automated tests for the places code or the page | Mocked Nominatim tests | Per the working agreement (tests are for state machine, matching, concurrency, payments). Verified by hand with curl and a Playwright click-through kept outside the repo |
| M2.2 | The backend proxies OSRM; the browser never calls it | Browser calling OSRM directly | One place for errors, timeouts, and the fare; the OSRM port is only published on localhost for debugging |
| M2.2 | OSRM wants longitude first and sends GeoJSON as `[lng, lat]`: `services/routing.py` handles both, once, and the API returns `path` as `[lat, lng]` pairs | Converting in the frontend or in several places | Leaflet and the rest of the app use latitude first; a swapped pair places a route in the wrong country without any error. Verified: the first path point is `[12.97, 77.59]` |
| M2.2 | The server recomputes the estimate at ride creation and never trusts the client; `RideCreate` has no estimate fields and extras are ignored; the estimate is stored on the ride (`distance_m`, `duration_s`, `fare_estimate`) | Trusting the preview the page showed | A client could send any fare. The stored estimate is also what the ride shows later, whatever the pricing rule becomes |
| M2.2 | `create_ride` checks for an active ride (409) before calling `estimate_ride` | Estimating first | A rider who already has a ride gets 409 with no OSRM call, also when OSRM is down. `create_ride` calling `estimate_ride` is a use case calling a use case, not a helper chain |
| M2.2 | Fare arithmetic is integer paise with half-up rounding (`(rate * quantity + half) // divisor`), `fare = max(subtotal, min_fare)`, and no rounding of the total to whole rupees | Floats, or rounding the total up to a rupee | No float drift; the breakdown always adds up to the total exactly (checked by hand in verification and in tests) |
| M2.2 | The economy pricing rule is seeded by a hand-written data migration (`0003`, `op.bulk_insert`, in paise; downgrade deletes the row) | Seeding in app startup or in a script | The rule is part of the database history, so every fresh `alembic upgrade head` has it; tests re-insert it after each truncate |
| M2.2 | Snap radius 300 m (`radiuses=300;300`) and minimum trip distance 200 m | OSRM's default snapping (snaps to any distance), no minimum | A point in a lake or park is rejected instead of silently routed from the nearest road far away; a trip of a few metres is not a ride |
| M2.2 | `NoRoute` and `NoSegment` both become 422 "No route found...", everything else unexpected 502; the body is read whatever the HTTP status | Trusting the status code | Observed on the real OSRM: both are HTTP 400 with the code in the JSON body |
| M2.2 | The OSRM extract is the region clipped to the city box plus 0.05 degrees (osmium `--strategy=complete_ways`) | Clipping exactly to the box, or running the whole region | A route between two points near the edge may use roads just outside the box; the clipped data builds in about a minute and is far smaller |
| M2.2 | OSRM with the MLD algorithm and the car profile; image pinned to `ghcr.io/project-osrm/osrm-backend:v26.10.0-debian` (same tag in `prepare.sh` and compose) | `latest`, CH, the plain tag | MLD was the requested algorithm and prepared the city in about a minute. Since 26.4.1 the registry only has distro-suffixed tags (the plain `v26.10.0` does not exist), so the newest stable release is the `-debian` multi-arch tag; pulled and run on x86_64 |
| M2.2 | One-time `osrm/prepare.sh`; it reads `.env` with grep and cut, never `source`; the `osrm` service is not in the backend's `depends_on` and has no healthcheck | A Dockerfile that builds the data, a healthcheck, making the backend wait | `NOMINATIM_USER_AGENT` has spaces and parentheses and would break `source`. The image has no curl. The API must start when OSRM is down |
| M2.2 | The route path is not stored on the ride; the rider page asks for it once per ride | Storing a polyline column, caching in Redis | Smallest change that works; M3 live tracking may want a cache (open issue) |
| M2.2 | `is_inside_bounds` in `utils/geo.py` (pure, no settings), used by `services/places.py` and `services/rides.py` | Two inline checks | Used in two places, so it qualifies as a utility |
| M2.2 | `routing.get_route` is called through the module (`from app.services import routing, pricing`) | `from ... import get_route` | Tests replace one attribute and every caller sees the fake |
| M2.2 | Every view change from code in the rider page is not animated (`animate: false`) | Leaflet's default animation | Leaflet silently ignores a view change that arrives during a zoom animation, so an animated result-click zoom swallowed the route fit (see the bugs log) |
| M2.3 | Presence and positions live in Redis only: no Postgres table, no migration | A `drivers.online` column or a location table | Live positions change every few seconds and are worthless after a minute; Postgres keeps durable data only |
| M2.3 | The presence key (30 s TTL, refreshed by every update) is the source of truth for "online"; the GEO set is a possibly stale spatial index, with no cleanup job | Deleting GEO members on expiry (needs keyspace notifications or a sweeper), a sorted set of timestamps | A closed page just stops pinging and the key expires on its own. Readers (M2.4) check the key and may remove members they find stale |
| M2.3 | The GEO member and the key name use `drivers.id` | `users.id` | `rides.driver_id` points to `drivers.id`, so matching can use the member directly |
| M2.3 | Longitude-first is handled once, in `repositories/drivers.py` (`GEOADD`, `GEOPOS`); everything above uses lat, lng | Converting in several places | A swapped pair puts a driver in another country without an error. A test reads `GEOPOS` directly and checks longitude comes first (verified: `GEOPOS` showed 77.59 first, 12.97 second) |
| M2.3 | Going online carries the first location (`POST /drivers/me/online` with `{lat, lng}`) | Going online with no position, then a ping | An online driver always has a position, so the presence key and the GEO member are written together |
| M2.3 | `refresh_location` uses `SET ... XX EX 30`; only if that wrote does it `GEOADD` | `EXISTS` then `SET` | Check and write are one atomic step, so a stray ping (a late request after going offline) can never resurrect an offline driver |
| M2.3 | Going online or pinging requires `approved` (403 with the status); an outside-the-city point is 422 on both | Letting anyone go online, checking only on go-online | Resolves the M1.2 issue "approval gates nothing". A ping can move a driver, so it is checked too |
| M2.3 | An active ride blocks going offline (409) | Allowing it and ending the ride | A driver cannot vanish mid-trip; the ride must be finished or cancelled first. Going offline twice is 200 |
| M2.3 | Rejecting a driver (any status other than approved) removes them from Redis right after the commit | Waiting for the key to expire | An online driver who is rejected must not be matchable for up to 30 more seconds |
| M2.3 | The heartbeat rides on the existing 3 s poll: every `refresh()` reads the presence and, while online, sends a location ping (one ping every 3 s, TTL 30 s) | A second timer, `navigator.geolocation` | One loop, no new timer to keep in step with; ten missed polls are needed before a driver drops off |
| M2.3 | The driver picks their position by clicking the map; no GPS, no reverse geocoding | Browser geolocation, Nominatim | Simulated city and moving the driver by hand is the test tool; no Nominatim call from the driver page |
| M2.3 | Tests use Redis database index 1 (`REDIS_DB`), a flush before each test, and a guard that checks the connected index first | Prefix keys, mocking Redis, flushing dev | Real Redis behavior (XX, TTL, GEO) is what is tested, and `FLUSHDB` on the wrong database would wipe dev data |
| M2.3 | One `RedisError` handler in `main.py` returns 503 `Cache is unavailable` | A plain 500, try/except in each service | Presence is the first feature that cannot work without Redis; the 503 is honest and the rule stays in one place |
| M2.3 | Go online hides while online (one toggle button), and the marker stays after going offline | Showing both buttons always, clearing the marker | One clear action at a time; going back online is a single click |
| M2.4 | Matching runs inside `create_ride`, in one transaction committed once (insert ride and first event, find a driver, one status change, commit) | A separate "match" call after the ride is saved, or committing REQUESTED first | A Redis or OSRM failure partway leaves no ride at all, so there is never a stuck REQUESTED ride. Verified: Redis stopped gives 503 and the row count in `rides` does not change |
| M2.4 | A ride that finds nobody ends as NO_DRIVER_FOUND and `POST /rides` still answers 201; the rider can request again at once | Answering 404 or 409, keeping the ride REQUESTED and waiting | It is a normal terminal ride with its own timeline; NO_DRIVER_FOUND is not in `ACTIVE_RIDE_STATUSES`, so there is no 409 on the retry. A waiting window arrives with offers in M3.3 |
| M2.4 | Straight-line distance from the PICKUP, nearest first, ties go to the lower `drivers.id` (compare `(distance_m, driver_id)`) | Road distance or ETA from OSRM, an arbitrary tie | One cheap Redis call and no OSRM call per candidate; the tie-break makes the result the same every time. Ranking by road ETA is later work |
| M2.4 | The presence key is the truth for "online"; a GEO member without one is stale and is removed (`ZREM`) when matching finds it | A cleanup job, keyspace notifications | No new moving part. The index cleans itself where it is read. Verified: after 35 s the stale member was still there, then a request removed it |
| M2.4 | One Postgres query returns the ids that are approved and have no active ride (`NOT EXISTS` on rides) | One query per candidate, or loading drivers and filtering in Python | One round trip for up to 50 candidates; returns ids only, so no eager loading. One `MGET` does the same for presence |
| M2.4 | `GEOSEARCH` is capped at `COUNT 50` candidates | No cap | Bounds the work of one request. Known cost: with 50 or more busy drivers nearest the pickup, a free driver farther out could be missed (open issue) |
| M2.4 | System-made status changes have a null `actor_user_id` (`change_ride_status` takes `int | None`) | Using the rider's id as the actor | The audit trail says who really acted; the rider did not choose the driver |
| M2.4 | Removed `POST /admin/rides/{id}/assign`, `assign_driver`, and `AssignDriverRequest`; a POST to the old path now answers 405 (the static mount takes it) | Keeping the endpoint | It was a stand-in for matching and can no longer work (a ride is never left REQUESTED). Grep found nothing else using it |
| M2.4 | `find_driver` is a plain function with no lock, queue, `FOR UPDATE`, or Redis reservation, on purpose | Protecting against two requests picking the same free driver now | M4.1 reproduces the double assignment on purpose. Seen here: 2 simultaneous requests with one free driver gave the same driver to both riders in 5 of 5 runs. **Reproduced in M4.1:** the same setup (`--riders 2 --drivers 1`) gave one driver two offers (I1) and, once both were accepted, two rides (I2) in 10 of 10 runs. **Fixed in M4.2:** the same setup gives exactly one offer in 10 of 10 runs |
| M2.4 | The rider page takes the ride from the `POST /rides` answer, not from `/rides/active` | Relying on `/rides/active` as before | A NO_DRIVER_FOUND ride is not active, so `/rides/active` answers 404 and the page would have shown the empty form instead of the finished ride |
| M2.5 | The simulator uses only the public API and the local OSRM: no direct Postgres or Redis access, no imported backend code, haversine duplicated on purpose | Reading Redis for speed, importing `app` code | A fake driver is an ordinary client, so it exercises the same paths as a real one (auth, approval, presence, rides) and breaks when they break. Duplicating 5 lines of haversine keeps the script standalone |
| M2.5 | It runs on the host with only `httpx` (same pin as the backend), in a venv; no Docker service | A compose service, extra libraries, Locust now | Starting, stopping, and `kill -9` are one shell command; the load test is M7.2 and the ride generator M4.1 |
| M2.5 | The fleet lives in a circle (default radius 5 km around the city center), not the whole city box | Spreading drivers over the whole box | Matching only looks 3 km around the pickup; drivers scattered over about 30 km would almost never be near a pickup. Uniform in the circle: `r = radius * sqrt(random())` |
| M2.5 | Driver accounts are deterministic (`sim-driver-NNN`, constant password) and setup skips whatever is done, so reruns reuse the accounts | Random names, a cleanup step, always registering | A rerun costs no duplicates (verified: 5 users after 3 runs) and `--seed` repeats the same start points. The 401 on login is how it detects a missing account |
| M2.5 | Admin credentials come from `--admin-email`/`--admin-password` or `SIM_ADMIN_EMAIL`/`SIM_ADMIN_PASSWORD`; the simulator never creates an admin | A bootstrap admin, a config file, `.env` | Admins can only come from `create_admin.py` (M1.1); the env fallback keeps the password out of the shell history |
| M2.5 | Positions move along OSRM routes at a constant speed (`--speed-kmh` x a per-driver factor 0.8 to 1.2), interpolated by the real time since the last tick; no traffic or slowing at junctions | A fixed distance per tick, road-class speeds | A late tick (a slow backend) still moves the right distance, and the factor stops all drivers moving in step. Verified: within 2 percent of speed x factor |
| M2.5 | A ping every 3 seconds, the same as the driver page's heartbeat (M2.3), each driver's first tick staggered by a random 0 to 3 s, and each tick scheduled from the previous scheduled time | Sleeping 3 s after each tick, all drivers starting together | Ten missed pings are needed to expire the 30 s presence; staggering avoids pings arriving in lockstep; a fixed schedule does not drift |
| M2.5 | What a driver does comes from `GET /rides/active` every tick (the plan is rebuilt when the (kind, ride id) pair changes), not from remembered state | Keeping a state machine in the simulator | A restart resumes a trip from the ride status (verified with `kill -9` while DRIVER_ASSIGNED and IN_PROGRESS), and a rider cancel is noticed the next tick. A 409 on arrive/start/complete is ignored for the same reason |
| M2.5 | The simulator drives whole trips (arrive, wait 2 ticks, start, complete) | Leaving assigned drivers parked | Otherwise an assigned driver stays busy forever and the fleet drains after a few requests, which would make every later test meaningless |
| M2.5 | Pings are clamped to the city box before sending | Sending raw road positions | A road that leaves the bounding box would answer 422 and look like a failed ping; none of the runs saw a 422 |
| M2.5 | Setup is limited to 5 drivers at a time, with a 30 s HTTP timeout | Registering all at once | Login and register hashing (argon2) blocks the backend's event loop (known from M1.1); 150 new accounts in one burst still took 153 s and pushed ping time to 2.5 s (see the open issues) |
| M2.5 | The httpx client uses `keepalive_expiry=2` (below uvicorn's 5 s keep-alive timeout) | The default (5 s) | With equal values a request can land on a connection the server has just closed (`RemoteProtocolError`, seen 8 times in the first 200 driver run, 0 in the later ones). The fix is on the client; the backend is untouched |
| M2.5 | A start point must have a route to the fleet center, and the center must be within 300 m of a road (checked at startup) | Only snapping the start point to a road | A start snapped onto a road that is cut off from the rest of the network left a driver parked forever (seen in the Redis outage check: every wander route answered "no route") |
| M2.5 | No automated tests for the simulator; it is verified by running it | pytest for a dev tool | Per the working agreement. The 18-step run (counts, positions, trips, restarts, outages) is the test |
| M3.1 | The token travels in the first message (`auth`), within 5 seconds; the socket is accepted first | Token in the URL query string, cookie, a one-time ticket endpoint | URLs end up in logs and browser history, and browsers cannot set headers on a WebSocket. Nothing else to build. Anything but a text JSON auth object closes with 4401, no message in time with 4408; tokens are never logged or echoed |
| M3.1 | No `Origin` check | Allow-listing origins | The token is not a cookie, so the browser never attaches credentials by itself and there is no cross-site WebSocket hijacking risk: a foreign page has no token to send |
| M3.1 | Envelope `{type, data}` in both directions; `auth_ok`, `pong`, `error` reserved | Per-message ad-hoc shapes | One shape the frontend can switch on; later milestones only add types |
| M3.1 | The registry is a module-level dict `connections: dict[int, list[WebSocket]]`, no class, with no `await` between the count check and the append | A `ConnectionManager` class, a lock | The event loop only switches at `await`, so synchronous check-then-mutate cannot interleave; a class would add nothing |
| M3.1 | At 5 sockets per user the NEWEST wins: the oldest is closed with 4409 | Rejecting the new connection | A page reload must never be locked out by a dead socket that the server has not noticed yet |
| M3.1 | ONE Redis channel carrying `user_id` in each message; each process filters locally | One channel per user, per-ride rooms | One subscription per process whatever the user count, and no subscribe/unsubscribe churn when sockets come and go. Cost: every process receives every message (open issue) |
| M3.1 | The channel name includes `REDIS_DB` (`ws:events:0` dev, `ws:events:1` tests) | A fixed name | Pub/sub ignores database numbers, so a fixed name would let the test run's events reach the dev backend's sockets and the reverse. Verified: `PUBLISH ws:events:1` returned 0 subscribers while the dev listener was on `ws:events:0` |
| M3.1 | Pub/sub stays fire and forget; REST is the source of truth | Queues, replay, Redis streams, acknowledgements | A lost event is repaired by the client re-fetching state on reconnect (M3.4); a durable queue is a bigger system than this project needs |
| M3.1 | The endpoint opens a short database session just to authenticate (`database.async_session()` called at call time) | `Depends(get_db)` | A request-scoped session would hold a Postgres connection for as long as the socket lives (one connection per socket). Calling it through the module lets the tests replace it |
| M3.1 | `user_from_token(db, token)` in `security.py` is the one place a token becomes a user; `get_current_user` calls it | Duplicating the JWT decode in the socket router | HTTP and WebSocket auth cannot drift apart; HTTP behavior is unchanged (curl and tests re-checked). It catches `jwt.InvalidTokenError` (as before) and `ValueError`, not a bare except |
| M3.1 | Each send runs under `asyncio.wait_for(..., SEND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)`; on failure or timeout the socket is removed and closed | No timeout | A stalled client would otherwise block the listener forever. NOTE: the listener still sends one message at a time, so a stalled client delays everyone by up to the timeout. Accepted on 2026-10-09 (see the open issues); per-socket queue and writer task rejected for now to keep M3.1's structure |
| M3.1 | The listener resubscribes after 1 second and logs once per outage (down, then recovered) | Logging every retry, exponential backoff | One line per outage keeps the log readable; 1 s is fine for a local Redis |
| M3.1 | A malformed channel message is dropped with a warning | Closing the listener, crashing | Whatever someone publishes must never stop delivery to everyone else. I also require `data` to be an object (the spec only listed `user_id` and `type`) |
| M3.1 | Application-level `ping`/`pong` messages | Relying on protocol ping frames | Browsers cannot see or send protocol pings, so a page cannot tell whether the connection is alive. uvicorn still sends protocol pings (20 s) to drop dead peers |
| M3.1 | `--ws-max-size 65536` on uvicorn (compose and Dockerfile) | uvicorn's default of 16 MiB | Our messages are tiny; a 100 KB frame is refused by the library (close code 1009, verified) |
| M3.1 | Tests run the real app under uvicorn on an ephemeral port inside the test's event loop, with `database.async_session` replaced by the `_test` session maker | `TestClient`, mocks | httpx cannot speak WebSocket and TestClient runs the app in another thread and loop, which clashes with asyncpg and the Redis client |
| M3.1 | `lifespan` in `main.py` starts the listener as a task and cancels and awaits it on shutdown | Starting it in the first connection | Events must flow before any socket exists in this process, and the backend must still start with Redis down (the task just retries) |
| M3.1 | Pinned `websockets==17.2` (the version `uvicorn[standard]` already installed) | No new package | Only the tests import it directly, so it is pinned instead of left as an implicit dependency |
| M3.2 | `driver_location` is published from the existing ping endpoint (`update_location`, right after `refresh_location` succeeds), at the cost of one extra Postgres read per ping (`get_active_for_driver`) | A separate publisher task, a Redis cache of the driver-to-ride mapping | No new moving part and no new state to invalidate when a ride changes; the read is one indexed query (see the open issues for the measured cost) |
| M3.2 | The event goes only to `ride.rider_id` and carries only `ride_id, lat, lng, updated_at` | Adding the driver id, name, or user id; also sending it to the driver | The rider needs nothing else, and an event must not carry personal data. Verified with live sockets: the driver's own socket and another rider received nothing, both tabs of the rider did (and a mutation that published to the driver's user id fails 5 tests) |
| M3.2 | Driver details come from a separate `GET /rides/{id}/driver`, not from new fields on `RideResponse` | Adding name, vehicle, and location to `RideResponse` | `RideResponse` is returned by 8 endpoints and its queries stay unchanged; the location lives in Redis, which a ride query should not touch |
| M3.2 | The details endpoint exposes only name, vehicle, and location, and location only while the ride is active (null for COMPLETED and CANCELLED even when the driver is online) | Returning the whole driver (email, phone, license, verification status), or the live position of a finished ride | A rider must not learn where a driver is after the trip, or anything private about them. Checked in the JSON and in a test with a phone number set on the driver |
| M3.2 | A REST fetch gives the first position and the socket gives the updates; an update with an older `updated_at` than the stored one is dropped | Waiting for the next ping after a reload; last write wins | A reloaded page has a marker at once, and a slow REST answer cannot overwrite a newer socket update (`updated_at` has one-second resolution, see the open issues) |
| M3.2 | Smoothing is `requestAnimationFrame` interpolation over `ANIMATION_MS = 3000`, with a 500 m snap, and it respects `prefers-reduced-motion` | CSS transitions or transforms on the marker | CSS transitions fight Leaflet's own zoom and pan transforms and do nothing for SVG circle markers. Measured in headless Chrome at maximum zoom: the largest single-frame step was 1.4 px, the tooltip stayed glued to the circle (0 of 330 frames off by more than 2 px), a click-moved driver 1863 m away showed as ONE jump, and with reduced motion the marker moved only in jumps exactly 3 s apart |
| M3.2 | The animation is time-based (`t = (now - start) / ANIMATION_MS`) | Fixed steps per frame | A tab whose frames paused catches up from the clock. Measured with frames held for 20 s: one jump (about 60 m at maximum zoom), then at the current position about 2 s later (open issue) |
| M3.2 | The drawn marker lags the real position by up to about 3 s | Extrapolating or predicting | A glide between two known positions never shows a place the driver was not at |
| M3.2 | The socket opens once at login and has no reconnect, client ping, or REST fallback | Reconnecting with backoff now | M3.4 owns resilience. Until then a closed socket shows "disconnected (code N). Reload the page to reconnect." (seen with code 1012 after `docker compose restart backend`, and no reconnect after 12 s) |
| M3.2 | The socket code stays in `rider.js` | A shared `ws.js` now | Only the rider page uses a socket; M3.3 moves it when the driver page needs one |
| M3.2 | The driver page gets pickup and drop-off markers only | A route to the pickup, a socket on the driver page | Deferred from M2.3 and M2.4; nothing more was asked. The markers are `interactive: false`: a click on either one moved the driver to within 2 m of the marker's coordinates |
| M3.2 | The map fits once, when the driver marker is first created for a ride; nothing moves or zooms it afterwards | Auto-follow, fitting on every update | A rider who zooms or pans must not be fought by the page. Verified: the map pane's transform was identical 8 s after a pan, while the driver kept moving |
| M3.3 | Offers live in a Postgres table `ride_offers` (status, deadline `expires_at`, `pickup_distance_m`, `responded_at`) | Redis keys with a TTL | Durable, auditable (who was offered what and what they did), and a restart loses nothing. Verified: a backend stopped for 19 s past a deadline expired the offer about 1.5 s after it started again, with no API call |
| M3.3 | One offer at a time, ordered by straight-line distance from the pickup | Broadcasting to several drivers, ranking by ETA | Simple and fair, and it keeps the double-booking race (M4) about drivers rather than about who answered first. ETA ranking is later work |
| M3.3 | The state machine did not change: REQUESTED now means "offers are being tried"; DRIVER_ASSIGNED happens only on accept; NO_DRIVER_FOUND when nobody qualifies or the offers run out | New states such as OFFERED or SEARCHING | Every rule, test, and page that knows the nine statuses keeps working; the offer details live in their own table |
| M3.3 | Matching is recomputed for every offer from live presence and availability (`offer_to_next_driver` runs again after each reject or expiry) | A candidate list fixed when the ride is requested | A driver who went offline or took another ride in the meantime is skipped, and a driver who came online can be reached. Cost: one GEOSEARCH, MGET and Postgres query per offer |
| M3.3 | A driver with a PENDING offer is unavailable (`NOT EXISTS` in `get_available_ids`) | Letting one driver hold several offers | A driver deciding on an offer is busy; this is a plain check, not a lock (see the open issues) |
| M3.3 | Unique (ride_id, driver_id) and a partial unique index on (ride_id) where status is PENDING | Only checking in the service | The constraints are the real protection: a driver can never be offered the same ride twice and a ride never has two open offers, whatever the code does. The index condition is written as `status = 'PENDING'` because the enum is stored by name (checked with `\d ride_offers` and by inserting a second PENDING row, which failed) |
| M3.3 | 15 second timeout and at most 5 offers per ride (constants in `services/matching.py`) | Settings in `.env`, 30 seconds, unlimited offers | Nothing to configure yet; 5 x 15 s is a bounded wait of about 75 s for the rider |
| M3.3 | The ride and its first offer are created in one transaction; NO_DRIVER_FOUND is immediate when nobody qualifies for the first offer | Committing the ride first, offering afterwards | A Redis or OSRM failure leaves no ride, event, or offer (verified: 503/502 and unchanged row counts). A request with nobody in range does not make the rider wait |
| M3.3 | Commands go over REST (accept, reject); the WebSocket carries only small "something changed" events with no personal data; the pages react by calling their REST refresh and keep polling | Accept and reject over the socket; events carrying the offer details | REST is the source of truth, so a missed event is harmless (verified: with PUBLISH blocked the offer was still found by polling). Nothing private can leak through a socket |
| M3.3 | Events are published AFTER the commit, and `events.publish` is best-effort (catches `RedisError`, logs one warning, returns 0) | Publishing before the commit; letting a publish error fail the request | A client that refreshes on an event must see the new data (a test reads the database through a new connection at publish time). A request that already committed must not fail or be undone because Redis could not publish. The only justified try/except there |
| M3.3 | Lock order is the RIDE row first, then the OFFER row, in accept, reject, expiry, and cancel. The first read of an offer before the lock selects only the two ids (columns, not the entity) | Locking the offer first; selecting the entity first | Two paths that lock in the same order cannot deadlock. SQLAlchemy's identity map would return an earlier unlocked copy of the entity for the locked read, so the status check under the lock would be stale; selecting columns keeps the locked read the first load (the other option was `populate_existing`) |

| M4.1 | `--sequential` control run | No control | Proves the tool has no false positives: exit 0 and every second request of a rider gets 409 |
| M4.1 | Exit codes 0 (clean), 1 (violation), 2 (the check did not run: psql, setup, login, no fleet, all NO_DRIVER_FOUND, no snapshot) | 0 whenever the tool ran | A clean result must never be reported when the check did not happen |
| M4.1 | Cleanup works from the database, repeats until empty, and waits 3 s after Ctrl+C or a failure | Cleanup from a list the run remembers | It also clears broken state (a rider with two active rides) and leftovers of a crashed run, and it catches rides that in-flight requests create after the first listing |
| M4.1 | Snapshots only, no history of overlaps; `snap` also makes the (unsnapped) driver positions; `setup_accounts` does one account and callers `gather` them | Sampling thread, timeline analysis; a worker pool inside `setup_accounts` | The allowed function set has no room for helpers, and a snapshot after the burst already shows the broken state because the broken state persists until cleanup |
| M4.2 | Three critical sections, each needs its own lock: **C1** `create_ride` (check "rider has no active ride", then insert; violated I3), **C2** `offer_to_next_driver` (check "driver is available", then insert the offer; violated I1), **C3** `accept` (check "driver has no active ride", then assign; violated I2, and it needs a driver holding two pending offers, which C2 allowed) | One lock around the whole ride request, or a lock on the ride | The decisions are about a person (the rider, the driver), not about a ride: two different rides compete for one driver, and one rider competes with themselves.
