- **M4.2:** two bugs, both in the new test code, none in the backend. (1) **A failing lock-holder test hung the whole suite.** The first versions of tests 6 and 8 opened a separate session that held `FOR UPDATE`, and released it at the END of the test body; when a mutation made the test fail halfway, the open transaction kept the row locked and the next test's `TRUNCATE` (in the autouse cleanup) waited for it forever (a mutation run took 10 minutes until the session was terminated by hand). Fix: the `hold_row` fixture closes every holder session at teardown, and test 8 wraps its requests in `asyncio.wait_for(..., 5)`, so "no lock timeout" fails the test instead of hanging it (mutating `lock_timeout` away used to hang, now it fails). (2) **The F1 holder kill did nothing the first time:** the plan's `pkill -9 psql` does not exist in the db container, and my replacement matched `psql` at the start of the command line while the process is started by its full path (`/usr/lib/postgresql/16/bin/psql`), so the "killed" holder was never killed; found because the script printed nothing after "killing", and fixed by matching `*bin/psql*`. Also found while doing it: killing the psql CLIENT does not release a lock held by a running `pg_sleep` (see F1). A mistake in my own commands: `pkill -f "sleep 600"` matched the shell command that contained that text and ended the run (exit 144). The first delay-50 `drivers` run after each fleet run was diluted by simulator drivers whose presence keys had not expired yet (the tool's warning caught it); rerun after waiting.

## Open issues and TODOs

- **M7.3: the demo recording is not made** (a manual step; the script is in the README). **The Mermaid diagrams in `README.md` were written carefully but never rendered** in this session (no Mermaid tool; GitHub renders them): open the README on GitHub or in a Mermaid-aware preview once and fix any syntax error. No concurrent-rides figure for the load test exists, only users.
- **M7.2: the numbers come from ONE machine** on which the generator, the fleet, Postgres, Redis, OSRM and the backend share 12 cores and 7.2 GB, and Docker networking differs by platform.
- **M7.2: one backend process only** (and in `--reload` mode); multi-worker uvicorn was out of scope, so per-process metrics and sweepers are untested under load.
- **M7.2: the bottleneck is a hypothesis.** The single event loop at 102 percent CPU (3.8 ms per request) fits four signals, but the full pool (15 connections, up to 576 requests in flight) is not ruled out. Next: a cProfile of the loop, `LOG_LEVEL=WARNING` (the access line at INFO for polling traffic has an unmeasured cost) and a larger pool, one at a time; then one fix with a written prediction. Login (argon2 on the event loop, 6 req/s) is the most expensive endpoint but is not part of the steady-state polling load.
- **M7.2: the fleet is simulated and fixed at 80 drivers,** sends fewer requests per driver than the driver page, opens no WebSocket; rider WebSockets, Nominatim and Stripe are not load tested; load users are fake riders with fixed behavior; the database starts empty, so history and admin queries will slow as it grows; Postgres runs at its defaults.
- **M7.2: a slow Redis takes unrelated endpoints down** through the connection pool (see the bugs log). Candidate: do not hold a database session across Redis calls, or time out Redis faster.
- **M7.2: after a step above the knee the system needs minutes to drain** (rides in progress finish on their own: the quiet wait after a full run was 3 to 7 minutes).
- **M7.1: exception messages are not logged,** so some failures are harder to debug: only the class, the frames, and for database errors the SQLSTATE and the constraint remain.
- **M7.1: counters reset when the backend restarts, and a crash between the commit and the hook loses a count.** The chaos check says it was skipped after a restart. Prometheus handles resets (`rate` and `increase`), a restart shows as a drop.
- **M7.1: the registry is per process.** Several uvicorn workers need one scrape target per process; counters are summed, but the gauges that read the database (`rides_active`, `offers_pending`, the pool, `drivers_online`) are the same in every process and must be read with `max` (the dashboard does). Only one target (`backend:8000`) is configured. WebSocket gauges cover the process's own sockets.
- **M7.1: the gauge task reads every online driver position per refresh** (`get_online_positions`: ZRANGE, MGET, GEOPOS): 4.1 ms at 20 drivers, 7.0 ms at 200, linear in the fleet. It could be a `ZCARD` plus presence count; kept for reuse of the existing function.
- **M7.1: every request is logged at INFO with no sampling,** polling included: about one line per driver per second, 20 MB/hour at 20 drivers, 102 MB/hour at 100, against 30 MB kept by Docker per service (about 84 and 17 minutes). Raising `LOG_LEVEL` to WARNING removes the access lines; sampling or a shorter polling would be the next step (M7.2).
- **M7.1: `user_id` in the logs is pseudonymous personal data** kept in the container logs (3 x 10 MB), as are the ids of rides and offers.
- **M7.1: the metrics token is visible in the Prometheus container environment** (`docker inspect`) and is the same as the backend's; there is one shared token, no rotation.
- **M7.1: Prometheus keeps 3 days, the dashboard is hand-maintained JSON (`allowUiUpdates: false`), and there are no alert rules or Alertmanager.** The sweeper panel shows seconds since the last success but nothing pages anyone.
- **M7.1: no tracing, and no request id in WebSocket events, REST calls made by the simulator, or the pages.** A request id exists per HTTP request and per WebSocket connection only.
- **M7.1: Redis latency, Postgres server metrics (locks, connections, cache hit ratio) and container metrics are not measured;** the DB pool and the statement duration seen from the app are.
- **M7.1: the `unmatched` route label merges every 404 that no route answers** (and the frontend mount's 404s), so a flood of probing looks like one route. A `static` label merges all served files.
- **M7.1: offer outcomes are only visible through `offers_pending` and the logs** (no counter by outcome).
- **M7.1: the gauge task logs an ERROR with a stack every 5 seconds per failing block while Postgres or Redis is down** (two lines per refresh during an outage), and the sweeper one per failure streak. Noisy, but bounded by the outage.
- **M7.1: `external_call_duration_seconds` marks an OSRM answer with a 5xx status as `ok` when its body parses as JSON,** because the OSRM site is wrapped around the call and the parse (no existing check raises there); Nominatim and Stripe failures that the code turns into an error count as `error`. A cancelled request (client gone) counts as `error`.
- **M7.1: `ws_closes_total` cannot tell an empty close frame from a dropped connection** (uvicorn gives 1005 for both); both are counted as 1006.
- **M7.1: Grafana's own database and Prometheus data live in named volumes that `docker compose down -v` deletes with everything else** (`down` without `-v` keeps them, but it also removes the whole project's containers, see the results).
- **M7.1: the UI click-through was not run in a browser** (no browser tool in the session).
- **M7.1: dev data added by the verification:** admin `m71-verify-admin@example.com` (id 684) and the riders `m71-rider@example.com`, `m71-batch-N@example.com` and the driver `m71-driver@example.com`; about 100 rides from the stress and chaos runs; the `.env` has `LOG_LEVEL`, `METRICS_TOKEN` and `GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` appended.
- **M6.3: history is ordered by request order (ride id), not by completion time.** A long trip that was requested earlier but finished later sits lower than a short trip requested after it. Windows filter on the request time too.
- **M6.3: legacy rides show less.** Rides settled before M5.1 have a null `final_fare` and no `cancelled_by`, so their rows have no money line, and rides from before M5.2 have no zone. The rows say nothing wrong, they just say less.
- **M6.3: history rows and saved addresses are kept for ever, with no deletion or retention policy, and are personal data** (where a person went, and the addresses they saved). There is no way to delete a trip from history, and the drivers' lists show the pickup and drop-off addresses of ALL their past rides (nothing about the rider).
- **M6.3: a saved place outside the city bounds after a config change** stays in the list but fails with 422 at estimate time, because the bounds are checked when a place is created and not again later.
- **M6.3: opening a past trip asks OSRM for its route again** (`POST /rides/estimate`, the path is not stored), so it shows an error if OSRM is down or the points are now outside the city. The error comes from the first `refresh()`, which then skips the driver, receipt and rating loads until the next poll (the route key is set before the call, as for any ride).
- **M6.3: opening is blocked while the person is busy** (a rider with a ride in progress, a driver with a ride or a pending offer), by design: the finished-ride view shows one remembered ride.
- **M6.3: the history pages do not refresh by themselves** (no polling, no event). A ride that finishes while My trips is open shows after Refresh. And the page forgets which view it was in when it is reloaded (`state.view` is not stored).
- **M6.3: the saved places cap is enforced only by the lock.** The database cannot count, so a code path that inserts a place without `lock_owner` could exceed 10 (I24 would show it, and the unique index would still protect the labels).
- **M6.3: the lock on the user row is shared.** `FOR UPDATE` on `users` is the same row `users.lock` takes for `create_ride`, and by Postgres lock rules it also conflicts with the `FOR KEY SHARE` that every insert referencing the user takes (ride events with the user as actor, ratings, wallet entries, rides, top-ups). Nothing else takes it as a blocking `FOR UPDATE` (checked with grep: `users_repo.lock` in `create_ride`, `lock_owner` here). So a saved-place request and a ride, rating or settlement of the same rider can wait for each other for the few milliseconds one holds the row. `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` would not conflict with the key-share locks; the spec said `FOR UPDATE` and this was not measured.
- **M6.3: the trip lists have no summary totals, search or export,** and the driver's totals stay in the M5.4 earnings section.
- **M6.3: `ended_at` and `completed_at` are redundant** for COMPLETED rides (the last event is the completion); for cancelled rides only `ended_at` exists.
- **M6.3: the history queries rely on `pk_rides` and `ix_rides_rider_id` / `ix_rides_driver_id` and sort per person.** Measured with `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` (see "History and saved places results"): a person with few rides gets a bitmap scan on the owner index and a tiny sort; the busiest riders and drivers get a backward walk over `pk_rides` with a filter on the owner, which reads many rows when the person's rides are far apart in id. A `(rider_id, id)` and a `(driver_id, id)` index would make both cases an ordered index scan with no filter and no sort; they were not added (the rides table is out of scope for this milestone).
- **M6.3: the driver page's finished-ride view draws no pickup or drop-off markers** (only active rides and offers get them), so an opened past trip shows the addresses and the timeline but not the points. The rider page shows both.
- **M6.3: a pending or rejected driver can read their own history through the API but the page shows My trips to approved drivers only.**
- **M6.3: the chaos run rarely reaches the limit of 10 saved places** (11 different names, favoured creates, but a rider must hold 10 at once) and never answers 404 for a vanished place (each rider manages only its own places); the `places` scenario is what covers the cap and the races.
- **M6.3: the browser was not available,** so the rider and driver pages were exercised headlessly (see "History and saved places results"); real Leaflet, phone width, the print preview and the Network tab were not looked at.
- **M6.2: approving and rejecting drivers is not audited and has no reason field.** Nothing records who did it or why (the pricing editor has an audit table, driver status does not).
- **M6.2: admins cannot cancel or reassign a ride, refund, manage riders (ban or suspend), adjust a wallet from the page (the API exists), or create other admins** (still `create_admin.py`).
- **M6.2: a direct SQL edit of a pricing rule bypasses the version and the audit by design:** it changes neither, so I23 cannot see it, and an admin with the form open would not get a 409 for it.
- **M6.2: a rule edit applies to rides already requested or in progress at completion** (the whole rule is re-read: base fare, per km, per minute, minimum fare), capped at 150 percent of the ride's own estimate. A rider can be charged more than the fare they were quoted, up to the cap, because an admin edited the rates in between.
- **M6.2: the surge steps, the minimum demand, the 150 percent cap, the matching radius and the offer timeout are constants** and cannot be edited (only the surge cap and the rates can).
- **M6.2: fixed UTC offsets are wrong across a daylight saving change:** a window that spans one has buckets that are an hour off on one side. There is no timezone database.
- **M6.2: stats queries scan the window and the live snapshot reads every online driver position on every call** (see the plans and timings in "Admin dashboard results"). Many open admin tabs multiply that cost, and the page polls even in a background tab. `ride_events` has no index on `to_status` (the assignment-time query scans it), the rides list filtered by a rare driver or status can read the whole table (no `(driver_id, id)` or `(status, id)` index), and the driver search is `ILIKE` over sequential scans.
- **M6.2: the live snapshot is not atomic across Postgres and Redis,** and the 1000 row caps (drivers and rides) hide anything above them (the page says so when `truncated`).
- **M6.2: `GET /admin/drivers` is a 503 when Redis is down** because `online` comes from the presence keys; so the Drivers tab is empty during an outage, and approve and reject (which also touch Redis) answer 503 too.
- **M6.2: the admin page ignores a manual change of the URL hash until the page is reloaded** (the hash is read once, on load).
- **M6.2: `admin.js` is a single long file** (an approved exception), grouped by tab.
- **M6.2: the Leaflet tiles still come from the public OpenStreetMap server** (light use only); the admin map adds a tile load on every open Live tab.
- **M6.2: the live map and the lists show riders' names and addresses to every admin.** There are no admin roles or access log.
- **M6.2: rating comments are shown to admins as typed** (rendered as text, verified), with no moderation or search.
- **M6.2: `services/drivers.list_for_admin` and `repositories/drivers.list_all` are now unused** (the list moved to `services/admin` and `repositories/admin`); they were left because those two files were off limits for this milestone. Delete them in a later cleanup.
- **M6.2: the first Pricing form is for `rules[0]`** (economy, the only rule): there is no way to create or delete a rule or a vehicle type.
- **M6.1: no way to edit, delete, or moderate a rating,** and no tool to rebuild a summary if a rating is ever removed: it would need a manual recompute from `ratings` (I21 would report the difference at once).
- **M6.1: ratings do not affect anything:** not matching, not offers, not account standing, and a low-rated driver is offered rides like any other. Drivers do not see the rider's rating before accepting.
- **M6.1: a plain mean is noisy with few ratings.** It is hidden from others below 3 ratings but not smoothed.
- **M6.1: only completed trips can be rated,** not a cancelled trip with a fee.
- ~~M6.1: a rating can only be given from the finished-ride view or the API~~ Resolved in M6.3: "My trips" lists the trips of the person, shows `can_rate`, and "Open" shows the rating form for a trip inside the 7 day window. (The 7 day window is still a constant in `services/ratings.py`, not a pricing-rule setting.)
- **M6.1: no collusion or abuse detection.** One rating per ride, but rides are cheap to create, so a pair of accounts can raise each other's averages.
- **M6.1: comments are stored as typed** and must always be rendered as text (the M6.2 dashboard must too). **Resolved in M6.2: verified in the browser with `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` as a name, an address and a comment; nothing executed.** `GET /admin/ratings` returns them with both user ids and no names.
- **M6.1: the hottest summary row serializes concurrent ratings of one popular driver.** In the `ratings` scenario 360 requests (120 distinct ratings, three copies each) on one driver took about 4.4 s, 10 to 12 ms each, in one process and in two. A real driver gets ratings one trip apart, so this is a stress number, not a real limit; if it ever mattered the options are a batched or deferred summary update (which would give up the same-transaction guarantee and I21).
- **M6.1: 422 answers echo the request body** (FastAPI's default), including the comment, back to the person who sent it; it is never logged, and nobody else receives it.
- **M6.1: ids skip** because a rejected duplicate (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) still consumes a sequence value.
- **M6.1: dev data left behind:** an admin `m61-admin@example.com` (password in the verification scripts only), riders `m61-rider-*`, `m61-reg-*`, `m61-ui2-r*`, drivers `m61-driver-a` and `m61-ui2-driver`, about 1300 ratings (mostly stress accounts, whose summaries keep growing from run to run: the scenario compares changes, not absolute values), and ride 33738 whose `completed_at` was edited by hand twice during the window checks. No earlier open issue was about ratings, so this milestone closes none.
- **M5.4: no payouts and no cash settlement.** The driver's balance with the platform (`settlement.net`) only accumulates: nothing records the platform paying a driver or a driver paying their cash commission, so a driver with many cash rides can owe a lot with no way to pay it, and a driver with many wallet rides is owed money with no way to receive it. There is no driver wallet or ledger.
- **M5.4: the commission is read at settlement, not at request or accept time,** so the driver does not know their rate when accepting an offer (the rate shown on a row is the rate of that moment).
- **M5.4: the cancellation fee split is a simple choice** (the same percent as a trip). Whether the driver should get more of it, or all of it, is a business decision.
- **~~M5.4: the commission can only be edited in SQL until M6.2 adds the pricing rule editor.~~ Resolved in M6.2: the Pricing tab edits it (0 to 100).** One percent for every driver and every kind; no per-driver or tiered rates.
- **M5.4: windows use settlement time** (`ride_earnings.created_at`), not trip time, so a trip that ends just after midnight belongs to the next day. There is no grouping by day, no export and no CSV.
- **M5.4: receipts are derived on every request,** which is safe only while the stored inputs (the breakdown, the payment row, the ledger entry) are never rewritten. There are no tax or GST lines. ~~No receipt list or past-trips list; the rider page shows only the receipt of the ride it saw finish and loses it on reload~~ Resolved in M6.3: a past receipt is reopened from My trips ("Open" shows the same receipt section, with "Print receipt").
- **M5.4: no refunds, so no earning reversals.** A refund feature would have to add reversing rows (the table is append-only).
- **M5.4: drivers see the pickup and drop-off addresses of their own rides** in the earnings list (not the rider's name, email, phone or id).
- **M5.4: a summary reads all of a driver's rows in the window** (a bitmap scan on `ix_ride_earnings_driver_id_id` and a hash aggregate; see the query plans). Fine for thousands of rows per driver, a per-day rollup would be needed beyond that; the all-drivers revenue reads the whole window and uses sequential scans at this size.
- **M5.4: one more insert per settlement and one more pricing-rule read in `charge_ride`;** include them in the M7.2 load test. The foreign key on `ride_earnings.driver_id` takes a KEY SHARE lock on the driver's row until the commit (no explicit lock was added): it makes a matching `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` skip a driver whose ride is being settled for those milliseconds, and an accept for that driver wait for the settlement's commit; the chaos runs showed no effect. If it ever shows up in the load test, the earning's `driver_id` could be read from the ride instead of being a foreign key.
- **M5.4: Live Stripe is still not run** (see M5.3); the money views do not touch Stripe.
- **M5.4: the dev database gained test accounts and rides** (m54-*, m54ui-*, stress and simulator riders, 20 extra synthetic settled rows for one browser-test driver) that nothing prunes.
- **M5.3: Live Stripe was NOT run** (no `sk_test_` key in `.env`; everything above ran against `simulator/fake_stripe.py`). Not verified against the real service: the exact parameter names beyond the ones written down in the Stripe docs fetched on 2026-10-09 (Checkout Session create, the `Stripe-Signature` scheme, idempotency keys, event types), Stripe's real 409 and idempotency errors (written from the docs and memory: a concurrent request with the same key answers 409, a reused key with other parameters answers 400), a declined card (`4000 0000 0000 0002`), `stripe events resend`, and a session that really expires. To do by hand: put a test key and the `whsec_` of `stripe listen --forward-to localhost:8000/webhooks/stripe` in `.env`, recreate the backend, pay with `4242 4242 4242 4242`, resend the event several times (the balance must not change), pay with the declined card (no credit), and let a session expire or press "Check status".
- **M5.3: cash is assumed collected.** A cash payment row says the ride was paid in cash, nothing checks it. A cash cancellation fee may be uncollectible (the driver may not be with the rider any more).
- **M5.3: no refunds, chargebacks, disputes or payouts.** There is no driver wallet or payout (resolved in part in M5.4: ~~the platform fee split is undefined~~, the split is defined and stored per payment; payouts stay open, see above; driver earnings are views, there is still no driver wallet), ~~no receipts~~ (resolved in M5.4: rider receipts exist, derived from stored values), no per-day limits and no fraud checks. The wallet and the admin adjustments exist only for riders. INR only.
- **M5.3: no reconciliation between Stripe and the ledger.** Pending top-ups accumulate when no webhook or sync ever arrives, and there is no limit on pending top-ups per rider; nothing prunes them, nor `stripe_events` (Stripe keeps its idempotency keys about 24 hours, ours stay for ever).
- **M5.3: a negative balance is possible** only when the cancellation fee in the pricing rule is raised above a rider's balance (the ledger stays consistent, the rider cannot request a wallet ride until topped up), or when an admin debits a wallet during an active wallet ride (the debit is checked against the balance, not against `available`). I15 would show it.
- **M5.3: the webhook endpoint has no IP allowlist or rate limit,** and the Stripe API version is not pinned (no `Stripe-Version` header), so an account upgrade can change event shapes.
- **M5.3: every settlement now costs about four extra writes** (a payment, and for wallet rides a wallet lock, an entry and a balance update) and `GET /wallet` costs one extra query for the reservation; include them in the M7.2 load test. The wallet row is a hot row per rider only; there is no cross-rider contention.
- **M5.3: the success redirect `id` is only a hint** (sync checks that the top-up belongs to the caller); the browser can be sent to whatever address the API returns (checked: http or https only); `crypto.randomUUID` needs a secure context (localhost is one).
- **M5.3: a retry of a top-up more than 30 minutes after its row was created is refused by Stripe** (the fixed `expires_at` is then too soon): the rider starts a new top-up.
- **M5.3: the fake Stripe needs `--host 0.0.0.0` on this machine** and then listens on every interface (it warns).
- ~~**M5.1: no payment is taken** (M5.3).~~ Resolved in M5.3: the settlement creates the payment and, for wallet rides, the ledger entry, in the same transaction. ~~The final fare is stored and not charged yet.~~ Resolved in M5.3 as well.
- **M5.2: edge effects at zone borders.** Supply is counted in the driver's own cell, so a driver 10 m across a border is invisible to the neighbor zone even though matching would serve a pickup next to it (seen in the real-traffic runs: drivers in `tdr1w` and `tdr1y` did not help `tdr1v`). The surge of two adjacent zones can differ a lot.
- **M5.2: no hysteresis,** so the multiplier can flap around a step threshold between two snapshots.
- **M5.2: the snapshot is O(online drivers) per refresh** (one ZRANGE, one MGET, one GEOPOS and an availability query over all of them; per-zone `GEOSEARCH` would scale better). A refresh costs two Postgres queries and three Redis calls: 6 to 19 ms with 40 drivers online, 3 to 4 ms with none (Surge results). Not measured with hundreds of drivers (M7.2).
- **M5.2: duplicate recomputes at cache expiry** are possible (no lock); measured 5 recomputes for 4 windows across two processes.
- **M5.2: a quote can be up to 15 seconds stale, and a quote is protected only against increases** (`accepted_surge_percent`): a rider whose zone calmed down gets the lower price, a rider whose zone got busier gets a 409 and a new estimate.
- **M5.2: one rider with several accounts can inflate demand** (distinct riders are counted, not people): three accounts requesting from a pickup with no driver in range start surge for everyone there for 3 minutes.
- **M5.2: unmet demand inflates surge for 3 minutes even after drivers return,** and stress and chaos runs do the same (the next run starts surged; the README says so). Nothing ends a NO_DRIVER_FOUND ride early.
- **M5.2: offer-wave spikes.** A REQUESTED rider counts as demand and a driver with a pending offer is not supply, so a burst of requests (20 at once, 40 drivers around) briefly shows 2.0x and a rider re-requesting in that window pays it, until the offers are accepted. Candidates: count only NO_DRIVER_FOUND, or count a pending-offer driver as supply.
- **M5.2: zone sizes shrink in longitude with latitude** (4.77 km wide at Bengaluru, about 3.4 km at 45 degrees north); H3 would not.
- **M5.2: one vehicle type** (`economy`): the snapshot and the cap are not per vehicle type.
- **M5.2: drivers do not see surge** as such; the driver page shows the surged estimate as a plain fare. ~~and do not earn more from it yet (M5.4 splits fares)~~ Resolved in M5.4: commission applies to the whole payment, surge included, so the driver's earning grows with surge.
- **M5.2: the cap and the steps are constants/rule values:** the steps are code constants (still not editable, see the M6.2 issues). **The cap part is resolved in M6.2: the Pricing tab edits `surge_cap` (1.0 to 2.0).**
- **M5.2: surge multiplies the minimum fare** (a 1 km trip at 2.0x costs 160 instead of 80), by design.
- **M5.2: surge data is not stored anywhere for analytics** beyond the ride rows (`pickup_zone`, `surge_percent`, the breakdown); the snapshot lives 15 s in Redis.
- **M5.2: the demand query depends on `ix_rides_created_at`** and the table is small (26045 rows): the planner uses the index (0.32 ms against 9.6 ms for a sequential scan). Check it again in the M7.2 load test.
- **M5.2: the rider page was not tested in a browser** (no browser tool in the session); see Surge results for what a person still has to click through.
- **M5.2: dev data left behind:** an admin `m52-admin@example.com` (password in the verification scripts only, not in git) and a few thousand NO_DRIVER_FOUND stress rides at 150 to 200 percent surge from the verification runs.
- **M5.1: the first and last ping interval of every trip are not measured** (pings are recorded only while IN_PROGRESS): about 25 to 75 m at city speeds, which hurts short trips (the 90 km/h trip lost about 136 m of 3005).
- **M5.1: straight-line segments underestimate road distance on curves.**
- **M5.1: two clients pinging for one driver at once can double count or lose a segment** (the read-modify-write in `record_trip_point` is not atomic).
- **M5.1: a driver who stops pinging in the middle of a trip is billed the straight-line distance over the gap** (if the speed is plausible).
- **M5.1: manual driver-page trips fall back to the estimated distance** (map clicks teleport the driver); only the simulator gives distance-based fares.
- **M5.1: the cap (150 percent) and the 50 percent rule are constants, not editable** (M6.2 may move them into the pricing rule).
- **M5.1: the cancellation quote can go stale** if the free window runs out between the quote and the cancel (the charge is the later, higher one).
- ~~**M5.1: the cancellation fee goes to nobody yet** (M5.4 splits it); the driver is not paid for a trip the rider cancelled late.~~ Resolved in M5.4: a cancellation fee is split like a trip fare and shows in the driver's earnings (the driver's share is owed to them, see the settlement); payouts are still open.
- **M5.1: cancels of rides by an admin or by other paths do not exist yet**, so only the rider and the assigned driver are settled.
- ~~**M5.1: the driver sees the rider's fare**~~ (the whole trip fare and breakdown are in `RideResponse`; a driver payout view is M5.4). Resolved in M5.4 in the sense that matters for it: the driver now has an earnings view with the fare, the platform's fee and their own earning. `RideResponse` still carries the fare and breakdown to the driver by design (the driver collects cash).
- **M5.1: no payment is taken** (resolved in M5.3, see above). ~~Surge is not applied (M5.2: the multiplier must be locked on the ride at request time).~~ Resolved in M5.2: `rides.surge_percent` is locked at request time and `settle_completed_ride` reuses it.
- **M5.1: one extra Redis round trip (a read and a write) per ping during IN_PROGRESS and one more Postgres read per rider cancel from ASSIGNED**; include them in the M7.2 load test.
- **M5.1: the DB `now()` (event times) and the backend clock (`datetime.now`) are assumed to agree** for the free window.
- **M5.1: the fare is not rounded to whole rupees.**
- **M5.1: the UI was not tested in a browser.** This session had no browser tool. Both pages were loaded in headless Chrome (no script error), the rest of the click-through (fare section, confirm box, driver notes, phone width) is for you to do by hand.
- Dev data from M5.1: riders `m51-rider-a@example.com`, `m51-rider-b@example.com`, `m51-multi-01..10@example.com`, driver `m51-driver-a@example.com`, all with password `m51-pass-1234`; admin `m43-admin@example.com` (`m43-admin-pass`) was reused. Rides 23527 to 26001 are test trips. Dev Redis was not flushed (stopped and started once).
- **M3.1: a stalled WebSocket client delays everyone.** `listen_for_events` sends one message at a time, so a client that stops reading blocks the listener until `SEND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (5 s) expires, and the close of the dropped socket can block for another 5 s. Measured against the dev server (200 messages of 100 KB for a client with a 4 KB receive buffer): the healthy user's message arrived after 9.99 s, not within a second as the milestone asked; the stalled socket was dropped (warning logged). The test uses a 1 s timeout and allows 2 s + 1. **Decision (2026-10-09): leave it as it is.** A real fix needs concurrent delivery with per-socket ordering (for example one bounded queue and one writer task per socket, aborting the connection on timeout), which goes beyond the "two functions, no queues" structure of M3.1. Expect M3.2 live tracking to freeze for all users for up to about 10 s when one client stalls (a phone that loses signal, a frozen tab). Revisit if that shows up in M3.2 testing or in the M7.2 load test.
- **M3.1: a dropped stalled socket is only dropped from the registry.** The close frame cannot be sent to a client that is not reading, so its TCP connection and the endpoint task stay until the client goes away; the "disconnected" log line appears only then, and the client still believes it is connected. A shutdown with such a client open was not tested (in the pytest teardown it hung until the client socket was closed).
- **M3.1: a socket stays open after its token expires, and after the user is deleted or rejected.** The token is checked only at connect (so a reconnect after expiry gets 4401, but an open socket is never closed). Revisit in M6.
- **M3.1: no rate limit on inbound messages** (a client can send pings as fast as it likes; frames are capped at 64 KB).
- **M3.1: every process receives every message** because there is one channel for all users (a process without the user's socket drops it). Fine locally; revisit if the M7.2 load test uses several workers.
- ~~M3.1: events sent while the user is offline or the listener is down are lost~~ Mitigated in M3.4: the pages re-fetch their state over REST after every reconnect. Events are still not replayed, so a page whose socket stayed open while the listener was down relies on the 3 s poll.
- **M3.1: a dead listener task is invisible in `/health`** (still open after M7.1: only the sweeper got a heartbeat metric; the listener's lines carry `component: ws_listener`). An unexpected exception inside the listener (anything but `RedisError`) would end the task silently. The pub/sub connection also has no socket timeout or health check, so a silently dead TCP link would not be noticed (not tested).
- ~~M3.1: no connection metrics yet~~ Resolved in M7.1: `ws_connections` (accepted sockets not yet closed, per process) and `ws_closes_total{code}`; per-user counts are in the registry only.
- **M3.1: the pong reply (endpoint task) and events (listener task) can send on one socket at the same time.** Checked: 200 numbered events with 100 pings sent at the same time (dev server, step 9) and the same with 100 and 50 in the test arrived in order, every ping got its pong, no corrupted frames. No problem seen, but not proven impossible.
- **M3.1: `CLIENT KILL TYPE pubsub` in the tests also drops the dev backend's listener** until it reconnects (seen: under a second, no log line, because redis-py reconnects by itself).
- **M3.1: `events.publish` does not stop callers from using the reserved types** `auth_ok`, `pong`, `error`.
- **M3.1: not run on a real browser by hand.** The browser check ran in headless system Chrome driven by Playwright (the seven steps below, with the exact console snippet in `page.evaluate`), not by typing in DevTools.
- **M3.4: a driver whose presence expired during a long outage stays offline** until they press Go online (the 30 s presence key is gone, and the heartbeat ping answers 409). The simulator rejoins by itself.
- **M3.4: an open socket is not closed when its token expires or the user is rejected or deleted;** it is checked only at connect (see the M3.1 issue above). Revisit in M6.
- **M3.4: hidden tabs throttle timers,** so a dead connection can be detected later in a background tab. Mobile browsers may close sockets in the background (untested on real phones).
- **M3.4: the REST fallback doubles the request rate** of a rider page while the socket is down (one extra `GET /rides/{id}/driver` per 3 s poll).
- **M3.4: every open socket now sends a ping every 20 s;** include it in the M7.2 load test.
- **M3.4: a half-dead connection leaves a zombie socket on the server** until uvicorn's own ping timeout, and it counts toward the 5 sockets per user limit meanwhile (the reconnecting client then evicts the oldest with 4409, possibly the zombie, possibly a healthy other tab).
- **M3.4: the rider has no stale-location warning** when the driver's updates stop.
- **M3.4: the failed driver-details fetch is still retried only when the driver key changes** or the socket reconnects (plus the REST fallback while the socket is down).
- **M3.4: the UI was not tested in a browser.** This session had no browser tool. The ws.js logic and the real stack were tested with Node (see the verification note); the page wiring (status lines, Reconnect button, REST fallback, phone width) and the manual click-through list from the task are still to be done by hand.
- Dev data from M3.4: rider `ws-harness@example.com` (id 374, password `harness-pass-1`), admin `ws-harness-admin@example.com` (id 375, password `harness-admin-1`), ride 181 (COMPLETED, by the simulator's `sim-driver-001`), and the simulator accounts 001 to 010. Dev Redis was not flushed.
- Dev data from M3.1: one new ride (id 66, COMPLETED, by `sim-rider-a@example.com` and a simulated driver) and rider `sim-rider-b@example.com` (id 302, from M2.5). The throwaway users `m31-throwaway@example.com` and `m31-gone@example.com` were deleted. Dev Redis (database 0) holds no extra keys.
- Drivers cannot edit a profile or vehicle, or resubmit a rejected profile. A rejected driver is stuck unless an admin approves them again.
- Resolved in M2.3: approval now gates going online and location pings (403).
- argon2 hashing is synchronous and blocks the event loop (about 100 ms per login or register). Measured in M7.2: the login probe gives 6 req/s and a p50 of 1.8 s at 20 users (about 110 ms of event loop per login). Not fixed (M7.2 stopped before the fix); a threadpool is the candidate.
- Register has a check-then-insert race: two simultaneous requests with the same email can both pass the check, and the second hits the unique constraint and returns 500 instead of 409. Rare; revisit if it ever matters.
- Two simultaneous requests can pick the same free driver, and one rider can still create two rides by simultaneous requests (the checks in `create_ride` and `get_available_ids` are check-then-act). Intentional; M4.1 reproduces it on purpose. M2.4 step 9 DID show it: one free driver, two riders, two simultaneous `POST /rides`, and both got the same driver in 5 of 5 runs. **RESOLVED in M4.2** (was reproduced in M4.1: I1 in 20 of 20 default rounds (worst 10 offers on one driver), I2 in 20 of 20 (worst 9 active rides on one driver), I3 in 10 of 10 riders-scenario rounds (12 to 13 of 20 riders got two rides per round); after the fix the same commands give 0 violations, see "Fix and compare results").
- Resolved in M3.3: the assignment is no longer final: a driver accepts or rejects an offer, or it times out, and the next driver is tried (`ride_offers`). Still true: a request with nobody in range ends as NO_DRIVER_FOUND at once (no waiting window for a driver to appear).
- Resolved in M3.2: the rider sees the driver's name, vehicle, and live position, and the driver page shows the pickup and drop-off markers.
- Matching ranks by straight-line distance, not road ETA.
- A driver who goes online in the instant a stale cleanup runs can lose their GEO member; their next ping (within 3 s) adds it back.
- `create_ride` keeps a database transaction open while it calls OSRM and Redis. Fine locally; check it in the M7.2 load test. **Partly addressed in M4.2:** the rider lock is now taken AFTER the routing call, so the row lock is never held across OSRM; the transaction itself (and its pooled connection) is still open during the call.
- With 50 or more busy drivers nearest the pickup, a free driver farther out could be missed (`MAX_CANDIDATES = 50`).
- The driver page still says "Give this id to the admin so they can assign rides to you." (not changed in M2.4 by request; the text is out of date). A driver whose ride the rider cancelled sees the CANCELLED ride until they press "Wait for a new ride" (existing M1.4 behavior); the driver is matchable again straight away.
- Resolved in M2.4: matching filters `GEOSEARCH` results by presence and active ride and removes stale members; the admin assign endpoint was removed.
- ~~OTP is not checked on `/start` until M3.5~~ Resolved in M3.5: `/start` needs the trip code (wrong code 400, state checked first).
- Presence expires 30 seconds after the last ping, also during an active ride. If a driver's page stalls, live tracking drops; revisit in M3.4.
- Every ping now costs TWO Postgres reads: the driver (to find the id and check approval) and, since M3.2, the active ride (to find the rider to publish to). Measured on 2026-10-09 with the simulator (50 drivers, `--speed-kmh 90 --seed 1`, one uvicorn worker with `--reload`, all steady-state intervals, one run each so noise is unknown): M2.5 recorded 14 to 15 ms average ping and 0 failed over 1906 pings; today the code from before M3.2 gave 17 ms and 0 failed (1250 pings in 80 s), so this machine is about 2 to 3 ms slower than in M2.5; the M3.2 code with no rides gave 21 ms and 0 failed (1223 pings in 78 s), about 4 ms (about 20 percent) more than the control for the extra read; with 10 riders each holding a ride and a socket (about 10 drivers on rides, 184 `driver_location` frames delivered in 60 s, 0 failed requests) the average stayed 20 to 21 ms and 0 pings failed (the first interval, which includes setup, was 111 ms against 60 ms for the control and 105 ms for the no-rides run, then 24 ms; setup noise). Both reads are candidates for caching in the M7.2 load test. Not measured: more than 10 rides, several workers, a stalled client during tracking.
- A driver rejected mid-ride loses presence, but the ride continues over REST. Such a driver also gets 409 on go offline (the active ride) although already offline.
- Pings are not rate limited.
- The 503 handler covers every Redis failure, including the places cache (a Redis outage now also answers 503 on place search instead of 500).
- Not run in a real phone browser: the phone-width check was a 375 px headless Chrome. The click-through script lives outside the repo (scratchpad); tiles were mocked.
- A driver who is cancelled out of a ride stays on `rides.driver_id` (the history keeps who was assigned), so that driver still gets 409, not 404, on further actions for that ride.
- Dev database also holds an admin `m24-admin@example.com`, riders and drivers `m24-*` and `m24ui-*`, and about 60 rides from the M2.4 checks; dev Redis holds no live presence for them (keys expire; matching removed their stale GEO members). Dev database also holds an admin `m23admin@example.com`, riders and drivers `m23*` and `m23ui-*`, and a few rides from the M2.3 checks. Dev Redis (database 0) holds `drivers:geo` and presence keys for those drivers until the keys expire.
- Dev database contains leftover rides, users, and drivers from the M1.3, M1.4, and M2.1 manual checks (curl walkthrough and browser click-throughs; M2.1 left an admin `m21admin@example.com`, several `m21*` riders and drivers, and a few rides; M2.2 left an admin `m22admin@example.com`, riders and drivers `m22*` and `m22ui-*`, and a few rides, one of them still REQUESTED, plus a second `pricing_rules` id after the downgrade/upgrade check). Dev Redis holds cached places for a day.
- JWT in `sessionStorage` is readable by any script on the page. Acceptable for a learning project: Leaflet is the only third-party script, loaded from unpkg at a pinned version with an SRI hash (M2.1).
- ~~The admin page cannot list rides or see which drivers are online until M6.2.~~ **Resolved in M6.2 (the M1.4 and M2.3 issues): the Rides and Drivers tabs list them and the Live map shows who is online.**
- The pages still poll ride status every 3 s as the safety net behind the socket (since M3.5 every status change is also pushed, so it normally shows within about a second). A failed poll leaves its error message up until the next button press.
- The M1.4 browser click-through lives only as a throwaway script outside the repo (no automated frontend tests, by decision). Rerun it by hand with the 12-step list if the pages change a lot.
- The public OSM tile and Nominatim servers are for light use only. The M7.2 load test must never call them: use OSRM and fixed coordinates. VERIFIED for the M2.5 simulator: `grep -i "nominatim\|openstreetmap" simulator/simulator.py` finds nothing, every request goes through the two httpx clients whose base URLs are the API and OSRM, and nothing from the simulator reaches Nominatim.
- Resolved in M2.2: `POST /rides` and `POST /rides/estimate` now reject points outside the city box (`is_inside_bounds`).
- Resolved in M2.2: `CITY_*` and the OSRM extract match, because `osrm/prepare.sh` builds the extract from the `CITY_*` values (Bengaluru placeholders, southern-zone extract). Still open: set `NOMINATIM_USER_AGENT` to your own contact email (the default text still says "replace with your contact email").
- OSRM durations are free-flow driving times with no live traffic, so durations and the time part of the fare are optimistic.
- ~~Surge is not applied until M5.2.~~ Resolved in M5.2 (estimate, ride and settlement). ~~The final fare in M5.1 should reuse `pricing.calculate_fare` and the same pricing rule.~~ Resolved in M5.1: `settle_completed_ride` calls `calculate_fare`.
- The route path is not stored on the ride: the rider page re-requests it once per ride. M3 live tracking may need to cache it.
- OSRM data must be regenerated when the city changes: edit `CITY_*` and `OSM_EXTRACT_URL` in `.env`, delete `osrm/data/`, run `osrm/prepare.sh`. The prepared data is not refreshed when OpenStreetMap changes.
- `routing.py` makes a new `httpx.AsyncClient` per call, like `places.py`. Fine locally; share one client if this grows. Every point change in the rider page costs one OSRM call and one database read, with no rate limit.
- The admin page does not show fares or distances yet (the driver page shows the trip fare since M5.1: resolved for the driver page only). Rides created before M2.2 have null estimate fields; the rider page shows dashes for them.
- Not exercised against the real OSRM: the 5 s timeout and the "any other code gives 502" branch (only OSRM being stopped, `NoSegment`, and `NoRoute` were). The rider page fit/animation behavior was checked in headless Chrome only.
- `places` makes a new `httpx.AsyncClient` per uncached call. Fine at 1 request per second; share one client if this grows.
- The "no address" 404 path was not seen against the real Nominatim: Bengaluru has no open water, and a lake centre still resolved to a nearby building. It is verified against a local stub that returns `{"error": ...}`.
- The busy (429) message was seen in the browser from the real limiter once, right after a reverse call; it depends on timing, so a faster or slower network can miss the 1.1 s window.
- The page has no automated frontend tests. The M2.1 and M2.2 Playwright scripts live outside the repo (scratchpad); the M2.2 one mocks Nominatim and the map tiles but uses the real OSRM, backend, and database.
- Swagger Authorize has not been clicked through in a browser in M1.1 or M1.2 (no browser tool). `/docs` serves 200 and the OpenAPI schema declares `HTTPBearer` on `/auth/me` and on all six M1.2 routes and all nine M1.3 routes (`/docs` served 200 in M1.3).

- Resolved in M3.3: the socket code left `rider.js` and lives in `shared/ws.js`, used by the rider and driver pages (the M3.2 decision "the socket code stays in rider.js until M3.3").
- ~~M3.2: a closed socket stops tracking until the page is reloaded~~ Resolved in M3.4: ws.js reconnects by itself; after 4409 or 4400 the Reconnect button resumes, and the rider's marker moves through REST polling meanwhile.
- **M3.2: a failed `GET /rides/{id}/driver` is not retried** until the driver key (`rideId:driverId`) changes, so the section stays empty (the error shows once) until a reload. By design, so a failure is not retried on every 3 s poll.
- **M3.2: `updated_at` has one-second resolution**, so a stale REST answer in the same second as a socket update can win and move the marker back by one update (it corrects itself at the next ping).
- **M3.2: going online during a ride publishes no location until the next ping** (a simulated driver whose presence expired calls it mid-ride, so the rider can miss one update, up to 3 s).
- ~~M3.2: ride status changes are still polled every 3 s; M3.5 could push them~~ Resolved in M3.5: every status change is pushed to both people as `ride_updated`; the 3 s poll stays as the safety net.
- **M3.2: events sent while the rider is offline are lost**; the page recovers its position from REST on reload (and never otherwise, see the first item).
- **M3.2: the glide is straight-line interpolation**, not map-matched, so on a curved road the marker cuts corners between two pings (at 30 km/h a ping is about 25 m).
- **M3.2: the details endpoint answers 503 when Redis is down** even though name and vehicle come from Postgres.
- **M3.2: the driver's real name is shown to the rider** (no display-name or first-name-only rule).
- **M3.2: a hidden tab jumps once when it returns** (one move of up to the stale distance, under 500 m; see the bugs log).
- **M3.2: the stalled-client issue above now also delays `driver_location` for everyone** (about 10 s) when one socket stops reading. Not exercised in M3.2.
- **M3.2: browser checks ran in headless Chrome only** (Playwright, throwaway scripts outside the repo, OSM tiles and `/places/reverse` mocked): headless never hides a tab, so the background-tab step was emulated by holding `requestAnimationFrame`; no real phone. The "You" tooltip on the driver map can sit behind the "Pickup" tooltip when the driver is at the pickup.
- **M3.2: the rider page keeps showing the "Driver id" row** next to the new "Your driver" section (not asked to remove it).
- Dev data from M3.2: admin `m32-admin@example.com` (password `m32-admin-pass`), riders `m32-r1@example.com`, `m32-r2@example.com`, and `m32-load-01` to `m32-load-10@example.com` (password `rider-pass-1`), driver `m32-d1@example.com` (password `driver-pass-1`, drivers.id 236, approved), driver `m32-xss@example.com` named `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` (password `driver-pass-1`), and 22 rides (ids 67 to 88: 12 COMPLETED, 9 CANCELLED, 1 NO_DRIVER_FOUND, none active). Dev Redis (database 0) holds `drivers:geo` (stale members) and the cached places from earlier milestones, no presence keys.
- Resolved in M3.3: the simulator accepts, rejects, or ignores offers by rate.
- **M3.3: double offers and double booking were possible (resolved in M4.2).** Two simultaneous ride requests can both see the same free driver and both create an offer to them (the check is plain, no lock; the partial unique index only limits ONE ride), and two simultaneous accepts for two offers of one driver can both pass the active-ride check. M4.1 reproduces this on purpose. It DID show up in the step 15 simulator run: 20 riders requested at the same instant against 30 drivers and 12 pairs of overlapping offers to the same driver were found afterwards (up to 5 drivers holding two PENDING offers at one sampled second); no driver ended up with two active rides (the simulator never answered the hidden second offer, which expired unseen). **RESOLVED in M4.2** (reproduced in M4.1: the controlled `drivers` scenario gave I1 and I2 in 20 of 20 rounds, the `fleet` scenario I1 in 5 of 5 rounds and I2 in 0 of 5; after the fix all of them give 0, see "Fix and compare results").
- ~~M3.3: a driver who goes offline, or is rejected by an admin, keeps a pending offer until it times out~~ Resolved in M4.3: the sweeper withdraws the offer of a driver with no presence key (reason `driver_offline`) within about a second, whatever the cause.
- **M3.3: a driver who cancels after accepting ends the ride** (CANCELLED) with no re-dispatch.
- **M3.3: `MAX_CANDIDATES` (50) can hide a free driver behind busy ones**, and now also behind drivers already offered this ride. Ranking is straight-line distance, not ETA.
- **M3.3: the rider sees only "Looking for a driver" for up to about 75 seconds** (5 offers x 15 s) with no progress or count.
- ~~M3.3: `ride_updated` covers only offer-driven changes, the rest is polled until M3.5~~ Resolved in M3.5: arrive, start, complete, and cancel send `ride_updated` to both people.
- ~~M3.3: the sweeper aborts a pass on the first exception~~ Resolved in M4.3: each offer is handled in its own try/except (one warning per offer id, the pass goes on); only outage errors abort the pass. A dead sweeper task became visible in M7.1 (the heartbeat metric), see the entry below.
- ~~M3.3: the socket does not reconnect~~ Resolved in M3.4. Events sent while a driver's socket is down are still lost, and the page re-fetches over REST on the reconnect (the 3 s poll covers the gap).
- **M3.3: expiry uses one backend clock** (`datetime.now(timezone.utc)`), so several hosts with different clocks would need the database's `now()`.
- **M3.3: the simulator quantizes response times to its 3-second tick**, so a "1 s" answer arrives up to 3 s later.
- **M3.3: sweepers overlapped little in the two-server check.** With the real 1-second sweepers in two backend processes the split was 2 vs 26 expiries; to force contention three extra tight-loop sweepers (50 ms) were added: 30 expiries, each logged exactly once, no IntegrityError, no deadlock, no traceback. Accept racing the sweeper is not tested (M4).
- **M3.3: step 15 numbers (input for M7.2).** `--drivers 30 --speed-kmh 90 --seed 1`, default rates, 20 riders requesting at the same instant around the centre, one uvicorn worker with `--reload`: 18 rides were assigned and ALL 18 completed through arrive, start, and complete; 2 ended NO_DRIVER_FOUND (offers REJ and EXP,REJ). 33 offers in total: 18 ACCEPTED, 4 REJECTED, 11 EXPIRED. The simulator counted 26 seen (18 accepted, 4 rejected, 4 ignored); the other 7 expiries were second offers it never saw (see the first item). Offers per ride: 1 offer for 11 rides, 2 for 5, 3 for 4 (table below). Seconds from request to DRIVER_ASSIGNED: 6.0 to 8.7 s for rides accepted on the first offer (the simulator's 3 s tick plus its 1 to 6 s delay), 21.6 to 24.3 s after one ignored offer, 27.7 to 38.4 s after two. Pings: 6016 ok, 0 failed, average 16 ms over 605 s. With `--accept-rate 0 --reject-rate 0` (everyone ignores): 6 simultaneous rides each made exactly 5 offers, all EXPIRED, and ended NO_DRIVER_FOUND after 76 s; the simulator saw 27 offers, ignored 27.
  | ride | offers | statuses | final | s to assigned |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 129-134, 146 | 1 | ACC | COMPLETED | 6.8 to 8.7 |
  | 140, 141, 148 | 1 | ACC | COMPLETED (finished after the script's 420 s cut) | 6.0 to 14.0 |
  | 136, 138, 144, 145 | 2 | EXP,ACC | COMPLETED | 21.6 to 24.3 |
  | 143 | 3 | REJ,EXP,ACC | COMPLETED | 27.7 |
  | 137 | 3 | EXP,REJ,ACC | COMPLETED | 30.7 |
  | 139, 147 | 3 | EXP,EXP,ACC | COMPLETED | 37.0 to 38.4 |
  | 135 | 2 | EXP,REJ | NO_DRIVER_FOUND | - |
  | 142 | 1 | REJ | NO_DRIVER_FOUND | - |
- **M3.3: UI checked in headless Chrome only** (Playwright from a throwaway venv, script outside the repo, system Chrome; OSM tiles and `/places/reverse` mocked): see the verification paragraph. The background tab was emulated by pausing the page's JavaScript for 9 s through the DevTools protocol (headless Chrome never hides a tab); no real phone, no Safari or Firefox.
- Dev data from M3.3: admin `m33-admin@example.com` and riders `m33-r1` to `m33-r6`, `m33-load-01` to `m33-load-20`, drivers `m33-a`, `m33-b`, `m33-c` (all password `m33-password`; drivers.id 238 to 240), UI-test accounts `m33ui-*` (password `m33ui-password`), and about 180 rides, none of the M3.3 ones active. Dev Redis (database 0) holds no presence keys.
- ~~M3.5 adds an OTP check on start, so the simulator must send it~~ Resolved in M3.5: the simulator sends `--otp` (default `1234`) on start.
- **M3.5: the trip code is the same fixed value for every ride** (`1234`), so anyone who knows it can start any trip once the driver has arrived. A real version uses random digits per ride (`secrets`), still stored on `rides.otp`.
- **M3.5: no attempt limit on wrong codes.** A driver can try `0000` to `9999` (or the simulator can retry every 3 s forever).
- **M3.5: arrive and complete are not checked against the driver's location**; they work from anywhere.
- **M3.5: rides that were assigned before this milestone have no code** and could never be started; the two in dev (rides 47 and 52) were cancelled through the API before the checks.
- ~~M3.5: cancelling has no fee~~ Resolved in M5.1 (the reason is still not chosen by the user). A driver who cancels after accepting ends the ride with no re-dispatch (the rider requests again; the same driver can be matched again at once).
- **M3.5: every status change now costs one extra Postgres read (the driver's user id) and up to two publishes.** Include it in the M7.2 load test.
- **M3.5: the acting user's other tabs also receive `ride_updated`** (both people get it, whoever made the change), which only costs a redundant refresh.
- **M3.5: the trip code is stored in plain text** in `rides.otp` (it exists only between assignment and start or cancel).
- **M3.5: a rider whose `/otp` request fails sees no code until the next page load** (`otpKey` is set before the call so a failure is not retried every poll). A theoretical race: if the trip starts between the poll that reads the ride and the `/otp` call, the page shows the 409 message once.
- **M3.5: the driver page keeps a finished ride on screen until "Wait for a new ride"** (M1.4 behavior); after the rider cancels, the driver sees "The rider cancelled this ride." and "Wait for a new ride", is matchable at once, and an offer can appear next to the finished ride.
- **M3.5: the UI was checked in headless Chrome only** (Playwright from a throwaway venv, scripts outside the repo, real OSM tiles and Nominatim through the backend): `inputmode="numeric"` is set on the code input but the numeric keypad itself was not seen on a real phone; no Safari or Firefox; no really hidden tab.
- **M3.5: 5 old rides are still active in dev** (ids 16, 25, 37, 38, 67, all REQUESTED, from M1 to M2 checks, with no pending offer), so their riders cannot request new rides. They are not from this milestone and were not touched.
- Dev data from M3.5: admin `m35-admin@example.com` (id 376, password `m35-admin-pass`), riders `m35-r1`, `m35-r2`, `m35-r3` (ids 377 to 379), drivers `m35-a` and `m35-b` (users 380 and 381, drivers.id 253 and 254, approved), riders `m35-sim-01` to `m35-sim-10` and `m35-wrongcode` (all `@example.com`, password `m35-pass-1234`), and 43 rides (ids 182 to 225: 25 COMPLETED, 15 CANCELLED, 3 NO_DRIVER_FOUND, none active). Dev Redis (database 0) was restarted during the Redis-down check (it has no persistence, so it lost its keys) and now holds about 20 keys, mostly cached `places:reverse:*` entries from the browser run, and no presence keys.
- There is a ride-request generator for the race (`simulator/stress.py`, M4.1) (M7.2 added the Locust harness in `loadtest/`: RESOLVED); rides otherwise come from the rider page or curl.
- **M4.1: the invariant snapshots are sampled.** They run once after the burst, once after the accepts, and about every 1.3 s in the fleet scenario (each `docker compose exec` costs about 0.3 s), so a violation that appears and disappears between two snapshots is missed. The broken states in M4.1 persist until the cleanup, so none was missed there, but a fix that only shortens the window could look clean.
- ~~M4.1: the I4 check (REQUESTED ride without a PENDING offer) is a baseline for M4.3~~ Resolved in M4.3: I4 is now one of seven invariants checked in every scenario including chaos, and it never fired (I4 to I7 cannot be legitimately violated even for an instant).
- **M4.1: stress accounts and cancelled rides accumulate in the dev database.** 100 riders (`stress-rider-001` to `-100`) and 40 drivers (`stress-driver-01` to `-40`) exist and are kept on purpose (setup reuses them); every run adds one ride per request it sends, and the cleanup cancels them (the M4.1 checks created about 3000 rides for the stress riders, of 3227 rides in total, and 1404 offers to stress drivers). `--cleanup-only` leaves no active ride and no online stress driver, but nothing deletes them.
- **M4.1: the tool's timing is not repeatable.** `--seed` fixes the random points, not the interleaving, so the numbers in "Baseline for M4.2" vary between runs (the violation itself did not: 100 percent in every run). A fixed result is exit code 1 until M4.2.
- **M4.1: the rider can never lose the race in a visible way.** Each double-booked rider sees a normal DRIVER_ASSIGNED ride with the right driver; nothing tells them the car is also committed elsewhere (see the damage list in the baseline).
- Simulated drivers move at constant speed with no traffic. A driver restarted after its presence expired (about 30 s) starts from a new random point (rerunning within 30 s resumes from the last position). A driver resumed from presence is not checked for a disconnected road (only new start points are).
- One simulator process handles at most 200 drivers (`--drivers` is capped; no multi-process mode).
- A ride leg whose OSRM route fails (for example a pickup not routable from where the driver is) is retried every tick and the driver stays put until it works; there is no fallback.
- **Scale results for M7.2 (this machine, one uvicorn worker with `--reload`, Postgres and Redis in Docker, each run on the same host as the backend):** 50 drivers (45 new accounts): setup 19.3 s; steady state 250 of 250 pings ok per 15 s (0 failed over 1906 pings in 125 s), average ping 14 to 15 ms; while setup ran the first interval averaged 232 ms. 200 drivers with 150 NEW accounts: setup 153.4 s (about 1 s per new account at 5 at a time); average ping rose from 261 ms to 2.5 s during the registrations; 3 pings and 5 ride polls failed with `RemoteProtocolError` (before the keep-alive change); 5891 ok in 155 s; the run was stopped as setup finished, so it has no steady-state window. 200 drivers with reused accounts (after the change): setup 37.2 s, then 1000 of 1000 pings ok per 15 s (6243 ok, 0 failed over 111 s), average ping 25 to 29 ms (about 133 requests per second at that point). So pings are not the limit at 200; the first thing to break is the event loop blocked by argon2 hashing (register and login), which delays everything else. Not measured: more than 200 drivers, several processes, a worker count above 1.
- The login hashing blocking from M1.1 is now measured: about 1 s per new account at concurrency 5 (153 s for 150 new accounts), and ping time up to 10 times higher while accounts register. Candidate fix (not done, M7.2 stopped at the baseline): run the hashing in an executor.
- During the 40 s Redis outage every ping answered 503 and took 5 to 6 s (the backend waits on the dead connection), so a driver's tick stretched from 3 s to 6 s; each of the 30 drivers logged the 503 once and recovered through the 409 then online path (30 `presence had expired` lines). With OSRM stopped for 25 s only the 2 drivers whose leg ended needed a route; the rest kept driving their existing route, pings never failed, and the 2 resumed when OSRM came back.
- Simulated drivers can be seen only on the map of the rider whose ride they are on (M3.2); ~~the admin map comes in M6.2~~ **resolved in M6.2: the Live map tab shows every online driver.**
- The driver page and the simulator conflict if both control the same driver: the page pings its own clicked position every 3 s. Do not log in to a `sim-driver-*` account on `/driver/` while the simulator runs (documented in the README).
- Dev data from M2.5: admin `sim-admin@example.com` (password `sim-admin-pass`), 200 driver accounts `sim-driver-001` to `sim-driver-200` (approved, one vehicle each; passwords `sim-driver-pass`), riders `sim-rider-a@example.com` and `sim-rider-b@example.com` (password `rider-pass-1`), and 8 rides (ids 58 to 65). Dev Redis (database 0) lost its stale GEO members during the runs (matching removes them) and holds no live presence once the simulator is stopped.
- ~~M4.2: no database constraint backs the locks up~~ Resolved in M4.3: three partial unique indexes (`uq_ride_offers_one_pending_per_driver`, `uq_rides_one_active_per_driver`, `uq_rides_one_active_per_rider`), proven by the tests that run with the locks disabled.
- **M4.2: a lock holder that hangs (not crashes) keeps its row.** Postgres releases the locks of a dead CLIENT only when the server notices: at once if the session is idle in its transaction, but not while a query is still running (`client_connection_check_interval` is 0 here, see F1). `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` is 0 (disabled) and `lock_timeout` is 0 server-wide (the app sets 3 s per transaction with `SET LOCAL`). So a hung holder keeps its driver or rider locked: matching skips the driver, accept and `POST /rides` answer 503. No server setting was changed (out of scope).
- **M4.2: under contention every rider walks the candidate list.** Each candidate costs one `try_lock` and, when the lock was free, one more availability query. Measured with a throwaway counter in the test harness (all riders at once): 20 riders and 3 drivers, 2.4 to 2.65 extra queries per request (43 to 49 `try_lock` and 4 to 5 re-checks over 20 requests); 50 riders and 5 drivers, 4.8. The cost grows with riders per free driver and is bounded by `MAX_CANDIDATES` (50) candidates, 2 queries each. Without contention it is 2 extra queries per request.
- **M4.2: cross-process correctness was verified with two uvicorn processes on one host and one Postgres,** not on separate machines (clock differences matter only for offer deadlines, which are compared with each process's own clock, as before). All measurements come from one development machine (one uvicorn worker with `--reload`, `docker compose` on a laptop), so absolute latencies are not comparable with production.
- **M4.2: `MAX_CANDIDATES` (50) can still hide a free driver behind busy ones.** The GEO search returns the 50 nearest members before the availability filter; in a dense area a rider can get NO_DRIVER_FOUND although a free driver is the 51st. Unchanged by M4.2, and made no worse (skipped drivers come from the same list).
- **M4.2: the accept path still reads presence from Redis.** The presence check in `accept` is not part of the row lock (see "Known races"), so a driver whose key expires between the check and the commit is still assigned. `go_offline` has the mirrored race. Candidates for M4.3.
- **M4.2: `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` stays set for the rest of the transaction** after `users.lock` or `drivers.lock`, so a later statement in the same transaction that has to wait for a lock would also time out after 3 s and surface as a 500 (not a 503). Nothing in the current flows waits there; noted in case a later change adds a blocking statement after the lock.
- **M4.2: `pkill` is not installed in the backend or db containers,** so the second uvicorn is stopped by recreating the container or by a small /proc scan (see the README).
- **M4.3: a driver who vanishes AFTER accepting leaves the ride assigned** until someone cancels it (nothing watches a driver with an active ride; presence expiring does not matter to the ride). Out of scope on purpose.
- **M4.3: a driver who cancels after accepting ends the ride** (CANCELLED) with no re-dispatch to another driver. Out of scope on purpose (the same open item as the M3.3 note above).
- **M4.3: there is no driver no-show timeout and no auto-cancel of abandoned assigned rides.** A ride whose driver never arrives stays DRIVER_ASSIGNED; the chaos scenario does not count such rides as stuck.
- ~~M4.3: a dead sweeper task is invisible in `/health`~~ Resolved in M7.1 for SEEING it: `sweeper_last_success_timestamp_seconds`, `sweeper_errors_total`, `sweeper_tick_duration_seconds` and the dashboard panel "seconds since the sweeper last succeeded" (stays below about 5 s). A crashed task still does not restart and nothing alerts. The paused-backend check shows what it looks like: I5 lists the offers more than 10 s late; recovery after `unpause` was automatic (empty at the first poll, under a second). A crashed task (as opposed to a failing pass, which `sweep_forever` catches) would not restart.
- **M4.3: a driver is unavailable for up to about a second after an offer expires,** until the sweeper handles it (the pending-offer index and availability count an expired but unswept offer). Measured: 46 to 1027 ms after the deadline, median 1006 ms (8 samples, polled every 20 ms); a sweep every 1 s plus the time of a pass.
- **M4.3: the sweeper reads all pending offers every second and makes one MGET.** Cost under the heavy chaos load (60 riders, 20 drivers): 6 pending offers on average (max 10), `list_pending` median 2.7 ms (max 22.7), the MGET median 0.6 ms (max 2.0), per process; with two processes it is done twice. Limit 200 offers per pass: with more pending offers than that the oldest are handled first and the rest wait a pass.
- **M4.3: chaos and stress runs come from one development machine and a small fleet** (10 to 20 drivers, 30 to 60 riders, one or two uvicorn processes with the database in the same host). Timing is not repeatable: `--seed` fixes the agents' choices, not the interleaving. Most chaos rides end at once as NO_DRIVER_FOUND (supply), so only about one request in five gets an offer.
- **M4.3: `MAX_CANDIDATES` (50) can still hide a free driver behind busy ones** (unchanged from M4.2 and M3.3; the GEO search returns the 50 nearest members before the availability filter).
- **M4.3: the hung (not crashed) lock holder from M4.2 is still open.** A request stuck mid-query keeps its driver or rider row locked; matching skips that driver and accept or `POST /rides` answer 503 after 3 s. The new indexes do not change that. `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` would bound it (not set).
- **M4.3: the browser click-through was not run** (no browser tool was available in the session). The backend behavior behind it was checked by tests, WebSocket events, and curl; the driver page text for `driver_offline` is one string in `driver.js` and was only checked by reading the served file. To do by hand: (1) driver tab online, rider requests, the driver clicks Go offline: within about 2 s the offer panel closes with "The offer was withdrawn because you went offline."; (2) request again with an offer showing, reject the driver in the admin tab: the same notice within about 2 s and the driver tab shows the rejected message; (3) with a second driver tab online the offer moves to it within about 2 s and the rider tab still says "Looking for a driver"; (4) the normal flow, countdown, tracking, and reconnect still work.
- **M4.3: a connection refused or a name-resolution failure is an `OSError`,** so the sweeper treats every `OSError` as an outage and aborts the pass. A plain `DBAPIError` ("the database system is shutting down") during a Postgres shutdown is NOT in that list and is logged as a per-offer failure for a few seconds. Acceptable; widen `OUTAGE_ERRORS` if it becomes noisy.
- Dev data from M4.3: admin `m43-admin@example.com` (id 537, password `m43-admin-pass`), made with `create_admin.py`; the stress accounts from M4.1 are reused, and about 16 700 rides, 9 400 offers, and their events are in the dev database. The Docker stack was started at the beginning of this milestone (it was not running).

## How to run and test

```bash
# load test (M7.2): stop the dev backend first; see README "Load testing"
#   .venv-load/bin/python loadtest/run.py seed|reset|probe|run|report ;  .venv-load/bin/pytest loadtest
#   docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.loadtest.yml stop backend-load postgres-load redis-load   # when finished; then: docker compose start backend
cp .env.example .env                  # first time only; set CITY_* and OSM_EXTRACT_URL for your city
osrm/prepare.sh                       # first time only (M2.2): downloads a regional extract (558 MB for the default), clips it to the city, builds the OSRM data in osrm/data/
docker compose up --build             # start db, redis, osrm, backend
curl localhost:8000/health            # expect 200 {"status":"ok","postgres":"ok","redis":"ok"}
docker compose stop redis             # /health now returns 503 with redis "error"
docker compose start redis
docker compose down                   # stop
docker compose down -v                # stop and delete the database volume (full reset)

# database migrations (run after every fresh database)
docker compose exec backend alembic upgrade head                              # create/update all tables and seed the economy pricing rule
docker compose exec backend alembic downgrade base                            # remove all tables (postgis stays)
docker compose exec --user $(id -u):$(id -g) backend alembic revision --autogenerate -m "message" --rev-id 0002
docker compose exec db psql -U uber -d uber -c '\dt'                          # inspect tables

# driver onboarding (M1.2): use a driver token and an admin token from /auth/login
curl -X POST localhost:8000/drivers/me/profile -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"license_number":"DL-12345"}'
curl -X POST localhost:8000/drivers/me/vehicle -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"plate_number":"KA01AB1234","model":"Swift","color":"white"}'
curl "localhost:8000/admin/drivers?status=pending" -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN"
curl -X POST localhost:8000/admin/drivers/1/approve -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN"

# tests (M1.3): uses a separate database named <POSTGRES_DB>_test, created automatically
docker compose exec backend pytest

# rides (M1.3, offers since M3.3): POST /rides offers the ride to the nearest free online driver within 3 km of the pickup (201 REQUESTED), or ends as NO_DRIVER_FOUND (201)
curl -X POST localhost:8000/rides -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"pickup_lat":12.9716,"pickup_lng":77.5946,"pickup_address":"MG Road","dropoff_lat":12.9352,"dropoff_lng":77.6245,"dropoff_address":"Koramangala"}'
# try matching by hand: approve two drivers, put both online (driver tab: click the map, Go online, or the curl below), then request as a rider
# since M3.3 the nearer driver gets an OFFER (the ride stays REQUESTED until the driver accepts); a second rider gets the other driver; a third gets NO_DRIVER_FOUND
curl -X POST localhost:8000/rides/1/arrive   -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER"    # then /start, /complete
curl -X POST localhost:8000/rides/1/cancel   -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER"
curl localhost:8000/rides/active -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER"
curl localhost:8000/rides/1/events -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER"

# routing and fare (M2.2): needs osrm/prepare.sh to have run and `docker compose up -d osrm`
curl -X POST localhost:8000/rides/estimate -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"pickup_lat":12.9716,"pickup_lng":77.5946,"dropoff_lat":12.9352,"dropoff_lng":77.6245}'   # 7794 m, 769 s, 16916 paise
curl "http://127.0.0.1:5000/route/v1/driving/77.5946,12.9716;77.6245,12.9352?overview=false"      # OSRM directly (longitude first)
docker compose exec db psql -U uber -d uber -c 'SELECT * FROM pricing_rules;'
docker compose stop osrm              # estimates now answer 502 "Routing is unavailable"; a rider with an active ride still gets 409 on POST /rides

# places (M2.1): needs NOMINATIM_USER_AGENT with your contact email and the CITY_* values in .env, then `docker compose up -d`
# Never script or loop these: the public Nominatim server allows 1 request per second and light use only
curl localhost:8000/places/map-config -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER"
curl "localhost:8000/places/search?q=mg%20road" -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER"
curl "localhost:8000/places/reverse?lat=12.9757&lng=77.6063" -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER"
docker compose exec redis redis-cli keys 'places:*'

# driver presence (M2.3): use a driver token of an approved driver; presence lives in Redis only
curl -X POST localhost:8000/drivers/me/online   -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"lat":12.9716,"lng":77.5946}'
curl -X POST localhost:8000/drivers/me/location -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"lat":12.9352,"lng":77.6245}'   # keep pinging: the key expires after 30 s
curl localhost:8000/drivers/me/presence -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER"
curl -X POST localhost:8000/drivers/me/offline -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER"
docker compose exec redis redis-cli GEOPOS drivers:geo <driver_id>        # prints longitude first, then latitude
docker compose exec redis redis-cli TTL driver:<driver_id>:presence       # about 30 right after an update; -2 once expired
docker compose exec redis redis-cli ZCARD drivers:geo                     # members in the GEO set (may include stale ones)

# driver simulator (M2.5): runs on the host (not in Docker); needs an admin made with create_admin.py
python -m venv .venv-sim && .venv-sim/bin/pip install -r simulator/requirements.txt          # once
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/simulator.py --drivers 30 --admin-email ... --admin-password ...   # or SIM_ADMIN_EMAIL / SIM_ADMIN_PASSWORD; Ctrl+C takes the drivers offline
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/simulator.py --drivers 30 --speed-kmh 90 --seed 2 ...             # fast trips, repeatable start points
# try matching by hand: start 30 drivers, request a ride from /rider/ with a pickup near the city center; do NOT open a sim-driver-* account on /driver/ while it runs
docker compose exec redis redis-cli GEOSEARCH drivers:geo FROMLONLAT 77.5946 12.9716 BYRADIUS 3 km ASC WITHDIST   # who matching will pick (longitude first)

# frontend (M1.4, map added in M2.1): open each in its own tab; every tab keeps its own login
#   http://localhost:8000/admin/    (log in with an admin made by create_admin.py)
#   http://localhost:8000/driver/   (register, profile, vehicle, wait for approval, then click the map and press Go online)
#   http://localhost:8000/rider/    (register, pick two points on the map or by search, see the route and fare, request a ride; the nearest online driver within 3 km gets an offer panel with Accept / Reject and 15 seconds, then the next driver, or the ride ends as NO_DRIVER_FOUND)

# WebSockets (M3.1): backend only; see README.md for the protocol and the browser console snippet
docker compose exec redis redis-cli PUBSUB NUMSUB ws:events:0                    # 1 listener per backend process (dev channel uses database index 0)
docker compose exec redis redis-cli PUBLISH ws:events:0 '{"user_id": 5, "type":"test","data":{"hello":"world"}}'   # returns the number of processes listening
docker compose exec backend pytest tests/test_websocket.py                       # the live-server tests (about 15 s)

# live tracking (M3.2): run the simulator at 30 km/h, request a ride on /rider/ near the city center, watch the blue marker glide, and in DevTools, Network, WS, a driver_location frame about every 3 s
curl localhost:8000/rides/<ride_id>/driver -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER"      # name, vehicle, last known location (location is null once the ride has ended)
docker compose exec backend pytest tests/test_tracking.py                       # the 17 tracking tests (about 14 s)

# offer flow (M3.3): a driver's pending offer, accept / reject, and the rows
curl localhost:8000/drivers/me/offer -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER"                     # 200 OfferResponse (expires_in counts down) or 404 No pending offer
```
