```bash
curl -X POST localhost:8000/offers/<offer_id>/accept -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER"    # 200 with the ride; /reject gives 204
docker compose exec db psql -U uber -d uber -c 'SELECT id, ride_id, driver_id, status, pickup_distance_m, expires_at, responded_at FROM ride_offers ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 10;'
docker compose logs backend | grep -E "Offer [0-9]+ |sweeper"                              # one INFO line per offer event; the sweeper logs only failure streaks
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/simulator.py --drivers 30 --accept-rate 0.5 --reject-rate 0.3 --response-delay-max 4 ...   # drivers answer offers; --accept-rate 0 --reject-rate 0 makes everyone ignore them (a ride ends after 5 offers, about 75 s)
docker compose exec backend pytest tests/test_offers.py                                     # the 26 offer tests (about 15 s)

# trip flow (M3.5): the fake trip code is 1234 (FAKE_OTP in services/rides.py), stored on rides.otp from DRIVER_ASSIGNED until the trip starts or the ride is cancelled
curl localhost:8000/rides/<ride_id>/otp -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER"                  # rider only: {"otp":"1234"} while DRIVER_ASSIGNED or DRIVER_ARRIVED, else 409
curl -X POST localhost:8000/rides/<ride_id>/start -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"otp":"1234"}'   # 400 for a wrong code, 409 if not DRIVER_ARRIVED, 422 for a badly formed code
docker compose exec db psql -U uber -d uber -c 'SELECT id, status, otp FROM rides ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 5;'
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/simulator.py --drivers 20 --otp 0000 ...                    # a wrong code: each driver logs "wrong trip code for ride N" once and waits at the pickup
docker compose exec backend pytest tests/test_trip.py                                      # the 51 trip tests (about 32 s)
# two tabs by hand: driver tab (click the map, Go online) and rider tab (pick two points, Request ride); the driver accepts, arrives, types the code the rider sees, completes

# fares (M5.1): quote what a cancel costs, then see the fare columns of the last rides
#   curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER_TOKEN" localhost:8000/rides/<id>/cancellation-fee      # {"fee": 3000, "reason": "driver_arrived"}
#   docker compose exec -T db psql -U uber -d uber -c "SELECT id, status, final_fare, fare_breakdown FROM rides ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 10"
#   docker compose exec redis redis-cli HGETALL ride:<id>:trip      # the distance meter of a trip in progress
# surge (M5.2): the snapshot, by zone (admin token); refresh=true recomputes
#   curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_TOKEN" "localhost:8000/admin/surge?refresh=true"
#   docker compose exec redis redis-cli GET surge:snapshot
# earnings, commission and receipts (M5.4): the platform's revenue (admin token), a driver's own summary and entries, a rider's receipt; the commission is edited on the admin page's Pricing tab since M6.2 (or the SQL below)
#   curl -s "localhost:8000/admin/revenue?since=2026-10-09T00:00:00Z" -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_TOKEN"
#   curl -s localhost:8000/drivers/me/earnings -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER_TOKEN"

#   docker compose exec -T db psql -U uber -d uber -c "UPDATE pricing_rules SET commission_percent = 25 WHERE vehicle_type = 'economy'"   # restore 20 afterwards
# ratings (M6.1): rate a completed trip (rider or driver token), see the status and your own average, and read them as an admin
#   curl -s -X POST localhost:8000/rides/<id>/rating -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER_TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"score": 5, "comment": "Smooth ride"}'
#   curl -s localhost:8000/rides/<id>/rating -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER_TOKEN"        # can_rate, reason, expires_at, mine
#   curl -s localhost:8000/ratings/me -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER_TOKEN"              # count and the real average
#   curl -s "localhost:8000/admin/ratings?user_id=<user id>&max_score=2" -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_TOKEN"
#   .venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --scenario ratings --riders 20 --rounds 5      # one driver, three copies of every rating at once; RATINGS CLEAN
# admin dashboard (M6.2): open http://localhost:8000/admin/ (log in with an admin made by create_admin.py); the stats and the pricing editor by curl:
#   curl -s -G -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_TOKEN" localhost:8000/admin/stats --data-urlencode "since=2026-10-10T00:00:00Z" --data-urlencode "until=2026-10-11T00:00:00Z" -d bucket=hour -d utc_offset_minutes=330
#   curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_TOKEN" localhost:8000/admin/pricing-rules     # the version is in the rule
#   curl -s -X PATCH localhost:8000/admin/pricing-rules/economy -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"version": 1, "per_km": 1500}'
# trip history and saved places (M6.3): finished rides of the caller, newest first; a driver reads /drivers/me/history; saved places are rider only
#   curl -s "localhost:8000/rides/history?status=COMPLETED&limit=5" -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER_TOKEN"
#   curl -s -G localhost:8000/rides/history -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER_TOKEN" --data-urlencode "since=2026-10-01T00:00:00Z" -d before_id=380
#   curl -s localhost:8000/drivers/me/history -H "Authorization: Bearer $DRIVER_TOKEN"
#   curl -s -X POST localhost:8000/saved-places -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER_TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"label": "Home", "address": "12 MG Road", "lat": 12.9716, "lng": 77.5946}'
#   curl -s -X PATCH localhost:8000/saved-places/<id> -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER_TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"label": "Flat"}'
#   curl -s -X DELETE localhost:8000/saved-places/<id> -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER_TOKEN"
#   .venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --scenario places --riders 8 --rounds 5      # PLACES CLEAN; also with --riders 20 and --api-url http://127.0.0.1:8000,http://127.0.0.1:8001
# the invariants I1 to I24 are checked by every stress.py scenario and by: docker compose exec -T db psql -U uber -d uber -At -F '|' < simulator/invariants.sql

# wallet and payments (M5.3): the local fake Stripe, then the three .env values (see README, "Payments and wallet"), then recreate the backend
#   python simulator/fake_stripe.py --host 0.0.0.0 --webhook-secret whsec_fake_local_secret
#   curl -s -X POST localhost:8000/admin/wallets/<rider id>/adjust -H "Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -H "Idempotency-Key: fund-$(date +%s)" -d '{"amount": 50000, "note": "funding"}'
#   curl -s -X POST localhost:8000/wallet/topups -H "Authorization: Bearer $RIDER_TOKEN" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -H "Idempotency-Key: $(uuidgen)" -d '{"amount": 30000}'   # then open checkout_url, or: curl -X POST localhost:12111/pay/<session id>/complete
#   .venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --scenario payments --webhook-secret whsec_fake_local_secret --riders 20 --rounds 5      # needs the fake; PAYMENTS CLEAN
# concurrency stress test (M4.1, expected result since M4.2: exit 0): host only, needs docker compose (for psql) and an admin; STOP simulator.py for the drivers and riders scenarios
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --scenario drivers --rounds 20 --admin-email ... --admin-password ...   # I1 and I2: 20 riders, 3 drivers (defaults); exit 0 = no violation, exit 1 = an invariant broke
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --scenario riders --rounds 10 ...                                      # I3: every rider sends the same request twice at once
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --scenario fleet --rounds 5 --riders 20 ...                            # against a running simulator.py --drivers 30 --speed-kmh 30 --seed 1
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --scenario drivers --sequential --rounds 3 ...                         # control run: one request after another, exit 0
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --scenario drivers --rounds 1 --keep-last-round ...                    # leave the last round's state to look at
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --cleanup-only                                                         # cancel the stress riders' rides, take the stress drivers offline
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --scenario chaos --seed 1 ...                                       # M4.3: 30 riders and 10 drivers act at random for 60 s, then up to 45 s to settle; prints CHAOS CLEAN (exit 0) or CHAOS FOUND PROBLEMS (exit 1)
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --scenario chaos --riders 60 --drivers 20 --chaos-seconds 120 --tolerate-5xx ...   # heavy run; --tolerate-5xx while you restart the backend or stop redis by hand
docker compose exec -T db psql -U uber -d uber -At -F '|' < simulator/invariants.sql                            # the four invariants by hand: an empty section holds

# observability (M7.1): JSON logs, /metrics, Prometheus + Grafana (README, "Observability")
#   docker compose logs backend --no-log-prefix | jq -c 'select(.ride_id == 42)'      # one ride; also .request_id == "<id>", .level == "ERROR", .duration_ms > 500, .db_queries > 20
#   curl -s -H "Authorization: Bearer $METRICS_TOKEN" localhost:8000/metrics         # METRICS_TOKEN from .env; empty means 404
#   docker compose --profile observability up -d                                       # Grafana http://127.0.0.1:3000 (admin, GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD), Prometheus http://127.0.0.1:9090
#   docker compose --profile observability rm -sf prometheus grafana                   # stop only these two (down would remove the whole project)
#   .venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --scenario chaos --seed 1 --metrics-token "$METRICS_TOKEN"   # also compares /metrics with SQL; without the token those checks are SKIPPED

# two backend processes (M4.2): the second port is published by docker-compose.yml; the second uvicorn does not auto-reload, restart it after code changes
docker compose exec -d backend uvicorn app.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8001
.venv-sim/bin/python simulator/stress.py --api-url http://127.0.0.1:8000,http://127.0.0.1:8001 --scenario drivers --rounds 20 ...   # and --scenario riders --rounds 10
# stop it by recreating the container (no pkill in the image): docker compose up -d --force-recreate backend

# auth (M1.1)
docker compose exec backend python create_admin.py --email admin@example.com --name "Admin" --password 'at-least-8-chars'
curl -X POST localhost:8000/auth/register -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"name":"Rita","email":"rita@example.com","password":"rider-pass-1","role":"rider"}'
curl -X POST localhost:8000/auth/login -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"rita@example.com","password":"rider-pass-1"}'          # returns access_token
curl localhost:8000/auth/me -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN"
# Swagger: http://localhost:8000/docs -> Authorize -> paste the access_token
```

After changing `requirements.txt`, rebuild: `docker compose up -d --build backend`.

Run `alembic revision` with `--user $(id -u):$(id -g)` so the new file on the host is not owned by root. Review every autogenerated migration by hand; a "check" autogenerate right after `upgrade head` should be empty.

Automated tests exist for the ride state machine (M1.3), driver presence (M2.3), matching (M2.4), and the WebSocket plumbing (M3.1). M1.1 and M1.2 were verified by hand with curl and psql. M1.4 was verified with curl for every call the pages make, and with a headless Chrome click-through of the full flow in three tabs. M2.3 was verified with curl and redis-cli (auth matrix, TTL and GEOPOS before and after pings, 35 s expiry, validation, active ride, reject, 20 concurrent pings, Redis stopped), two pytest mutation checks, a check that pytest leaves dev Redis alone, and a headless Chrome click-through of the driver page (map, go online, move, 40 s idle, reload, closed tab, offline, reject and approve, active ride, outside click, Redis down and back, phone width, the M1.4 ride buttons). M2.2 was verified with curl and psql (OSRM directly, the seed migration up/down/up and an empty autogenerate, the estimate endpoint with hand-checked arithmetic and path order, every 4xx/5xx path, OSRM stopped, ride creation with a fake fare in the body), a pytest mutation check on the fare logic, and a headless Chrome click-through of the rider page with the admin and driver tabs (route and panel, quick changes with a held-back stale answer, too close, outside the city, OSRM down and back, request, reload mid-ride, full trip to COMPLETED, a ride with null estimate fields, phone width). M2.1 was verified with curl (auth, validation, caching, limiter, failure paths, about 9 real Nominatim calls in total) and a headless Chrome (Playwright) click-through of the rider page covering search, map clicks, markers, fit-to-ride, polling not moving the map or wiping typing, the full ride, the XSS address, and phone width. M2.4 was verified with redis-cli and psql plus curl (GEOSEARCH distances against the matching result, the three-driver scenario, stale cleanup after 35 s, the 2.8 km and 3.2 km boundary, reject, the double-assignment demonstration, Redis and OSRM stopped with row counts, the removed route), four pytest mutation checks, pytest run twice with the dev data checked before and after, and a headless Chrome click-through of the admin, two driver, and three rider tabs (tiles and Nominatim mocked, OSRM real; script outside the repo).

M2.5 was verified by running it (no automated tests, by decision), with curl, redis-cli, and psql: 5 drivers with `--seed 1` (5 users, 5 approved drivers, one vehicle each, 5 presence keys), movement (within 2 percent of speed x factor over 15 s; GEOPOS prints longitude first), 20 samples against OSRM `nearest` (at most 0.2 m), start points inside the circle, zero failed pings, Ctrl+C (exit in 0.2 s, no presence keys), a rerun with no duplicate users, `kill -9` and a restart resuming at the old positions, 50 and 200 drivers (numbers in the open issues), matching against the fleet (the driver GEOSEARCH ranked first got the ride), a full trip with the five `ride_events` and the driver's GEOPOS approaching the pickup and then the drop-off, a rider cancel mid-approach, `kill -9` during DRIVER_ASSIGNED and during IN_PROGRESS with the trip finishing after the restart, a rejected driver (403, only that driver stopped, approved again and restarted), Redis stopped for 40 s and OSRM stopped for 25 s, Ctrl+C with 50 drivers and one mid-ride (409 ignored, 0.4 s), the grep for Nominatim/OpenStreetMap (nothing) plus `ss` showing only connections to ports 8000 and 5000, and `pytest` (131 passed) and `/health` (200) afterwards. The browser click-through ran in headless system Chrome with Playwright from a throwaway venv and script outside the repo (OSM tiles and `/places/reverse` mocked in the browser, so no OSM or Nominatim server was called): the admin table listed all 200 sim drivers as approved; the rider page requested a ride from two map clicks, showed the estimate, was assigned a sim driver at once, and walked DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, IN_PROGRESS, COMPLETED on its own with the timeline growing from 2 to 5 rows (246 s for a 3.7 km route at `--speed-kmh 90`); a second request was matched to another driver; a pickup about 10 km from the center ended as NO_DRIVER_FOUND. Not tested: the driver page was deliberately not used with a sim driver (the README warns against it), a real phone browser, Ctrl+C on Windows, and more than 200 drivers.

M3.1 was verified with the 30 new tests plus by hand against the dev server: throwaway scripts in the backend container (the `websockets` client), redis-cli, curl, and a headless Chrome run. Auth succeeds for the three roles, every failure case closes 4401 (garbage, expired, tampered, a really deleted user, missing and non-string token, ping first, binary first frame) and "no message for 6 s" closes 4408 after 5.0 s; after auth ping/pong, the `error` reply, invalid JSON, a JSON array, a message without type, and a binary frame (4400), and a 100 KB frame (1009). Fan-out through `redis-cli PUBLISH ws:events:0` reached the rider (PUBLISH returned 1) and not the driver, then both of the rider's sockets; `ws:events:1` reached nobody (0). A second backend process on port 8001 and a third script calling `events.publish`: both sockets received it and PUBLISH returned 2. 200 events with 100 pings: all in order, 100 of 100 pongs. 6 sockets: the first closed with 4409, the other five got the event. A normal close and a `kill -9` of a client both logged a disconnect with a decreasing count. Stalled client: see the first open issue (9.99 s). Five bad channel messages gave five warnings and no traceback, and the next valid event arrived. Redis stopped for about 10 s: one "down" line, one "recovered" line, the existing socket stayed open and answered pings, a new socket authenticated, NUMSUB returned to 1, and a publish was delivered. The backend restarted with Redis down served `/health` 503 (redis "error"), login 200, and static pages 200, and its listener connected when Redis returned. `CLIENT KILL TYPE pubsub` recovered in under a second. `docker compose stop backend` with two sockets open took 0.42 s with no traceback (clients got 1012) and no pubsub client was left; a `--reload` restart with a socket open also ended without a traceback. curl: `/auth/me` gave 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` for no, tampered, expired, and deleted-user tokens, 200 for a valid one, and a rider token on `/drivers/me` gave 403. `pytest` passed twice (161 tests) with the dev Postgres counts and the Redis database 0 key set identical before and after; four mutation checks each failed at least one test. The simulator (10 drivers, `--speed-kmh 90 --seed 3`) ran 319 s with 1058 pings and 0 failed, matched a ride request and completed it through all four states. Browser (headless Chrome, Playwright, throwaway script outside the repo; two rider tabs, real UI login): console auth_ok, a redis-cli PUBLISH shown in the console, a publish shown only in the addressed tab, a wrong token closed 4401, seven sockets for one user closed the two oldest with 4409, ping/pong, Redis stopped and started with the socket staying open, and the rider page still logged in after a reload (no page errors). Not tested: a real phone browser, several uvicorn workers beyond the two-process check, Windows.

M3.2 was verified with the 17 new tests (178 in total, run twice with the dev Postgres counts and the Redis database 0 key set identical before and after) and by hand against the dev server. Three mutations each failed tests and were reverted (publish to the driver's `user.id`: 5 tests; lat and lng swapped: 4; a location for finished rides: 2). By hand with curl, redis-cli, and `websockets` scripts in the backend container: details for the rider, the driver, and an admin 200 (location equal to `GEOPOS`, whose first number is the longitude), another rider 404, no token 401, an unknown id 404, a NO_DRIVER_FOUND ride 404, no email, phone, or license in the JSON; 5 pings reached the rider's socket as exactly `ride_id, lat, lng, updated_at` with the ping response's `updated_at` and latitude 12.97 (not swapped), the other rider and the driver received nothing, and a second tab of the rider received the sixth; pings after ARRIVED and IN_PROGRESS published, after COMPLETED (7.6 s) and after a rider cancel (5 s) nothing, with the details location null and name and vehicle present; 35 s without pings gave presence expired, location null, a ping 409 with nothing published, go online during the ride published nothing, and the next ping did; 50 pings arrived in order with matching values; Redis stopped for about 10 s: pings and details 503 `Cache is unavailable`, the open socket stayed open, one "down" and one "recovered" line, and events resumed on the same socket. Load numbers are in the open issues. A simulator trip still reaches COMPLETED (many did), the M3.1 tests pass, and the pages and their files and `/health` return 200. Browser (headless Chrome through Playwright, throwaway scripts outside the repo, two rider tabs and a driver tab): the Your driver section, "connected", the fit once, frames every 3.0 s, a smooth glide with no jumps at maximum zoom, no drift between circle and tooltip, no movement of the map for 8 s after a pan, reload with the marker back after 0.44 s, both tabs gliding, reduced motion (jumps 3 s apart), ARRIVED (3.6 px from the pickup, still), IN_PROGRESS (ends 6 px from the drop-off), COMPLETED (marker gone, name and vehicle kept, no tracking lines), Request a new ride clearing it, a backend restart (code 1012, no reconnect, reload reconnects), a driver moved by hand (still marker, "Last location update" every 3 s, a 1863 m move shown as one jump), the driver page's markers (fitted once, map not moved by polling, clicks on both markers moved the driver to within 2 m of them, gone after the ride), the driver named `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` shown as plain text with no element and no alert, and 375 px width on both pages without horizontal scroll. Not tested: a real hidden tab (emulated), a real phone, the stalled-client case during tracking.

M3.3 was verified with 26 new tests and the updated old ones (204 in total, run twice, dev Postgres counts `346|238|151|471|200|1` and the Redis database 0 key set identical before and after) and by hand against the dev server. Four mutations each failed at least one test and were reverted: (a) dropping the "already offered this ride" exclusion (`test_reject_offers_the_ride_to_the_next_driver_and_never_back`, an IntegrityError from the unique constraint), (b) no expiry check in accept (`test_answering_after_the_deadline_is_409_even_before_the_sweep[accept]`), (c) the rider's cancel not cancelling the pending offer (`test_the_rider_cancelling_closes_the_pending_offer`), (d) `offer_created` published to the rider (`test_only_the_offered_driver_gets_offer_created`). By hand with curl, psql, redis-cli, and scripts using the `websockets` client from a scratch venv outside the repo: the migration (`\d ride_offers` showing both foreign keys, the unique constraint, the partial unique index `WHERE status = 'PENDING'`, and the status/expires_at index; a second PENDING row for one ride and a repeated (ride, driver) pair both failed inside a rolled-back transaction; downgrade then upgrade worked; a check autogenerate was empty and its file deleted). Three drivers at 500, 1200, and 2000 m: one PENDING offer for the nearest (TTL 15.06 s, distance 500), only the REQUESTED event, the full `OfferResponse` for the nearest and 404 for the others, the nearest's socket got exactly `offer_created {offer_id, ride_id}` and nobody else got anything. Reject chain A then B then C: each reject 204 with `responded_at`, the matching `offer_closed`/`offer_created` events, a second reject 409, the ride NO_DRIVER_FOUND with a null actor, `ride_updated` to the rider, 3 offers to 3 distinct drivers. Expiry: the next offer was created 0.30 s after the deadline. Accept: 200 with `driver_id` = the driver's `drivers.id`, actor = the driver's user id, `ride_updated` to the rider and `offer_closed` "accepted", a second accept 409, another driver 404, and a ping reached the rider as `driver_location`. Accept 50 ms after the deadline: 409 "This offer has expired" (before the sweep), "no longer available" after. Cancel while pending: ride and offer CANCELLED, `offer_closed` "ride_cancelled", accept 409, the next ride offered to the same driver. Busy rules (pending offer and accepted ride both make the second rider NO_DRIVER_FOUND), offline accept 409, admin-rejected accept 403 with the ride staying REQUESTED. Durability: a quick `docker compose restart backend` left the offer PENDING and it expired on time; a backend stopped for 19 s across a deadline expired the offer and made the next one about 1.5 s after startup, with no API call. Two sweepers: 6 simultaneous riders, 20 simulated drivers ignoring everything: all rides ended NO_DRIVER_FOUND after 76 s, no ride had two PENDING offers, no repeated (ride, driver), at most 5 offers per ride, every expiry logged once, no IntegrityError, deadlock, or traceback; then the contention variant with three extra tight sweepers (open issues). Redis stopped with an offer about to expire: pings and ride creation 503, row counts unchanged, the expired offer stayed PENDING, ONE sweeper warning (not one per second), and after Redis returned the offer was processed within about a second with ONE "recovered" line; OSRM stopped: 502 and unchanged counts. With the backend restarted while Postgres was DOWN: `/health` 503, static pages 200, one sweeper warning, and one recovery line when Postgres returned. Best-effort publish: `ACL SETUSER default -publish` (reverted) made `POST /rides` and accept answer 201 and 200 with a warning per failed publish, and the offer was found by polling. The simulator runs are in the open issues. Idle sweeper cost: 10 scans of `ride_offers` in 10 s. Regression: the state machine through an offer (illegal start 409, arrive, start, complete, illegal cancel 409), the estimate (4366 m, 354 s, 11419 paise), presence, `/places/map-config`, `/rider/`, `/driver/`, `/admin/` and all their JS and CSS and `/shared/ws.js` and `/health` returned 200, and the backend log held no traceback across several `--reload` restarts. Browser (headless system Chrome through Playwright, throwaway script outside the repo, real UI logins, tiles and reverse geocoding mocked): 41 checks passed and 0 failed: two driver tabs online with "Live updates: connected"; the offer panel on the nearest driver 0.9 s after the request with plain-text addresses, "Pickup is 0.5 km from you", "Trip: 6.1 km, 9 min", the fare, "Respond within 15 seconds", a progress bar, and green and red markers, the map not moving over two polls; reject moved the offer to the other driver while the rider still said "Looking for a driver"; an unanswered offer closed with "The offer expired." after 14.4 to 15.0 s and the rider page showed "No drivers are available nearby" 0.02 s later; accept switched the driver tab to the ride, the rider saw the driver, vehicle, and live tracking, moving the driver on its map updated the rider, and the trip ran to COMPLETED on both pages; a rider cancel closed the panel with "The rider cancelled the request."; a reload showed the remaining 8 s, not 15; a 9 s JavaScript freeze showed 5 s (clock value 4.6); at 375 px both buttons were inside the viewport with no horizontal scroll; a pickup address `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` was shown as text with no image and no alert; the same driver in two tabs got the offer in both, accept in one closed the other in 0.64 s, and an offline driver's Accept showed "You are offline. Go online first." without breaking the page; after `docker compose restart backend` the driver tab said "Live updates: disconnected (code 1012). Offers still arrive within a few seconds by polling.", a new offer still appeared in 1.9 s and accepting worked, and a reload restored "connected"; no page errors. Not tested: a real phone browser, Safari or Firefox, a really hidden tab (emulated), the stalled-client case during offers, accept racing the sweeper, and the double-booking races (M4).


M3.4 was verified with the 204 backend tests (unchanged, run twice) and with throwaway Node 22 harnesses (container `node:22-slim`, nothing committed): 35 logic tests on fake timers and a fake WebSocket (backoff caps 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 30, 30, 30 s at both ends of the jitter, the 10 s stability reset, every close code, heartbeat and pong, the 10 s connect watchdog, `disconnect()` in every state, `connect()` twice, the wake-up listeners, never more than one live socket), mutation-checked against 12 broken copies of ws.js (all caught, after one test was tightened), and a real-stack harness against `ws://localhost:8000/ws`: `docker compose restart backend` (reconnecting for about 12.5 s, open again with `reconnected: true`), `docker compose pause backend` for 48 s (dead connection found 24 s after the pause, hung connects abandoned after 10 s each, open 1 s after the unpause), `docker compose stop backend` for about 120 s (retry delays 1, 1, 3, 8, 8, 17, 30 s, open again within one retry period), Redis stopped for 12 s (no status change at all), six tabs (the first got 4409 and stayed closed for 30 s, `reconnectNow()` reconnected it and evicted the oldest of the rest), a tampered token plus a backend restart (4401, session cleared, one reload, no more attempts), and published events (delivered before and after a reconnect, not delivered when published while the backend was down). Regression: the five static URLs and `/health` returned 200; the simulator (`--drivers 10 --speed-kmh 90 --seed 3`) ran 387 s with 1287 pings ok and 0 failed, and ride 181 was offered, accepted, and completed while a harness socket received 104 `driver_location` events and one `ride_updated`. Try the outage cases by hand: `docker compose restart backend`, `docker compose pause backend` (then `unpause`), `docker compose stop backend` (then `start`), and six tabs of one rider login.

M3.5 was verified with the 255 tests (204 before plus 51 in `test_trip.py`, run twice with the dev Postgres counts and the Redis database 0 key count identical before and after) and by hand against the dev server. Before anything else the two dev rides stuck in DRIVER_ASSIGNED with no code (47 and 52) were cancelled through the API, `docker compose up --build` ran, and `alembic revision --autogenerate` produced an empty migration (deleted). With curl, psql, and `websockets` scripts in the backend container: after the accept `rides.otp` was `1234`; the rider's `GET /rides/{id}`, the driver's `/rides/active`, the admin's `GET /rides/{id}`, the events list, and the accept response had no `otp` key; `/otp` gave the rider 200, the driver and admin 403, another rider and an unknown id 404, no token 401, and a REQUESTED ride with a pending offer 409; arrive sent exactly `{ride_id, status: "DRIVER_ARRIVED"}` to the rider and the driver and nothing to a third rider, and `/otp` still worked; a wrong code `0000` gave 400 with the ride, the event count, and all three sockets unchanged, and no body, `"12"`, `"abcd"`, `"12345"`, and the number 1234 each gave 422; the right code gave 200, `started_at` set, `otp` NULL, `ride_updated` IN_PROGRESS on both sockets, `/otp` 409, and a second start 409; a start from DRIVER_ASSIGNED gave 409 with the right and the wrong code; complete gave `completed_at`, `ride_updated` COMPLETED on both sockets, and the five events in order with the right actors. Cancel matrix: rider from DRIVER_ASSIGNED, rider from DRIVER_ARRIVED, driver from DRIVER_ASSIGNED, and driver from DRIVER_ARRIVED each ended CANCELLED with `otp` NULL, the canceller as the event actor, and `ride_updated` CANCELLED on both sockets (the rider requested again at once and the same driver took the next ride); cancel during IN_PROGRESS by both and after COMPLETED by both gave 409 with no `ride_updated`; cancel from REQUESTED with a pending offer gave the offered driver `offer_closed` "ride_cancelled" and no `ride_updated`, and the rider `ride_updated`. Races, five rounds each with a new ride: two simultaneous starts gave one 200, one 409, and exactly one IN_PROGRESS event every time; a start racing a cancel (rider cancelling in odd rounds, driver in even) gave exactly one winner every time (4 times the cancel, once the start) and the status and events always agreed. Redis stopped: arrive, start, and complete of one ride and a cancel of another all answered 200 (the log held 8 `Could not publish ride_updated` warnings (two per status change, four changes) and one listener warning, no traceback), a new ride request gave 503 `Cache is unavailable`, and after Redis returned the state in psql was right (Redis has no persistence, so dev presence was lost). Simulator (`--drivers 30 --speed-kmh 90 --seed 1`, 10 riders requesting at the same instant around the center): all 10 rides went REQUESTED, DRIVER_ASSIGNED, DRIVER_ARRIVED, IN_PROGRESS, COMPLETED, the rider read the code `1234` in every one, 357 s, and the log held 10 `trip started`, 10 `trip completed`, and no warning; Ctrl+C logged `stopping, taking drivers offline`, left no presence keys, and exited. With `--otp 0000` and one ride the driver arrived, logged ONE `wrong trip code for ride 214` over about 24 s of retries, the ride stayed DRIVER_ARRIVED with the rider's code still readable, the rider's cancel ended it, and the driver logged `ride 214 is gone, wandering again` with no errors. Regression with curl: estimate, presence (online, presence, offline, online), offers (accept, reject then B's offer then expiry to NO_DRIVER_FOUND), `driver_location` events to the rider during a ride, a `docker compose restart backend` between arrive and start (ride and code intact, a new socket got the next event, start worked), and `/rider/`, `/driver/`, `/admin/`, their JS and CSS, `shared/*`, and `/health` all 200. Browser (headless system Chrome through Playwright from a throwaway venv, scripts outside the repo, the real UI, real tiles, and real Nominatim through the backend with about 1.5 s between clicks): the manual click-through steps 1 to 10 ran and passed, and step 11 in part (presence, the offer countdown, live tracking, and reconnect were all used along the way) (driver online by a map click, rider request, the offer 0.2 s later with the countdown, accept, "Your driver is on the way", name and vehicle, the code `1234` at 40 px, the Driver marker following a map click on the driver tab; arrive reached the rider in 83 ms with the code still shown; `0000` showed "Incorrect trip code" with the status unchanged and the typed value still there after 10.5 s of polling, `1234` moved both tabs to IN_PROGRESS and removed the code, and no Cancel button showed on either page; the marker followed again, complete showed "Trip completed" on both with a 5 row timeline and the marker gone 209 ms later, and Request a new ride worked; a rider cancel showed the driver page "The rider cancelled this ride." in 185 ms, a driver cancel showed the rider "The driver cancelled this ride. You can request a new one." and the driver "You cancelled this ride."; reloads in DRIVER_ASSIGNED and DRIVER_ARRIVED brought the code back and the driver's form back empty; the same rider in two tabs and the same driver in two tabs updated 1.12 to 1.16 s after an arrive click; a backend restart between arrive and start reconnected both pages in 3 s with the state, the code, and a half-typed `12` intact, and the start then worked in all four tabs). Step 9 (UI against the simulator, `--drivers 20 --speed-kmh 30`, a 1.2 km trip) took 459 s: the rider page stepped through every state with no page reload, the code shown only while assigned or arrived, and the timeline growing from 1 to 5 rows. Step 10 at 375 px: no horizontal scroll on either page, the code 309 px wide at 40 px, the input 309 x 54 px, buttons 40 px high, `maxlength` held `123456` to `1234`, and taps worked. Mutations, each reverted and the 119 tests of `test_trip.py` and `test_rides.py` passing again: removing the code check failed 2 tests, `ride_updated` only to the rider 8, `otp` in `RideResponse` 46, not clearing the code on start 6. Not tested: a real phone (the numeric keypad was not seen), Safari or Firefox, a really hidden tab, and the M4 races beyond the basic pairs.

M4.1 was verified by running the tool against the dev stack (no automated tests, by decision: the regression tests belong to M4.2) and with psql and redis-cli. Before anything else: `git status` showed only the two new files, `docker compose exec backend pytest` passed (255 tests, 98 s, run once), `/health` answered 200, the simulator was stopped, and `invariants.sql` run by hand showed I1 to I3 empty and I4 listing 5 old REQUESTED rides without an offer (cancelled through the API). Control runs (`--sequential`) exited 0 with zero violations and a 409 for every second request of a rider, again after the cleanup changes. The smallest reproduction (`--riders 2 --drivers 1`) broke I1 and I2 in 10 of 10 runs; the default, 50-rider, and 100-rider runs broke them in every round; the riders scenario broke I3 in 10 of 10 rounds; the fleet scenario broke I1 in 5 of 5 rounds and never I2 (explained in the baseline). Database evidence, the damage on a kept state, Ctrl+C (3 interrupts, 0 rides left, no stress driver in Redis), two consecutive `--cleanup-only` runs (both exit 0, clean invariants), failure handling (wrong database name and stopped db container: exit 2, `INVARIANTS NOT CHECKED` or the admin-login error; wrong admin password, API down, center outside the city, missing credentials: exit 2 or an argument error with no traceback; `--riders 1`, `--rounds 51`, `--repeat 6`, one of lat/lng: argument error), the import guard (`pip install -r simulator/requirements.txt`, exit 2), and the final checks (no Nominatim or OpenStreetMap host in the code, only SELECT statements, a write refused by Postgres in the tool's own session mode, stress drivers absent from Redis after cleanup) are described above. The artificial `sleep(0.05)` experiment was NOT run because the natural rate was 100 percent.

M4.2 was verified in the order of the plan: before any change `git status` was clean, `docker compose exec backend pytest` passed (255 tests), `SHOW default_transaction_isolation;` answered `read committed` and nothing in `app/` sets another level; the exception class of the lock timeout was found by provoking it. Then the two mechanisms were built behind a switch, tested in each mode (271 passed and 5 strict xfails per run, twice per mode; the 5 xfails are the race tests in mode none), compared with the matrix in "Fix and compare results" (24 runs plus the F1 and F2 experiments and a per-mode log pass), and the loser and the switch were removed. On the final code: 263 tests passed twice, six mutation checks each failed the intended tests and passed again after the revert, the stress commands all gave exit 0 (sequential control; drivers x20: offers 60 of 60; riders x10; 100 riders and 10 drivers: 30 of 30; the fleet scenario; both scenarios against two processes; the 10-run minimal reproduction: exactly one offer each time), the offer flow was exercised end to end with a script over HTTP (reject chain, expiry, accept, trip with the code, cancel, and a rejected, expired, completed, and cancelled offer each left the driver free), `simulator.py --drivers 30 --speed-kmh 90 --seed 1` completed all 10 requested rides, `/rider/`, `/driver/`, `/admin/`, and `/health` answered 200, the invariants on the dev database were empty and there was no `lock:*` key in Redis.

M4.3 was verified in the order of the plan. `git status` was clean on master, the full suite passed before any change (263 tests) and the dev database held no violators (the I1 to I7 queries and a plain duplicate check per index were empty, so nothing needed cleaning; no admin password was known, so `m43-admin@example.com` was created with `create_admin.py`). Migration `0005` was autogenerated and reviewed (only the three indexes, each with its `postgresql_where`), `alembic upgrade head` ran, `\d rides` and `\d ride_offers` show the three indexes with their WHERE clauses on the stored literals, a `BEGIN` ... `ROLLBACK` session with savepoints showed all three violations failing with the index name, `downgrade -1` then `upgrade head` worked, `alembic revision --autogenerate -m check` was empty (deleted), and the row counts (rides 16430, offers 9220, events 37090, users 534, drivers 294) were identical before and after. A throwaway SQL file (not committed) dropped the indexes inside a transaction, inserted rows breaking each of I1 to I7, and `invariants.sql` listed each offender; after `ROLLBACK` the counts were unchanged and the indexes were still there. The suite (302 tests) passed twice. The mutation checks and the stress, chaos, failure, paused-sweeper, and curl results are in "Edge cases". Not done: the browser click-through (no browser tool), see the open issues.
